Every 429 named in these flows carries `Retry-After` in whole seconds (since 2026-10-08, rule 680): the window of the bucket that refused, through `retryLater` in `src/lib/rate.ts` (an hour 3600, a day 86400, the 15-minute login and reset windows 900), 86400 for Reem's day of new items, and for a media start its own window or 60 s when the refusal is a full queue or a held lock (`BUSY_RETRY_S`). The JSON bodies are unchanged.

## 1. Signup (email first, then name and password)

Files: `src/app/[lang]/app/signup/page.tsx`, `src/components/app/AuthForm.tsx` (`SignupForm`), `src/lib/auth-client.ts`, `src/app/api/auth/signup/route.ts`, `src/lib/auth.ts`, `src/lib/referral.ts`, `src/lib/track.ts`.

1. Page redirects an already signed-in user to `/{lang}/app` (`signup/page.tsx:11`).
2. Client step 1: email is validated with `^[^\s@]+@[^\s@]+\.[^\s@]+$` (`AuthForm.tsx:46`). Step 2 asks for name and password; the client requires length >= 8, one letter, one digit (`passwordChecks`, `src/lib/auth-client.ts`). Each rule shows a check or cross and the words "met"/"not met" for screen readers, one `aria-live` line counts them ("2 of 3 password rules met"), and the submit button is `aria-disabled` (still focusable, described by the rules list): pressed early it names the unmet rules in a `role="alert"` message and moves focus to the password (rule 434, 2026-10-01). Query params read: `ref` (referral code), `url` (site for onboarding), `next` (kept only when `safeNext` accepts it: a same-site path, rules 543 and 694, kept in its normalised form, `src/lib/safe-next.ts`). Since 2026-10-08 the password input is a `PasswordField` with a show toggle that never submits (rule 696).
3. `POST /api/auth/signup` with `{email, name, password, lang, ref, phone?}`.
4. Server: rate limit 5 per IP per hour (429 `slow_down`); normalizes email (trim, lowercase); `validEmail` (the address regex, and no `:` or `#`); `strongPassword` (8+, a letter, a digit); optional phone needs >= 9 digits. Then, inside `withLoginLock(email)` (`lock:login:{email}`, the lock invite acceptance takes too), `getLogin(email)`: an owner account or a member login at the address gets 409 `exists`, the same answer for both (2026-09-29); otherwise `createUser`.
5. `createUser` (`auth.ts:155`): bcrypt cost 10, plan `trial`, 50 credits, `trialEnds` = now + 3 days, name clipped to 80 chars. Writes `user:{email}`, adds to `users:all`. It also stamps `sessionsAfter` = `createdAt`, so a session issued for an earlier account at the same address never works again. Since 2026-10-12 it also writes `consent` (`signupConsent` in `src/lib/consent.ts`, rule 1325): the terms and privacy versions from `src/lib/legal.ts` (`TERMS_VERSION`, `PRIVACY_VERSION`), the time, the signup language and `via: "signup_notice"`, built from code and never from the request (section 65). Member logins are never created here (section 26).
6. `registerCode` writes `ref:{CODE}`. If `ref` matches `^[A-Za-z0-9]{6,10}$`, `applyReferral` runs (section 15).
7. `createSession(email)` sets cookie `thakeen_session`. `track(email,"signup")`.
8. Errors (rule 433, 2026-10-01): the form posts through `postJson`, which never throws, and clears its busy state in `finally`. A 429 says "too many sign-ups from this connection, try in an hour", a dropped connection says to check the connection, a 5xx or an HTML error page says the problem is on our side; known codes (`exists`, `bad_email`, `weak_password`, `bad_phone`) keep their messages, and an unknown code never reaches the screen raw. Client navigates to `next`, or `/{lang}/app/start?url={url}` when a site URL was carried (the owner reviews and confirms it there before anything is read, section 6), else `/{lang}/app/start`. A carried URL never starts onboarding by itself: until 2026-09-28 it went straight to `/{lang}/app/chat?onboard={url}`, so a link like `/en/app/signup?url=attacker.example` chose the new workspace's brand.
9. Rate limit 5 per IP per hour; no email verification. Cloudflare Turnstile (2026-10-01, rule 426) when `TURNSTILE_SITE_KEY` and `TURNSTILE_SECRET_KEY` are both set: the form shows the widget on step 2 and sends its token as `turnstile`; the route verifies it (`captchaRefusal`, `src/lib/turnstile.ts`) after the input checks and before `getLogin`, so without a passing token no account is created and no address is confirmed to exist (400 `captcha_failed`, 503 `captcha_unavailable` when Cloudflare does not answer). A refused submit resets the widget (tokens are single use). With either key unset nothing is checked.

## 2. Login

Files: `login/page.tsx`, `AuthForm.tsx` (`LoginForm`), `src/lib/auth-client.ts`, `src/app/api/auth/login/route.ts`.

1. `POST /api/auth/login {email, password}`.
2. Rate limits 20 per IP and 8 per login per 15 min (429 `slow_down`). `getLogin(email)` finds the login: the owner record `user:{email}` or a member record `member:{email}` (the owner record wins should both exist); since multi-workspace M1 (2026-10-19) it also reads the person (`person:{pid}` in the same round, the precedence and stale guard of `personFromRaw`) and, for a person no legacy record backs, takes the owned account or the oldest live membership from `orgs:{pid}` (no membership left: the address is nobody's, the same 401 as an unknown address). `verifyPassword(login.person, password)` compares against THAT login's own hash (a `person:` row older than the legacy record's last password change never lends its hash, plan reconciliation 5), so the owner's password never opens a member login and a member's never opens the owner's. Unknown login or bad password: 401 `invalid` (same response for both).
3. Owner: 403 `suspended` (only after a correct password). Member (2026-09-29): 401 `invalid` when the owner record is missing, `memberOf` does not name it, or the member was accepted before the owner record was created; 403 `suspended` when the owner is suspended; 403 `disabled` when the member is paused by the seat limit (section 26 step 13).
4. `createSession(login email)`, `track(owner email, "login")` (analytics count accounts), `{ok:true}`. Client goes to `next` when `safeNext` accepts it (a same-site path: no control character, no backslash, a single leading `/`, same origin once parsed, and not `//host` once its dot segments resolve; the client goes to the normalised path; rules 543, 694) or `/{lang}/app`. Every later request resolves a member session to the owner's account again (section 5).
5. No lockout beyond the rate limits.
6. The form (rule 433, 2026-10-01) says "wrong email or password" only for a 401; 429 says to wait 15 minutes, 403 `suspended` and `disabled` have their own messages, a dropped connection and a 5xx each say what happened, and the button always comes back. Since 2026-10-08 the password field is `autocomplete="current-password"` with a show toggle (`PasswordField`, rule 696).

## 3. Logout

`src/components/app/LogoutButton.tsx` posts to `POST /api/auth/logout` (`src/app/api/auth/logout/route.ts`), which calls `destroySession()` (`auth.ts`): deletes the session record (`dropSession`: the hashed key `session:h:{sha256(token)}` and the legacy raw key, rule 1346) and the `thakeen_session` cookie. It does not touch `nashr_admin`, `thakeen_return` or `thakeen_impersonate`.

## 4. Password reset

### Forgot
Files: `forgot/page.tsx`, `AuthForm.tsx` (`ForgotForm`), `src/app/api/auth/forgot/route.ts`, `src/lib/notify.ts`, `src/lib/rate.ts`.

1. `POST /api/auth/forgot {email, lang, turnstile?}`. Bad email format: 400. Then the per-IP limit (8 per 15 min), so junk tokens cannot buy unlimited siteverify calls. With both Turnstile keys set, the token is verified next, before the per-address limit and the lookup (rule 427): a missing or rejected token is 400 `captcha_failed` whether or not the address has an account, and does not use up the address's three links.
2. Rate limits: 8 per 15 min per IP (before the token check) and 3 per 15 min per email (after it). When exceeded the route still answers `{ok:true, mail}`.
3. `getLogin(email)`. When the login exists and mail can reach a customer (`mailReady()`: `RESEND_API_KEY` and `MAIL_FROM` both set): a 32-byte hex token is generated, its SHA-256 is stored as `pwreset:{hash}` with `{email, kind}` (`kind` is `owner` or `member`) for 3600 s, and a link `${appOrigin()}/{lang}/app/reset#t=...` is emailed (the token in the URL fragment, never a query string, so it stays out of server, proxy and edge logs and the Referer header; rule 546) to the login's own address. Since 2026-10-08 that email is multipart: the text as before, and an HTML part in the form's language (`resetPassword`, `src/emails/reset-password.ts`, section 49) whose button and fallback link carry the same fragment, so Resend click tracking must stay off for the sending domain. A member whose owner record is gone gets no link and the same answer.
4. Response is always `{ok:true, mail:boolean}`; `mail` says whether email delivery is configured, not whether the account exists. The forgot page shows a WhatsApp link when `SUPPORT_WHATSAPP` is set.

### Reset
Files: `reset/page.tsx`, `AuthForm.tsx` (`ResetForm`, the same rules display and error handling as signup, rules 433-434), `src/app/api/auth/reset/route.ts`. `ResetForm` reads the token from `location.hash` once, removes the fragment with `history.replaceState`, and posts it in the JSON body; a `?token=` query is never read (rule 547). Since 2026-10-08 the new-password input is a `PasswordField` with a show toggle (rule 696).

1. `POST /api/auth/reset {token, password}`. Rate limit 10 per 15 min per IP (429).
2. Password rule server-side: length >= 8, one Unicode letter, one digit. Token must match `^[a-f0-9]{64}$`.
3. Looks up `pwreset:{sha256(token)}`. Missing: 400 `bad_token`. Single use: `pwresetuse:{hash}` is taken with SET NX (1 h) before the record is deleted, so two requests racing on one link cannot both pass (the second gets 400 `bad_token`).
4. `getLogin(record.email)`: the login must still be of the kind the record names (a record without `kind`, written before teams, is an owner's), else 400 `bad_token`, so a member removed since, or an address that changed sides, gets nothing. A suspended owner (for either kind) gets 403 `suspended`; the token is spent either way.
5. Owner: under `lock:person:{pid}` then `withUserLock`, `revokeTeamSessions` and the account save, then `updatePerson(pid, {passHash, sessionsAfter, emailVerifiedAt})` with both locks held (multi-workspace M1, 2026-10-19; the link proves the address, rule 769), then `revokeAllMcp`, the login's Google link (`unlinkGoogle`, rule 2301) and the sign-in (step 7). A `p_` owner (one no legacy record backs) is reset the same way: its new hash lands on `person:` only, never on the account record. A person lock held past the wait answers 409 `busy`. Every owner session, every member session and every MCP token dies with the old password; each member signs back in with its own. Since rule 2992 (2026-10-22) this covers EVERY account the person owns (`ownedBy`, src/lib/email-move.ts, the lister the address-change revert uses), not only the one `getLogin` picked: the other owned accounts' `teamSessionsAfter` is stamped first, each under its own user lock inside the person lock (an account whose owner is no longer this person is skipped), then the MCP tokens and grants, the linked calendars, and the feed and booking links of every owned account's owner and roster go. A legacy owner owns exactly one account, so nothing changes for it.
6. Member (2026-09-29): the membership must still hold (`getMembership` of the owner's account); `updatePerson(pid, {passHash, sessionsAfter: now})` writes the person and, for a legacy member, the member record (a `p_` member: `person:` only), which signs out that member's other devices; the owner's hash, sessions and tokens are untouched. The member's own feed, booking links and Google link go too, and should the person also own workspaces (a `p_` person), their MCP tokens, grants and linked calendars (rule 2992). Then the sign-in (step 7), unless the member is seat-paused: the password is set but the answer is 403 `disabled` with no session.
7. Since 2026-10-19 (rule 2300) the sign-in is `finishLogin(login, {via: "password"})`, the one every way in shares, never `createSession` directly: a login with two-step sign-in on gets 401 `mfa_required` with the challenge cookie and no session, and `ResetForm` then opens `/{lang}/app/login?mfa=1` for the code. A reset link proves the mailbox, not the authenticator.

### Change password while signed in
`PATCH /api/account {currentPassword, newPassword}` (`src/app/api/account/route.ts`): session and `forbid(u, "password.change")` (every login; refused to a founder impersonating); `strongPassword`; the current password is checked against the ACTING login's own hash, never the account's for a member (401 `wrong_password`), behind `rate:pwchange:{login}`: 10 wrong passwords per hour, counted before the compare and given back on a right one (429 `slow_down` with Retry-After, rule 2991, the `mfapw:` pattern). Since multi-workspace M1 (2026-10-19) both kinds write through `updatePerson(pid, {passHash, sessionsAfter})` (the person row and its legacy record, "Credential writes" in section 5), and a person lock held past the wait answers 409 `busy` with nothing written. Owner: the new hash and `sessionsAfter` on `person:` and `user:`, then `revokeAllMcp` of every account the person owns (`ownedBy`, rule 2992; before, only the workspace the session acted in) and the login's Google link (`unlinkGoogle`, rule 2301). Member (2026-09-29): `person:` and the member record only, with its own `sessionsAfter = now` (its feed, booking links and Google link go too); the owner's hash, sessions, MCP token and connected apps are untouched. Either way this device's session is destroyed and a new one issued for the same login, so only the other devices are signed out. Since 2026-10-08 the profile's current and new password inputs are `PasswordField`s with a show toggle (rule 696); account deletion on the same page still asks for the password with the browser's `prompt()`, and `DELETE /api/account` counts wrong ones in its own `rate:acctdelete:{login}` (10 per hour, 429 `slow_down`, rule 2991).

## 5. Session handling and cookie names

| Cookie | Set by | Value | Attributes | Lifetime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `thakeen_session` | `createSession` (`src/lib/auth.ts:234`), `api/admin/impersonate/route.ts` (restores the founder's saved token) | 32-byte hex token; server side `session:h:{sha256(token)}` (since 2026-10-12, rule 1345; a session written before is still read from `session:{token}` until it ends) holds `{email, at, imp?}`: the LOGIN email (owner or member), the issue time, and on an impersonation session the founder behind it (`imp`) | httpOnly, sameSite lax, `Secure` unless plain http to the loopback interface, path `/` | 30 days (cookie and Redis TTL); 2 hours for an impersonation session, and one opened from a key session also ends with it |
| `nashr_admin` | `createAdminSession` (`src/lib/auth.ts`), called by `api/admin/login/route.ts` | A random 32-byte hex id naming `adminsession:{sha256(id)}` (never the key) | httpOnly, lax, Secure by transport | Cookie: 12 hours (the absolute cap, not renewed). Record: 15 minutes, renewed at most once a minute by each admin request and by each request through a customer impersonation it opened, never past 12 hours from the key entry |
| `thakeen_return` | `api/admin/users/[email]/route.ts:120` | The founder's own session token, saved while impersonating | httpOnly, lax, secure in production | 2 hours |
| `thakeen_impersonate` | `api/admin/users/[email]/route.ts:121` | Email of the impersonated user (display and audit only; `byOf` never reads it: who acted comes from the session record's `imp`) | httpOnly, lax, secure in production | 2 hours |
| `thakeen_lang` | `src/components/LangToggle.tsx:8` (client JS) | `ar` or `en` | not httpOnly, lax | 1 year |
| `thakeen_theme` | `src/lib/theme.ts` `themeCookie`, written by `ThemeToggle` (client JS, rule 1204) | `system`, `light` or `dark`; read only by the head script and `ThemeScope` in the browser, never by the server | not httpOnly, lax, `secure` on https | 1 year |
| `thakeen_rail` | `src/lib/rail.ts` `railCookie`, written by the fold button in `Sidebar` (client JS, rules 3123-3127) | `collapsed` or `open`; read by the app layout (`cookies()`, `railCollapsed`) only to draw the desktop rail folded or open on the first paint | not httpOnly, lax, `secure` on https | 1 year |

Since 2026-10-13 (rules 1810-1813, an expand release): the cookie and browser-storage names live in `src/lib/cookie-names.ts`. Every one was `nashr_*` and is now `thakeen_*`; reads try the new name, then the old one, writes use the new name and clear the old one where the code writes the cookie, and sign-out, "stop viewing" and a used OAuth state delete both (`destroySession` also ends the session record behind each name). `nashr_admin` and `nashr_admin_2fa` keep their names until the staff-identity change rewrites their code. The contract release, after the 30-day cookies written before the rename have expired, deletes the `LEGACY` map and the fallbacks.

`currentUser()` (`src/lib/auth.ts`, `resolveSession`; rewritten 2026-09-29): cookie, `sessionRecord` (`session:h:{sha256(token)}`, then the legacy `session:{token}` for a 64-hex token, rule 1345), then in one parallel round `user:{login}`, `member:{login}` and `activebrand:{login}`.

- Owner login (`user:` exists; it wins should both exist): `null` when suspended or when the session predates `sessionsAfter` or `devicesAfter` (rule 1170). Otherwise the record with `brandId`, `scope` and actor `{kind:"owner", role:"owner"}`, or `{kind:"founder", role:"owner", impersonating: imp}` when the session carries `imp`.
- Member login: `null` when the member is disabled, its role or `memberOf` is malformed, the session carries `imp`, or the session predates `member.sessionsAfter` or `member.devicesAfter`. Then the owner record and the owner's brand list are read in one round, and `null` again when the owner is missing or suspended, the session predates `owner.teamSessionsAfter`, or the member's `acceptedAt` is earlier than the owner's `createdAt`. Otherwise the OWNER's record with actor `{kind:"member", email, name, lang, role}` and scope `scopeFor(owner, brandId)`, where `brandId` is the member's own active brand when it is one of the owner's active brands, else `default`.

Membership is re-read on every request; a session never caches the owner. `saveUser` strips `actor`, `scope` and `brandId`. Sessions are not rotated and not bound to IP or user agent; they stop working through the revocation stamps (Revocation, below), suspension (read time), the login being removed, or the account being wiped (the record is gone).

Session ids (account-id R1 and multi-workspace M1, 2026-10-19, rules 2132 and 2136): `createSession(email)` writes `pid` (the person) and `acct` (the account the session stands for) beside `email` and `at`, from `sessionIds` in `src/lib/auth.ts`: `getLogin(email)`, then `pickAccount(person)` in `src/lib/membership.ts`. The pick order: the account a caller prefers (`prefer`, none in M1), then `lastorg:{pid}`, each only while the person still holds it on the forward records (the account record and `mbr:{aid}:{pid}`, rechecked with `roleIn` and `membershipHolds`); then the account the person owns; then the oldest live membership (`acceptedAt`, ties by account id). A legacy owner or member (its person read from `user:` or `member:`) gets its home account: the owner's own id, the member's `memberOf`, exactly what R1 wrote. With `MULTI_ORG` off the pick is always the home account: `createUser` writes the owner's `orgs:` entry and the team writes (section 91) write `mbr:` and `orgs:` for every member, but each names only the login's home account, nothing writes `lastorg:` for a real login in M1, and no route passes `prefer`. An impersonation session keeps R1's shape (`pid` = the account's owner, `acct` = the account). A failure while reading the ids writes the R1-shaped record without them (`email`, `at`); `currentUser` reads that as before.

Resolution on persons and memberships (multi-workspace M1, 2026-10-19, rules 2111, 2119, 2131, 2132, 2135; `architecture.md` 4(f) item 8): a session with `pid` and `acct` resolves in ONE round after the session read, for an owner, a legacy member and a `p_` member alike (a member pays one round less than before), and ONLY into the account the record names: the person (the index still names `pid`, not suspended, not older than its stamps), the account (exists, not suspended, verified) and the role (`roleIn`, and for a membership `membershipHolds` and `teamSessionsAfter`) are re-checked on every request, so a removed, left or paused membership, or a suspended account, ends that account at the next request while the person's other accounts keep working; a suspended person resolves nowhere. The actor carries `pid` and `home`. The active brand is per (person, account): `activebrand:{pid}:{aid}`; in the person's home account the legacy `activebrand:{login}` is read first and a switch writes both keys alike (so a rollback keeps the choice, and a choice a rolled-back build made still wins after the roll forward); elsewhere the legacy key is never read or written. A session written before R1 resolves exactly as before through `resolveLegacy` and is never rewritten on read. `currentPerson()` answers the person with or without a valid account (the "no workspace" state); no route calls it in M1.

Credential writes (multi-workspace M1, 2026-10-19, rules 2132 and 2139; `src/lib/person.ts`): `updatePerson(pid, patch)` is the one writer of a person's password hash, `sessionsAfter`, `devicesAfter`, name, language and `emailVerifiedAt`. Under `lock:person:{pid}` it reads the person as `getPerson` does (row, `user:{pid}`, `member:{pid}` in one round), writes the legacy record that backs the person FIRST (an owner's account under `lock:user`, re-checked with `ownerOf`; a member's record under `lock:member`, re-checked with `memberPid`), then `SET person:{pid}` as that view plus the patch. The two revocation stamps (`sessionsAfter`, `devicesAfter`) are always the LATEST of the row, the legacy record (as read, and again as it is under its lock when mirrored) and the patch, so a sign-out written by a rolled-back build (R1's "sign out other devices" moves only the legacy `devicesAfter`) is never undone; `personFromRaw` reads them the same way when the row wins. The row's `createdAt` is the legacy birth (an owner's `createdAt`, a member's `acceptedAt`) and every stamp is mirrored, so `personFromRaw`'s birth and stale guards never fire on a row this writer made, and a build rolled back to R1 reads the new credentials from the legacy record. A `p_` person (no legacy record) writes `person:` only. `emailVerifiedAt` only fills an absent stamp, never moves an account's existing date, and also stamps every account in `orgs:{pid}` whose `ownerPid` is this person (plan reconciliation 11; one `pmap`, each under its own `lock:user`); a member's proof stamps no account. Lock order: person, then member or user; locks are not reentrant, so a caller holding one passes `personLocked` or `userLocked` (the account whose lock it holds and whose record it saved). A person lock held past the wait throws `person_lock_timeout`, which the profile, password, sign-out-others, reset, verify-code and founder routes answer as 409 `busy`. A new login's row is written whole by `createPersonRecord` under the caller's `lock:login` and then `lock:person` (signup and Google signup: `createUser` takes the person lock before its first write, so a timeout answers 409 `busy` from signup and the failed-sign-in redirect from the Google callback, with nothing written), so a leftover row of a deleted account at the same id lends it nothing (reconciliation 6). The address move writes it without the person lock: it runs inside `lock:user`, which comes after `lock:person` in the order, and the new address is not yet claimed, so nothing else can write that person; the moved account and the new row carry the same, later `devicesAfter`. A Google signup's proof is written on the person and the account in the same write; account deletion (`wipeAccount`) deletes the owner's `person:` and `orgs:` keys. A Google signup's person has no hash; its legacy account keeps a random one an R1 build compares. `User.passHash` is optional since this task: no reader takes it from the account (`ownPasswordMatches` and `actingLogin` read the person).

Which account a request acts in (multi-workspace M1, 2026-10-19, rules 2110, 2112, 2131; section 91): the session's `acct`, set at sign-in and re-verified on every request against the person, the account and the membership read in that request; no body, query, header or path segment names it, and no route reads a person or account id from the request (`tests/multi-org-scans.test.ts`). A paused, removed or forged membership ends that account at the next request while the person's other accounts keep working (`tests/multi-org-isolation.test.ts`). With `MULTI_ORG` off nothing changes `acct` after sign-in. From M2 the workspace is also in the page address (`/{lang}/app/w/{handle}/…`, decision 7), a handle chosen among the person's own memberships and re-checked the same way, with `x-thakeen-ws` as a second guard on state-changing calls (409 `workspace_missing`). Since M3 a session written before R1 is refused for a suspended person as well (section 91.1).

## 6. First-run onboarding inside the chat

Files: `start/page.tsx`, `src/components/app/DictionaryStart.tsx`, `src/components/app/StartLaunch.tsx`, `src/lib/dictionary.ts`, `src/app/api/onboard/confirm/route.ts`, `src/lib/onboard-confirm.ts`, `chat/page.tsx:35-50`, `src/components/app/ChatPanel.tsx:483`, `src/app/api/onboard/route.ts`, `src/lib/crawl.ts`, `src/lib/onboard.ts`, `src/app/api/onboard/plan/route.ts`, `src/components/app/OnboardCards.tsx`.

1. A user without `brand:{scope}` is redirected from the inbox, chat and reports pages to `/{lang}/app/start`. Since 2026-10-04 (rules 524-526) the start page shows a person who can both onboard and write the brand (`can(u, "onboard") && can(u, "brand.write")`, admin and owner) the Thakeen Dictionary game instead of the URL wizard; anyone else gets the member wait note, and once the brand exists everyone is sent to `/{lang}/app`. Steps 1-6 (brand name and sector, five voice words, a sample plan and drafts tagged «مثال», the approve tap, the analysis and measurement preview) live in the page's reducer: nothing is sent or saved, and leaving loses them. Step 7 offers two paths: «عندي رابط» collects a site URL (below), and «ما عندي موقع» asks what they sell and who buys, then saves the answers plus those two through `PUT /api/brand` (`noSiteBrand`: name, industry and tone from the answers, dialect and language from the page's language) and opens `/{lang}/app`. That save is create-only (`create: true`, rule 527): when a brand was built meanwhile, in another tab or by another admin, the route answers 409 `exists`, keeps that brand whole and the page opens `/{lang}/app` with it. A manual alternative under the title on every step links to `/{lang}/app/onboarding` (brand form, `PUT /api/brand`). A URL carried in from a link (signup `?url=`, start `?url=`, chat `?onboard=`) only pre-fills the field and shows a review note ("Check this link before Noor starts"); nothing is read until the owner presses Go. Since 2026-09-30 (rule 246) the start page passes `setupCost = onboardCost(u.brandId)` and the balance to the wizard: for the first brand (the default scope) it says setup is free; for an added brand it states the 9 credits for the build from the URL (the manual form is free) and the balance before Go, and when the balance is short it says so, turns Go off and offers "View plans" (the credits paywall). When a charge for this brand is already held (`User.onboardPaid[scope]`, a run that did not finish), it passes `setupPaid`: the wizard says the setup is already paid for and keeps Go on at any balance, as the server does.
2. Go posts `{url}` to `POST /api/onboard/confirm`: session and admin+ (`onboard`) required, URL trimmed and clipped to 300 characters (`onboardUrl`), length >= 4 else 400 `bad_url`, 409 `already_onboarded` when the workspace has a brand. For an added brand without a held charge (`User.onboardPaid[scope]`) it answers 402 `trial_over` or `no_credits` with `need` and `credits` and writes nothing (the wizard raises the paywall and says nothing was charged). Otherwise it writes `onboardok:{scope}` = the URL (1 day) and nothing else: no crawl, no model call, no charge, and answers `{ok, cost}` (0 or 9), plus `paid: true` when a charge is held. Since 2026-10-04 (rule 522) the body may also carry `answers`, the first-login game's (brand name, sector, five voice words, language; `parseAnswers` in `src/lib/dictionary.ts`): when sent they must be a finished game or the request is refused 400 `bad_answers` before anything is written; valid ones are stored as `onboardgame:{scope}` beside the URL for the same day, and a confirm without them deletes any left by an earlier one. The session cookie is SameSite=Lax, so another site cannot submit it for a signed-in owner. The wizard then routes to `/{lang}/app/chat?onboard={url}` (also on 409, where the chat opens as usual).
3. The chat page without a brand reads `onboardok:{scope}` and hands that URL to `ChatPanel`. The `?onboard=` value itself is never used to build: without a confirmation the page redirects to `/{lang}/app/start?url={value}` so the owner reviews it.
4. `ChatPanel` posts `{url}` to `POST /api/onboard`. Server requires a session, admin+ (`onboard`) and a URL of length >= 4, then, in this order: 409 `already_onboarded` when `brand:{scope}` exists; 403 `unconfirmed` unless the URL equals `onboardok:{scope}` (checked in code, before the paused check, the daily allowance, the lock and the crawl, so an unconfirmed URL costs nothing); 503 `paused`; for an added brand without a held charge, a 402 JSON answer `{ok:false, error, need, credits}` when the trial is over (`trial_over`), Noor's monthly cap in that scope would be passed (`agent_cap`, with `agent`, `spent`, `cap`) or the balance is under 9 (`no_credits`), before the daily allowance and the lock so a certain refusal costs none of the three tries; 429 after three runs a day per account; lock `onboardlock:{scope}` taken with an owner token (`db.lockOwned`, 330 s = `maxDuration` + 30, so a second run can only start once the first is over or was killed), a second concurrent run gets 409 `busy`; the run releases it with `unlockOwned`, which deletes it only while it still holds that token. Under the lock the brand and the confirmation are read again (409 or 403, lock released). Then, for an added brand (since 2026-09-30): `chargeOnboard(u.email, 9, {scope, agent: "noor", cap})` under the account lock reuses a held charge (`User.onboardPaid[scope]`, restarting its clock) or, with none, refuses an ended trial and charges, writing the debit and the hold in the same save; a refusal releases the lock and answers the same 402 JSON before any stream, and a new charge is followed by the agent-cap warning. The first brand charges nothing. Mock mode charges too. `ChatPanel` shows the paywall and "nothing was charged" on a 402, and on a 429 a daily-limit message that says this try charged nothing and a setup already paid for stays paid.
5. SSE stream (10 s ping). Steps: intro message, `crawl(url)` of the confirmed URL (server-side fetch of the user URL, 9 s timeout covering the body as well since 2026-10-19, follows redirects, only the first 2 MB of the page read (`responsePrefix`, rule 2246, branch only), `crawl.ts:45`), then `buildFromSite`: the crawled snapshot is sent as one `<site_snapshot>` block (`asData`) under a data-not-instructions line, the owner's hints outside it (`onboardUserMessage`, rule 574), in three parallel `claude-opus-5-5` calls (`claude-opus-5` until 2026-10-09) from the same snapshot (core: brand, brand.md, plan, goal, summary; identity: guidelines, opportunities; market: research, strategy), one retry per part; the status line advances as each part lands. The game's answers are read under the lock from `onboardgame:{scope}` (`confirmedAnswers`, validated again), never from the request: when present they replace the request's `hints` for the model, and their name, industry and tone are written over the built brand before it is saved, so the owner's own words win over the crawl (rule 523).
6. Writes: `saveBrand(scope)`, each returned file via `writeFile(scope, ...)`, `preferences.md` if absent, `onboardplan:{scope}`, deletes `onboardok:{scope}` and `onboardgame:{scope}` and drops the held charge (`settleOnboard`): one confirmation, one build, one charge. The `done` event carries the balance, which the chat's credit pill takes. Every assistant message is persisted to `chat:{scope}` via `push`. A failed or paused run keeps the confirmation, so "refresh to try again" works for a day.
7. Credits (since 2026-09-30, rule 246): the first brand's onboarding charges nothing; an added brand's charges `ONBOARD_EXTRA_BRAND_COST` (9, `src/lib/pricing.ts`) to Noor in its scope, as in step 4. When the run fails before `saveBrand` (a model error, `ModelPaused`, a crawl failure) `refundOnboard` gives the held 9 back and drops the hold in one save, so it happens once: a second refund finds no hold and gives nothing, and a refund whose save was written but reported an error has still dropped the hold, so the next run pays afresh rather than building free. The error event carries `refunded: 9` and the new balance, and the chat says the credits were refunded, the balance, and that a retry pays again. A failure after `saveBrand` is not refunded: the brand is delivered, the hold is dropped, the error event carries `saved: true` and the chat says the brand file was saved and to refresh (a rerun answers 409). A run the platform kills (`maxDuration` 300) runs no catch; its hold, which has no TTL, lets the next confirmed run reuse the charge however late the owner returns, and the start page says the setup is paid. The daily cron (`sweepOnboardHolds` on the default brand's entry) resolves every hold no run charged or reused for a day: refunded when its brand does not exist, dropped when it does (a success whose own drop failed), archived brands included. The charge, a refusal to pay and each refund are rows of that brand's activity log (section 32 step 2), so what the Governance page counts against Noor there has its rows. Onboarding runs once per workspace (409 once `brand:{scope}` exists) and at most three times a day per account, so an account sets up at most three brands a day.
8. `GET /api/onboard/plan` returns the stored plan; `POST {index, added}` flips the `added` flag (admin+, `onboard`). Phone is requested in-thread and saved through `PUT /api/profile` (`OnboardCards.tsx:75`), which needs `profile.account` (owner); the card is still shown to admins and members (`permissions.md` 5.26). Signup keeps the phone optional, but the Profile requires it (since 2026-10-01): an owner without one uses the app as before, and their first profile save is refused 400 `required` `phone` until a valid one is sent (`tests.md` 381).
9. While the stream runs, `OnboardProgress` (`ChatPanel.tsx`) shows each `status` event as a completed step with a bar that never reaches 100% before `done`. After it, the launch journey (section 19) celebrates its first milestone. A stream that ends without `done` or `error` (the server process died mid-run) shows the generic "Could not set up the workspace. Refresh to try again." instead of nothing (rule 771).

## 7. Chat turn with `generate_content` and `draft_items`

Files: `src/app/api/chat/route.ts`, `src/lib/chat.ts`, `src/lib/chat-budget.ts`, `src/lib/chat-allowance.ts`, `src/lib/noor.ts`, `src/lib/credits.ts`, `src/lib/judge.ts`, `src/lib/governance.ts`.

1. `POST /api/chat {text, conv?, agent?, stream?}`; session required; text clipped to 4000 (400 `empty` when nothing is left); `agent` falls back to `noor`, and an agent the plan does not include (`entitlements(u).agents`) is 403 `{ok:false, error:"agent_not_included"}` before anything below (rule 782; every plan includes all eight today, so nothing reaches it yet); reply language from header `x-lang` else `u.lang`. Then, before any model call and always as JSON (since 2026-10-01, rules 330-331): an ended trial (`trialOver(u)`, the predicate every spending path uses) is 402 `{error:"trial_over", limit:"trial", message}`, whose message (`chatRefusedText`, Arabic or English) links `/{lang}/app/billing` and which raises the subscription dialog; then the account's burst ceiling (`chatRefusal(u)` with `chatCaps(u)`: 30 an hour and 150 a day on a paid plan, 10 an hour and 30 a day on a trial, `src/lib/rate.ts`) is 429 `{error:"slow_down", retry:"hour"|"day", message}`, the message naming the account's own ceiling; read in the same round, the agent-scope ceiling of refused messages (only when the input gate is in enforce: 20 an hour and 60 a day per brand scope, rule 795, section 54 item 1) is answered before it with the same 429 `slow_down`, `retry` "hour" or "day", `Retry-After` and a `message` (`agentScopeSlowText`: too many requests outside what the team works on, try again later); then the plan's monthly chat allowance (402 `chat_allowance` or `chat_credits`, step 14 and section 37). An ended trial is refused first, so it uses up neither a ceiling nor a message of the trial's allowance. MCP `ask_noor` applies the agent check (the tool error "This agent is not included in this account's plan. Nothing was sent.") and then all three in the same order as tool errors (`isError`), its link absolute. `chat()` refuses an ended trial itself as well, before it writes the owner's message. `conv` is not validated against `convs:{scope}`; any string yields the key `chat:{scope}:{conv}`.
1b. A `conv` other than `default` with no record under the caller's scope (deleted in another tab, or an id the scope never made) is 404 `conversation_gone` before any count, reservation or save (2026-10-22, rule 2933): a turn never creates a conversation, so no `chat:{scope}:{conv}` history lands outside the `convs:` index the account wipe walks. Ids come only from `POST /api/conversations`.
2. `chat()` pushes the user message, builds context (the brand as one `<brand_file>` block encoded with `asData` in the cached workspace block, rule 573; this agent's 30 preferences from `listPreferencesFor`, the seven core memory files (`CORE_FILES`) up to 6000 chars each, file names, connected providers, the thread, and the agent's recent feedback with its chat thumbs, step 13), and loops up to 5 model calls (`CHAT_MAX_CALLS`, `chat.ts:422`). Since 2026-10-01 the thread goes through the history guard (`trimHistory` in `src/lib/chat-budget.ts`): the newest 16 messages that fit 24,000 characters together, each cut to 6,000, the owner's newest always kept, and a thread that starts with the assistant (the first-run onboarding writes its messages before the owner says anything) sent as it is, so a long thread cannot make every message dearer. Then `fitFirstCall` drops the oldest messages until the first call has room for at least 4,000 output tokens (`CHAT_FIRST_MIN_OUT`), or only the newest is left: a full workspace shortens the thread's memory first, then the answer (down to 2,048), and past that the turn ends with `LARGE_REPLY` (section 37 step 6; on Opus 5 sooner than on the Sonnets). Each call's `max_tokens` is what is left of the per-message ceiling (`chatTurnMaxUsd(model)`: `CHAT_TURN_MAX_USD`, $0.14 in model calls, `src/lib/pricing.ts`, times the model's chat multiplier) after what the turn has cost so far and an upper estimate of the call's input, at most 8,000 (`nextMaxTokens`). The estimate counts 3 characters a token (`CHARS_PER_TOKEN_EST`, below the measured 3.3); the first call prices the cached prefix (the tools and system blocks 1-2) as a cache write and the rest as input, and every later call prices the previous call's cache read and cache write tokens as a cache read, and the rest of its input, its output and the tool results added since as input. When less than 2,048 tokens would be left (`CHAT_MIN_OUT`), no further call is made (step 7). It works out who is talking once (`opts.by`, which the MCP route sets to `tokenBy(user)`, else `byOf(user)`) and passes each tool its turn (`ToolCtx`: the talking agent and `by`), which the tools put on their activity rows (section 32).
3. Tools available to every agent (`chat.ts:98-159`): `generate_content`, `draft_items`, `update_brand`, `read_file`, `write_file`, `import_catalog`, `generate_image`, `publish_item`, `list_inbox`, Reem's five free to-do tools (`agenda_get`, `agenda_next`, `todo_create`, `todo_update`, `todo_propose_done`, sections 25 and 36.4), and `handoff_note`, which leaves one of the agents' handoff notes (section 36.6). Reem's turn alone is also offered `contact_away` (section 39, step 9), `propose_meeting_tasks` (section 43, step 11) and the read-only `free_slots` (section 39 item 14; founder, 2026-10-10: `runTool` refuses it for any other agent, and MCP still lists it). Majed's handoffs are a different tool: only Majed's turn is offered `propose_handoff` (section 34 step 2). Since 2026-10-10 (rule 799, section 54 item 3) `runTool` first checks that the talking agent was offered the tool it is asked to run (`toolsFor(agent)`: `propose_handoff` is Majed's, `contact_away` Reem's): any other name, or one not offered to that agent, is `Unknown tool.`, before the role check, any other check or any write. `update_brand` only proposes (section 27). Since 2026-09-29 `runTool` refuses `update_brand`, `publish_item` and `import_catalog` unless the acting login may (`TOOL_ACTION` in `chat.ts`: `brand.write`, `publish`, `catalog.import`, admin+), and a user with no actor; `write_file` to a core memory file (`CORE_FILES`, after `safeName`) likewise needs `files.core.write` (admin+), as `PUT /api/files` does, while other file names stay open to every login. The refusal is a sentence for the model to relay and nothing changes. Since 2026-10-19 (rule 2306, `src/lib/file-proposals.ts`) a `write_file` that would replace the non-empty text of a core file an agent wrote is not written: it is stored as a proposal (`fileprop:{scope}:{id}`, 7 days, the full text plus the file's `updatedAt`) and shown under the reply as a card with the file name, length and the first 600 characters; at most 2 per reply. Only the owner's or an admin's Apply writes it, through `POST /api/files/proposals/[id] {decision}` (`files.core.write`): 410 once decided, 409 `stale` (closed, nothing written) when the file was saved again since. A new or empty core file is still written at once, and an owner-written one still goes to a `-imported` sibling. `import_catalog` (rule 2304) fetches only a URL whose host is the brand website's host or a subdomain of it, or a host the person chatting typed in this turn's own message (`catalogHostAllowed`); any other URL the model picks is refused with nothing fetched. The chat ceiling, the monthly chat allowance (since 2026-10-01) and the credits are the account's, shared by everyone in it.
4. `generate_content` (`chat.ts:589`), through `generateBatch` (section 8 step 13, with the turn's deadline, which bounds the batch's cross-agent review, section 33 step 2): needs a brand; expired trial returns a message and sets `limit:trial` (`batchRefused`, `chat.ts:531`); cost `TASK_COST[type]` (`src/lib/tasks.ts:24`); `charge`; `generate(type, ...)`; tasks saved with `source:"chat"` and `brief` (the tool's `notes`, first 500 characters, built once per batch by `briefOf`); `judgeLater`, whose scores are written later with `updateTask` (`judge.ts:145`, section 9 step 9), so an approval or edit made while the judge works is kept. In a turn with an agent, a type another agent owns passes the role gate first (`roleGate`, section 54 item 3, rule 798) and, under the founder's `enforce`, is refused before `generateBatch` with a switch card instead. `/api/generate` saves `source:"manual"` with the request's `notes`, MCP `generate_content` `source:"chat"` with its `notes`, the weekly plan `source:"scheduled"` with its fixed note, and `draft_items` its `brief`.
5. `draft_items` (`chat.ts:605`): types `social|whatsapp|ads|gbp` (`DRAFT_TYPES`), checked in code with `isDraftType` (`chat.ts:608`, since 2026-09-30, rule 250) before any read or charge, so a batch type the model sends is refused with "Not a draft type" and nothing created or charged, not only by the tool schema's enum; count clamped to 1..3; cost `max(1, ceil(TASK_COST/TASK_COUNT * count))` (`countCost` in `src/lib/generate-batch.ts`), 1 credit per item for these four types, at the model's tier and never under the type's draft minimum: `draftCharge` (`src/lib/task-costs.ts`, `DRAFT_MIN_CREDITS` social 2, ads 2 since 2026-10-24, rules 4030-4032; the ask-first card, a revision and an instructions test use the same price); otherwise identical.
6. Status for new tasks (`taskFromItem`): `approved` when `gov.autoApprove[type]` is true and no brand rule is broken, else `pending`.
7. Final assistant message stored with `items` (max 10 cards, built with `chatItemOf`: an item another agent flagged carries `xflag` and its card sends the owner to the inbox instead of offering Approve, section 33 step 10) and returned. A reply that carries a handoff card is saved first, and only then is the proposal marked shown (`deliver`, section 34 step 4). `limit` is `credits`, `trial` or `agent_cap` (`ChatLimit`) when a tool hit that wall. A turn the ceiling stopped before a closing answer ends with `CAPPED_REPLY` in the owner's language («وصل هذا الرد إلى حدّه فتوقفت هنا…» / "This reply reached its limit, so I stopped here…": what was done is on the cards and in the inbox, and "continue" carries on); one that ran out of calls or was refused ends with `DONE_REPLY` («تم. تحقق من صندوق الموافقة.» / "Done. Check your approvals inbox."). Since 2026-10-01 a call the model ended at `max_tokens` is never acted on (its tool calls may be cut off, so none of them runs): its text is kept with `CUT_NOTE` («(انقطع الرد عند حدّه. اكتب «كمّل» لأتابع.)» / "(The reply was cut off at its limit. Say "continue" and I will carry on.)"), or `CAPPED_REPLY` when it had no text; and when even the owner's message alone leaves the first call less than 2,048 tokens, no call is made and the reply is `LARGE_REPLY` (the workspace files are too large for one message: shorten them on the Files page or start a new conversation; "This message was not counted"), and the message is given back. In every ending without a closing answer, a limit a tool hit still raises its dialog (`withLimit`). The answer (JSON, or the stream's `message` event) also carries `chat`, the account's `ChatStatus` after the turn, and `charged`, the credits this message was billed (0 inside the allowance; section 37); the turn's model cost (`ChatTurn.usd`) is never sent to the browser.
8. `POST /api/chat/feedback {agent, value: 1|-1, reason?, at?}` (120 an hour per account, `FEEDBACK_PER_HOUR`; 429 `slow_down`) records the thumb in `fblog:{scope}`, keyed by the ACTIVE BRAND's scope (chat history is per brand, so its feedback is too). The old `fb:{email}:{agent}:up|down` lifetime counters are no longer written (2026-09-21).
9. Without a model key for the configured provider (`hasModelKey()` in `src/lib/model.ts`: `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`; with `LLM_PROVIDER=straitly`, `STRAITLY_API_KEY` or `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`) the chat returns a `[DEV MOCK]` message before any tool runs (`chat.ts:391`), and the message is given back to the allowance (no model call, section 37), and `generate` returns mock items (`noor.ts:198`). The batches that reach `generate` through `generateBatch` (`/api/generate`, MCP `generate_content`, `runWeekly`) still charge credits for the mock items; a routine answers `skip:mock` before its lock and charges nothing.
10. Cart-recovery drafts (`cart_recovery`, Noor, 5 items for 3 credits; `src/lib/cart-recovery.ts`): `generateBatch` calls `missingData` before `charge` on every batch (`/api/generate`, chat `generate_content`, MCP `generate_content`, and the routines, whose cart gate has read it already; chat `draft_items` refuses `cart_recovery` before that, since it is not a draft type); without a `storesales:{scope}` snapshot that names products left in carts they return `no_cart_data` (409 on the route) and charge nothing. `statsFor` hands the model a `CART DATA` block built from the snapshot (store, sync date, open carts, products most often left, best sellers) as escaped JSON inside `<cart_data>`, with no shopper data. After the reviewer, `generate` runs `capDiscounts`: any percentage (Latin or Arabic digits) that is not in the brand file's `offers` becomes `[رقم]%`. The drafts are a 3-message WhatsApp sequence (1 h, 24 h, 72 h, with `{{1}}` name and `{{2}}` cart-link placeholders) and 2 posts; Nashr never sends them, the owner pastes them into the store's own abandoned-cart automation. The Reports store card has a "Draft cart-recovery messages" button once a sync found products left in carts. `draft_items` now also passes `statsFor`, so small asks of Dana's types get the workspace data too.
11. Claude can be paused in code (`CLAUDE_PAUSED` in `src/lib/model.ts`; paused from 2026-09-28, on again since 2026-10-05, so `modelState()` is `on` in production with a key). `modelState()` is `mock` without a key (step 9, unchanged for dev and CI), `paused` with a key while the switch is on, and `on` otherwise. While paused no surface calls the model or keeps a credit, and none shows the step-9 placeholders: `/api/generate` answers 503 `model_paused` before `charge`; MCP `generate_content` returns an error text before `charge`; chat (web and MCP `ask_noor`) replies with `PAUSED_REPLY` in the owner's language and runs no tool; `/api/onboard` answers 503 `paused` before its daily allowance, its lock and the crawl, so no mock brand file is saved; `/api/tools/[tool]` answers 503 `paused` before the rate limit; `runWeekly` returns `retry:paused` before `charge`, which leaves the week open in the cron markers for the Monday and Tuesday catch-ups; `runRoutine` returns `retry:paused` before its lock and `charge`, which leaves the day open, and the next day's run asks again; the judge returns no scores; image and video prompts are fixed brand templates (`fixedImagePrompt` and `fixedVideoPrompt` in `src/lib/media/prompts.ts`: a photo, lifestyle, English-headline or design template by model, and a scene of the business for video, never the post's text), while the renderer is never paused and still charges for the render, and the inbox says so above the media buttons (`pausedNote`, or `pausedNoteImage` / `pausedNoteVideo` when a model renders only one kind for that item; no note when the placeholder is the only image on offer, `MediaControls.tsx`); the golden set refuses (`model_paused` on the admin route, `locked_or_no_model_key` on the cron). As a backstop `generate` and `buildFromSite` throw `ModelPaused`, and every caller that charged first refunds. To resume, set `CLAUDE_PAUSED` to `false` and deploy.
12. Goal, reviewer and judge (2026-09-29). Every generation and chat turn reads `memoryBlock(scope)` (`files.ts`), which opens with the owner's onboarding goal from `onboardplan:{scope}` when there is one: one line of at most 140 characters, flattened by `goalLine` so it cannot open a fake file section. The static prompt block of every agent (generation and chat) says to steer toward it and never to promise it as a result. The reviewer (`critique` in `noor.ts`) runs with `reviewerPrompt(type)`: named for the agent who owns the type, with that agent's own fit checks (`REVIEW_FOCUS`), one cached copy per agent. The reviewer and the judge both receive what the agent had, through `groundingBlocks` in `json.ts`: the batch notes as `<owner_notes>`, the memory block (goal included) as `<workspace_files>` and the WORKSPACE / STORE / CART DATA block as `<workspace_data>`, all escaped with `asData` and placed before `<items>`. Every `judgeLater` caller passes that grounding, so numbers taken from the owner's own data are neither rewritten to [رقم] nor scored as invented. The callers that charge first (`/api/generate`, MCP `generate_content`, chat `generate_content` and `draft_items`) read preferences, memory and data in parallel inside the `try` whose `catch` refunds, so a failed read after the charge gives the credits back. The judge may name one automatic-fail code per item (`AUTOFAIL` in `judge.ts`: invented_fact, results_promise, fake_proof, fake_scarcity, injected_instruction); code then caps honesty at 40 and the score at 60, below the regenerate line, and prefixes the notes with the code. Golden runs pass each case's notes and data the same way (`GOLDEN_VERSION` 5). Since 2026-10-05 the generation prompt carries the brand file as one `<brand_file>` block encoded with `asData`, with its rules (banned, preferred, offers, tone, city, language) stated outside the fence (`brandFileBlock` in `src/lib/noor.ts`, rule 572); the block is byte-stable across calls, so the cache holds.
13. Feedback to the owning agent (2026-09-29, `src/lib/feedback.ts`). Every prompt built for a known agent carries what the owner thought of that agent's own recent work: `/api/generate`, MCP `generate_content`, chat `generate_content` and `draft_items` (the agent that owns the type, `agentForTask`), the weekly plan (Noor) and the chat turn (the chat agent). `feedbackFor(scope, agent)` reads, in one parallel round, `decisions:{scope}` (items approved, edited with the mean `editPct`, and rejected in the last 30 days, each item counted once by its latest decision; a publish of a pending item is an approval, a reopen leaves the item undecided; the 5 newest typed reasons on rejections and edits, 200 characters each), the light task index (the 3 newest judge scores under 70 for this agent's items, with the automatic-fail code and 120 characters of the judge's note, "ready" dropped) and, for the chat turn only, `fblog:{scope}` (the 5 newest thumbs-down reasons on this agent's replies, 200 characters each; thumbs rate chat replies, so they never reach generation). The result is escaped with `asData` and placed under a header that calls it data, never instructions, inside `<recent_feedback>` in the uncached block of the prompt (`today` in `systemPrompt`, `now` in chat `system()`), so a new decision never rewrites a cached block. Callers read it with the preferences, memory and data inside the refunding `try`. It is empty when there is nothing, and when `gov.learning` is off (callers pass the governance they loaded; the chat turn reads it in the same round). The same prompts now take `listPreferencesFor(scope, agent)`: the newest 30 learned notes less those that name a task type another agent owns ("Owner rejected a pricing_review item" stays out of Noor's prompts); notes that name no type are brand-wide and stay. `preferences.md` still reaches every agent through the memory files. The golden set passes neither.
14. The gates of a turn, in order, all before `chat()` saves the owner's message (`api/chat/route.ts`, since 2026-10-01; MCP `ask_noor` the same, section 13): the session (401); the text (400 `empty`); an agent the plan does not include (403 `agent_not_included`, rule 782, step 1); an ended trial (402 `trial_over`, rule 330, step 1); the per-account burst limit `chatRefusal` (the account's own `chatCaps(u)` in `src/lib/rate.ts`: 30 an hour and 150 a day on a paid plan, 10 an hour and 30 a day on a trial; 429 `slow_down` with `retry`; a burst never touches the month's count); the agent-scope ceiling of refused messages, read with it in one round and answered first when the input gate is in enforce (429 `slow_down` with `retry` and `Retry-After`, no classifier call, rule 795, section 54 item 1; the burst ceiling has counted the request by then); then `reserveChatTurn`: inside the plan's allowance the message is counted, past it the answer is 402 `{ok:false, error:"chat_allowance", chat, credits}` while chat is paused, or, with the owner's "continue on credits" on, 402 `chat_credits` when the balance is below `chatOverflowHold()` (5 credits, the most one message can cost); nothing is taken from the balance before the turn. A refused message is not in the thread, never reached the model and cost nothing. While Claude is paused (step 11) the turn answers `PAUSED_REPLY` and the message is given back.
15. The streamed answer on the client (`send` in `ChatPanel.tsx`, rule 771). The stream carries `status` lines, a ping every 10 s and one `message` or `error` event at the end. A stream that breaks, or that ends cleanly without either event (the server process died mid-turn, for example a restart during a deploy), goes to the recovery: «انقطع الاتصال، أسترجع الرد…» / "Connection dropped, recovering the reply…", then up to 40 reads of `GET /api/chat?conv=` 5 s apart for an assistant message saved since the send. The server keeps working and saves the reply when only the browser's connection drops, so that reply is shown. When none is found the thread says "Connection lost. Try again." and the typed text goes back into the box unless something new was typed. A killed turn's counted message is not given back.

## 8. Credits: charge and refund

Files: `src/lib/credits.ts`, `src/lib/generate-batch.ts` (the one text pipeline, step 13; its callers are `api/generate/route.ts:21`, `chat.ts:595,613` (`generate_content`, `draft_items`), `api/mcp/route.ts:100`, `schedule.ts:168`, `routines.ts:258` (`routineFor`) and `handoffs.ts:548` (`runItemLocked`, the run of one item of Majed's handoffs, section 34); every caller with a request or invocation deadline passes it (`opts.deadline`), which bounds the batch's cross-agent review), `api/onboard/route.ts:131` (an added brand's 9-credit setup through `chargeOnboard`, since 2026-09-30), and `startMedia` in `src/lib/media/jobs.ts`, which the image and video routes and the chat tool `generate_image` reach through `renderImage` / `renderVideo` in `src/lib/media/start.ts`. Since 2026-10-01 also `reserveChatTurn` and `settleChatTurn` in `src/lib/chat-allowance.ts` (a chat message past the month's allowance with the owner's "continue on credits" on: before the turn only a check that the balance is at least `chatOverflowHold()`, nothing taken; after the reply `settleChatOverflow` in `credits.ts` bills its metered cost, section 37).

1. `withUserLock(email)`: spin on `lock:user:{email}` (SET NX, 10 s TTL), 60 tries at 50 ms (3 s) then throws `user_lock_timeout`. Re-reads the user inside the lock. A release that fails after the change was saved is logged, not thrown (`credits.ts:28`; the TTL frees the lock), so a throw from `charge` or `refund` always means the balance did not change there.
2. `charge(email, cost, meta?)`: fails without change when `credits < cost` (`reason: "no_credits"`, with `need`) or, with `meta`, when the agent's monthly cap would be passed (`reason: "agent_cap"`, step 9); else subtracts and saves, with `meta` also adding to the agent's counter in the same save.
3. `refund(email, n, spend?)` adds `n` back; balance is floored at 0 and deltas are truncated to integers. With the charge's result as `spend` it also lowers that agent's counter (step 9); without it, it is `adjust(email, +n)`.
4. Pattern: charge before the model call, refund in `catch` (for text batches in one place, `generateBatch`, step 13). Credits belong to the account, not the brand: every scope of one account draws from `user:{email}.credits`.
5. The lock guards only the read-modify-write of the user record and expires after 10 s. `unlock` is an unconditional `DEL` (`db.ts:169-171`), so a holder whose lock already expired would delete the next holder's lock.
6. Other balance writers: `renewIfDue` (monthly reset to `allowanceOf(u)` for non-trial plans, `credits.ts`; since 2026-10-22 on the plan's own day, not the 1st (rule 2936, `src/lib/renewal.ts`): the day is the Paymob `billing.anchor`, else `User.renewAnchor` (the day the founder set the plan), else the day of `handPlan.paidThrough`, else the 1st, and a new period renews only when it starts before the paid-through day (Paymob after its renewal charge, a hand plan after the founder extends it); every renewal and plan change writes `User.renewedOn` (Riyadh day) beside `renewedAt`; a record with only `renewedAt` keeps the 1st, or counts from its own day on or before that month's 1st; the chat count still restarts on the 1st; since 2026-10-12 not after a hand-set plan's paid-through day, `User.handPlan.paidThrough` compared with today in Riyadh by `handPlanEnded` in `src/lib/hand-plan.ts`, rules 1302-1305: the founder sets the day with the plan in `POST /api/admin/plan`, one month from today when not given, and may move it alone; a plan set before 2026-10-12 has no day and refills as before, the expand step of rule 679; since 2026-10-19 (founder decision, rules 2273-2275) the account is in the trial state from the day after its paid-through day: `trialOver` and the entitlements' `trial_over` read it at once, and the daily cron writes it (`endHandPlanIfDue` in `src/lib/hand-plan-end.ts`, section 89); a refill of a hand-set plan, which has no payment record, writes a `hand_refill` row to `admin:log` with the plan, the month, the balance before and after and the paid-through day; run by the daily cron and, since 2026-10-01, right before the charge of every paid text batch in `generateBatch` when `renewalDue(user)`, so whichever job runs first in a new month renews and the other finds it done (rule 332): the plan's credits from `src/lib/pricing.ts`, an Agency deal's credits, or Business's 1,500 for Agency without a valid deal; a stored plan `getUser` does not recognise reads as trial and is never renewed), `POST /api/admin/plan` (under lock; a plan change sets `allowanceOf(u)`), `applyReferral` (+50 to the new account) and `rewardReferrer` (+50 to the referrer at the friend's first payment, once, at most 10 a month), `POST /api/referral/share` (+20 per platform), and since 2026-10-01 `settleChatOverflow` (a chat message past the allowance: after the reply, its metered cost at the floor rate, at most `chatOverflowHold()` and at most the balance left, under the lock, with `User.chatCarryMicro` in the same save; section 37). Since 2026-10-01 the balance is also made of top-up packs (section 38, rules 440-454): `renewIfDue` and a plan change set only the month's plan credits (`setMonthly`), `adjust` and the founder's "+ credits" add to or take from them only (`addMonthly`, never a pack's credits), every debit (`charge` through `debit`, and `settleChatOverflow`) takes the month's plan credits first and then packs by earliest expiry (`spendCredits`), and a refund with the charge's receipt puts the credits back where they came from (`returnCredits`); `addTopup` and `voidTopup` (`POST /api/admin/topups`, through `addPaidTopup` and `voidPaidTopup`) are the only other writers. `chargeOnce` keeps the receipt on its `paidOnce` hold, so `refundOnce` and the weekly sweep put the credits back where they came from too.
7. Every other writer of the whole user record saves inside `withUserLock` too, on the record re-read under the lock, so none can put back a `credits` value (or the agent counters, step 9) read at request start: `PUT /api/profile` (name, phone and language, `profile/route.ts:158`; with brand fields the whole save runs inside `withBrandLock`, `:198`, taken before the first write, and a lock that stays taken answers 409 `busy` with nothing written), `PATCH /api/account` (password change, `account/route.ts:57`), `POST /api/auth/reset` (`reset/route.ts:64`) and the founder console's suspend, trial extension, password reset and language actions (`admin/users/[email]/route.ts:83-110`). A member's profile, password or reset writes only the member record, under `withMemberLock` (section 4, section 26).
8. Trial expiry (`trialOver`) is checked in `generateBatch` (`generate-batch.ts:183`) for `/api/generate`, the chat tools `generate_content` and `draft_items`, MCP `generate_content`, the weekly plan and the routines (`runWeekly` and `routineFor` also check it first, before their weekly-cap reads, `schedule.ts:153`, `routines.ts:234`), and every media path: the image and video routes and the chat tool `generate_image` all go through `renderImage` / `renderVideo` (`src/lib/media/start.ts`), which answer 402 `trial_over` before anything else. It is not checked in the first brand's onboarding, catalog import, Reem's to-do tools or the free tools, none of which charges credits; an added brand's onboarding checks it before its 9-credit charge (section 6 steps 2 and 4: `onboard/confirm/route.ts:35`, `onboard/route.ts:45`, and `chargeOnboard` again under the account lock).
9. Per-agent spend and caps (2026-09-29). Attribution: a charge belongs to the agent that owns the task type (`agentForTask(type)`, computed in code, never taken from the request); an image or a video belongs to the owner of the item it illustrates; the weekly plan is Noor's; a routine's batch belongs to the owner of its type (Dana, Noor, Razan or Majed). Chat turns are not charged credits inside the plan's monthly chat allowance (only metered); since 2026-10-01 a message past it is billed only when the owner of a paid plan turned "continue on credits" on, and then after the reply by `settleChatOverflow`, not through `charge` (chat is no agent's work), so a cap bounds batches, drafts, images and videos, never chat messages (section 37). Every charging path (`/api/generate`, MCP `generate_content`, chat `generate_content` and `draft_items`, `runWeekly`, `runRoutine`, and `startMedia`, which the image and video routes and chat `generate_image` reach, step 12) reads governance BEFORE charging and calls `charge(email, cost, {scope, agent, cap: gov.agentCaps[agent]})`. Inside the same lock, `charge` refuses with `{reason: "agent_cap", spent, cap, agent}` when `spent + cost > cap`, without touching the balance; otherwise it debits and adds the cost to `User.agentSpend.by[scope][agent]` in the same `saveUser`, so parallel charges can never pass the cap. A record from an earlier month counts as empty: the month is the UTC calendar month and rolls at 00:00 UTC on the 1st, while `renewIfDue` resets the balance later, at the 05:00 UTC daily cron. Every refund after a metered charge passes the charge's result, and the counter goes down by the refund (never below 0) only while the record is still in the charge's month. `adjust` (referral and share bonuses), `POST /api/admin/plan` and `renewIfDue` never touch the counters.
10. At 100%: `/api/generate` and the image and video routes answer 402 `{ok:false, error:"agent_cap", agent, spent, cap}` before any model call; MCP `generate_content` returns a tool error (`isError`) with the same facts in a sentence, like its insufficient-credits answer; chat tools return a sentence for the model to relay and push `limit:agent_cap`, which `/api/chat` returns as `limit: "agent_cap"`; `runWeekly` and `runRoutine` return `agent_cap` (not a retry). The app's limit dialog (`LimitModal`, `limitFromError("agent_cap")`) explains the cap and links to Governance instead of billing. At 80%: the charge that crosses 80% of the cap returns `warn80`, and `warnAgentCap` (`notifications.ts`, called by every charging path) pushes one `credits` notification per brand scope, agent and month (fingerprint `agentcap80:{scope}:{agent}:{YYYY-MM}`, 35 days) naming the agent, the brand the cap belongs to (the name given to the brand, else its brand file's; the bell is per account and Governance shows the open brand, so the body says to switch to that brand) and "X of Y credits this month".
11. The owner sets the caps on the Governance page ("Monthly credit cap per agent"): one text field with a numeric keyboard per agent, empty for no cap (a field that is not a whole number blocks the save with an error instead of being dropped), with this brand's credits used this month rendered on the server from `User.agentSpend` (no request on mount), a "near the cap" badge at 80% and a "cap reached" badge at 100%. Caps and counters are per brand scope; the balance stays account-level. Only the owner may save them (`governance.write`); admins and members see the page read-only.
12. Media credits (`startMedia` in `src/lib/media/jobs.ts`, spec A24 and A32):
    - Order: the owning agent's monthly cap against this month's count as the session read it (402 `agent_cap {agent, spent, cap}`, step 10) and the balance pre-check against the session's balance (402 `no_credits {need, have}`), flight lock `mflight:{scope}:{taskId}:{kind}` (409 `in_progress`), account limits (429 `too_many`), the trial video marker (402 `trial_video_used`), the kie create throttle or Higgsfield admission (429 `busy` / `one_open`), and only then `charge` with the owning agent and its cap (step 9), which checks both again inside the ledger lock. A request refused before the provider throttle moves none of it (one refused at the charge has already counted one kie create), and through `try/finally` no refused request keeps the flight lock. A `charge` that throws (the ledger lock timed out) answers 429 `busy` with nothing charged; a refused `charge` answers 402 `agent_cap` or `no_credits`. The 80% warning of the agent's cap (`warnAgentCap`) goes out beside the submit and never stands in its way.
    - The charge comes before the submit: the credit buys the render. The job is then saved `queued` with its open-set member and `task.mediaJob[kind]` (through `updateTask`, section 9 step 9), and all three writes settle before anything is decided. If one failed, `refundUnsaved` (`jobs.ts:241`) refunds at once through `refundOnce`, removes the member and the task pointer (restoring the `mediaLast` the start had cleared), and the route answers 502 `media_failed` with the restored balance. If that refund fails too, the charge stands and only the job can return it: the queued record, its member and the task pointer are written again, the flight lock stays held, the 502 carries no balance (`mediaResponse` sends it as `media_pending`, so the inbox never says credits were returned), and the next poll or sweep ends the job `submit_lost` with the refund three minutes on.
    - A job ends only under `mjoblock:{jobId}`, after a re-read, and never changes again. Refund once (`refundOnce`, `jobs.ts:510`) is the one path every refund takes (`failLocked` and `refundUnsaved`), so duplicate webhooks, two tabs and the sweeps refund once, a job is `refunded` only after a refund that happened, and a pass that died holding the claim delays the refund by at most a minute instead of losing it:
      1. Read `mrefund:{jobId}`. `"done"`: the refund was made, the job is recorded `refunded: true` and nothing is paid again. `"none"`: nothing is owed, `refunded: false`.
      2. Otherwise take the claim: `db.lock(mrefund:{jobId}, 60)` (`REFUND_CLAIM_S`, far past the ledger lock's worst wait). A claim another pass holds makes this one throw `refund_pending`; the caller writes nothing and the job stays open for the next poll or sweep.
      3. Holding the claim: when the account that paid still exists with the same `createdAt` (`sameAccount`), `refund(email, credits, spend)`, with the charge's agent, scope and month stored on the job (`job.spend`), so the agent's monthly counter comes down with the balance (step 9). A ledger error (not `user_not_found`) releases the claim and is rethrown, so the job stays open and a later pass retries.
      4. Replace the claim with `"done"` once `refund` has returned, or `"none"` when the account is gone or was registered again (7 days, `JOB_TTL_S`). If that write fails, the claim expires in 60 s and the job record, saved next with `refunded`, is what stops a second refund.
      5. In `failLocked` the task is written next (`updateTask`: `mediaJob[kind]` cleared and `mediaLast[kind]` set, only while the task still points at this job), and only after that the job is saved `failed`; a task write that throws leaves the job open, and the next pass finds the refund already recorded in the marker.
      6. `finalizeLocked` reads the marker before delivering (`jobs.ts:429`): any value means a fail is under way, so it finishes that fail (or throws `refund_pending` while the claim stands) and a refunded render is never delivered.
    - Same-account refunds: the job stores the account's `createdAt` (`accountSince`). When the account is gone (`user_not_found` after `wipeAccount`) or was registered again since, the job ends `failed` with `refunded: false`, the marker is set to `"none"` and no notification is sent. The same check guards the owner's analytics (`track(email, "image")`) when a render finishes: a render that finishes after the account was deleted rebuilds none of the `uev:`, `ulast:` and `ufirst0:` records `wipeAccount` removed.
    - Trial accounts keep one video, from the 12-credit models only (`TRIAL_VIDEO_MODELS`: Quick clip, Clip with sound, Photo to motion; other video models are 402 `plan_required`). The video start asks `entitlements(u).media.video` (rule 781) rather than the plan name, and the inbox page passes the same list to the model cards; a founder video grant (`User.grants.video`, rule 780) opens every video model to a trial but not a second clip. `mtrialvid:{email}` (14 days) is taken at the start and deleted again if that video is refunded.
    - A finished render adds its credits to `task.mediaCredits` (`withResult` in `jobs.ts`; on a task that predates the field, an existing generated image first counts as `IMAGE_COST`), even when a newer render already replaced it on the card; reports and the founder overview read `t.mediaCredits ?? (t.image ? IMAGE_COST : 0)`. The task records the job in `mediaApplied` (the last 8) in the same write, so a finalize retried after a failed write never counts a render twice.
    - The placeholder card (no `FAL_KEY`, or `img-fast` switched off) is not charged at all; before 2026-09-29 it was charged and refunded. It never replaces artwork: `placeholder` (`src/lib/media/start.ts:47`) writes the card through `updateTask` only while the task as stored has no generated image; otherwise nothing is written and the answer is 409 `model_unavailable` (`:66`), on the route and in the chat tool ("keeps its current image. No credit was used"). The card writes `mediaCredits: 0` when the task had none, and `liteOf` does not count it as an image, so neither the reports nor the founder overview count a credit or an image for it.
13. One text pipeline (2026-09-29, `src/lib/generate-batch.ts`). `/api/generate`, the chat tools `generate_content` and `draft_items`, MCP `generate_content`, the weekly plan and the routines all call `generateBatch(user, scope, {type, notes, count?, source, via, gov?, brand?, brief?, forcePending?, settle?, by?, leadBrief?, parent?, deadline?, once?, commit?, model?, agentScope?, notesGate?})` with the caller's own scope (`sc(u)`, or `user.scope ?? user.email` where the scope is already on the user), and keep only their own response shapes, messages, tracking and limits. In order:
    1. The model switch: while paused, a typed `paused` refusal before any read or charge. Chat is the exception: its turn answers while paused before any tool runs, so a tool called outside a turn keeps the backstop (`generate()` throws `ModelPaused`, the charge is refunded). `/api/generate` keeps its own 503 before reading the body.
    2. The brand file and governance (read together, or taken from the caller: the scheduled paths have read them for the weekly cap), then `no_brand`, then `trial_over`, then `missingData` (`missing_data` with the code, `no_cart_data`).
    2b. The input gate on the owner's notes (rule 796, section 54 item 2), only when the caller passes `notesGate` (`/api/generate` and MCP `generate_content`): after step 2, on this batch's own brand read, before the renewal and the charge. A block is the typed refusal `agent_scope {message}` or `agent_scope_slow {retry}`, charges nothing, saves nothing, and goes through `refuse()` (the caller's `settle` runs). In `shadow` the check runs beside the rest of the batch and is awaited before `generateBatch` returns on every path.
    3. The owning agent (`agentForTask(type)`, computed in code) and the charge with its monthly cap (`gov.agentCaps`): `agent_cap {agent, spent, cap, credits}` or `no_credits {need, credits}` without a debit, then `warnAgentCap`. A small ask pays `countCost(type, count)`: the chat's `draft_items`, whose type `runTool` has already checked against `DRAFT_TYPES` (rule 250); since pricing phase 1 was merged in, `generateBatch` also throws `not_draft_type` for a count on any other type before any read or charge, and `draftCredits` in `src/lib/task-costs.ts`, which the margin-floor test estimates, is the same formula (rule 241). A full batch pays `TASK_COST[type]` (weekly plan 2, weekly report 3 and follow-up cadence 4 since 2026-09-30). Since 2026-10-08 either base is priced at the batch's model (`opts.model`; left out or `undefined`, `PRICED_AT.generate`, Opus 5): `cost = creditsFor(base, model, "generate")`, computed once and charged (step 15.2). A `model` that is not exactly a catalog id (`null` included) throws `unknown_model`, a catalog entry with neither `auto` nor `pick` (internal only, outside the margin-floor test) throws `model_not_offered:<id>`, and one whose `tasks` allow-list leaves the type out throws `model_task_not_allowed:<id>:<type>`, all before any read or charge. On the owner paths (app, chat, MCP) such a refusal is one `cap_reached` (outcome `spent/cap`) or `no_credits` activity row with what the batch would have cost; the scheduled paths record it as their own outcome instead (section 32 step 3). Since 2026-10-01, right before the charge, a paid account due its monthly credits (`renewalDue(user)` on the copy in hand) is renewed with `renewIfDue` under the ledger's lock (rule 332), and with `once` the charge is `chargeOnce(claim)`: a hold the claim already has is reused, not charged again (rule 333), when its credits are exactly `cost`; a hold of another price is swapped (step 15.2).
    4. Inside the refunding try, in one `Promise.all`: `listPreferencesFor`, `memoryBlock`, `statsFor`, `feedbackFor`; then `generate()` (with `crossReview: gov.crossReview` and the caller's `deadline`, section 33: the review is skipped with less than 60 s of it left). A caller that may start a batch late checks `BATCH_RUN_MIN_MS` (180 s) itself before calling: the handoff run (`too_late`), the auto-run and the routines (`retry:late`). A read or a generation that throws is refunded with the charge's receipt (the agent's counter comes down too) and answered `failed {error, credits}`; since 2026-10-12 a reply with no usable item (an empty array, or no element with a string body) is such a throw (`empty_generation` in `generate()`, before the reviewer; rule 1306, issue #79), so an empty batch is never paid for, while a short batch (fewer items than asked) is still charged in full (open question); the route maps it to 502 (503 for `ModelPaused`), the chat tools, MCP and the scheduled paths throw the error as before.
    5.
