Every item becomes a task through `taskFromItem` (`source`, `brief` = the given brief or `briefOf(notes)`, status by `initialStatus` on the item's type, channel, title and body (since main's security gates were merged in, the channel decides whether a `{{1}}` or `[name]` slot may pass: only on a WhatsApp, email, SMS or call draft, and an unfilled `[رقم]` holds every channel; section 10) or always `pending` with `forcePending` or a cross-agent "flag", the brand's first language, one `createdAt` for the batch, `pb`, the prompt versions, section 31, and `crossReview`, section 33), the tasks are saved together (`Promise.allSettled`, no `await` in a loop, every save settled before the guard decides) and then `commit` runs, both still inside the refunding try since 2026-10-01 (a failed save or commit refunds, through `refundOnce` for a claim, exactly once, and `dropTasks` removes every task of the batch, the ones whose save landed included, so a refunded batch leaves nothing approvable; rule 334); after the try, the batch's one `generated` activity row (the owning agent, `by`, `via`, up to 10 task ids, the count and the credits) and one `cross_reviewed` row per reviewing agent are written, and `judgeLater` gets the grounding (notes, memory, data block), the requested count and the `via` for the judge's rows (section 32).
    5a. The output check before save (rule 801, section 54 item 4), inside the try, after the tasks are built and before they are saved: with the `output` layer not `off`, each task's title and body (`itemText`) go to `scopeCheck("output", …, {where: "items", itemId})`, five at a time (`pmap`), with the other brands' names read once for the batch (shared with step 2b). `enforce`: the items inside the team's work are kept; if none is kept, or the batch was charged once (`once`, a claim that is never partly refunded), it throws `AgentScopeRefused` and the whole charge is refunded (`failed`, nothing saved); otherwise the withheld share, `ceil(charged × held / items)`, is refunded after the try on the batch's receipt (since 2026-10-19 the same one refund also covers a short batch, `batchKeeps`, step 5b) (the agent's cap usage drops with it) and the batch answers `held` and `refunded` (what landed, 0 when the refund failed), with the activity row and `cost` at the charge less the refund. `shadow` runs the checks beside the save and `generateBatch` awaits them on every path. A classifier error keeps the item.
    5b. A short batch (founder decision 2026-10-19, rules 2276-2279, section 89): a batch that delivers fewer items than asked (`asked` is the `count` given, else `TASK_COUNT[type]`) keeps `batchKeeps(charged, asked, made, held)` = `floor(charged × min(made, asked) × (made − held) / (asked × made))` and refunds the rest once, after the try, on the receipt (a claim's batch through `refundOncePart`, which lowers its hold in the same save). 3 of 10 costs 3/10; more than asked is never charged more; a full batch with nothing held keeps all of it. The batch answers `short {asked, delivered}` and `refunded`; the activity row and `cost` are the net. An empty batch still throws and is refunded whole (rule 1306).
    `settle` runs on every refusal and on the refund: a routine uses it to give back the attempt it counted before the charge (`routinetry`). Every refusal is typed and charges nothing; no caller calls `charge`, `generate`, `saveTask` or `judgeLater` for a batch on its own (`tests/generate-batch.test.ts`).

14. Model call budgets (since 2026-10-01, issue #28, rules 335-336). Every model client is `anthropic({timeoutMs, maxRetries})` (`src/lib/model.ts`), never the SDK defaults (a 10-minute timeout and two retries, longer than any route's maxDuration). The SDK's timeout stops at the response headers, which a stream sends at once, so every charged call also passes `until(deadline, { cap })`, an abort signal from the caller's one deadline (`deadlineFor(maxDuration)`, 15 s under the platform's limit): a slow model makes the call throw, and the caller's refund runs, before the platform kills the function (a killed function runs no catch). The SDK's per-request `timeout` replaces the client's, so `until` sets it to the smaller of the time left and `cap`, the call's per-attempt budget below; without the cap one attempt could wait the whole time left (rule 336). The SDK's wait between attempts takes the same signal. The budgets (`MODEL_BUDGET`):

    | Call | Client (per attempt, retries) | Ends by |
    |---|---|---|
    | `generate` (`/api/generate`, MCP, chat tools, handoffs, routines, the Sunday plan) | 240 s, 1 retry | the caller's deadline, else 240 s from its start; the reviewer only with 30 s left and at most 90 s; the cross-agent review only with 60 s left and at most 45 s, no retry |
    | chat turn (`/api/chat`, MCP `ask_noor`) | 120 s, 1 retry | a turn starts only with 20 s of the request left and its stream ends 5 s before the deadline; a turn cut short saves `OUT_OF_TIME_REPLY` (what tools made stays in the inbox) |
    | onboarding (three parts) | 240 s, 1 retry | the request's deadline, taken before the crawl; a failed part is retried only with 60 s left |
    | judge | 90 s, 1 retry | its own attempts (after the response; it charges nothing) |
    | image and video prompts | 20 s or the time left, none | per call (`write` in `media/prompts.ts`) |
    | free tools | 45 s, 1 retry | the route's deadline, 45 s after the request (maxDuration 60), the wait between attempts included: a passing 529 or 429 is retried instead of costing a visitor one of the five daily uses; since 2026-10-09 on `claude-haiku-5-5`, and a reply with no text (a Haiku refusal, which has no server-side fallback) answers 502 `failed` and is not counted (rule 900) |

    The weekly cron and "Run now" pass their deadline to `runWeekly`, so a plan started at 160 s of the invocation still ends, or is refunded, before 300 s; the routines cron's deadline is `deadlineFor(maxDuration)` too (it was the kill time itself).

15. Credits by model tier (2026-10-08, spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-08-straitly-model-routing-design.md` section 6; `src/lib/models/credits.ts`, server only). A task or a chat message costs its base credits times its model's tier multiplier RELATIVE TO THE SURFACE'S DEFAULT MODEL (`PRICED_AT` in the import-free `src/lib/models/tiers.ts`: generation Opus 5, chat Sonnet 5.5), so the default keeps today's price: `tierMultiplier(model, surface) = TIER_MULTIPLIER[tier(model)] / TIER_MULTIPLIER[tier(PRICED_AT[surface])]`. Generation: Opus 5 x1, a standard model (the Sonnets) x0.5, an economy one x0.3. Chat: the Sonnets x1, Opus 5 x2, an economy model x0.6. Since rule 902 (2026-10-10) generation with a task is priced at that TYPE's writer (`pricedAt("generate", type)` = `TASK_MODELS[type].write`, src/lib/task-models.ts), so Automatic is x1 on every type, Opus 5.5 on a Sonnet-written type x2 and Sonnet 5.5 on an Opus-written type x0.5; `PRICED_AT.generate` (Opus 5.5) is left for a caller without a task (costs.md section 4.2).
    1. `creditsFor(base, model, surface, task?)` = `max(1, ceil(base x multiplier))` (`generateBatch` passes the batch's type), the product rounded to six decimals first so a floating-point excess is never a whole extra credit; a base that is not a finite number of at least 0 throws `invalid_cost`. An id that is not exactly a catalog id (unknown, `constructor`, `__proto__`, not a string) is priced as the surface's default (x1), never cheaper and never NaN; `generateBatch` refuses one before charging anyway (step 13.3), and `route()` (`src/lib/models/route.ts`, item 5 below) validates a stored pick first (item 6 supplies it). A surface that is not a `PRICED_AT` key, or a `PRICED_AT` model missing from the catalog, throws.
    2. One value per batch, the credits finally charged: the balance lost, the `generated` activity row's `credits` and `BatchDone.cost` are the same number, and a failed batch gives back exactly it (`refund` of `cost`, or `refundOnce`, which gives back the hold `chargeOnce` stored). Without a claim it is `cost`. With one (a claim paid in an earlier, killed run, possibly on another model) `chargeOnce` reuses the stored hold only when its credits equal `cost` exactly, and the batch reports the hold's credits (that same number); a hold of any other price is given back and `cost` charged in the same locked save (`OnceCharge.replaced` names what was given back), so there is no moment with nothing held and a retry is never charged at the old model's price. When `cost` cannot be paid even with the old hold given back (`no_credits` or `agent_cap`), nothing is saved: the old hold stays and the daily sweep resolves it (`resolveOnceHolds`, rule 337: refunded when the week has no plan). No field is added to the hold.
    3. Allow-lists: a catalog entry's `tasks` names the types it may run (unset: all). Until rule 902 the two standard Claude models left out `article` and `followup_seq`, which missed the margin floor at x0.5 against Opus; priced against each type's writer every type keeps the floor on Sonnet 5.5 and Opus 5.5, so neither has an allow-list today (Haiku 5.5's is empty; `docs/system/costs.md` section 4.2). `modelAllowsTask(entry, type)` in `src/lib/models/catalog.ts` is the one check; `generateBatch` enforces it before charging (step 13.3), and `route()` hands it only models that already passed the pick checks (plan Task 6 applies the allow-list to the stored pick).
    4. Chat: each model's per-message ceiling is `chatTurnMaxUsd(model)` = `CHAT_TURN_MAX_USD` ($0.14) x its chat multiplier ($0.28 on Opus 5), the default of `nextMaxTokens` (`src/lib/chat-budget.ts`), and its overflow hold is `chatHoldFor(model)` = `chatOverflowHold(maxCostPerCreditUsd(), chatTurnMaxUsd(model))` (2 at x1, 4 on Opus 5), which `reserveChatTurn(u, model)` checks and carries to `settleChatTurn` (section 37). Against the allowance a message counts `chatUnitsFor(model)` = ceil(its chat multiplier) messages (coordinator ruling 2026-10-08, option a: 1 on the Sonnets, 2 on Opus 5, 1 on an economy model), so each unit carries at most $0.14: `reserveChatTurn` computes it once and carries it on the gate (`units`), INCRBYs the counter (or, past the allowance, the overflow counter) by it, and `settleChatTurn` gives back exactly `gate.units` when the turn failed (a gate whose units are not a whole number of at least 1 throws `invalid_units` before anything moves). A message whose units would pass what is left is refused before any model call, or goes wholly on credits with the switch on. `reserveChatTurn` throws `unknown_model` for an id not in the catalog, `model_not_offered:<id>` for an entry with neither `auto` nor `pick`, `model_not_chat:<id>` for one without `caps.tools` (chat runs tools), and `chat_allowance_unpriced` for a chat multiplier that is not a finite number above 0, all before anything is counted. The gate carries the model (`gate.model`) and the caller runs the turn on that same model: `POST /api/chat` and the MCP `ask_noor` resolve `route("chat").model` once, hand it to `reserveChatTurn`, and pass `gate.model` to `chat()` (`ChatOpts.model`; `chat()` falls back to `route("chat").model` only when called without one, which no route does). Today `route("chat")` is `DEFAULTS.chat` (Sonnet 5.5) for everyone, so a message counts 1.
    5. Where the model is chosen (2026-10-08, plan Task 5; `src/lib/models/route.ts`, server only, no store, no network). `route(purpose, ctx?)` is the one place that names the model for a call: `chat`, `generate`, `review`, `xreview`, `judge`, `onboard`, `imagePrompt`, `tool`, and since 2026-10-10 `meeting` (Reem B2b's meeting tasks, Sonnet 5.5, section 43). Each default is exactly the model the call hard-coded before (`DEFAULTS`: chat Sonnet 5.5, generate and onboard Opus 5, the rest Sonnet 5), so nothing a customer pays or receives changed. A default, and every automatic choice, must be a catalog entry with `hosting === "west"` and `auto === true`, checked in `route.ts` itself and not only in the catalog data (a default that is not throws `default_model_not_auto` before any call). A customer's stored pick (`ctx.stored`, item 6 below) can change only `chat` and `generate`, and only in this order: an exact catalog id (else `unknown`), offered to customers (else `not_pickable`), a non-west model needs `ctx.confirmedChina === true` (else `china_unconfirmed`), chat needs tool use (else `needs_tools`), and generation with `ctx.task` needs a model allowed to run that type (`modelAllowsTask`, else `task_not_allowed`); a rejected pick runs the default and `rejected` says why. Nothing stored is `undefined` or `null`: the default, nothing rejected. The judge is its default (Sonnet 5), except when that is the model that generated the batch (`ctx.generatedBy`): then the cheapest other automatic west model whose rate is no higher on any of the four buckets, else the default, so `ESTIMATE_MODELS.judge` stays an upper bound for the margin floor (Sonnet 5 judges Sonnet 5.5 and Opus 5 output; a Sonnet 5 batch is judged by Sonnet 5.5). One decision per batch and per turn: `generateBatch` resolves the batch's model once (`generateRouteFor`, item 6) and that requested model is the one `creditsFor` prices, the one `generate()` asks for (`GenOptions.model`, so `generate()` does not route again) and `generatedBy` for the judge. `generate()` asks the API for the server-side fallback (`betas: ["server-side-fallback-2026-07-01"], fallbacks: "default"`, another model answering when the requested one is overloaded) only when the requested model is `DEFAULTS.generate`, and retries without it on a 400 only then: a pick was priced at its own tier, so it runs on the requested model or fails and is refunded; `crossReview` and `buildFromSite` resolve once and give every parallel call the same model. `ESTIMATE_MODELS` (`src/lib/task-costs.ts`) is derived from `DEFAULTS`, and `tests/model-route-calls.test.ts` records the model each SDK call receives. The only `claude-` literals left under `src` are the `DEFAULTS`, the catalog, `PRICED_AT` (import-free for the browser) and `TASK_MODELS` (the `RATES` fallback in `usage.ts` was removed on 2026-10-10, rule 970).
    6. A customer's pick (2026-10-08, plan Task 6; `src/app/api/models/route.ts`, `src/lib/models/overrides.ts`). Three stored fields, all optional and additive (rule 679, expand only; absent on records saved before): `Conversation.model` (the chat pick of one conversation, `conv:{sc(u)}:{id}`; the default thread has no record, so no pick), `Brand.model` (the generation pick of the active brand, `brand:{sc(u)}`, used by every batch of that brand: manual, chat, MCP, scheduled, routine and handoff), `User.chinaHostingOk` (the owner's one confirmation that a China-hosted model may be used, `user:{u.email}`). Only `PUT /api/models` writes them (`PUT /api/brand` keeps `Brand.model` as stored and ignores one in its body); it checks the pick in code (catalog id, `pick`, tool use for a conversation, the STORED confirmation for a non-west model) and `null` deletes the field. `{confirmChina: true}` goes alone: sent with `target`, `id` or `model` it is 400 `bad_request` and nothing is written. Every writer of a conversation record (the pick, a turn's `touchConversation`, `PATCH` rename, `DELETE`, and the account wipe, rule 905) holds that conversation's lock (`convlock:{sc(u)}:{id}`, SET NX with an owner token, 10 s TTL, up to 60 tries 50 ms apart), reads the record again under it and writes only while it still exists. So a stale copy is never written back (a pick the owner cleared does not return when a turn ends) and a deleted conversation is never recreated outside the `convs:` index (the pick then answers 404 `not_found`). When the lock is not taken within about 3 s nothing is written: the pick, a rename and a delete answer 503 `busy` with `Retry-After: 1` and the owner presses again; a turn's touch is skipped (logged without the account or the id), the reply is still delivered, and only the sidebar's count and time miss that one turn. Onboarding never stores a pick: the route drops a `model` field from the brand the model built before `saveBrand`, so a new workspace starts on Automatic. Onboarding runs only while the workspace has no brand file and writes the whole file, so if the brand file is ever removed and the workspace onboarded again, any pick stored before is gone and the customer is back on Automatic. `GET /api/models` gives the picker its list (`pickerView()`), each pick's credit multiplier per surface, the messages one chat message on it counts, and whether China is confirmed. A conversation pick that landed answers `{ok: true, chat}`: `chatStatus(u, chatRouteFor(u, {model}).model)`, the account's chat status (the counters the chat page shows under the box, a member's being the owner's) for the model that conversation runs on from now, worked out with the chat page's own resolver on the record as written; no rate and no model id. If that read fails after the write (a Redis HTTP error), the pick still answers `{ok: true}` without `chat`: the client keeps the pick it shows and the line keeps its value, and the warning names no account, conversation or model. A brand pick and the confirmation answer `{ok: true}`. At call time the callers hand what they already read under `sc(u)` to `route()`, which validates it again (a pick the catalog later dropped falls back): web chat reads the conversation record (`getConversation(sc(u), conv)`, nothing read for the default thread or a malformed id) and routes `chatRouteFor(u, conversation)` once, before `reserveChatTurn`, so the count, the hold and the model called are that one model (`GET /api/chat` and the chat page show its hold); `generateBatch` routes `generateRouteFor(user, brand, type)` once after the brand read, so `ctx.task` is the batch's type. `confirmedChina` is always `u.chinaHostingOk === true` of the session's account (a member's session carries the owner's record), never a request field. A rejected pick runs the automatic choice and the customer is told, in the reply language (the request's `x-lang`, else the account's): chat puts `MODEL_UNAVAILABLE.chat` at the top of the reply, saved with it (so a reload shows it; a paused or mock reply made no model call and gets none), `POST /api/generate` answers `notice: MODEL_UNAVAILABLE.generate`, and the chat tools and MCP `generate_content` add `MODEL_UNAVAILABLE_NOTE` to their result for the assistant to relay. MCP `ask_noor` has no conversation record and routes `route("chat")`. The reviewer, the cross-review, the judge, onboarding, image prompts and the free tools never read a pick (`tests/model-override.test.ts`).
    7. The picker (2026-10-08, plan Task 7; `src/components/app/ModelPicker.tsx`, `src/lib/model-picker.ts`, copy in `src/lib/app-i18n.ts` `models`). Two places: the chat header (`ChatModelPicker`, one conversation's pick) and, under the brand form once the brand file exists, "Default model for content" (`BrandModelField`, the active brand's pick, with its own Save; `PUT /api/brand` never changes it). Each reads `GET /api/models` on mount and shows nothing of its own: the label, the tier, a "Hosted in China" badge for a `hosting: "china"` row, and the server's multiplier for that surface formatted `x0.6` / `x1` / `x2` (two decimals at most, trailing zeros trimmed); chat adds "Each message counts as N messages" when `chatUnits` is above 1 (Arabic in the dual, plural and singular forms), the brand page shows the range of the server's per-type `generateByTask` over the types the model may run (rule 902, e.g. `x1–2`) and words it "{x} per batch" / «{x} لكل دفعة» (a multiplier of the batch's credits, never a credit count). A row the server sent no finite multiplier for is left out, and the chat list also leaves out a row whose `tools` is false (the server refuses it there). Automatic is always first, has no multiplier, and sends `model: null`; the selected entry is the stored value while the list offers it, else Automatic. For chat the stored value is, latest first (`storedPick`): a pick made in this panel, the open conversation's record as the chat page read it (`getConversation(sc(u), conv)`, the same read that sets the allowance line's ceiling, passed as `active` with its id and `model` only, so a conversation older than the newest hundred listed still shows its pick), then `Conversation.model` from `GET /api/conversations`; for the brand `Brand.model` from the page's `getBrand`. A change is decided against the stored value itself (`pickChanges`): over a stored id the list no longer offers, Automatic shows as selected and choosing it (chat) or saving it (brand, Save enabled) sends `null`, which clears the stale record. The default thread has no record, so its picker explains "Start a new conversation to choose its model" instead of listing. Picking a China-hosted row while `chinaConfirmed` is false opens the app's confirmation dialog (`useConfirm`) once; only the owner, not a founder impersonating, is offered it (`canConfirmChina`, from the page: `can(u, "profile.account") && u.actor?.kind !== "founder"`), anyone else is told only the owner can allow it. On yes the picker sends `{confirmChina: true}` alone, then the pick; a refused confirmation never sends the pick. The change shows at once and rolls back on any refusal, as one pure step (`nextMadePicks` in the chat panel, keyed by the conversation the pick was made on, so a refusal that arrives after the person moved to another conversation restores that one; `nextBrandPick` for the brand field, which keeps the refused choice selected for another try unless the list dropped it). A chat pick that landed hands the answer's `chat` (`pickedChat`, shape-checked) to the panel, which replaces its allowance line with it as it came while that conversation is still the open one, so the overflow line's "at most N credits" is the new model's; the client works out nothing. The chat trigger is disabled while a message of the panel is in flight (`pickerLocked`, the panel passes `replying={busy}`): that reply's own status arrives after the pick's and would put the old model's ceiling back. Refusals: 503 `busy` (a conversation's lock or the brand's, `Retry-After: 1`; since rule 906 the brand's is no longer 409, and the client still reads a 409 `busy` as busy for a server older than that change) says try again in a moment; `unknown_model`, `not_pickable` and `china_unconfirmed` say the model is not available and reload the list; `not_found` reloads the conversation list (chat) or asks to save the brand file first (brand); 401 goes to the login page; 403 on the confirmation says only the owner can allow it; 429 says too many changes this hour. The chat refusal note goes by itself after `NOTE_MS` (8 s), on Escape and on the next pick. The list is a plain list of buttons, the chosen one `aria-current`, reached with Tab; the trigger's `aria-controls` names the popover (which holds the list, the default-thread hint or the load failure) only while it is open, and Escape closes it and gives focus back to the trigger. The picker module imports only types from `models/tiers.ts` and the component nothing from `src/lib/models/` (`tests/client-bundle.test.ts`), and no picker string names a model (`tests/model-picker-copy.test.ts`).
    8. Metering by model and provider (2026-10-09, plan Task 8; `src/lib/usage.ts`, `src/lib/model.ts`, `src/lib/admin-stats.ts`, the Billing tab of `AdminConsole.tsx`). Global counters only, in the `_all` month, never per account: `meter` adds `usage:_all:{YYYY-MM}:model:{modelKey}:calls|usd_micro` (the catalog id, else `other`) and `usage:_all:{YYYY-MM}:provider:{anthropic|straitly}:calls|usd_micro` (the call's `provider` argument, else `effectiveProvider()`, `src/lib/model.ts`: `straitly` only with `LLM_PROVIDER=straitly` and `STRAITLY_API_KEY`, the same decision `anthropic()` makes, else `anthropic`; anything else is ignored) inside its existing `Promise.all`; the micro-dollars are the same number the other counters get, so `costUsd` and the rates are untouched. `withDirectFallback` adds one to `provider:fallback` each time it re-sends a request to Anthropic (fire and forget; a store error changes nothing about the retry or the logs). A pick `route()` refused is counted by `noteRejectedPick(purpose, reason)` under `pickreject:{chat|generate}:{reason}` where the caller reads `routed.rejected` (`POST /api/chat`, `generateBatch`), best effort: it never changes the turn or the batch and logs one fixed line on a store error. The month's numbers come back from `getUsage("_all")` (one `mget`; empty for an account) through `overview().modelUsage`. Counted from this release; no backfill.

## 9. Approval and rejection of a task

Files: `src/app/api/tasks/[id]/route.ts`, `src/lib/task-version.ts`, `src/components/app/Inbox.tsx:181`, `ChatPanel.tsx:43`, `TodoPanel.tsx` (walk Approve), `src/lib/secretary-walk.ts` (the walk card's `version`).

1. `PATCH /api/tasks/{id} {status?, editedBody?, note?, scheduledFor?, version?, notNow?}`; session required; the change is applied with `updateTask(sc(u), id, ...)` (`tasks/[id]/route.ts:39`, step 9), so only tasks under the caller's active scope resolve (404 otherwise, `:80`). Since 2026-09-29 the role depends on the result: a request that leaves the item `approved` needs admin+ (`publish`) when the item was not approved before or its text changes; editing, rejecting, reopening and rescheduling need any login (`drafts`). The check runs before anything is saved: `drafts` before the task is read, `publish` inside the `updateTask` change on the task as stored, so an approval that landed while the request waited is judged correctly, and a refusal writes nothing and answers 403 `forbidden`. Preference notes, `approve`/`reject` tracking and the decision records (step 8) are collected inside the change and run after the response (`after()`). A request that needs `publish`, or sends `status: "approved"`, must carry `version`, the `taskVersion` (`src/lib/task-version.ts`: SHA-256 of title, `editedBody ?? body` and the generated image) of the card it was made from (rules 560-564). It is compared inside the `updateTask` change with the item as stored: missing or not a string is 400 `version_required`, different is 409 `{error: "changed", task}`, and nothing is saved (no status, no `decidedAt`, no decision, no preference note). The inbox, the chat card and Reem's walk card send it; on `changed` they show the item as it is now.
2. `scheduledFor`: `null` clears, `YYYY-MM-DD` sets.
3. `editedBody` different from `body`: stored (max 20000 chars). When `gov.learning` is on, a preference note is pushed to `prefs:{scope}` and appended to `preferences.md`. The reason typed with an edit or a rejection is `cleanNote(note)`: a string only, one line, control and direction characters removed, at most 300 characters (rules 565-566); the append to `preferences.md` runs under `prefsfile:{scope}` (`lockOwned`, rule 567).
4. `status` in `approved|rejected|pending`: sets `decidedAt` on change, tracks `approve`/`reject`, and on reject with a note records a preference.
5. Any status may be moved to any other status, including after publishing, within the role rule of step 1: approving an item, or rewriting one that stays approved, is the owner's or an admin's; everything else is any login's. The task itself does not say which login decided; each decision record does (`by`, step 8): the owner, a member with its own login and role, or the founder through support access (`byOf`, section 30).
6. Brand gate (`src/lib/brand-rules.ts`): when the resulting status is `approved` and the item is customer-facing (`CUSTOMER_FACING`: social, article, whatsapp, ads, gbp, followup_seq, cart_recovery), the final text (`title` plus `editedBody ?? body`) is checked against the brand file's banned words. A hit returns 409 `{error: "brand_violation", violations}` and nothing is saved. Saving an edit as `pending` is allowed. Matching folds Arabic diacritics, tatweel and letter variants, accepts the Arabic prefixes و ف ب ل ك ال and suffixes for Arabic terms of three letters or more, and requires whole words for Latin and shorter terms. The inbox runs the same function in the browser to label cards and to check the editor as the owner types.
7. Auto-approval goes through `initialStatus(autoApprove, brand, item)` at every creation site (`api/generate`, `api/mcp` `generate_content`, chat `generate_content` and `draft_items`, the weekly schedule): an item that breaks a rule is created `pending` even when its type is auto-approved, and the chat tool reports how many were held.
8. Decision records (2026-09-29, `src/lib/decisions.ts`). The route works out the request's human decisions inside its `updateTask` change, from the task as stored (step 9), and after the task is saved appends them to `decisions:{scope}` (scope from `sc(u)`, never from the body): `edit` when the stored text changes (re-saving the same edit adds nothing; `editPct` uses `editPctOf`, the light index's measure), then `approve`, `reject` or `reopen` (back to `pending`) when the status changes. The owner's typed `note` goes on the rejection, or on the edit when nothing was rejected, cut to 300 characters. Each record carries `by`, who decided (`byOf(u)`, `src/lib/actor.ts`, worked out from the session in the route's first `Promise.all`, before the response and before `after()`, never from the body; section 30 step 1), the owning agent from `agentForTask(type)`, the title (80) and the judge's score and automatic-fail code at that moment. Records are written whatever `gov.learning` says, and read back into the owning agent's prompts only while it is on (section 7 step 13); a request stopped by the brand gate records nothing; items created approved by `initialStatus` never appear. Tracking, decision records and preference notes go out in one `Promise.all` inside `after()`; `preferences.md` is then read and rewritten as before. `recordDecisions` also writes one `decision` activity row per record in the same round (section 32; not for `publish`).
9. Whole-task writes (`updateTask`, `src/lib/brand.ts:357`): `saveTask` replaces the whole task, so a writer that saves a copy it read before slow work erases whatever landed meanwhile, a render that finished by poll, webhook or sweep included. Every writer of an existing task goes through `updateTask` instead: the approve and edit route (`tasks/[id]/route.ts:39`), the publish route and the chat tool `publish_item`, both through `publishOnce` (`src/lib/publish-once.ts:108`, and `:91` for an `earlier_live` answer; called at `publish/[id]/route.ts:40` and `chat.ts:729`), the judge's scores (`judge.ts:145`), the placeholder card (`src/lib/media/start.ts:63`) and every task write of the media engine (the start's pointer `jobs.ts:190`, the start's cleanup `:247,257`, finalize `:470`, fail `:555`). Each one:
   1. takes `tasklock:{scope}:{id}` (SET NX, 10 s TTL), trying 60 times 50 ms apart, else throws `task_lock_timeout`;
   2. re-reads the task; a task deleted meanwhile returns null and is not recreated;
   3. runs the writer's `mutate` once on a copy of the stored task, which applies only that writer's fields (or returns null to save nothing, as the brand gate does on a 409, the placeholder does over artwork, and the engine does when the task no longer points at its job or, at finalize, already took it in `mediaApplied`);
   4. saves through `saveTask` (pending set and light index follow) and releases the lock; a release that fails is logged, not thrown, and the TTL frees the lock.

   Creation sites (`/api/generate`, MCP `generate_content`, the chat tools, the weekly schedule and the routines, all through `generateBatch`, section 8 step 13; MCP `save_draft`) still call `saveTask(..., true)` for new tasks; the decision records (step 8) are an append-only list, not a task write. The scope is always the caller's `sc(u)` or the one stored on the media job. So a clip that finishes during a publish, an approval, an edit or a judge run keeps its media and credits, and an approval landing during a finalize keeps its status.

Owner rewrite (2026-09-29): an admin or owner who saves new text on an item that stays approved and has no `decidedAt` (auto-approval had passed it) sets `decidedAt` in the same change (`tasks/[id]/route.ts`), with the edit's decision record, so the judge's veto (section 28) and the chat's "nobody reviewed it" (section 10 step 10) no longer apply to it.

## 10. Publishing to a connector

Files: `src/app/api/publish/[id]/route.ts`, `src/lib/task-version.ts`, `src/lib/connectors/publish.ts`, provider modules, chat tool `publish_item` (`chat.ts:708`).

1. `POST /api/publish/{id} {provider, version, again?, live?}`; session and admin+ (`publish`); task from `sc(u)`. `version` is required (400 `version_required`); it is compared with the item read by the route (409 `changed` with the task) and again in `publishOnce`'s `check` under the publish lock (step 9). The chat tool `publish_item` needs the same action but sends no version: it publishes only items a person approved (`decidedAt`), and every such approval was bound to a version (rule 561); new media on an approved, unpublished item clears `decidedAt`, so chat refuses it until a person approves again (rule 576). Since rule 3012 every render that lands stamps the item with `mediaAt` (wall clock, `withResult` in `media/jobs.ts`; rule 679 expand, a new optional field typed as `MediaStamped` in `task-version.ts`), and the chat tool asks `personSawCurrent`: with a stamp, only a `decidedAt` after it or a publish record more than 120 s younger than it (`PUBLISH_READ_MARGIN_MS`: the inbox publish is version-bound, image included, and that margin covers the seconds between its read and its record) counts, so an image re-rendered after an item went to Facebook is never sent to Instagram from chat unseen; without a stamp (older items) the rule is as before. Under the publish lock the chat tool compares `taskVersion` (title, text, image) instead of the text hash, so a render that lands during the agent-scope check is not sent either.
2. `publishTask(scope, task, provider)`: loads and decrypts `conn:{scope}:{provider}`; throws `not_connected` when absent. WordPress and Shopify create drafts; Telegram and X publish live (`publish.ts:24-44`). WordPress (rules 618-622): a post is a draft unless the body says `live: true` for that one item (any other value is a draft; `live` on another platform is 400 `live_unsupported`; the site user must hold `publish_posts`, else 502 `wordpress_cannot_publish_live` before anything is sent, which leaves no result-unknown marker). The item's artwork (`hasArtwork`) is copied into `/wp-json/wp/v2/media` (https only, png/jpeg/webp/gif, at most 8 MB, through `safeFetch`) and set as `featured_media`; a failed copy still makes the draft and answers `notes: ["image_failed"]`. `again` updates the post named by the `id` in the item's own WordPress record (digits only; never an id from the request) and sends a status only when `live` is also set, so what the owner did in WordPress stays; the update replaces the title and body with Thakeen's text and does not touch the image; a 404 makes a new draft. The title is model text (the first H1, else the item's title): WordPress, which stores it as HTML and whose themes print it unescaped, gets it through `escapeTitle` (`& < > " '` as entities), and Shopify, whose article title is plain text that themes usually escape themselves, through `plainTitle` (`<` and `>` become ‹ ›), so a planted `<img onerror>` heading never runs on the site (rule 3010). The record stores `id` for WordPress only. The chat and MCP tool never pass `again` or `live`. Body is `editedBody ?? body`; X clips to 280 chars, Telegram to 4000.
2b. Salla has no article target (since 2026-10-05, rule 628). Salla's Merchant API has no endpoint to create a blog article or a draft, so the registry lists Salla with `products` and `orders` only, `targetsFor` never returns it, and the publish route answers `wrong_target` for it. When Salla is connected, an article card in the inbox shows a note (`copyOnlyNote` in `targets.ts`, text `sallaArticle`) that nothing is sent and the owner copies the text with the card's copy button into Salla's blog editor.
3. Before publishing, the route and the chat tool apply the brand gate from section 9 to the item's current text with `publish: true` (since 2026-09-29 no placeholder of any kind, not even a WhatsApp `{{1}}`, goes out), including items approved before the word was banned: 409 `brand_violation` from the route, a refusal naming the word from the tool. On success `publishOnce` (step 9), for the route and the chat tool alike, appends to `task.published` and force `status = "approved"` (the route also sets `decidedAt` when the item was pending: publishing it is the owner's decision), applied through `updateTask` (section 9 step 9) to the task as stored after the publish, not to the copy read before it, so a render that finished during the publish keeps its media and credits. The route answers with that stored task. Both the HTTP route and the chat tool refuse a provider `targetsFor` does not list (`wrong_target`); the route refuses a rejected item (the owner's click is the approval), and the chat tool publishes only items already approved.
4. Provider error messages (up to 200 chars of the remote response) are returned to the caller in `error` (`publish/[id]/route.ts:65`).
5. Nothing publishes on a schedule. `scheduledFor` only drives calendar display and "due" notifications (`notifications.ts:138`).
6. Targets (`connectors/targets.ts`, no runtime imports, shared by the route, the chat tool and the inbox buttons): `connectedTargets` turns the stored connections into provider ids and adds `facebook` when the Meta connection has a chosen Page (`meta.pageId`) and `instagram` when that Page has an Instagram professional account (`meta.igId`). `targetsFor` offers `facebook` for `social` and `cart_recovery` items on the `instagram` or `facebook` channel, and `instagram` only for those items on the `instagram` channel that carry a generated image (`hasArtwork` from `src/lib/media-catalog.ts` at `targets.ts:33`: an `imageUrl` with `imageProvider` fal, kie or higgsfield; a placeholder card, an image with no recorded provider and a clip are not offered). Both publish through the `meta` connection (`CONNECTION_OF`). A video is never a publish target in this round; the inbox says so under every clip (`videoNoPublish`).
7. Facebook (`connectors/meta.ts` `postToFacebook`): `POST /{page}/feed` with `message`, or `POST /{page}/photos` with `url` and `caption` when `hasArtwork(task)` (`publish.ts:46`), with the Page token as a Bearer header. `publishTask` checks `hasArtwork` again before an Instagram post (`publish.ts:51`, `instagram_needs_image`), so the rule holds at the call that posts, not only in the target list. Instagram (`postToInstagram`): `POST /{ig}/media` with `image_url` and `caption` (2,200 characters at most), `GET /{container}?fields=status_code` until `FINISHED` (8 tries, 1.5 s apart), `POST /{ig}/media_publish`, then the `permalink`. Markdown headings and bold are stripped from the text. Graph error code 190 is reported as 401 and codes 10 and 200 as 403, so `refusedToken` marks the Meta connection for reconnecting.
8. Publishing a `pending` item through the route is the owner's approval: it sets `decidedAt` and appends a `publish` record to `decisions:{scope}` (section 9 step 8; `publishOnce` sets `decidedAt` inside its `updateTask` change and reports `approving`, and the route records the decision). An item that was already approved, by the owner or by auto-approve, records nothing; a failed record is logged and does not turn a finished publish into an error. Every publish that went out, from the route or the chat tool `publish_item`, is one `published` activity row with the provider (section 32), in the same round as the tracking and the decision record; a refused, busy, duplicate or unknown attempt (step 9) returns before it and writes none. An `earlier_live` answer (step 9) is a post that went out: the attempt it answers ended `publish_unknown` and wrote nothing, so the route writes that attempt's row, and for a pending item its `publish` decision (`publishOnce` sets `decidedAt` to the attempt's time and reports `approving`), at the attempt's time before it answers 409. Only an `again` gets that answer, and the chat tool never sends one.
9. One publish per item and platform (2026-09-29, `src/lib/publish-once.ts`). The route and the chat tool send through `publishOnce(scope, id, provider, {again, check})`: it takes `publock:{scope}:{id}:{provider}` (70 s from the publish route, just above its 60 s; 330 s from chat and MCP, above their 300 s; a second press gets 409 `publish_busy`), reads the item and runs the caller's `check` on that copy (rejected, brand gate with `publish: true`, for the route the version, and for the chat tool the person's approval), refuses a platform with an attempt marker left behind (409 `publish_unknown`, checked first because it is newer) and a platform already in `task.published` (409 `already_published` with its `url`), unless `again` is true. It writes `pubtry:{scope}:{id}:{provider}` before calling the platform and removes it after the post is recorded. An error removes it only when it proves nothing went out (`mayHavePosted` reads only the connector's leading status, `x 403: …` or `meta feed 400: …`: a 4xx, a refused token, a duplicate and the checks made before sending are refusals); anything else, and a kill at the time limit, leaves it, and the answer is `publish_unknown` from the first attempt, so the owner checks the platform before pressing again. X and Telegram sends time out at 45 s, so a slow platform is answered before the route's 60 s kill. Telegram errors carry the HTTP status (`telegram 400: …`); X's 403 for a repeated text is `x_duplicate` and Facebook's `(#506)` is a duplicate too (409 `duplicate_post`), which no longer marks the X connection for reconnection. A duplicate answer to an `again` after an unknown attempt proves that attempt went live: it is recorded on the item (no URL, the attempt's time; a pending item becomes approved with `decidedAt` at that time), the route writes its records (step 8), and the answer is 409 `earlier_live` with that `at`. In the inbox a 5xx without a reason or no answer at all is shown as the "check the platform first" question, and the reason a publish button is off is written under the buttons, not only on hover. The inbox shows that question with a "Publish again" button (`Inbox.tsx`, `AGAIN`); the chat tool never repeats a publish.
10. The chat tool `publish_item` refuses an item only auto-approval passed (no `decidedAt` and never published by the owner) and refuses every call over MCP (2026-09-29). The inbox's publish buttons follow the publish-strength gate, so a WhatsApp draft with `{{1}}` (approved as a template) offers no Telegram post.

## 11. Connector OAuth: connect and callback

Files: `src/components/app/IntegrationsPanel.tsx:91,218`, `src/app/api/connect/[provider]/start/route.ts`, `.../callback/route.ts`, `src/lib/connectors/{registry,oauth,store}.ts`.

1. Browser navigates to `GET /api/connect/{provider}/start?lang=&shop=`. Session and admin+ (`connectors`) required. Provider must be `kind:"oauth"` and have both client env vars set (503 otherwise).
1a. Starts are limited (2026-10-19, rule 2242, branch `claude/api-platform-integrations-ce2972`). Each start writes a state and, for a provider routed through Composio, opens a pending connection on the one Composio account every tenant shares, so after the role, provider and availability checks the route takes a hit in `rate:connectstart:{sc(u)}`, 20 an hour, every provider together. Over it the browser is sent back to `/{lang}/app/b/{u.brandId}/settings/connections?error=slow_down` with `Retry-After: 3600`; nothing is asked of Composio and no state or cookie is written. A refused role or an unknown provider costs nothing.
2. `shop` (Shopify only in practice): lowercased, scheme and path stripped, `.myshopify.com` appended when missing; anything that is then not `SHOP_HOST` (a single-label `*.myshopify.com`) is refused with 400 `bad_shop` before any redirect.
3. State: 16 random bytes hex. `oauthstate:{state}` = `{email: sc(u), provider, lang, shop, verifier, actor}` for 10 min, where `actor` is the acting login (2026-09-29). PKCE pair only when `oauth.pkce` (X). The same state goes into the `thakeen_oauth` cookie (httpOnly, SameSite=Lax, Secure as the session cookie, path `/api/connect`, 10 min).
4. Redirect to the provider authorize URL with `redirect_uri = ${APP_ORIGIN}/api/connect/{provider}/callback`.
5. `GET .../callback?state=&code=`: the `thakeen_oauth` cookie is read and cleared, and `takeState` reads and deletes the state (single use). Since 2026-10-22 (rule 2931) `takeState` is one GETDEL, so two callbacks racing on one state cannot both get it, and the callback's save, a disconnect (`DELETE /api/connect/{provider}`) and the token refresh all run under the one connection lock `connlock:{scope}:{provider}` (`withConnectionLock` in `connectors/fresh.ts`): the refresh reads the record again before its save and never writes an old grant's tokens over a new connection or brings a deleted one back. A lock held through every wait is 409 `busy` on DELETE and `error=busy` on the callback, with nothing written. Missing state or code: `error=`. A state issued for another provider: `error=invalid_state`. The cookie must equal the state (`sameSecret`) and the signed-in account must own the state's scope, else `error=session_mismatch` and no code exchange. The acting login must also be the state's `actor` (a state written before `actor` existed completes only for the owner), else `error=session_mismatch`; then `connectors` is checked again, so a role lost while the provider's page was open ends with `error=forbidden`. Shopify also needs a valid `hmac` over the query (`validShopifyHmac`, else `bad_hmac`) and a query `shop` equal to the state's (`bad_shop`).
5a. Where the callback returns (2026-10-21, rule 894, branch `claude/brand-parts-bc-29277a`). Every redirect goes to the brand of the state's scope (`brandIdOfScope(st.email)`, the scope the connection is written to, never a request value or the session's stored choice): `/{lang}/app/b/{brand}/settings/connections?…`, or `/{lang}/app/b/{brand}/todo?cal…` for Reem's calendars. With no state (an error before it is read) the old `/{lang}/app/integrations` or `/todo` path is kept and the `[...legacy]` page sends it to the session's brand.
6. `exchangeCode` posts to the provider token URL (Basic auth for X, body credentials otherwise; Shopify URL built from `shop`). All scalar fields of the token response are kept as `data`.
6b. Reem's calendars (`gcal`, `mscal`, flagged `secretary` in the registry, Reem B3): `start` gates on `calendar.link` (owner only) instead of `connectors`; the callback uses the same state, cookie, login and actor checks, re-checks `calendar.link`, exchanges the code and hands it to `connectCalendar` (`src/lib/secretary-gcal.ts`: brand still active, proof read from the Google `id_token` or Graph, an earlier link dropped with its sealed tokens and no revoke call to Google (ruling R7), the new link written in state `choose` under `withCalLock`, the owned lock on `"callink"` that the Working days route's `"cal"` lock does not share), then returns to `/{lang}/app/todo?cal=choose` or `?cal_error=<invalid_state|session_mismatch|forbidden|denied|brand_inactive|token_failed|provider_error>`; it never reaches `saveConnection`, and `POST .../key` answers 404 for these providers. Then the Todo page (owner only, `calendar.link`) calls `GET /api/secretary/calendar-link/calendars` (live list of the account's own calendars, the ids kept on the link for 10 minutes, the page gets indexes) and `PUT /api/secretary/calendar-link` `{ choice, declare?, rev }`: under the same lock the token is refreshed, the proof is read again (a Google refresh with no `id_token` keeps the stored domain, ruling R3; Outlook asks Graph every time), a save whose proof is not `domain` needs `declare: true`, and the live list must still show the calendar as owned; the save sets state `ok`, clears the old events and schedules the first sync. `DELETE` drops the link, its events and its sync entry and never calls a provider's revoke endpoint (ruling R7).
6c. Reem's calendar through Composio (2026-10-25, rules 4260-4266, an expand step under rule 679). With `gcal` in `COMPOSIO_PROVIDERS` and `COMPOSIO_AUTH_GOOGLECALENDAR` set, `start` for `gcal` opens a Composio connection (the same owner-only `calendar.link` gate, the same state with `via: "composio"`, `toolkit: "googlecalendar"` and the pending `accountId` kept server-side, the cookie and the actor) and a refusal goes back to `/todo?cal_error=provider_error`. The callback comes back with no code: `composioVerify` checks the account is ACTIVE and this workspace's, then `connectCalendarVia` (`src/lib/secretary-gcal.ts`) lists the account's own calendars through `GOOGLECALENDAR_LIST_CALENDARS`, takes the primary one's id as the account, drops and releases an earlier link, and writes the link in state `choose` with `via: "composio"`, the sealed account id in `cid`, `tokens: ""` and `proof: null`; an account that was not stored is deleted at Composio again. Composio gives no `id_token`, so no domain is proven and the owner always declares (rule 912 unchanged). The sync (`syncLocked`) skips the token refresh for such a link and reads the chosen calendar through `GOOGLECALENDAR_EVENTS_LIST` with the fixed `fields` selector (no description, attendee names, place or meeting link), the same 7-day window and the same `keepGoogle` filter; a `gcal 401` answer loses the link (reconnect), any other failure keeps the events and retries. The catalog lists the two read tools and no write tool, so Reem cannot create, move or delete an event through it. Unlink, a reconnect, an archived brand and an account wipe revoke and delete the connected account at Composio (`dropLinkFor`); a failed revoke is logged by name and never keeps the link. A deleted calendar is not detected through Composio (it retries every 15 minutes). The managed app's scopes are Composio's, not Thakeen's: the founder reads them on the consent screen before switching it on.
7. `saveConnection(st.email, ...)` writes `conn:{scope}:{provider}` (encrypted) and adds to `conns:{scope}`; the scope comes from the state record, not from the current cookie. `track(scope.split("#")[0], "connect")`.
8. Every use of a stored connection (publishing, product import) goes through `getFreshConnection` (`src/lib/connectors/fresh.ts`): an OAuth token that expires within 5 minutes (a provider with `oauth.refreshLeadDays`: that many days, Zid 60, step 14u) is refreshed first (`grant_type=refresh_token`) under the owned lock `connlock:{scope}:{provider}` (`db.lockOwned`, released with `db.unlockOwned`, so a request whose 30 s lock lapsed never frees the next holder's), and a rotated refresh token is saved. No refresh happens without the lock (rule 2192, 2026-10-18): a request that loses it waits up to 20 times 250 ms, re-reading the connection each time and using the token the holder saved; if the token is still due after the last wait it throws `RefreshBusy`, which the connect route and the publish route answer as 503 `busy` with `Retry-After: 5`, and nothing was refreshed or sent. A refused refresh, a missing refresh token, or a 401/403 from the platform sets `needsReconnect` and the call fails with `reconnect_required`; the Integrations page then shows the connection with a Reconnect button.
9. A new connection saved by the callback replaces the record, which clears `needsReconnect`.
10. `DELETE /api/connect/{provider}` writes `null` to the connection key (the id remains in `conns:{scope}`; `listConnections` skips nulls). `POST /api/connect/{provider} {action:"import_products"}` imports products for shopify, woocommerce, odoo, prestashop, salla, zid into `products.md`. The Zid callback reads the manager profile (`GET /managers/account/profile`) and keeps the store id in `meta.storeId`, the store name in `meta.account` and its address in `meta.site`; every Zid call sends the `authorization` token as the bearer, `access_token` as `X-Manager-Token`, and `Store-Id`. A 403 on the Zid product import answers `zid_access_denied` without marking the connection for reconnecting (the Integrations card shows a permissions message); a 401 marks it. Roles: `DELETE`, `select_page`, `select_org` and `select_ad_account` need `connectors` and `import_products` needs `catalog.import` (all admin+); `sync_sales` and `sync_ads` are any login's (`read`); another action is 400 `bad_action`.
10. Meta (`connectors/meta.ts` `connectMeta`, run by the callback after the code exchange): the short-lived user token is traded for a long-lived one (`grant_type=fb_exchange_token`, app credentials in the POST body), `GET /me/accounts` lists the Pages with their Page tokens and linked Instagram accounts, and the first Page with Instagram (else the first Page) is chosen. `data` (encrypted) holds the user token, every Page token and the chosen `page_token`; `meta` holds only `pageId`, `igId`, `account` and, with more than one Page, `pages` (ids, names, Instagram usernames). No expiry is stored: Page tokens from a long-lived user token do not expire. An account with no Page returns `error=meta_no_page` and saves nothing. `POST /api/connect/meta {action:"select_page", pageId}` switches the Page only to one Meta returned (400 `unknown_page` otherwise). With `META_CONFIG_ID` set, the authorize URL sends `config_id` (Facebook Login for Business) instead of `scope`. The scopes are the six publishing permissions only; Meta Ads and WhatsApp are not requested and their catalog cards no longer borrow the Meta connection's status.
10c. Imported product lines are plain and capped (2026-10-19, rule 2247, branch `claude/api-platform-integrations-ce2972`). Every `import_products` importer (Shopify, WooCommerce, Salla, Zid, Odoo, PrestaShop) writes `products.md`, the core memory every agent reads, through `productsMarkdown` in `src/lib/connectors/products-md.ts`: each field is one line of plain text (tags out, whitespace and newlines collapsed, runs of `*` made one), the name capped at 140 characters, the price at 40 and the description at 200, at most 150 rows, a row without a name left out, and the count returned is the rows written. A link is kept only when it is an absolute http(s) URL of at most 300 characters without credentials; for a host the owner typed in (WooCommerce, Odoo, PrestaShop) it must also be on that store's host or its www twin, so a hostile store cannot point the agents' content at another site. A WooCommerce or Odoo answer that is not a list fails with `woo unexpected_products` or `odoo unexpected_products` and writes nothing.
10d. The no-key catalog import goes the same way (2026-10-19, rule 2248, branch `claude/api-platform-integrations-ce2972`). `importCatalog` in `src/lib/catalog.ts` (the Files page's `POST /api/catalog` and chat's `import_catalog`) keeps a product link from a sitemap only when it is on the site's own host or its www twin (the host the typed URL redirected to), so a sitemap or robots.txt cannot send the import to another site. Since rule 2251 (branch `claude/connector-hardening-leftovers`) the sitemaps themselves are held to it too: a robots.txt `Sitemap:` line or a sitemap index child on another host is skipped, never fetched (a site that cross-submits its sitemap from another host falls back to its homepage links). Each page's title, price and description are made plain and capped by `plainLine`, and the file is written through `productsMarkdown` with that host, under the heading and Thakeen's own note to Noor. An import that found no product leaves the owner's `products.md` as it was (it used to replace it with an empty list).
11. `POST /api/connect/salla {action:"sync_sales"}` (`store-sales.ts`, `connectors/salla.ts` `sallaSales`): reads `GET /orders` for the last 30 days (up to 10 pages) and `GET /carts/abandoned` (60 a page, up to 3 pages) with a fresh token, reduces them to totals (order count, revenue by currency without canceled or refunded orders, open carts and their value, top 10 products each), and stores them in `storesales:{scope}` and in the file `store-sales.md`. No customer name, phone, email or checkout link is kept. Records whose amount cannot be read are counted as `unread`. `memoryBlock` includes `store-sales.md`, so every generation sees the totals; the Reports page shows them with a Sync button when Salla is connected.
11a. Manual syncs and imports are limited (2026-10-19, rule 2243, branch `claude/api-platform-integrations-ce2972`). Any member may press the sales card's sync, and each press reads several pages of orders and carts from the store; an import reads the whole catalog (from a user-supplied host for WooCommerce, Odoo and PrestaShop) and rewrites `products.md`. After the role check and the connection read, `sync_sales` takes a hit in `rate:storesync:{sc(u)}` and `import_products` in `rate:catalogimport:{sc(u)}`, six an hour each, every store together; over either the route answers 429 `slow_down` with `Retry-After: 3600` and asks the store nothing. The click counts even when the store fails. The stores cron calls `syncSales` itself and is not counted. The sales card shows the Integrations page's `slow_down` words. Since rule 2249 (same branch) the no-key catalog import shares the `rate:catalogimport:{sc(u)}` bucket: `POST /api/catalog` (after the role check and once a site is known, before any fetch) answers 429 `slow_down` with `Retry-After: 3600` and the Files page shows the same words, and chat's `import_catalog` answers that nothing was changed, so six imports an hour per workspace in all, store or site, whichever way they were started.
12. `POST /api/connect/shopify {action:"sync_sales"}` (`store-sales.ts`, `connectors/shopify.ts` `shopifySales`, shared totals in `connectors/sales.ts`): the same snapshot from Shopify's Admin GraphQL API on the connected `*.myshopify.com` host only (see step 13). `orders` (last 30 days, 100 a page, up to 5 pages, 5 line items each) asks for no customer, email, phone or address field; `ordersCount` gives the full count. `abandonedCheckouts` (`created_at` in the last 30 days `AND status:open`, up to 3 pages) is read for totals and line titles only, with `abandonedCheckoutsCount` for the full count; `carts.capped` is set only when the count is missing and more pages remain. Revenue uses `currentTotalPriceSet` (after partial refunds and edits) and leaves out canceled, `REFUNDED` and `VOIDED` orders. HTTP 403 or an `ACCESS_DENIED` error means the app lacks protected customer data access to orders and returns `orders_access_denied` without marking the connection for reconnecting; HTTP 401 does mark it. One store per workspace: the card shows the store last synced while it stays connected, else the first connected of Salla and Shopify.
13. Every Shopify call (`shopifyGql`) is a POST to `https://{shop}/admin/api/2026-07/graphql.json` with the stored token: products (`importShopify`, 50 active products), the blog article (`publishShopifyArticle`: `blogs(first: 1)` and `shop.name`, then `articleCreate` with `isPublished: false` and the shop as author), and the sales queries. The shop must match `SHOP_HOST` before any request. A `THROTTLED` answer is retried twice, 2 s apart. The queries were validated against the Admin schema Shopify ships in `@shopify/dev-mcp` (2025-10, the oldest supported version) and use no deprecated field. Shopify requires GraphQL for new public App Store apps; the REST Admin calls are gone.
14. `POST /api/connect/zid {action:"sync_sales"}` (`store-sales.ts`, `connectors/zid.ts` `zidSales`): the same snapshot from Zid's Merchant API on `api.zid.sa` only, with both Zid tokens and `Store-Id` (a connection without a store id asks the profile once). Shapes come from Zid's official Python SDK and its fixtures; what they do not establish is handled in code. Orders: `GET /managers/store/orders?payload_type=default&sort_by=desc` (the only list payload with products), 100 a page, up to 5 pages, with a `date_from` a day early and no `date_to` (a UTC `date_to` can be a day behind Riyadh and drop the current day's orders) — Zid does not document what the date filter applies to, nor whether `total_order_count` honours it, so every row is checked against the 30 days on `created_at` (read as Riyadh time; the zone is undocumented), paging stops at the first page that reaches past the window, at a short page, or at `total_order_count`, and the count is always the orders read, never `total_order_count`. When the page cap stops it with orders left, `orders.capped` is set and the count reads "or more". Orders are de-duplicated by id. Revenue parses the decimal-string `order_total` under the order's own currency (never a default) and leaves out orders whose status is canceled, reversed or being reversed, or whose payment was refunded or voided; `partially_reversed` still counts. A list answer without an `orders` array fails the sync. Carts: `GET /managers/store/abandoned-carts` (100 a page, up to 3 pages, `capped` past that; the pagination block is not documented, so a short page ends it) counts the carts open now (not limited to the 30 days, like Salla's), leaving out phase `completed` and counting a cart seen on two pages once, and sums `cart_total`; product names come from the detail view of the newest 20 carts (four at a time; a failed detail is skipped). Errors: 401 marks the connection for reconnecting; 403 on orders answers `zid_access_denied` without marking it (the app lacks the permission, which a new connection does not fix); 403 on any cart page keeps the orders, drops whatever carts were read and sets `carts.unavailable` (any other cart error fails the sync), which `store-sales.md`, the Reports card and Dana's STORE DATA show as unknown rather than zero. About 28 requests at most, under Zid's 60 a minute. No customer name, phone, email, cart link or WhatsApp text is read into the snapshot.
14b. `POST /api/connect/woocommerce {action:"sync_sales"}` (`store-sales.ts`, `connectors/woocommerce.ts` `wooSales`): the same snapshot from the store's REST API v3 on the owner's own host, through `safeFetch` with `httpsOnly` (the key and secret travel in a Basic header, so a plain-http store is refused in verify, import and sales alike: `unsafe_url:not_https`). `GET /wp-json/wc/v3/orders?per_page=100&orderby=date&order=desc&after=…&dates_are_gmt=true&_fields=id,status,total,currency,date_created_gmt,date_paid_gmt,line_items,refunds`, up to 5 pages, with `after` a day early; the 30 days are enforced on `date_created_gmt` (GMT, no zone mark) because whether a store honours `after` is not assumed, `X-WP-TotalPages` only stops the paging early, and the count is never taken from `X-WP-Total`. `_fields` keeps billing, shipping, customer id and payment details out of the response. Revenue is paid orders only (founder, 2026-10-05): `pending` and `on-hold` (WooCommerce's awaiting payment confirmation), and any order whose `date_paid_gmt` is present and empty, count as orders and are summed in `orders.pending`, not in revenue; `cancelled`, `refunded`, `failed`, `trash` and checkout drafts count in `canceled` and in no total; a partly refunded order counts its total minus its (negative) refunds, a fully refunded one counts as canceled; each order is summed in its own currency. Carts are `unavailable` (core WooCommerce keeps none): unknown, not zero. 401 and 403 are one error, `woo_access_denied` (WooCommerce answers 401 for a wrong key and for a key without access to orders), which asks for a new Read key; a key connection is not marked for reconnect. A body that is not a list is `woo unexpected_orders`.
14c. Twice a day (`GET /api/cron/stores` at 02:00 and 14:00 UTC, the cron bearer; `store-sales.ts` `syncStoreIfDue`, rules 623-627): for each active workspace, one store read like the button's: the store of its own snapshot while still connected, or its only connected store when it never synced; several stores and no snapshot is `choose` (the owner picks with the button), a connection marked for reconnect is skipped (`reconnect`), a snapshot under 10 hours old is left (`fresh`). A failure keeps the last good snapshot with its own date and writes `storesyncfail:{scope}` (`{at, provider, error}`, 14 days, a status or known code only); a 5xx or network error throws so the batch retries it once, a key without access or a 4xx answers `failed:{code}` without a retry. The note shows under the store card (`reports.store.syncFailed`) until a sync, scheduled or by the button, succeeds. Ads keep their own once-a-day read in `cron/daily` (`syncAdsIfDue`).
14d. Shopify compliance webhooks (2026-10-18, rules 2168-2171, branch `claude/api-platform-integrations-ce2972`; `connectors/shopify-compliance.ts`). Shopify requires every App Store app to answer `customers/data_request`, `customers/redact` and `shop/redact`, holding customer data or not. One URL takes all three: `POST /api/shopify/compliance`, public, per IP `allow("shopifyhook:ip:{ip}", 120, 60)` (429), body capped at 64 KB (413), and `X-Shopify-Hmac-Sha256` (base64 HMAC-SHA256 of the raw body under `SHOPIFY_CLIENT_SECRET`, `sameSecret`) checked before the body is parsed: 401 otherwise and while the secret is unset, as Shopify's review expects. A signed delivery of another topic answers 200 and keeps nothing; a body without a `*.myshopify.com` `shop_domain` answers 400. The two customer topics keep a receipt of topic, store and time in `shopify:compliance` (500 newest) and nothing else: Thakeen holds no Shopify customer record (step 13's queries ask for no customer, email, phone or address field), so `customer`, `orders_requested` and `orders_to_redact` are never read. `shop/redact` (Shopify sends it 48 hours after an uninstall; 30 days to complete) also sets the store's field in the fixed hash `shopify:redact`. The request names no Redis key: at the start of each stores cron run (step 14c), `applyShopRedactions` takes every workspace of `dailyRoster()`, active and idle, and in each one whose own stored Shopify `meta.shop` is a pending store and whose `connectedAt` is before the request, deletes the connection and, when the snapshot is Shopify's, `storesales:`, `storesyncfail:` and `store-sales.md`. The owner's `products.md` stays (their file; they can delete it). The store leaves the hash, with a `shop/redact:done` receipt naming how many workspaces it touched, only after the pass over every workspace finished; a failed pass is logged by error name, keeps the hash for the next run and does not stop the syncs (`shop_redactions` in the cron's answer, -1 then). Releases: two new fixed keys and no changed shape, so one release, no expand/contract step.
14e. Shopify scopes (2026-10-18, rule 2174, same branch). The Shopify app asks for `read_products read_orders write_content` and nothing else: `products` needs `read_products`; `orders`, `ordersCount`, `abandonedCheckouts` and `abandonedCheckoutsCount` need `read_orders` (Shopify's Admin GraphQL reference, 2026-10-18); `blogs` and `articleCreate` need `write_content`. `read_checkouts` and `read_customers` were asked for and never used; Shopify's review rejects unneeded scopes, and `read_customers` alone would open the protected-customer-data review for customer records. A store connected before keeps its wider grant until it reconnects (no stored shape changes). tests/registration-packs-current.test.ts pins the registry list, each top-level field of every query in `shopify.ts`, and that each direct app's row in docs/Registration_Packs.md names the registry's scopes word for word.
14f. Direct apps versus Composio-only providers (2026-10-18, rule 2175, same branch). `isDirectApp` in `connectors/registry.ts` is true for an OAuth provider that is not in `COMPOSIO_ONLY`; the start route refuses the others unless Composio routes them. LinkedIn, TikTok and the Google connector (Business Profile, GA4, Search Console) are Composio-only, so no platform app of ours, no thakeen.com callback and no `LINKEDIN_CLIENT_*` / `TIKTOK_CLIENT_*` name is needed for them; their `oauth` entries stay only to renew a direct connection made before. The founder console's Integrations page lists callbacks for direct apps only (the Google row shows `/api/connect/gcal/callback` alone and is Reem's calendar), and its LinkedIn and TikTok rows need `COMPOSIO_AUTH_LINKEDIN` and `COMPOSIO_AUTH_TIKTOK`, the names `composioReady` reads, with the Composio switch as their gate. docs/Registration_Packs.md "Current values" has rows for direct apps only and a paragraph for these three.
14g. Token expiry (2026-10-18, rule 2176, same branch). `tokenExpiry` in `connectors/oauth.ts` is the one reading of a token answer's expiry, at connect (the callback route) and at every refresh (`fresh.ts`): `expires_in` (seconds from now) first, else Salla's `expires`, taken as a Unix time above 1e9 and as seconds below it (Salla's docs write it both ways); neither gives no expiry (Shopify's offline token). Before this a Salla connection whose answer had only `expires` kept no expiry, was never refreshed early, and was lost when its 14-day access token lapsed although its one-month refresh token still worked. Salla refresh tokens are single use; `fresh.ts` already refreshes under a per-connection lock. Salla's docs (Authorization, read 2026-10-18) also say a published Salla App Store app must use Easy Mode, where Salla sends the tokens to a webhook (`app.store.authorize`) instead of redirecting to our callback; Thakeen implements only the redirect (Custom Mode), which Salla allows for testing. Easy Mode is not built: the Salla app stays unpublished until it is (Registration_Packs, checklist job `platform-apps`).
14h. Connection health (2026-10-18, rules 2177-2178, same branch). After the shop redactions, the stores cron (`/api/cron/stores`) runs `tallyConnections` (`connectors/health.ts`) over the same roster scopes: one `listConnections` per workspace through `pmap`, then one write to the fixed key `connections:health` holding, per registry platform, how many workspaces hold a connection, how many of those must reconnect (`needsReconnect`) and how many go through Composio. Counts only: no workspace, account, store or token is kept; a provider id the registry does not know is skipped. A failed tally is logged by error name and the syncs still run; the answer carries `health_workspaces` (-1 on failure). The founder console's Integrations page shows the counts busiest first (step 5 of section 69), so a platform that starts refusing tokens shows as a number before owners complain.
14i. Token keep-alive (2026-10-18, rule 2179, same branch). Salla's refresh token lasts a month when unused and its access token 14 days, and a connection is refreshed only when something uses it, so a paused brand, a suspended account or a workspace whose snapshot reads another store lost its Salla connection after a month. Between the shop redactions and the connection count, the stores cron runs `keepTokensAlive` (`connectors/keepalive.ts`) over the roster: a connection of a provider with `oauth.refreshTokenDays` (only Salla, 30, from its docs) whose access token has expired, not marked `needsReconnect`, not through Composio, is refreshed through `getFreshConnection` (its lock, the rotated refresh token saved, a refusal marks `needsReconnect` and is counted as lost). At most 60 a run, six at a time; the rest wait for the next run, 12 hours later, well inside the 16 days left. A connection with no stored expiry (saved before rule 2176) is left alone. The answer carries `kept_alive` (`{due, refreshed, lost, failed}`, null when the pass failed).
14j. Reconnect follow-up (2026-10-18, rule 2200, same branch). A refused connection (`needsReconnect`, set by a refused refresh, a 401/403 or the keep-alive of step 14i) used to show only on Settings > Connections and on the next failed publish. The daily job's `followUps` (`notifications.ts`, step 6, before Reem's overdue alerts) now reads the workspace's connections once and, when any registry platform other than Reem's calendars is refused, puts one bell entry naming them all (`Reconnect Salla, X` / `أعد ربط ...`, with the brand name on a multi-brand account), kind `info` (so the owner's Updates switch governs it), linking to `/{lang}/app/settings/connections`. Its fingerprint is the sorted provider and refusal time pairs for 7 days: a second run is silent, a new refusal is a new entry, and a connection still broken a week later is named again. Only platform names go in the entry, never an account, store or token.
14k. Zid's bearer field (2026-10-18, rule 2201, same branch). Zid's docs (Authorization, read 2026-10-18) write the token answer's bearer field `Authorization`; `zidHeaders` read only `authorization`, and `oauth.ts` stores every key with the case it arrives in, so a capitalised answer would have sent `Bearer ` with nothing after it on every Zid call. `zidAuthorization` reads either spelling and is the only reading. The same page gives the manager token and the refresh token one year each and advises refreshing about ten months in; `getFreshConnection` refreshes five minutes before the access token's expiry, which may be too late if both lapse together. An early refresh for Zid needs a lead time in `fresh.ts` and waits for the refresh-lock change (rule 2192, branch `claude/distracted-tharp-551077`); step 14u adds it.
14l. Keep-alive counts on the Integrations page (2026-10-18, rule 2202, same branch). The stores cron hands the keep-alive's counts of step 14i to `tallyConnections`, which keeps them in the same `connections:health` row as `keepAlive` (`{due, refreshed, lost, failed}`; null when the pass failed; left out when none ran). `connectionHealth` reads them back with zeros for a broken count, and `GET /api/admin/integrations` answers them as `connections.keepAlive` (null when none is recorded). The founder console shows one line under the connection counts, with a warning badge when any token was refused or failed. Counts only.
14m. Shopify `app/uninstalled` (2026-10-18, rule 2203, same branch). After the connect callback saves a Shopify connection, `subscribeUninstall` asks the store once, best effort, for `webhookSubscriptionCreate(topic: APP_UNINSTALLED)` with `uri` `{appOrigin()}/api/shopify/compliance` (step 14d's URL; no scope needed). A failure or `userErrors` answer (for example "already taken" on a reconnect) returns false and never fails the connect. The URL takes the topic beside the three compliance topics (`isShopHookTopic`; `COMPLIANCE_TOPICS` stays the three Shopify's review checks): a signed delivery whose body's `myshopify_domain` is a `*.myshopify.com` store (400 otherwise; the custom `domain` and `shop_domain` are not read for it) logs an `app/uninstalled` receipt and sets the store in `shopify:redact` at once, so the next stores run drops the dead connection exactly as step 14d does, instead of waiting 48 hours for `shop/redact` and showing the owner a misleading "Reconnect Shopify". A store connected again after the uninstall time is a new grant and stays.
14t. The uninstall time is the event's own (2026-10-19, rule 2244, same branch). Shopify retries a delivery that was not answered 200, for hours. The pending store's time in `shopify:redact` was the receipt time, so an uninstall retried after the owner had connected the store again was stamped later than the new connection, and the next stores run dropped the live grant. `recordCompliance` now stores `eventTime`: Shopify's `X-Shopify-Triggered-At` header (RFC 3339, nanoseconds accepted) when it parses and is not later than the receipt, else the receipt time. The header is not covered by the HMAC; it can only move the time earlier, which drops fewer connections, never more. A pending time never moves back: an older retry keeps the later time already held (one read of the fixed hash). The receipt in `shopify:compliance` keeps the time it was received.
14n. One `app/uninstalled` subscription per store, and the stores made before 14m (2026-10-18, rule 2204, same branch). `subscribeUninstall` first asks the store for `webhookSubscriptions(first: 1, topics: [APP_UNINSTALLED], uri: ...)` with our URL and creates nothing when one exists, so a reconnect or a second try never adds a duplicate. The connect callback now subscribes before the save and, when the store answered, saves the connection with `meta.uninstallHook` (time). The stores cron, after the keep-alive and before the count, runs `backfillUninstallHooks` over the same roster: each Shopify connection with a real shop, no mark, not marked for reconnect and not through Composio is subscribed (at most 30 a run, six at a time), and the mark is saved only when the store answered and the connection read back is still the same grant (same `connectedAt`, no mark), so a reconnect in between is never overwritten with the old token. A failure stays unmarked and is retried by the next run; `uninstall_hooks` (`{due, subscribed, failed}`, null when the pass failed) is in the cron's answer. The mark is a time, not a secret, and shows in the owner's data export with the other connection details.
14o. Uninstall-webhook counts on the Integrations page (2026-10-18, rule 2205, same branch). The stores cron hands the counts of step 14n's pass to `tallyConnections` beside the keep-alive's (`tallyConnections(scopes, now, kept, hooks)`), which keeps them in the same `connections:health` row as `uninstallHooks` (`{due, subscribed, failed}`; null when the pass failed; left out when none ran). `connectionHealth` reads them back with zeros for a broken count, `GET /api/admin/integrations` answers `connections.uninstallHooks` (null when none is recorded), and the founder console shows one line under the keep-alive line, with a warning badge when a subscription failed (a store that keeps failing is often a wrong `APP_ORIGIN` or a dead token). The compliance receipts list labels an `app/uninstalled` receipt in both languages. Counts only.
14p. A store's sales data goes with its connection (2026-10-18, rule 2206, same branch). `DELETE /api/connect/{provider}` for a store (Salla, Shopify, WooCommerce, Zid) runs `dropSalesSnapshot(sc(u), provider)` beside `deleteConnection`, as X's read goes with X (rule 747): the snapshot, its failure note and `store-sales.md` are deleted when the snapshot came from that platform, so Dana's reports, Razan's forecast and Majed's review never use the totals of a store that is gone, and another store connected later starts from its own first sync. A snapshot from another platform, and the owner's `products.md`, stay. The disconnect confirm of a store says so in both languages before the owner says yes.
14q. Another store of the same platform (2026-10-18, rule 2207, same branch). `storeIdentity` names the store a sales connection points at, as its connect step stored it: Shopify's `meta.shop`, Zid's `meta.storeId`, the host of Salla's and WooCommerce's `meta.site` ("" when unknown).
