The OAuth callback and the key route read the earlier connection of that platform before the save and, after it, `dropSnapshotIfStoreChanged` drops the snapshot, failure note and `store-sales.md` when both stores are known and differ and the snapshot is that platform's (step 14p's `dropSalesSnapshot`), so the reports never show one store's totals under another's name; the new store starts from its own first sync. The same store again (a reconnect), an unknown store on either side, or another platform's snapshot: nothing is dropped. `products.md` stays.
14r. The stores cron's time budget (2026-10-18, rule 2208, same branch). Every provider call may take up to 30 s (`PROVIDER_TIMEOUT_MS`), and the passes before the syncs (step 14l's redactions, the keep-alive, step 14n's subscriptions, the connection count) used to run again on every chained hop and to come on top of the syncs' 240 s start budget, which counted from the batch's own start. Now they run on the first hop only (a `?chain=n` hop only continues the syncs, and its answer leaves their fields out), `backfillUninstallHooks` starts no store after its deadline (60 s from the request's start; stores not started stay due and are picked up next run), and the syncs' start budget is what is left of 240 s since the request started, so the function stays inside its 300 s limit. Workspaces not started go to the next hop as before.
14s. The subscription queue (2026-10-18, rule 2209, same branch). Step 14n's pass took the due stores in roster order and a failure left no trace, so 30 stores that keep failing (a missing scope, a closed store) would take every run's slots and the stores after them would never be subscribed. A failed attempt now saves its time as `meta.uninstallHookTried` on the same grant (same `connectedAt`, no mark yet, read back after the call), and the due stores are ordered never tried first, then the oldest attempt, the roster's order among equals. A success saves the `uninstallHook` mark and drops `uninstallHookTried`. Old code ignores the new key; nothing to convert.
14u. Zid's early refresh (2026-10-18, rules 2210-2213, branch `claude/token-lock-zid-refresh`, cut from main with rule 2192). The registry's `oauth.refreshLeadDays` is how many days before the stored expiry a provider's token is refreshed; only Zid sets it, 60, so a token that lasts a year is refreshed about ten months in, as Zid's docs advise, and never in the last minutes when its one-year refresh token lapses with it. `refreshLeadMs` reads it (five minutes for every other provider) and `refreshDue(c, now)` is the one test of whether a connection is due, used by `getFreshConnection` and the keep-alive (rule 2210). Inside the lead time but outside the last five minutes the token still works, so there a lock another request holds through every wait returns the connection as it is instead of `RefreshBusy`, a refresh that fails without the platform refusing the refresh token (anything but 400, 401 or 403 from the token URL) logs and returns the connection unmarked, and a connection with no refresh token is returned unmarked; a later use or the next cron run tries again. A refusal still marks `needsReconnect`, and in the last five minutes rule 2192 and the old marking hold unchanged; no refresh ever happens without the lock (rule 2211). The stores cron's keep-alive (step 14i) also picks connections of a provider with `refreshLeadDays` once `refreshDue` says so, and counts a connection as refreshed only when `getFreshConnection` returns it no longer due, else as failed (rule 2212). Zid's refresh body carries `redirect_uri` (the callback URL), which its docs list for the refresh request; other providers' refreshes do not send it (rule 2213). Zid's `expires_in` is read as seconds (`tokenExpiry`, rule 2176); a connection with no stored expiry is never due.
15. Dana's report tasks (`weekly_report`, `board_deck`, `dashboard_spec`) get `statsFor`: the workspace KPIs and, when `storesales:{scope}` holds a snapshot, a STORE DATA block from `salesDataBlock` in `store-sales.ts` — the snapshot as escaped JSON with its sync date and age, the rule that there is no previous period to compare with, a note when the totals cover only the latest orders, a stale-sync line past 7 days, and currency codes limited to three letters. The workspace block's "sales are NOT in this data" line is replaced by a pointer to STORE DATA only when a snapshot exists. Traffic and ad spend are likewise said to be missing only without SITE DATA and AD DATA (section 50 step 5, rule 730).

### 11a. Connecting and publishing through Composio (branch `claude/composio-api-install-1d5bfc`, rules 645-652)

Files: `src/lib/connectors/{composio,composio-catalog,via-composio}.ts`, the three connect routes, `publish.ts`, `targets.ts`, `account.ts`. Applies to a provider named in `COMPOSIO_PROVIDERS` (Meta, X, LinkedIn, TikTok); the others keep the flow above.

1. `GET /api/connect/{provider}/start?toolkit=` (same session and admin+ check): `toolkit` must be one `PROVIDER_TOOLKITS[provider]` lists (Meta: `instagram`, `facebook`), otherwise the provider's first. `composioConnect` asks Composio for a link for the workspace's opaque id (`composioUserId(scope)`) with `callbackUrl = ${APP_ORIGIN}/api/connect/{provider}/callback?state=` and `allowMultiple: true` (a reconnect holds the old account until step 2 revokes it). A link that is not https `*.composio.dev` deletes the pending account. Any failure redirects back to `/{lang}/app/integrations?error=` (`connect_failed`, `composio_rate_limited` or `provider_not_configured`), never raw JSON, and logs only `errorShape` (class, status, Composio code). Every toolkit is pinned to a dated version (`TOOLKIT_VERSION`): the SDK refuses to execute on "latest". The toolkits of the first staging `COMPOSIO_PROVIDERS` list were re-pinned on 2026-10-18 to versions whose schemas hold every allowlisted argument (rule 2163); twitter and metaads keep older pins until the founder's own apps exist. `npx --no-install jiti scripts/check-composio-pins.ts [toolkit ...]`, with a Composio project key in `COMPOSIO_API_KEY`, reads every allowlisted tool at its pinned version and exits 1 on a tool missing there or an argument drift (rule 2166); run it before a pin changes or a provider is added to `COMPOSIO_PROVIDERS`. The state record also holds `via:"composio"`, the toolkit and the pending account id; the browser never carries the id back.
2. Callback: state and cookie as above, no `code`. The account must be ACTIVE within 10 s and listed under this workspace's id, or it is deleted and `error=connect_failed`. `connectViaComposio` reads the profile with listed read tools only (Facebook Pages as `id,name`, never Page tokens; Instagram user; X username; LinkedIn author urn) and saves `conn:{scope}:{provider}` with `data[toolkit] = accountId` and `meta.via = "composio"`, merging a second Meta toolkit into the same record. A replaced account is revoked. If anything fails before the record is saved, the new account is deleted at Composio (`composioDiscard`), so it cannot stay ACTIVE there.
3. Publishing: `publishTask` sends the same task to `via-composio.ts` when the record is a Composio one. Every call goes through `composioWrite`/`composioRead`: the tool and its argument names must be in `composio-catalog.ts`, 600 calls per workspace per hour, 20 s timeout, and the result is shaped like the direct connectors' errors so `publish-once` holds an unknown result and reconnects on a refused token. LinkedIn and TikTok publish only this way; TikTok sends an image as a draft to the owner's inbox.
4. Disconnect and account deletion revoke every account at Composio first (rule 651). When the platform cannot revoke (Composio answers `CONNECTED_ACCOUNT_REVOCATION_NOT_SUPPORTED` or `CONNECTED_ACCOUNT_NOT_REVOKABLE`, as for an expired Meta connection) the account is still deleted at Composio and the disconnect goes through; any other revoke error keeps the record as `revoke_failed` (rule 4270).
5. Choosing the account or Page (2026-10-25, rules 4200-4206, `src/lib/connectors/account-choice.ts`). Composio cannot force the platform's own chooser: the installed SDK's `connectedAccounts.link` takes `callbackUrl`, `alias`, `allowMultiple` and `experimental` only, and Composio's Facebook guide says the account is the one Meta's picker returned and cannot be switched on an issued token. Meta and LinkedIn remember the earlier grant and the browser's login, so a second connect can skip the screen. The Integrations panel therefore (a) says under Connect, for Meta and LinkedIn, that the platform may reuse an earlier choice, (b) offers «Change allowed accounts» on a connected Composio card of those two providers, to owner and admin only (display only; `/start` is unchanged and still `connectors`), which opens a dialog with two steps (remove the app you allowed in the platform's settings, which may list it as Composio and not Thakeen; reconnect), one row per toolkit with that toolkit's own settings link (Instagram's is not Facebook's) and the existing `/start?toolkit=` reconnect, and (c) on a Composio Meta record says that one Page was found, or that Instagram follows its own login and not the Page chosen. It does not promise a fresh consent from Reconnect alone: removing the app stops the current connection until the reconnect. No route, permission or stored shape changed.

### 11b. Drafts: WhatsApp templates, private YouTube uploads, email drafts (branch `claude/composio-api-install-1d5bfc`, rules 657-660)

Nothing in this section reaches a customer or the public; the owner finishes each draft in the platform's own tool.

1. Targets (`targets.ts`): an item with `channel: "whatsapp"` goes to `whatsapp`; one with `channel: "email"` to `brevo` and `klaviyo`; a social item whose `videoUrl` is our own copy (`isOurMediaUrl`: the disk store, or a legacy Blob copy) to `youtube`. WhatsApp and YouTube are targets only from a Composio record.
2. Gate: `gateTask(..., { publish: true, provider })` holds every placeholder as before, except that numeric `{{n}}` variables pass when the provider is `whatsapp` (`TEMPLATE_TARGETS` in `brand-rules.ts`): they are the template's variables. The publish route, the chat publish action and the inbox buttons pass the provider.
3. WhatsApp: connect reads the business account (`WHATSAPP_GET_BUSINESS_ACCOUNT_DETAILS`; none answers `no_whatsapp_business`). Publish builds a template with `whatsappTemplate` (variables renumbered 1..k with neutral examples, category MARKETING, language from the item) and calls `WHATSAPP_CREATE_MESSAGE_TEMPLATE`. Empty, over 1,024 characters or a variable at the start or end is refused before sending; Meta's immediate REJECTED is a refusal (`whatsapp 400`).
4. YouTube: connect lists the owner's channel (`YOUTUBE_LIST_CHANNELS`, `mine: true`; none answers `no_youtube_channel`). Publish stages our clip with Composio (`composioStageFile`: our own copy only, handed over as a 24-hour signed link, rule 1755, 30 s) and calls `YOUTUBE_UPLOAD_VIDEO` with `privacyStatus: "private"`, which the catalog's `fixed` values enforce in `composio.ts`. The answer links YouTube Studio.
5. Email: Brevo reads the account's senders, takes the first active one (none: `brevo_no_sender`) and creates a campaign with name, subject, sender and HTML only (no recipients, no send time). Klaviyo creates a CODE template. The HTML is the item rendered by `render` (md.ts), whose links pass `safeHref` (rules 545, 694). Both use the stored API key on fixed hosts.
6. Errors: the refusals above are in `publish-once`'s REFUSED list; `whatsapp`, `youtube`, `brevo` and `klaviyo` errors carry the provider's status, so a 4xx is a refusal and a 5xx may have posted.

## 12. API-key connectors

Files: `src/app/api/connect/[provider]/key/route.ts`, `src/lib/connectors/{wordpress,woocommerce,keyed}.ts`.

1. `POST /api/connect/{provider}/key` with the provider's `keyFields` (`registry.ts:28-81`). Session and admin+ (`connectors`) required; provider must be `kind:"key"`.
2. Every declared field is required. `type:"url"` fields get `https://` prefixed when they do not start with `http`.
2a. Failed checks are limited (2026-10-19, rule 2240, branch `claude/api-platform-integrations-ce2972`). Step 3 asks an outside service whether the credentials work, so before it the route takes a hit in two buckets of the caller's own workspace, `rate:keycheck:hour:{sc(u)}` (10) and `rate:keycheck:day:{sc(u)}` (30), every key provider together; over either it answers 429 `slow_down` with `Retry-After` (an hour or a day) and asks nothing. A check that passes gives both hits back (the rule 1183 pattern), so only failures count and a team reconnecting is never refused. A missing field, an unknown provider or a member without `connectors` is refused before the hit and costs nothing. The panel already shows `slow_down` in both languages (`src/lib/connect-errors.ts`).
3. Verification is a live call to the user-supplied host: WordPress `/wp-json/wp/v2/users/me`, WooCommerce `/wp-json/wc/v3/products` (https only), Odoo `/jsonrpc`, PrestaShop `/api/products`, Telegram `getChat`, Brevo `/v3/account`, Klaviyo `/api/accounts/`. Failure: 400 `verify_failed` with a `detail` string derived from the remote response or fetch error.
3a. Every one of those checks has a deadline (rule 2241, 2026-10-19, branch only): the user-supplied hosts through `safeFetch`'s default 30 s, Telegram and Brevo through `providerTimeout()` in `keyed.ts`, and Klaviyo's `/api/accounts/` in the route itself through `providerTimeout()`, which it lacked. A source scan holds every direct `fetch` under `src/lib/connectors` and `src/app/api/connect` to a deadline.
3b. Every reply of a host the owner typed in is read up to a cap (2026-10-19, rule 2245, branch only). WooCommerce, Odoo, PrestaShop and WordPress answers, at connect, import, sales sync and publishing, go through `responseBytes` in `src/lib/read-capped.ts` instead of `res.json()` or `res.text()`: 8 MB (`STORE_REPLY_BYTES`), the featured image 8 MB (`MAX_IMAGE_BYTES`), checked against `content-length` first and then counted while reading, so a host that lies about or leaves out the length is cut off at the cap. Past it the stream is cancelled and the call fails with `too_large`; an import writes nothing, and a WooCommerce sales sync fails as `woo unexpected_orders`. A source scan holds the WooCommerce, WordPress, Odoo and PrestaShop code free of `.json()`, `.text()`, `.arrayBuffer()` and `.blob()`.
3c. Every reply of a provider at a fixed host is read up to a cap too (2026-10-19, rule 2250, branch `claude/connector-hardening-leftovers`). Shopify, Salla, Zid, Meta, Meta Ads and X answers, every OAuth token exchange and refresh (`oauth.ts`), and Telegram, Brevo and Klaviyo in `keyed.ts` go through `responseJson` or `responseText` with `PROVIDER_REPLY_BYTES` (8 MB, the same number as `STORE_REPLY_BYTES`): the host is not the owner's, but its answer still shares the instance's memory with every workspace. Past the cap the call fails with `too_large`. An error body that is too large is read as empty, so the error keeps its status (`{provider} token {status}`, `x {status}: `) and a revoked token is still told from a passing failure. A source scan holds those eight files free of `.json()`, `.text()`, `.arrayBuffer()` and `.blob()`.
4. On success the credentials are stored encrypted under `conn:{sc(u)}:{provider}`; `meta` (site, account name) is stored in clear.

## 13. MCP server and OAuth 2.1 authorization

Files: `src/app/api/mcp/route.ts`, `src/app/api/mcp/token/route.ts`, `src/lib/mcp.ts`, `src/lib/oauth.ts`, `src/app/api/oauth/*`, `src/app/oauth/authorize/page.tsx`, `next.config.ts`.

### Personal token
`GET /api/mcp/token` returns only `exists`; `POST` revokes then creates `thakeen_` (rule 1813; older `nashr_` tokens keep working) + 24 random bytes base64url, stores `mcptoken:{sha256}` and `mcptoken-of:{email}` → that sha256 with no TTL, and returns the token once (it cannot be read back, rules 552, 555); `DELETE` revokes the personal token only. All three are owner only (`mcp.token`, 2026-09-29): a member and a founder impersonating get 403. The slot is keyed by the acting login, which past the gate is the owner.

### OAuth 2.1
1. Discovery: `/.well-known/oauth-authorization-server` and `/.well-known/oauth-protected-resource` (rewrites). Issuer and endpoints derive from `APP_ORIGIN`.
2. Client registration: `POST /api/oauth/register` is open to anyone. Keeps up to 10 `http(s)` redirect URIs, issues `client_id`, and a `client_secret` only when `token_endpoint_auth_method = client_secret_post`. Rate limited to 30 per IP per hour (429 `slow_down`); no expiry.
3. Authorize: `/oauth/authorize?client_id&redirect_uri&response_type=code&code_challenge&code_challenge_method&state&scope`. Anonymous users are redirected to `/ar/app/login?next=...`. The page checks client, exact `redirect_uri` membership, `response_type=code`, presence of `code_challenge`; shows Allow/Deny with the client name supplied at registration.
4. Approve: `POST /api/oauth/approve` (form post, session required; since 2026-10-19, rule 2305, the consent page's `ConsentForm` sends it with `fetch` and `Accept: application/json`, gets `{ok, redirect}` and navigates there with `location.assign`, because Chrome applies the enforced CSP's `form-action 'self'` to the 303 after a form post and blocked the redirect to the client's own origin; a plain form post still gets the 303) re-validates client and redirect URI, requires `code_challenge_method=S256`, issues a 10 min single-use code bound to client, redirect URI, challenge, email and `scope` (free text from the request, default `noor`). Deny redirects with `error=access_denied`. Owner only (`oauth.approve`, 2026-09-29): a member and a founder impersonating get 403, and the consent page shows them no form, only who is signed in.
5. Token: `POST /api/oauth/token` (JSON or form; client auth via Basic, body secret, or none for public clients; a secret is compared with `sameSecret`; 60 requests per IP per 15 minutes, checked first, 429 `slow_down`). `authorization_code`: the code (stored as `oauthcode:{sha256(code)}`) is taken with GETDEL, so it is single use under concurrency (rule 549), then client id, redirect URI and PKCE S256 are verified. Issues access token `nashr_...` stored as `mcptoken:{sha256}` → `{email, grant}` for 30 days and refresh token `thakeen_r_...` in `oauthrefresh:{sha256}` for 90 days; the grant `mcpgrant:{email}:{id}` holds both hashes.
6. Refresh: `grant_type=refresh_token` rotates: the old refresh record is taken with GETDEL (rule 548), the grant must exist and list it (rule 550), the grant is rewritten with SET XX (rule 551), and a new pair is issued; client id must match. The previous access token is deleted and also refused at once, because the grant no longer lists it. A refresh token with no grant is refused.
7. `GET /api/mcp/grants` lists the account's grants and `DELETE ?id=` or `?all=1` revokes (Revocation, below); both are owner only (`oauth.grants`) since 2026-09-29.

### MCP calls
`POST /api/mcp` JSON-RPC. `initialize`, `ping`, `notifications/initialized`, `tools/list` answer without authentication (`mcp/route.ts:63-65`). Everything else needs `Authorization: Bearer thakeen_...` resolved by `userFromBearer` (`src/lib/mcp.ts:36`): owner accounts only (a token naming a member login is refused), a suspended account refused, `scope` set from the owner's active brand like a browser session and only while it is one of the account's active brands (its `brands:` list read in the same round; an archived or unknown brand, or a list the store failed to answer, is the default brand, rule 3030), actor `{kind:"token", role:"owner"}`. Every tool is mapped to an action (`MCP_TOOL_ACTION` in `src/lib/mcp.ts`: `ask_noor` `chat`, `generate_content` `generate`, `list_inbox`, `read_file`, `list_files`, `get_brand_rules` and `check_brand` `read`; `save_draft` `drafts`); an unmapped tool is refused (-32602) and so is one `can()` refuses. Tools: `ask_noor` (full chat loop with all fifteen chat tools, including `publish_item`, Reem's five to-do tools and `handoff_note`; the loop runs with `via: "mcp"`, so `runTool` refuses publishing, core-memory writes, `import_catalog` and handoff notes; since 2026-10-01 it passes the same gates as the web chat (section 7 step 14) before `chat()` saves anything: the message trimmed and cut to 4,000 characters, an empty one a tool error ("Empty message. Nothing was sent.") that counts nothing; the burst limit (a tool error); then the account's one monthly allowance, shared with the app (`reserveChatTurn`), whose refusal is a tool error in plain words (`refusalText`: the messages used, when they renew, that nothing was sent, and what the owner can do on the Subscription page). The turn then runs with the request's deadline, the per-message ceiling and the history guard, and is settled like a web turn; when 10% or less of the allowance is left the answer ends with `[chat: N of M messages left this month]` ("in the trial" on a trial), and a message that continued on credits ends with `[chat: this month's M messages are used; this message continued on credits and was billed N credits]`, `generate_content` (charges credits through `generateBatch`, section 8 step 13, with `by` = `tokenBy(user)`; trial expiry is checked, section 8 step 8), `list_inbox`, `read_file`, `list_files`, `get_brand_rules` (the active brand's tone, dialect, audience, products, offers, preferred words, channels and the enforced banned words as JSON; free), `check_brand` (`{text, type?}`, up to 20,000 characters, checked with the same `gateTask` as the approve button; returns `pass`, `violations` and a summary; internal types are not held; free, no model call), `save_draft` (below).

### MCP `save_draft` (2026-09-29): drafts from the owner's own assistant
Lets an owner write with their own Claude or ChatGPT subscription and still review in Nashr. `{type, body, title?, channel?, notes?, lang?}`, one item per call (`src/lib/external-drafts.ts`; action `drafts`, any role, and an MCP bearer is always the owner):
1. `parseDraft` validates before anything is read or written: `type` must be in `TASK_TYPES` (`isTaskType`, so `constructor` and friends fail); `body` is required, at most 20,000 characters; `title` at most 200 (defaults to the body's first line, 80 characters); `notes` at most 500 (kept as `brief`); `channel` a lowercase `[a-z0-9_-]{1,30}` name (defaults to the type); `lang` `ar` or `en` (defaults to the brand's first language). Control and bidi-override characters are stripped. A field over its limit is refused with a tool error, never truncated. Any other argument (`scope`, `email`, `id`) is ignored.
2. `draftAllowed(user.email)` (`rate.ts`): 30 an hour and 150 a day per account, across its brands. Over the limit: tool error, nothing saved.
3. The brand of `sc(u)` (the bearer's active brand, set by `userFromBearer`) is read; without one: tool error, nothing saved.
4. The brand gate runs in code (`gateTask`, the approve and publish routes' check). Status is `initialStatus(false, ...)`, i.e. always `pending`: the owner's auto-approval does not apply to text the owner has not seen in Nashr. A draft that breaks a rule is saved anyway, held like an agent's draft (the inbox names the rule, `PATCH /api/tasks/{id}` refuses to approve it with 409, publish refuses it), and the tool answer returns `brandCheck.violations` so the assistant can fix and save again. This matches every other creation path, which keeps a violating item pending rather than dropping it.
5. `saveTask(sc(u), task, true)` with `source:"external"` (ad and social types get an ad code there, as on every path). No credits, no model call, no judge, no cross-agent review (section 33; the inbox card says "unreviewed · written outside Thakeen"), no trial-expiry check (like `check_brand`, it spends nothing).
6. Answer: JSON with `saved`, `id`, `status`, `type`, `channel`, `title`, `brandCheck` and `credits: 0`. 401 responses carry `WWW-Authenticate` pointing at the resource metadata. CORS is `*`.

## 14. Founder console

Files: `admin/page.tsx`, `src/components/admin/{shared,Overview,Users,UserSheet,Usage,Billing,Quality,Log,AdminNav,AdminForm,DataTable,GoldenPanel,WishlistPanel,LeadsPanel,PartnersPanel,PaymentsPanel,EntitlementsTable}.tsx` and `users-copy.ts` (one component per section, rule 874; the panels moved from `components/app/` on 2026-10-11, rule 1181), `src/components/app/AdminLogin.tsx`, `src/lib/admin-fetch.ts`, `src/app/api/admin/*` (including `admin/media`), `src/lib/auth.ts:321-500`, `src/lib/admin-stats.ts` (`overview`, `renderSpend`), `src/lib/admin-users.ts` (the Users table query), `src/lib/admin-input.ts` (the form parsers), `src/lib/admin-audit.ts` and `admin-audit-shape.ts` (the log), `src/components/app/ImpersonationBanner.tsx`.

1. Page access: `isAdminCookie()`; otherwise 404, or the key form when `?login=1` (`admin/page.tsx`). Since rule 2230 (2026-10-18) `?login=1` shows the key form to anyone without a staff or key session (`showKeyForm` in `src/lib/admin-gate.ts`), the founder's customer session included; before, that session counted as a console session and the page rendered the overview again. `isAdminCookie()` accepts a live key session or a founder signed in as themselves (`isFounderActor`, 2026-09-29): not a member of a founder's account, not a founder impersonating.
2. Key login: `POST /api/admin/login {key}`; 5 attempts per 15 min per IP; `sameSecret` compare; opens a key session (`adminsession:{sha256(id)}` with a fingerprint of the key, `at` and `seen`) and sets `nashr_admin` to the random id. The session ends after 15 minutes without an admin request and 12 hours after the key entry at most; `adminKeySession` renews it on each admin request, and `keyImpLive` on each request through a customer impersonation it opened (one `SET XX EX`, at most once a minute), so the idle window counts from the last renewal (14 to 15 minutes after the last request). Once it ends, the console's next admin call is 401 and the console replaces itself with a bilingual "key session ended" message linking to `?login=1` (or to the sign-in page for a founder session), never an empty user table (`adminFetch` in `src/lib/admin-fetch.ts`, rule 436). `DELETE` (the console's "End key session" button) deletes the record and the cookie (no auth needed). Rotating `WAITLIST_ADMIN_KEY` ends every key session.
3. Data: `GET /api/admin/overview`, `/plan` (all users with usage, and since 2026-10-01 each account's chat: `chatUsed`, `chatAllowance`, `chatLeft`, `chatPeriod`, `chatOverride`, `chatOverflowOn`, `chatOverflowUsed`, `chatCredits`, `chatUsd` and `usdPerMsg`, read with `chatStatus` beside the usage in the same `pmap` row, so a trial's count is its trial count; `chatUsed` is the raw count `ChatStatus.counted`, which can pass an allowance lowered mid-period, `chatOverflowOn` is the status's (off on a trial), `chatCredits` what the messages continued on credits were billed this month, and `usdPerMsg` is `chatUsdPerMessage` in `src/lib/admin-stats.ts`: this month's chat cost over every message sent, `counted` plus the messages on credits, null when none was), `/users/{email}` (user without `passHash`, default-scope brand, tasks of all brands, usage, the account's `chat` status since 2026-10-01 with `counted` and `overflow.credits`, and `chatUsdPerMsg`, the list's cost per message; connections metadata), `/log` (last 50), `/evals`, `/golden`, `/export` (CSV of all users), `/media` (the media provider check, step 9). The user detail pane shows the model cost per kind of request and, since 2026-09-29, per agent (`getUsage().byAgent`: calls and dollars for the work each agent did, onboarding counted as Noor's), and, after the tasks list, the latest 100 activity rows of each brand (`listActivity` per scope from the account's own brand list, in one `Promise.all`; section 32 step 6), as text.
4. Actions via `POST /api/admin/plan {email, plan?, deal?, addCredits?, seats?, chatAllowance?, videoGrant?, reason?}` and `POST /api/admin/users/{email} {action}`: `suspend`, `unsuspend`, `extendTrial` (1..90 days), `resetPassword` (returns a temporary password once), `setLang`, `impersonate`, `delete` (`wipeAccount`). All write to `admin:log` except `setLang`. The log `actor` is the founder's email or `"key"` (`adminActor`, 2026-09-29); `POST /api/admin/golden` still writes `"founder"`. `POST /api/admin/plan` also takes `seats` (an integer 1..500 sets `User.seats`, `null` or 0 clears it, anything else 400 `bad_seats`) and runs `enforceSeats` after a plan or seat change (section 26 step 13). Since 2026-09-30 (rules 243-244): `plan` is one of trial, starter, pro, business, agency; a plan change sets the balance to `allowanceOf(u)` and restarts the renewal cycle; `deal: {credits, priceSar}` sets an Agency contract (400 `bad_deal` or `below_floor` under 999 SAR, 1,500 credits or the Business rate; 400 `deal_needs_agency` on any other plan, nothing saved), `deal: null` or leaving agency clears it, and with it the seat override that holds the deal's seats unless the same request sends `seats` (logged as `seats:plan`, and the team is refitted), and a deal change alone keeps the balance until the next renewal; `addCredits` must be a finite number of at most 100,000 either way (400 `bad_credits`); the log's before and after carry the deal. Since 2026-10-01 (rule 316): `chatAllowance` sets the founder's override of the plan's chat messages (a whole number 0..100,000, `isChatOverride`; 0 stops chat for the account), `null` or `""` clears it back to the plan's, anything else is 400 `bad_chat` before any read or write; it is written under the user lock in the same save, dropped like the seat override when the contract ends (leaving Agency, or clearing the deal) unless the same request sets it (logged `chat:plan`), and logged as `chat:N` with `chat` in the before and after. The console's user table has a Chat column ("used / allowance", "(override)", "+N on credits", red at 10% or less left and at 0; the tooltip gives this month's chat cost, the cost per message and the owner's switch) whose number opens a prompt for the override, checked with `isChatOverride` before it is sent; the detail pane shows the messages used of the allowance for the period, the messages on credits, the switch ("not offered on a trial") and the chat cost; the users CSV has a `chat_allowance` column. The console lists plans from `PLANS`, shows an Agency row's deal behind an "Agency deal…" control ("no deal: Business" without one) and sets it from prompts for credits, price and seats, pre-checked with `parseDeal`; an account's first deal is sent with `plan: "agency"` so its allowance applies at once, and a change to an existing deal asks whether to apply it now (the same) or at the next renewal (the deal alone). Its Paying and Trial tiles count live accounts only, like MRR; the console's user table shows each account's seat limit (marked when overridden), sets the override from a prompt, and reports who was paused or came back and how many invites were cancelled. `resetPassword` also stamps `teamSessionsAfter`, signing every member out. Member logins never enter `users:all`, so the console looks an exact address up instead: `GET /api/admin/users/{email}` answers `{ok, kind:"member", member:{email, name, role, memberOf, invitedBy, acceptedAt, disabled, ownerExists, onRoster}}` for a member login, and `POST /api/admin/users/{email} {action:"removeMember", reason?}` deletes it (`forceRemoveMember`, under the same locks as any removal; logged), which frees the address, for example one taken through a hand-passed invite link. Every other action on a member address is 400 `bad_action`. Since 2026-10-01 (rules 446-448, section 38): the account's card has a Top-ups block (each live pack: credits left of the pack, expiry, what was paid and on which date, the payment reference and who activated it) with "Add top-up" (prompts for the pack, the amount paid excluding VAT, the payment date and the reference, then `POST /api/admin/topups {action:"add", paymentId}` through `adminFetch`; disabled on a trial; it also records the pack's payment in the Payments panel, with the partner's commission when one is due, rule 454, so the founder never records a top-up there) and "Void" per pack (`{action:"void", id}`, after a confirmation); the users list shows each balance's share from packs (`topupCredits` on the `/plan` rows) and "+ credits" now adds goodwill credits to the month's plan credits only; the Overview and Billing tabs show "Top-ups this month" (`overview().revenue.topupSarMonth`) next to MRR, never inside it. Since 2026-10-08 (rule 783): `videoGrant: {until, note?}` opens every video model to the account through the end of the UTC day `until` (a real day after today and at most 366 days ahead, a note of at most 200 characters; anything else is 400 `bad_grant` before the account is read), `null` clears it; a plan change keeps it; it is logged as `video:all@{until}` or `video:plan`, with `video` in the before and after, and each `GET /api/admin/plan` row carries `videoGrant`. Since 2026-10-08 (rule 784) the account's detail has an entitlements table (credits, seats, chat month, chat rate, video models, agents, active and ends, each with the plan's value, the override that differs from it, the effective value, what is used and when it resets) and a "Video grant" button beside "Extend trial": it prompts for the UTC day (empty clears the grant, and sends nothing when the account has none) and a note, sends `videoGrant` to `POST /api/admin/plan`, shows the server's `bad_grant` as the other actions show their errors and reads the detail again.
5. Impersonation: the console asks for a reason and a length (15, 60 or 120 minutes; rule 1177) in a form, then posts `{action:"impersonate", minutes, reason}`; when this browser's `thakeen_session` is already an impersonation session (its record has `imp`) the route answers 409 `impersonation_active` and logs nothing (rule 1350: stop the first view, then start the next); otherwise it saves the caller's current `thakeen_session` into `thakeen_return` (that length), sets `thakeen_impersonate`, then `createSession(target, minutes, {imp: who})`, whose record carries `exp` (its end; `currentUser` refuses it from then on, and resolves the founder actor with `since` and `until` for the banner's countdown; a record from before 2026-10-11 has no `exp` and shows `at` + 2 hours): the session record names the founder (or `"key"`) and resolves to actor kind `founder`, which is refused account deletion, password change, every team change, the MCP token, OAuth approval and the console itself (`permissions.md` 0.1). Logged with `expiresInMin` (the chosen minutes, 120 when none was sent), the reason and the acting founder; opened from a key session it also ends with that key session (15 minutes without a request on either side, 12 hours at most), logged as `idleMin:15, endsWithKeySession:true` (rule 435).
6. Return: `POST /api/admin/impersonate` (form post from `ImpersonationBanner`, rule 1177: `app/layout.tsx` renders it when the session's actor is a founder, `user.actor.kind === "founder"`, never from the cookie alone; it is sticky at the top of the main column, names the account from the session, counts down to `actor.until`, the session's `exp` or `at` + 2 hours for an older session, and its Stop button is 44px). No admin check; rate limit 30 per IP per 15 min first (429). The caller's `thakeen_session` record is deleted when it is an impersonation session or `thakeen_impersonate` is present; if `thakeen_return` names a live session it becomes `thakeen_session`, else `thakeen_session` is deleted. Both helper cookies are cleared. `impersonate:stop` is logged only when the burned record carried `imp`, with `actor` = that `imp` and `email` = the record's email, never the cookie value (rules 540-542). Then 303 to `{appOrigin()}/{lang}/admin/users` (rule 904: never the request URL).
7. `POST /api/admin/golden {agent?, resume?}` runs the golden set (up to 300 s). `GET /api/admin/golden` also returns `hashes`, the prompt versions deployed now (section 31), and the Golden panel marks an agent whose prompts changed since the previous run with a "prompt changed" badge.
8. Render cost (Billing section, `src/components/admin/Billing.tsx`; spec 4.9). `overview()` (`src/lib/admin-stats.ts`) fetches `renderSpend()` in the same `Promise.all` as `getUsage("_all")`; `renderSpend(month = monthKey())` reads `render:_all:{month}:m:{model}:{jobs|failed|credits|usd_micro}` for all 11 catalogue models and `render:_all:{month}:kie_centicredits` in one `Promise.all` (45 reads, no loop awaits) and sums the provider and month totals in code (`usd = usd_micro / 1e6`, `kieCredits = centicredits / 100`). `Overview.revenue` gains `renderCostUsd`, `renderByProvider` (`{usd, jobs, failed}` per provider) and `renderByModel` (`{jobs, failed, credits, usd}` per model). The gross margin is now `(MRR − (model USD + render USD) × 3.75) / MRR`, and "credits used this month" counts `t.mediaCredits ?? (t.image ? IMAGE_COST : 0)` per item. The tab shows a fifth tile "Render cost this month $", a "Per model" table (model, done, failed, credits kept, USD, $ per credit), one line per provider, and a "Check media providers" button.
9. `GET /api/admin/media` (`src/app/api/admin/media/route.ts`, `isAdmin` else 401, `maxDuration = 60`, `dynamic = "force-dynamic"`), behind that button: one `Promise.all` of `kieAdapter.check` (the kie balance) when kie is configured, `higgsfieldAdapter.check` (`/estimate` per model with its real body, plus the Seedance text-to-video body) when Higgsfield is, `probeStore` (write, read back and delete a tiny file in `MEDIA_DIR/.tmp`, and count the builds the folder has outlived, rule 1762), the sizes of the three open sets, `renderSpend()`, `offModels()` and `mjobs:recent` (0..49); each check is bounded by a 10 s `abortAfter` and a throw becomes `{error: "unreachable"}`. Answer: `{ok, configured: {fal, kie, higgsfield, store, webhooks}, modelsOn, available, store: {status, survivedBuilds, usage, limits, freeBytes}, kie, higgsfield, open: {fal, kie, higgsfield}, spend, off, recent}`, where `kie` and `higgsfield` are `null` when not configured, `webhooks` is `!!callbackUrl("kie")`, and `recent` rows keep only `{id, model, state, error, providerCredits, costUsd, at}`. Flags only: no variable value is ever in the answer (the `MEDIA_MODELS_ON` ids are not secret). The console prints it as plain text, with a false flag, `webhooks: off`, a Blob probe that is not `ok`, an error string and each switched-off model in red.
10. Leads tab (2026-10-01, #134; `src/components/admin/LeadsPanel.tsx`, `src/lib/leads.ts`): reads `GET /api/admin/leads`, enterprise and early-adopter requests (support tickets from `ContactForm`, whose "Name:" and "Company:" lines are lifted out) and affiliate applications, newest first, 50 per page, with a toggle to every support ticket and a CSV export (`csvSafe`). Opening the tab posts `{action: "seen", until}` with the time of the newest lead it showed; the server stores that time (clamped to now, never moved backwards) in `admin:leads:seen`, so a lead that arrived between the load and the post stays unread, and the tab label shows how many sales leads are newer. A warning shows while `mailReady()` is false (new leads may not reach the founder's inbox) or `WAITLIST_TO` is unset; both say that affiliate applications are never emailed and appear only in this tab. Everything shown is visitor text, rendered as text.
11. Cost per model and provider (Billing tab, 2026-10-09; section 8 item 15.8). `GET /api/admin/overview` (`isAdmin` first, 401 otherwise) carries `modelUsage`: this month's calls and USD per catalog model (and `other`) and per provider from `getUsage("_all")`, the requests re-sent to Anthropic after a gateway failure, and the customer picks the router refused, keyed `purpose:reason`. The tab lists the models by cost with USD per call, the providers, the fallback count and the refused picks, with one muted line saying the USD is at catalog (list) rates and that a request re-sent to Anthropic counts under `straitly`; the block renders nothing when the answer has no `modelUsage`. No new route, and nothing in it reaches a customer.

12. Users table (rule 1178, 2026-10-11): `GET /api/admin/plan?view=table&q=&plan=&filter=&sort=&dir=&page=&per=` filters (text over email, name, phone and plan; a plan; one state: suspended, unverified, trial ending within 24 hours, under 20 credits), sorts (joined, email, credits, plan, trial end; the email breaks ties) and pages (25, 50 or 100) on the server over the stored account fields (`src/lib/admin-users.ts`), then reads usage and chat for the page's rows only (one `pmap`). It answers the page, `matched`, `page`, `pages`, `per`, the counts for each plan and state (`facets`, over the text match), the tiles over every account (`tiles`) and the month's totals. The console keeps the query in the address (`router.replace`, defaults left out), debounces the search by 300 ms and drops an answer to an older query. Without `view` the route answers the whole list as before (Overview's tiles).
13. Account sheet and forms (rules 1176, 1180): an account opens in a `Sheet` from the inline end, up to 48rem wide, with the tabs Overview (facts and every action), Billing (Payments panel, top-up packs), Usage (model use, chat, entitlements), Activity (tasks, brand activity) and Brand (the brand file as fields, text only). A member login opens in its own small sheet. Every change opens its form first (`useAdminForm` on `FormDialog`): the fields, the reason (3 to 200 characters, required in the console, checked before the fields; the routes keep it optional, an expand), the email typed again for a delete or a member removal, the solid danger button and `role="alertdialog"` for a destructive one; Cancel or Escape sends nothing; one form at a time. The Payments panel's void and the Partners tab's payout void ask the same way. No `prompt`, `confirm` or `alert` is left under `src/components/admin` (rule 1182).
14. Audit log page (rule 1179): `/{lang}/admin/log` reads `GET /api/admin/log` (rule 1175) with the section, actor, action and target filters from the address, shows who acted (and inside which account, `imp`), the section, the action, the target, the values before and after key by key (`auditDiff`, `src/lib/admin-audit-shape.ts`, client-safe) and the reason, all as text; "Older entries" adds the next 50 from `next`; a row written before 2026-10-11 carries an "older entry" badge; the page says the filters look through the newest `scanned` rows.

## 15. Referrals

Files: `src/lib/referral.ts`, `src/lib/partners.ts` (`recordPayment` → `rewardReferrer`), `src/app/api/referral/route.ts`, `src/app/api/referral/share/route.ts`, `refer/page.tsx`, signup route.

1. Code = first 8 alphanumerics of base64url SHA-256 of `"nashr-ref:" + email`, uppercased; stored at `ref:{CODE}`.
2. At signup with a valid `ref` (rule 556): the new account gets +`REFERRAL_CREDITS` (50) through `adjust`; `referrals:{referrer}` gets an entry, `referredBy:{new}` is set, and `referral:pending:{referrer}` (hash, field = a SHA-256 of the new address, value `{at, status: "pending"}`) records the referrer's bonus as pending. The referrer gets nothing at signup. Self-referral is blocked by email equality only.
3. Referrer bonus (rules 557-558, 2026-10-05): after `recordPayment` (`src/lib/partners.ts`) records a payment that stands (plan or top-up), and on a retry of the same payment id, `rewardReferrer(payer)` reads `referredBy:{payer}`, claims the referred account with one `SADD referral:paid:{referrer}` (only the caller that added it continues, so a bonus is decided once per referred account whatever payments follow, are retried, race or are voided; the claim is never released), then `INCRBY referral:month:{referrer}:{YYYY-MM}` (Riyadh month, `EXPIRE NX` 62 days). Above `REFERRAL_MONTHLY_CAP` (10) the pending record becomes `capped` and nothing is paid; otherwise `adjust(referrer, 50)` and `paid`. It runs outside the payer's lock; a failure is logged and never fails the payment. No clawback when that payment is later voided.
4. `GET /api/referral` returns code, count (sign-ups), pending (bonuses waiting for a first payment), earned (paid bonuses plus referrals from before 2026-10-05, which were paid at signup) and the list of referred emails with timestamps. Owner only (`referral`, 2026-09-29), as is `/api/referral/share` (step 5); the refer page shows other roles a note and reads nothing.
5. `POST /api/referral/share {platform}` (x, linkedin, whatsapp, instagram): +20 credits once per platform. A legacy per-platform key `shared:{email}:{platform}` counts as claimed; otherwise the claim is one atomic `SADD` to `shared:{email}` and only the caller that added the platform is paid, so parallel requests cannot each take the bonus (`share/route.ts:25-28`, since 2026-09-20). No proof of sharing is required.

## 16. Scheduled jobs

See `architecture.md` 2.6 and 4(d). Details:

- Self-calls (rule 904, 2026-10-10): every hop a cron makes to the app itself goes to `appOrigin()` (`APP_ORIGIN`), never to the request URL, which behind the Hostinger proxy is the internal address (`https://0.0.0.0:3000`). That covers the `runCronBatch` chain (`src/lib/cron-batch.ts`: the same path and query with `chain` raised by one, with the caller's bearer), the daily job's start of `/api/cron/weekly` on Sunday to Tuesday UTC when its batch did not chain, and the secretary tick's `/api/cron/evening?zones=…` (section 25).
- Daily (`src/app/api/cron/daily/route.ts`): per (user, active brand): `renewIfDue` (since 2026-10-01 it resets the month's plan credits only; top-up packs keep what is left in them); on the default brand's entry, since 2026-10-01, `saveSettledTopups` when `getUser` took an expired top-up pack out of the balance (`topupsDirty`), which writes the settled record under the ledger lock (outcome `topups_expired`, section 38 step 6); on the default brand's entry, since 2026-09-30, `sweepOnboardHolds` (an added brand's setup charge that no run charged or reused for a day is refunded when its brand does not exist and dropped when it does; outcome `onboard_refunded:n` / `onboard_settled:n`, and one `onboard_refunded` activity row in each refunded brand, section 32 step 2); on the same entry, since 2026-10-01, `sweepWeeklyHolds` (`src/lib/schedule.ts`, rule 337: a Sunday-plan hold `User.paidOnce[weekly:{scope}:{week}]` of a week that has ended and taken over 10 minutes ago is refunded when that week has no `weekly_plan` task the team made on its own (`isAutonomous`) in its brand, else dropped; after the renewal, so a refund adds to the new month's balance; outcome `weekly_refunded:n` / `weekly_settled:n`, `weekly_sweep_failed:...` on an error, which never stops the rest of the day); then, for every scope with or without a brand, `secretaryDaily` purges Reem's items deleted over 30 days ago and closes items whose linked inbox item was approved (section 25 steps 7 and 8); if no brand and account older than 10 min and `mailReady()` (key and `MAIL_FROM`, since 2026-10-01), a one-time nudge email through `sendOnce` (`notified:{acct}:nudge1` since rule 3022, the mail to the owner's address; a confirmed marker under the address from before counts as sent; written only after Resend accepted it; a refusal is reported as `mail_refused:nudge` and retried on the next run; a marker the old claim-before-send guard wrote, which proves no delivery, is cleared once per account and message when `mailReady()`, the message is due and the marker was written before `REPAIR_BEFORE` (read from its remaining TTL, so a marker written after MAIL_FROM went live is never cleared whatever the deploy order), then sent: founder decision 2026-10-01, `notifyrepair:` flag); else `runLifecycle` (day 1, 2, 3, 10 emails, once each per ACCOUNT: markers `notified:{acct}:lifecycle:*`, mail to the owner's address, and a marker under the address from before rule 3033 counts as sent; same `sendOnce` guard and the same one-time repair of an old marker while the note's window lasts, nothing without `mailReady()`), `runDaily` (brief stored at `brief:{scope}`, no model call; since 2026-09-29 with Reem's block, section 25 step 9), `followUps` (notifications with fingerprints; Reem's overdue follow-up is number 6. The brand-level ones, `due:{day}` and `pending:{n}`, and the Sunday plan's `weekly:{day}` go through `brandFingerprint(scope, …)`: an added brand's carries its brand id, the default brand's is unchanged, so every brand of a multi-brand account is nudged once, and on an account with more than one active brand the title names the brand (`brandTag`, `withBrand`). `credits` and `trial` stay once per account (#15)), and an email according to the notification choices read under the account (`profile:{scope}`, falling back to the account's `profile:{acct}`, rule 3022; `emails`: `all|important|none`; "important" also sends when a to-do is overdue or due today). The brief or follow-up email is claimed first with `SET NX` on `dailymail:{scope}:{UTC day}` (rule 3023): a run killed after the send and retried by the batch does not mail again, a refused send releases the claim. The hold sweep (`sweepHolds`) keeps a routine's hold whose receipt expired when the routine's `routinelast` marker was written within minutes after the charge (rule 3024: the receipt lives one period, the hold 8 days). Then, for every idle scope (an archived brand, and every brand of a suspended account), the same batch runs only the purge (`purgeIdle`), so a deleted item's text never outlives its 30 days there either. Links in the nudge and brief emails use `appOrigin()` (src/lib/origin.ts: `APP_ORIGIN`, else the one production fallback).
- Weekly (`src/app/api/cron/weekly/route.ts`, `schedule.ts` `runWeekly`): charges `TASK_COST.weekly_plan` (2 credits since 2026-09-30) to Noor. `weeklyPlan` is on by default only for the default brand; an added brand's is off until the owner switches it on (`getSchedule`, rule 247), and `saveSchedule` stores only fields set explicitly, so the daily run's `lastDaily` write never freezes a default. Records written before 2026-09-30 hold the old default; since the owner's switch stamps `weeklyPlanSetAt`, an unstamped stored `weeklyPlan` on an added brand is ignored, so those brands start off too. Skips when no brand, `weeklyPlan` off, trial over, weekly cap reached (`gov.weeklyCap`, counts the items made on their own in 7 days, `isAutonomous` in `brand.ts`: `source:"scheduled"`, the routines' included, and the items of Majed's auto-run, section 35; not an MCP `save_draft` draft), Noor's monthly credit cap reached in that brand (`agent_cap`) or insufficient credits (`no_credits`); the batch itself goes through `generateBatch` (section 8 step 13, `via:"weekly"`), which reads Noor's preferences, memory, data block and feedback inside the refunding try. Since 2026-10-01 (issues #29, #31): `generateBatch` renews a paid account due its monthly credits before the charge, so on a Sunday the 1st the 03:00 plan no longer meets last month's balance before the 05:00 daily renewal (rule 332); the charge goes through `chargeOnce` under `weeklyClaim(scope, weekStartUtc())`, so a run the platform killed after its charge leaves a hold the retry reuses and one week is never paid twice (rule 333); and the task saves and the `lastWeekly` marker (`opts.commit`) are inside the refund guard, so a failure before the marker refunds once and drops the hold, while a retry after the marker stops at `ranThisWeek` (rule 334). Tasks saved before a failed save or marker are removed with the refund (rule 334). A run killed with no retry in its week keeps its hold until the daily cron of a later week resolves it (`sweepWeeklyHolds`, rule 337): refunded when that week has no `weekly_plan` task the schedule made, dropped when it has one (the run saved its plan before it was killed: the charge stands). A run killed after its saves and before the marker leaves its plan in the inbox, so the retry of that week reuses the hold and plans again: two plans, one charge. Response body maps account email to result string.
- Golden (`cron/golden/route.ts`, `golden.ts`): fixed fictional brands, meters usage under `golden@nashr.internal`, notifies founder accounts on regressions. Each run records its prompt versions and tags a regression line "(prompt changed)" when that agent's prompts changed since the previous run (section 31). It never runs the cross-agent review (`crossReview: false`, section 33): the review is notes only, never changes what is scored, and would cost a call per reviewer per case.
- Media (`cron/media/route.ts`, `20 5 * * *`, `maxDuration = 300`): `sweepMedia(mediaDeps(), {deadline})` with no job limit; section 23 step 9. Not a step of the daily cron, whose per-workspace budget cannot hold an advance that may take most of a minute.
- Opportunistic media sweep: the image, video and poll routes schedule `maybeSweep` in `after()`; it runs `sweepMedia` with at most 6 jobs, only when it wins `msweep` (300 s) and at least 30 s of the request's deadline are left.
- Routines (`src/app/api/cron/routines/route.ts`, `src/lib/routines.ts`, 2026-09-29), daily at 06:00 UTC, after the 05:00 daily job has renewed credits. Four routines, each for an agent with real input data: `dana_weekly_report` (Dana, `weekly_report`, weekly), `noor_cart_recovery` (Noor, `cart_recovery`, weekly), `razan_cash_forecast` (Razan, `cash_forecast`, monthly) and `majed_monthly_review` (Majed, `strategy_review`, monthly). The other agents have none until they have a data source. All are on by default for every brand, added brands included (rule 1486, 2026-10-12; off by default before it): `routineOn` reads a missing switch as on and a stored `false` as off, and only the owner's switch on the Scheduled tasks page stores one (`PUT /api/schedule {routines: {id: boolean}}`, only known ids with boolean values, merged per id into `schedule:{scope}.routines`). Nothing stored changes, so an owner who turned a routine off before keeps it off. The caps are unchanged: trial, gate, weekly cap, agent cap and credits are checked in code before every charge. There is no "Run now" for a routine.
  1. Items: `dueRoutines(allUsers())` reads every (user, active brand) schedule in parallel, then the last runs of the routines that are on, and keeps those that are on and have not run in their period (the UTC week from Sunday, `weekStartUtc`, or the UTC month, `monthKey`). `runCronBatch` with job `routines`, period = today's UTC date, id `{scope}:{routineId}`, four at a time, no new item after 160 s, markers for 2 days. One item is one generation, and it needs 180 s of the invocation left before its hold and charge (`BATCH_RUN_MIN_MS`, measured from the route's `deadline`, the invocation's start plus 300 s), else it answers `retry:late` at once and charges nothing; a hop that ran something and left late items hands them to a fresh invocation (`RETRY_LATE` in `cron-batch.ts`: unlike `retry:locked` and `retry:paused`, which would only spin the chain, a late item starts in the next hop's first wave). A hop in which every item it took was late does not chain. The same batch also carries Majed's auto-run items (`dueHandoffs`, at most one per brand scope, id `{scope}:auto_handoff`, listed first; section 35). A missed day needs no catch-up route: the next day's run asks again and the period check decides. The batch's attempt count is per day, so charges left by killed runs are bounded by the runner's own count per routine and period (step 4).
  2. `runRoutine(user, id, deadline)` in this order: brand (`no_brand`); switched on (`off`); not yet run in its period (`routinelast:{scope}:{id}`, `already_this_period`); model state (`retry:paused` while paused, `skip:mock` without a key, since placeholders must never be charged); lock `routinelock:{scope}:{id}` (300 s, `retry:locked`); trial (`trial_over`); the last run read again under the lock; the gate; the invocation's time left (`retry:late` with less than 180 s, before the hold, the attempt count and the charge: a batch the platform kills after its charge keeps it and saves nothing); the weekly cap and the batch's hold; the attempt count (`attempts_exhausted`); the charge. The hold is deleted and the lock released in a `finally`, after the batch is saved.
  3. Gates, checked in code; a gate that fails answers `idle` or `no_data`, charges nothing and is final for the day (the next day's run asks again). Only Noor's gate asks for data newer than the routine's last run; Razan's and Majed's look at a fixed window, so they can run again in the next month on the same sync or decisions. Dana: an item the owner created or any decision in the last 7 days (light index `createdAt`/`decidedAt`, newest decision record), or a store sync (`storesales:{scope}.at`), an ad-account read (`adsync:{scope}:{provider}`) or a person's Refresh of the site or social figures (`ownersync:{scope}`, section 50 step 9; the daily cron's site and social reads do not count, rule 736) in the last 7 days; items made on their own (`isAutonomous`: the weekly plan, a routine or Majed's auto-run) do not count, or the team would keep itself busy for an absent owner; a handoff item a login ran, and an MCP draft, do. Noor: abandoned-cart data (`missingData` is null, else `no_data`) synced after the routine last ran (else `idle`). Razan: a store sales snapshot from the last 35 days (else `no_data`). Majed: decisions on at least 3 different items in the last 30 days, from the decision records or the light index's `decidedAt` (else `idle`).
  4. Weekly cap: the items made on their own in the last 7 days (`isAutonomous`: `source:"scheduled"` and Majed's auto-run items), plus the batches the brand's other routines or its auto-run hold right now (`routinehold:{scope}:{id}` and `handoffhold:{scope}` = their `TASK_COUNT`), plus this batch's `TASK_COUNT` must not pass `gov.weeklyCap` (`cap_reached`); the weekly plan, the routines and the auto-run share it (`holdBatch(scope, holdKey, count, cap)`). The cron runs a brand's routines side by side, so this check and setting the routine's own hold (360 s) happen under a short lock per brand (`routinecap:{scope}`, waited for up to about 2 s, else `retry:locked`); holds are read before the items, and a run deletes its hold only after its batch is saved (in `runRoutine`'s `finally`), so every batch is counted once in one of the two. Then the attempt count `routinetry:{scope}:{id}:{period}` (8 or 35 days) is raised before the charge and lowered again when the charge is refused or refunded; a platform kill leaves it raised, and once two such charges are left in the period the routine answers `attempts_exhausted` (final for the day, nothing charged) until its next period. Then `charge(email, TASK_COST[type], {scope, agent, cap: gov.agentCaps[agent]})` (`agent_cap`, `no_credits`) and `warnAgentCap`.
  5. The charge, the refund and the save go through `generateBatch` (section 8 step 13, `via:"routine"`, with the routine's attempt as `settle` and the invocation's `deadline`, which bounds the cross-agent review of Majed's monthly review, section 33). Inside the refunding try, one round: `listPreferencesFor`, `memoryBlock`, `statsFor(type)` (Dana and Majed: WORKSPACE DATA, plus STORE DATA when a store was synced; Noor: CART DATA; Razan: STORE DATA) and `feedbackFor`; then `generate` with the routine's note. A throw refunds with the charge's receipt, lowers the attempt count and is rethrown (the cron counts a failed attempt).
  6. The batch is saved in one parallel round with `source:"scheduled"` and `brief` = the routine id, judged with the same grounding (`judgeLater`), `routinelast:{scope}:{id}` is set, and one `pending` notification goes to the account (fingerprint `routine:{scope}:{id}:{period}`, 8 or 35 days). The answer is `ok:N`.
  7. The outcome of every run that took the lock is recorded in the activity log (`recordRun`, section 32 step 3); the Sunday plan's the same way, `via:"weekly"`.
- Users can trigger their own runs: `POST /api/schedule {run:"weekly"|"daily"}`. Any login may (the run is charged to the account); turning routines or the weekly plan on or off (`PUT /api/schedule`) needs admin+ (`schedule.write`). A weekly Run now passes the acting login's `byOf(u)`, so its rows name who clicked it (the owner, a member or the founder through support access); the cron's are the system's.

## 17. Support and contact tickets

`POST /api/support {email, message, type?, page?, lang?}` (`src/app/api/support/route.ts`): anonymous allowed; rate limit 10 per IP per hour; when a session exists the ticket records the acting login as `user` (a member's own address) and the account's email as `account`; pushes to `support-tickets`; emails `WAITLIST_TO` when set. `GET` (admin) returns the latest 500. Tickets have `status:"open"` and no update path. `POST /api/integrations` records `email` and `account` the same way.

`POST /api/affiliates` stores applications the same way (`affiliate-applications`), `GET` is admin only. Since 2026-10-01 both lists are read by the founder console's Leads tab (section 14).

## 18. Waitlist

`src/app/api/waitlist/route.ts`. `POST {email, company?, industry?, lang?}`: anonymous; dedupes on `waitlist:emails` through the shared store (`db`, 2026-10-01: memory in development; in a production deployment without Redis the request fails instead of pretending to store); the record is never logged (it holds an email address, rule 432). `GET [?format=csv]`: `isAdmin(req)`, like every admin route (the `x-admin-key` header, a key session or a founder session; never `?key=`). No UI in `src/` posts to this route at present.

## 19. Launch journey (milestones, levels, streak)

Files: `src/lib/journey.ts` (pure: `computeJourney`, `levelFor`, `decisionStreak`; the browser imports it), `src/lib/journey-load.ts` (server: one parallel round), `src/components/app/JourneyCard.tsx`; shown on the inbox (`app/page.tsx`) and at the top of the chat thread until complete (`chat/page.tsx`).

1. Seven milestones, each read from the workspace, never from a counter the interface keeps: `brand` (brand file exists), `guard` (at least one banned word), `approve` (an item approved by the owner, i.e. with `decidedAt`; auto-approved items do not count), `schedule` (any `scheduledFor`), `connect` (any connection), `publish` (any item with a live publish), `ai` (a personal MCP token or an OAuth grant).
2. Levels by milestones done: seed 0, sapling 2, palm 4, oasis 7. Points per milestone sum to 150. Points and levels are cosmetic, are not credits and buy nothing; the card says so.
3. Streak: consecutive UTC days, ending today or yesterday, with at least one owner decision (`decidedAt`).
4. Celebration: the card compares the done set and level with `localStorage` key `nashr:journey:v1` and plays confetti and a toast once per new milestone (level change louder). First visit in a browser celebrates only `brand`. Storage failures are ignored. The inbox dispatches `nashr:milestone` after a successful approve, date or publish so the card updates before the next server render. Motion is disabled under `prefers-reduced-motion`.
5. The owner can hide the card; the choice is kept in the same storage key.

## 20. Campaign results import (CSV)

Files: `src/lib/perf-import.ts`, `src/lib/perf-store.ts`, `src/app/api/performance/route.ts`, `src/components/app/PerformanceImport.tsx` (on the reports page).

1. The owner exports an ad-level report (Meta, Snapchat, TikTok, Google Ads, or an email tool) and drops the CSV on the reports page. The browser reads the text (2 MB at most) and posts `{csv, file, metric?}` to `POST /api/performance`.
2. `readRows`: RFC 4180 parse with the delimiter detected from the header; the header is the first of the first twelve rows naming two known columns (English or Arabic synonyms, most specific first: "link clicks" before "clicks"); totals lines are skipped; at most 5,000 rows. Names and text are stripped to one plain line (`clean`). Missing name column or metrics: 422 with a reason code.
3. `analyze`: the metric defaults to what the file supports (ROAS, then cost per result, CTR, open rate, conversion rate) and can be switched. A row is ranked only above the metric's sample minimum (500 impressions for CTR, 30 clicks for cost per result, 50 spend for ROAS, 30 clicks for conversion rate, 100 sends for open rate). Findings: top and bottom three, best against the median, wording patterns (offer, number, question, Arabic, short) compared by pooled metric when both groups have three or more ads and differ by 20% or more, best and weakest placement, and spend with no result when it is 10% or more of spend. Confidence is "medium" at ten times the sample minimum with five or more ads, otherwise "low"; never "high".
4. `savePerf` stores the snapshot in `perfimport:{scope}` and writes `performance-learnings.md`, which `memoryBlock` includes, so every later generation, the weekly plan included, reads it. The upload's own totals go to `adstats:{scope}:csv` in the same `Promise.all` (section 50). The file states that quoted names are data, not instructions, and that findings are comparisons, not promises. The reports card links to chat with prompts to write variants of the winners or plan next week.
5. No model call and no credits. `DELETE` removes the snapshot, the file and `adstats:{scope}:csv`. An upload replaces the last snapshot and the file, so replacing is deleting: `POST` needs admin+ (`performance.import`) and `DELETE` needs admin+ (`performance.delete`). A connected ad account's sync (section 21) reads the account's own numbers and stays open to every login. A member sees the findings with a note instead of the upload and remove controls.

## 21. Ad results from Meta and Snapchat (read only)

Files: `src/lib/connectors/ads.ts`, `src/lib/perf-store.ts` (`syncAds`, `syncAdsIfDue`), `api/connect/[provider]` (`sync_ads`, `select_ad_account`), `api/connect/[provider]/callback` (`connectAds`), `api/cron/daily`, `PerformanceImport.tsx`, `IntegrationsPanel.tsx`.

1. Connect: `meta_ads` is a separate OAuth connection from `meta` (Pages) with the scope `ads_read` only, so a pending App Review of `ads_read` never breaks Page publishing; it uses the same `META_APP_ID` and `META_APP_SECRET`. `snapchat` uses `snapchat-marketing-api`. After the code exchange, `connectAds` trades Meta's token for a long-lived one (about 60 days; no refresh token, so `fresh.ts` asks for a reconnect when it lapses) and lists ad accounts (`GET /me/adaccounts`, active only; Snap `GET /me/organizations?with_ad_accounts=true`, ACTIVE only). The first is chosen; `meta.adAccounts` lists the rest by id, name and currency. No active account: the callback returns `error=no_ad_account` and nothing is saved.
2. Read: `POST /api/connect/{meta_ads|snapchat} {action:"sync_ads"}`. Window: the last 30 whole UTC days ending yesterday. Meta: `GET /{act}/insights?level=ad&fields=ad_id,ad_name,impressions,inline_link_clicks,spend,actions,action_values&time_range=…`, following `paging.next` only on the Graph host (10 pages, 2,000 rows at most); purchases and purchase value from `omni_purchase`, else `purchase`, else the pixel purchase, never summed; ad copy from `GET /?ids=…&fields=creative{body,title}` for up to 50 ads, best effort. Snapchat: ad names from `GET /adaccounts/{id}/ads`, then `GET /adaccounts/{id}/stats?granularity=TOTAL&breakdown=ad&fields=impressions,swipes,spend,conversion_purchases,conversion_purchases_value`; spend and value are micro-currency and divided by 1,000,000; swipes are the clicks.
3. The rows go through `analyze` (section 20) and `savePerf` with `source` set to the provider, so the latest results, from an upload or an account, are what `performance-learnings.md` holds. `adsync:{scope}:{provider}` records the read time, and `adstats:{scope}:{provider}` keeps this account's own totals and, where one was read, the previous 30 days' (section 50, steps 1 and 6). No model call and no credits.
4. Daily: `dailyFor` calls `syncAdsIfDue`, which reads the connected ad account whose last read is oldest, once it is more than 20 hours old, one platform a day. A failure is recorded in the outcome (`ads_failed:…`) and does not stop the rest of the workspace's day; a 401 or 403 marks the connection for reconnecting.
5. Switch account: `{action:"select_ad_account", accountId}` accepts only an id in `meta.adAccounts`. Switching needs admin+ (`connectors`); `sync_ads` is any login's.
6. Nothing creates, edits, pauses or funds an ad. The readers send no request other than GET, apart from Meta's token exchange.
7. Through Composio (2026-10-05, branch `claude/composio-api-install-1d5bfc`, rules 653-655): when `COMPOSIO_PROVIDERS` names `meta_ads`, `snapchat`, `google_ads` or `tiktok_ads`, Connect goes through Composio (section 11a) with the toolkit `metaads`, `snapchat`, `googleads` or `tiktok_ads`. `connectViaComposio` lists the ad accounts with `adAccountsVia` (`src/lib/connectors/ads-via-composio.ts`: Meta `act_` accounts with status 1, Snapchat accounts of the login's organisations with a UUID id and status ACTIVE, Google customers from the accessible list, at most 10, manager accounts skipped, TikTok advertisers with an ENABLE status) and stores them with `accountMeta`; none answers `error=no_ad_account`. `syncAds` reads a Composio record with `adRowsVia`: Meta insights per ad paged by the cursor Meta returned; Snapchat ads (running first, at most 50) with stats per ad, money in micro-units; Google one constant GAQL query (`googleQuery`, only the two computed dates vary), `cost_micros / 1e6`; TikTok the integrated report per ad, revenue = spend × `complete_payment_roas`. One failed Snapchat or Google item is skipped; all failed throws the first error so a refused token asks for a reconnect. Google Ads and TikTok Ads have no direct path (`COMPOSIO_ONLY`). The catalog lists no write tool for these toolkits, so nothing here can create, edit, pause or fund an ad (the founder's rule of 2026-10-05: ads are fetched and analysed only). Meta's creative text is not read: wording findings use ad names only.

## 21a. Site figures from Google Analytics 4 and Search Console (read only, 2026-10-05, rule 656)

Files: `src/lib/site-stats.ts`, `src/lib/connectors/via-composio.ts` (`gaProperties`, `gscSites`), `api/connect/[provider]/callback`, `api/cron/daily`, `src/lib/stats.ts`, `src/components/app/SiteStats.tsx`, the Reports page.

1. Connect: the `google` provider exists only through Composio (`COMPOSIO_ONLY`), with two toolkits on one record: `google_analytics` (the first `properties/{digits}` the login can read; none answers `no_ga_property`) and `google_search_console` (the first verified site, `sc-domain:` or an http(s) URL; none answers `no_gsc_site`). Other properties and sites are kept in `meta.gaProperties` and `meta.gscSites`, and the owner or an admin chooses among them with `select_property` (section 50 step 8). A first read runs in the callback; its failure is logged and the daily run retries.
2. Read (`syncSite`): GA4 sessions, users, engagement rate, key events and purchase revenue by default channel group plus the top 10 landing pages, over the 28 days ending yesterday; Search Console clicks, impressions and position by date (position weighted by impressions) plus the top 15 queries, over the 28 days ending three days before the read. Both parts run together; one failing keeps the other and records a short error code. Both failing throws the first error. Stored at `sitestats:{scope}`. Each part also reads the 28 days before its window, and a figure not read is null (section 50 step 7).
3. Daily: after `syncAdsIfDue`, `dailyFor` calls `syncSiteIfDue`, which reads a Composio Google record whose last read is more than 20 hours old; a refused token marks the record for reconnect. `syncSiteIfDue(scope, now, true)` reads at once whatever the age, and `readSiteSoon` runs it after the response (section 50 step 7); any login's Refresh (`sync_site`) runs it at once, six an hour per workspace (section 50 step 9).
4. Dana: `statsFor` adds `siteDataBlock` after the store block: the totals inside `<site_data>` serialised with `asData`, queries and page paths cleaned and labelled as data from the public, with rules (null is not read; compare only with a part's `prev`, no change over time without one, section 50 step 7; the revenue's currency is not named so it is never converted or added to store or ad figures; users are never summed).
5. Reports: the site card (`SiteStats.tsx`) shows the same totals, a dash for a figure not read and a change only where `prev` was read; a direct (non-Composio) `google` record does not count as an analytics source.
6. Nothing writes to Google: the catalog lists no write tool for either toolkit.

## 21b. Instagram and LinkedIn page results (read only, 2026-10-08, rules 663-673)

Files: `src/lib/social-stats.ts`, `src/lib/publish-once.ts`, `api/cron/daily`, `src/lib/stats.ts`. Spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-08-social-results-via-composio-design.md`.

1. Parts: Instagram is read when the `meta` record is a Composio one holding an Instagram account and `meta.igId`; the LinkedIn page when the `linkedin` record is a Composio one with `meta.liOrg`; the Facebook Page when the `meta` record is a Composio one with the `facebook` part and a digits-only `meta.pageId` (section 50 step 13). A part not held is skipped, not failed. LinkedIn: `LINKEDIN_GET_SHARE_STATS` for the page over the last 28 whole days and the 28 before them (56 days in one call, split by day: section 50 step 11; DAY rows, summed in code: impressions, clicks, likes as reactions, comments, reposts; engagement is their sum over impressions, `null` at zero) and `LINKEDIN_GET_NETWORK_SIZE` (`COMPANY_FOLLOWED_BY_MEMBER`) for followers, `null` when that read fails, and the page's own views from `LINKEDIN_GET_ORG_PAGE_STATS` (section 50 step 12), `null` when that read fails. A 403 on the share statistics is `li_no_page_access`, written and not thrown.
2. Read (`syncSocial`): Instagram username and followers, account reach and views over 28 days and over the 28 days before (`prev`, section 50 step 11), then the posts of the last 28 days (25 per page, following Instagram's cursor until 30 posts or a repeated cursor; `paging.next` is never stored), then each post's views, reach, likes, comments, saves and shares four at a time, with one retry without views and shares. A timeout or a rate limit is not retried, and past the shared 30-second deadline (section 50 step 11) no further post (or media page) is asked for: those posts keep `null` figures. A number not read is `null`, never zero. Captions are cleaned and cut to 80 characters; a post link is kept only on `instagram.com`. A post is marked as Thakeen's from `socialpub:{scope}`, the post ids `publishOnce` recorded. Both parts run together and the snapshot at `socialstats:{scope}` keeps what succeeded, with a short error code per failed part (the v2 shape and its monthly history: section 50 step 10). A refused token is thrown with its provider after the rest is written; a personal Instagram account is recorded as `ig_not_professional` and not thrown, but only when the platform's refusal says so (professional, business account, nonexisting field, unsupported get request): a network failure, a timeout or an unknown error is a failed read and the last good snapshot stays; anything else is thrown only when nothing was read.
3. Daily: last in `dailyFor`, after the brief and its email, so a slow platform never costs the workspace its brief, `dailyFor` calls `syncSocialIfDue`, which reads the held parts whose records are not waiting for a reconnect, once the last read is more than 20 hours old. A failure is reported as `social_failed:…` and the workspace's day goes on; a refused token marks that record for reconnect. Any login's Refresh (`sync_social` on `meta`, `linkedin`, `youtube` or `tiktok`) runs `syncSocialIfDue(scope, now, true)` at once, six an hour per workspace (section 50 step 9).
4. Dana: `statsFor` adds `socialDataBlock` after the site block: the account totals and posts inside `<social_data>` serialised with `asData`, labelled as data, with rules (compare only with `prev`, never invent a change, section 50 step 10; followers are a count on the read date; each post's figures are its lifetime totals on the read date; LinkedIn engagement is a fraction, 0.0733 meaning 7.33%; `null` is unknown, never zero; a read older than 7 days carries its date).
5. Reports: the social card (`SocialStats.tsx`) shows the account, its followers, 28-day reach and views, and the five best posts by views then reach, a dash for a number not read, a link only to the platform's own post page (Instagram, or https `facebook.com` with no query string for the Facebook Page; `noopener noreferrer`), and «من ذكين» on Thakeen's posts; a personal account shows why it has no results. The LinkedIn page shows its followers, 28-day impressions, engagement rate and actions; a Composio LinkedIn record with no page chosen (`liNeedsPage`) or a 403 shows «اربط صفحة شركة». The followers tile unlocks only once a part was read (`socialRead`); a record counts as a social source only once a part can be read (`connectedSources` in `report-sources.ts`): a Composio Meta record with `meta.igId` or `meta.pageId` (the Facebook Page, read since rule 739), a Composio LinkedIn record with `meta.liOrg`; a direct record or a LinkedIn record with no page keeps the banner.
6. Nothing posts, replies or edits: the catalog lists the read tools only (rule 663).
7. LinkedIn pages (rule 671): connecting LinkedIn through Composio also reads `LINKEDIN_GET_COMPANY_INFO` (approved roles only) and keeps up to 20 company pages the member administers (`ADMINISTRATOR` or `CONTENT_ADMINISTRATOR`, URN `urn:li:organization:{digits}`) in `meta.liOrgs`, with cleaned names. One page is chosen at once (`meta.liOrg`); with several, the page chosen before stays only if still listed. A refused page list leaves the connect as it was, with no pages. Every connect drops `liPostAs`. `POST /api/connect/linkedin {action:"select_org", org}` (admin+) accepts only a listed URN (400 `unknown_org`) and sets posting back to the member, writing a `connector_changed` row `linkedin:post_as:me` when the page had been posting; it then reads the new page's results at once. The Integrations card shows the page picker.
8. Post as the page (rule 672): `POST /api/connect/linkedin {action:"post_as", value}` (admin+) takes only `me` or `org` (400 `bad_value`) and `org` only with a chosen page (400 `no_page`). `postToLinkedInVia` posts as `meta.liOrg` only when `liPostAs` is `org`; absent means the member. Choosing another page (`select_org`) and every reconnect set posting back to the member. The Integrations card shows «انشر باسم» only once a page is chosen.
9. First read: after a Composio connect of Instagram (the `meta` record, `instagram` toolkit) or LinkedIn, and after `select_org`, `readSocialSoon` reads at once whatever the last read's age, after the response (`after`); a failure only logs `social first read failed` and the daily run reads again. A LinkedIn connect with several pages and none chosen reads nothing until a page is chosen. Amended 2026-10-08 (section 50 steps 13-15): every Composio social connect (the Facebook Page, a YouTube channel, a TikTok account too) reads at once, and so does `select_page` on a Composio Meta record while the workspace's `socialsync` bucket lasts.
