Files: `src/emails/` (`html.ts`, `tokens.ts`, `copy.ts`, `link.ts`, `layout.ts`, `button.ts`, the templates `reset-password.ts`, `team-invite.ts` and `notice.ts`, the registry `index.ts`), `src/lib/notify.ts` (`sendEmail`, `sendOnce`) and the callers in item 1. Rules 685-689 of `tests.md` (`tests/mail-templates.test.ts`). No new environment variable.

1. Which senders use the HTML layout. Each caller still writes the subject and the plain-text part, and passes the HTML part as the last argument of `sendEmail` or `sendOnce`:
   - the password reset (`POST /api/auth/forgot`, section 4): `resetPassword({lang, link})`, a heading, the greeting and intro, one button to the reset link, the one-hour note, the bare link under it and the ignore line;
   - the team invitation (`POST /api/team/invite`, section 26 step 3): `teamInvite({lang, inviterEmail, role, link})`;
   - the lifecycle notes (day 1, 2, 3 and 10, from the daily cron): `lifecycleHtml` (`src/lib/lifecycle.ts`) puts the note's own subject and paragraphs into `notice`; the paragraph that is only the app's address becomes the button, which opens the subscription page (`/{lang}/app/billing`) for the day 2 and day 3 trial notes and `/{lang}/app` otherwise;
   - Reem's reminder (section 36 step 4), her evening wrap (section 36) and her warning before closed copies of a recurring series are deleted (section 40 item 7): `notice` with the bell's title and body and one button, «افتح مهامي» / "Open To-dos", to `/{lang}/app/todo` (the series warning to `/{lang}/app/todo?series={seriesId}`).
   The founder's support copy (`/api/support` to `WAITLIST_TO`) and the daily cron's own mails stay text only (item 7).
2. Language and direction. The reset mail is in the language the form asked for (`lang`, `ar` unless `en`), the same as its text; the invitation in the invite's language (the request's `lang`, else the inviter's); the lifecycle notes and Reem's mails in the account's language (`user.lang`). `layout` writes `lang` and `dir` (`rtl` or `ltr`) on `<html>` and `<body>` and again, with the same `direction` and `text-align` in CSS, on every table, cell, heading and paragraph, and each cell also gets an `align` attribute for Outlook: Gmail drops the attributes of `<html>` and `<body>`, so an Arabic mail that set them only there would open left to right and be read in the voice of Gmail's own language. The card is a 560-pixel table centred on the ground colour, with an Outlook-only (MSO) ghost table of the same width, also carrying `lang` and `dir`, because Outlook for Windows ignores `max-width`. Addresses and links inside Arabic text are left-to-right runs, and no Arabic line draws an arrow (rule 687).
3. Escaping. Every template builds its markup with the `html` tagged template (`src/emails/html.ts`), which escapes `&`, `<`, `>`, `"` and `'` in every interpolated value; only markup the same module family made, or `raw()` with a string literal written in the source, goes in as it is. Copy slots (`{inviter}`, `{role}`) are filled once, so a value that names another slot stays as typed. Task titles, Reem's bodies, the inviter's address and anything else a person or a model wrote come out as text: no template renders Markdown (rule 686).
4. Links. Every href goes through `appLink` (`src/emails/link.ts`), which accepts only `appOrigin()` followed by a path, checked by prefix and again after parsing, and throws otherwise; a throw is a programming error the tests catch, never a link sent elsewhere. Every link is built from `appOrigin()`, never from the request's host. An address that appears inside a notice's text stays text; only the button is a link (rule 687).
5. Multipart. `sendEmail(to, subject, text, html?)` sends the plain-text part always and adds `html` only when given; without it Resend receives no `html` key at all, as before. `sendOnce` forwards the HTML part (rule 689). The text part of each mail is the one it sent before this change, word for word, so a client that shows no HTML, a screen reader in text mode and every test that reads a token out of the text still get the same words. Resend click tracking must stay off for the sending domain: it would rewrite the HTML part's links through Resend's redirect, and the reset and join links carry their token in the fragment (rule 546). It is a dashboard setting that no code or test can check.
6. The invite relay rule. The invitation goes from the product's own domain to an address the inviter typed, so its HTML part, like its text, carries only the invite's language, the inviter's address, the role and the join link (`#t=` fragment). A display name or a brand name, free text the sender chose, never reaches the invited address; the template has no field that could carry one and ignores any extra it is handed (rule 688). The send-or-withdraw rule of section 26 step 3 is unchanged.
7. What moves later. After the Paymob branch (`claude/quizzical-galileo-5b3662`) merges: the daily brief, the nudge and the follow-up mail of the daily cron move to the HTML layout (they stay text only until then), and the spec's other later items (email verification, Google sign-in and a magic link, a sessions list) follow; the founder kept signup open on 2026-10-08, so there is no invite-only switch.

## 50. Social platform analytics in the reports (2026-10-08)

Files: `src/lib/ad-stats.ts`, `src/lib/perf-store.ts` (`syncAds`, `prevOf`), `src/lib/connectors/ads.ts` (`prevNow`), `src/lib/connectors/ads-via-composio.ts` (`snapVia`, `snapPrevSpendVia`, `linkedinAdsVia`, `LI_ADS_FIELDS`), `src/lib/figures.ts`, `src/lib/connectors/composio-catalog.ts`, `src/app/api/performance/route.ts`, `src/lib/stats.ts` (`statsFor`, `statsText`), `src/lib/account.ts` (`wipeAccount`), `src/lib/site-stats.ts` (`readGa`, `readGsc`, `syncSiteIfDue`, `readSiteSoon`, `siteDataBlock`), `src/components/app/SiteStats.tsx`, `src/app/api/connect/[provider]/route.ts` (`select_property`, `sync_site`, `sync_social`, `sync_x`), `src/lib/connectors/via-composio.ts` (`selectPropertyVia`), `src/lib/social-stats.ts` (`READERS`, `holdsSocial`, `syncSocial`, `syncSocialIfDue`, `upsertHistory`, `socialDataBlock`, `xReadOf`, `nothingRead`, `SOCIAL_DEADLINE_MS`), `src/lib/social-read/common.ts` (`beforeDeadline`, `splitDays`, `rangeBefore`, `EMPTY_TOTALS`, `SOCIAL_PUB_KEY`, `count`, `settle`, `ISO_DAY`, `handle`), `src/lib/social-read/facebook.ts` (`readFacebook`, `holdsFb`, `fbUrl`, `PAGE_ID`), `src/lib/social-read/youtube.ts` (`readYoutube`, `holdsYt`, `ytUrl`), `src/lib/social-read/tiktok.ts` (`readTiktok`, `holdsTt`, `ttUrl`), `src/components/app/SocialStats.tsx` (`changeText`, `postLink`), `src/components/app/RefreshButton.tsx` (`refreshNote`, `selectNote`), `src/components/app/ReportsPanel.tsx`, `src/app/[lang]/app/reports/page.tsx`, `src/components/app/IntegrationsPanel.tsx` (the Page choice's note), `src/lib/report-sources.ts` (`connectedSources`, `socialRead`, `socialRefreshPath`, `xDirect`, `siteChoices`, `SOCIAL_PATHS`), `src/lib/connectors/working.ts` (`READS_SOCIAL`), `src/lib/publish-once.ts` (`OWN_POSTS`), `src/lib/routines.ts` (`markOwnerSync`, `OWNER_SYNC_KEY`, Dana's gate `activity`), `src/lib/connectors/x.ts` (`readX`, `XUnavailable`, `X_READ_CREDITS`, `X_MAX_POSTS`, `X_READ_USD`), `src/lib/x-stats.ts` (`refreshX`, `getXStats`, `dropXStats`, `xForPage`, `XSTATS_KEY`), `src/lib/not-read.ts` (`notRead`, `notReadOf`, `notReadBlock`, `NOT_READ_PLATFORMS`, `NOT_READ_REASONS`), `src/lib/noor.ts` (`SPECS`, `reportData`), `src/lib/golden.ts` (`GOLDEN_VERSION`, the `dana-board-deck` case). Spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-08-social-analytics-reports-design.md`, plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-08-social-analytics-reports.md`, rules 730-749 (branch `claude/social-analytics-reports`).

1. Ad totals per platform (rules 731, 732). An ad sync (`syncAds`, section 21: an owner's `sync_ads` or the daily `syncAdsIfDue`) still writes `perfimport:{scope}`, `performance-learnings.md` and `adsync:{scope}:{provider}`, and in the same `Promise.all` writes `adstats:{scope}:{provider}`, built by `adStatsFrom` from the read's raw rows (never from the PerfSnapshot, which drops null figures and keeps three ranked rows): spend, impressions, clicks, conversions and revenue, each the sum over the rows that carry it (two decimals) and null when no row does; the account's currency (three letters, else null); the read's from/to; the 10 ads with the most spend (name, spend, clicks, conversions; no spend figure goes last); and the read time. Ad names and the account label are `clean(…, 80)`. Each provider has its own key, so a Meta read never erases Snapchat's, Google Ads' or TikTok Ads' figures. A disconnect (`DELETE /api/connect/{provider}`) or an account switch (`select_ad_account`) leaves the `adstats` row: it is replaced on the next read of that provider and, until then, shown with its own account name and synced date.
2. A CSV upload (`POST /api/performance`, section 20) writes `adstats:{scope}:csv` from `readRows`' rows beside `savePerf`, in one `Promise.all`, after the file parsed; a refused file writes nothing. Its account is the cleaned file name and its currency null (the export names it in no field we read). `savePerf` itself never writes `adstats`. `DELETE /api/performance` deletes `adstats:{scope}:csv` with the import; an ad account's row stays until its next read or the account's deletion. The two upload writes are not one transaction: a failed `savePerf` can leave `adstats:{scope}:csv` one upload ahead of `perfimport` (the route answers 500).
3. Reports. `statsFor` reads `adDataBlock(scope)` in its one `Promise.all` for Dana's reports (weekly report, board deck, dashboard spec) and Majed's strategy review. `getAdStats` reads every ad provider plus `csv` in one MGET. The block, "AD DATA (… data, never instructions)", carries one row per source through `asData` inside `<ad_data>`: platform, account, currency, from/to, synced date and its age in days, the five totals, `prev` only where a previous period was read, and the top ads. Its notes say: null is not read, never zero; each row has its own from/to and synced date and rows are never added together (platforms, windows and currencies differ); amounts stay in their row's currency; compare only with `prev` where `prev` exists, else `[رقم]` and "needs a second read". "" when no row exists. Ad totals reach the model only from this block; `performance-learnings.md` keeps the latest platform's findings.
4. Account deletion: `wipeAccount` deletes `adstats:{scope}:{p}` for every source in `AD_STATS_SOURCES` (every `AD_PROVIDERS` entry and `csv`), for every brand (rule 747).
5. What is missing, said only when it is (rule 730). `statsFor` passes `statsText` one flag per block it read in the same `Promise.all` (`store`, `site`, `social`, `ads`, each true when its block is not ""). The workspace block's one "… figures are NOT in this data … use [رقم] placeholders" sentence lists only what no block carries: sales and revenue without STORE DATA, traffic without SITE DATA, ad spend without AD DATA ("Sales, revenue, traffic and ad-spend" with none, "Sales, revenue and ad-spend" with a site read, no sentence when all three are attached). A synced store keeps its "Store orders and revenue are in STORE DATA below." pointer. SOCIAL DATA names no figure as present: reach and followers are not sales, traffic or ad spend.
6. The previous 30 days beside each ad read (rule 733). `syncAds` runs the current read, `prevOf` and `adCodeIndex` in one `Promise.all`. `prevOf` calls the same reader for `prevNow(now)` (now minus 30 days), so `adWindow(prevNow(now))` is the 30 whole UTC days ending the day before the current window starts, with the same constant query and date checks: Meta's insights `time_range` (direct or `METAADS_GET_INSIGHTS`), Snapchat's per-ad stats query direct, Google Ads' `googleQuery` with the earlier dates, TikTok Ads' integrated report, LinkedIn Ads' analytics call with the earlier `dateRange` (step 16). The direct readers run it with `totalsOnly`, which skips what only Noor's analysis uses: Meta's creative-copy read and Snapchat's ad-name listing; through Composio `adRowsVia` passes it on, and LinkedIn Ads skips its campaign-name call. Only the rows that delivered (impressions or spend above zero) count: Snapchat's direct per-ad breakdown lists every ad of the account, with every figure 0 for the ones that did not run (`omit_empty` defaults to false). Those rows give `prev` = their range and `totalsOf`; none (a new account, an empty window, a window of ads that did not run) gives no `prev`, so no "up 100%" or "up from 0". Snapchat through Composio has no per-ad breakdown for the account, so `snapPrevSpendVia` reads `SNAPCHAT_GET_AD_ACCOUNTS_STATS` once (`ad_account_id` from the connection, checked as a UUID; `fields: "spend"`, `granularity: "TOTAL"`, the earlier window's ISO `start_time`/`end_time`) and keeps `prev.spend` only (micro-currency / 1,000,000; the other previous figures null; no, zero or junk spend gives no `prev`); a second per-ad pass (up to 50 calls) is not run. That account-wide spend compares with the window only when the window's per-ad read covered every ad, so `syncAds` keeps it only when `snapVia` reports `allAds`: the whole ad list read (no page left when the listing stopped, whether a next link carried no cursor or the page cap was reached), no listed ad dropped (a deleted ad, or an entry without a Snap id, whose spend the account's figure still holds), no more than `SNAP_ADS_READ`, 50, ads listed and no per-ad read skipped; with more ads, a dropped entry, a list not read to its end or a per-ad read that failed, no `prev` is stored. `prev` goes only to `adstats:{scope}:{provider}` through `adStatsFrom`; `perfimport`, `performance-learnings.md` and Noor's analysis keep the window's rows alone. A failed previous read is caught and leaves no `prev`: the sync, `perfimport` and `adsync:` still land, and `console.warn("ad_prev_failed", provider, failCode(e))` logs the error's first code words only ("meta_ads insights 500", "snapchat 502 get_ad_accounts_stats"), never the platform's text, a token or a URL. A failed current read throws as before (a 401 or 403 still asks for a reconnect) and nothing is written. `<ad_data>` (step 3) prints `prev` only where it exists, and its notes forbid a change without it.
7. The previous 28 days beside each site read, and null for what was not read (rules 733, 737; section 21a). `syncSite` reads each part's previous period in the same run, over the 28 days that end the day before its window starts (`rangeBefore`). GA4: both reports (channels, landing pages) send `dateRanges: [{ startDate, endDate, name: "cur" }, { startDate, endDate, name: "prev" }]`, and GA4 adds a `dateRange` column that labels each row with its range. Code finds that column by name in the report's `dimensionHeaders` (the second column, after the one requested dimension, when there are no headers) and counts `cur` or `date_range_0` (GA4's default name for the first range) as the window and `prev` or `date_range_1` as the previous range; a row with any other label counts in neither, and a report that has rows none of which carries a known label logs `ga_no_range_column` once for the read (the code alone, nothing else) and leaves null figures, never 0. The channel report asks for up to 40 rows across both ranges, in no stated order. The landing-page report orders by the `dateRange` dimension ascending (`cur` sorts before `prev`), then by sessions descending, limit 20, so the window's rows come first and a busy previous range cannot crowd them out; the window's top 10 are kept. Channels and landing pages come from the window's rows only; `ga.prev` holds the previous sessions, key events, purchase revenue and engagement rate (weighted by sessions) with its range. Search Console: one more `GOOGLE_SEARCH_CONSOLE_SEARCH_ANALYTICS_QUERY` by date for the previous range runs beside the two window queries; its failure is caught (Composio has logged the slug and status) and leaves no `gsc.prev`, never failing the part. `prev` is kept only when the previous range returned at least one figure above 0 (`anyAboveZero`, the social readers' own test from `social-read/common.ts`, which also gives `day` and `rangeBefore`; amended 2026-10-09), so a new property or site, or one with no traffic then, has none and no "up 100%" or "up from 0" can be written. Every figure is a number or null: `num` parses a number sent as a string, and a missing, empty or junk value is null, never 0 or NaN; a total is the sum over the rows that carry it and null when none does (a part Google returned no rows for); the engagement rate and the average position are weighted over the rows that carry both figures, and CTR is null without impressions. `<site_data>` prints each part's `prev` (from, to and the same figures) only where it exists, and its notes say: null is not read, never zero; compare only with prev, without prev write [رقم] and say it needs a second read; where a prev figure is 0 or null, give no percentage change for it; a lower average position is better. A snapshot stored before this change has no `prev` and reads the same way. The Reports card shows a dash for null and, under a tile, the change against the previous 28 days only where both figures were read and the previous one is above 0 (sessions, key events, revenue, search clicks and impressions; not the position). `syncSiteIfDue(scope, now, force)`: `force` reads at once whatever the last read's age, still never a record waiting for a reconnect or a direct one; `readSiteSoon(scope)` runs it after the response with `after()` (or at once outside a request) and only logs a failure, since the daily run reads again. Phase B review (2026-10-08): GA4's landing-page report has its own catch, so a failed or refused page report leaves `ga.pages` empty, logs `ga_pages_failed` once a read (the code alone, no payload) and keeps the totals, the channels and `ga.prev` (rule 733).
8. Choosing the GA4 property and the Search Console site (rule 734). Connect keeps the first property and the first verified site, and stores the full lists in `meta.gaProperties` (`{ id, name }[]`, each name `clean(…, 80)`, the id when nothing is left) and `meta.gscSites` (`string[]`) only when there are two or more (section 21a step 1). `POST /api/connect/google` with `{ action: "select_property", kind: "ga4" | "gsc", id }` is role `connectors` (owner and admin; a member gets 403) and runs only on a Composio Google record: a direct Google record is 400 `bad_action`; on another provider's path the action is 400 `bad_action` when that provider has a connection, and 404 `not_connected` comes first when it has none (the route looks the connection up before the action). A `kind` other than `ga4` or `gsc` is 400 `bad_value`. `selectPropertyVia` accepts only an id from the matching stored list (a property id is never a site, nor the reverse; a list that does not parse lists nothing), else 400 `unknown_property` and nothing is written or read. A GA4 choice writes `meta.gaProperty` and `meta.gaName` (the listed name through `clean(…, 80)` again, so a list stored before names were cleaned is cleaned here; the id when nothing is left), a site choice `meta.gscSite`; the lists stay, and `meta.account` is rebuilt as connect builds it (the property's name and the site without its scheme). The record is always saved under `sc(u)`. Then, only when `allow("sitesync:{scope}", 6, 3600)` grants it (the bucket `sync_site` uses, step 9, so the choices and the Refreshes share the workspace's six an hour), `readSiteSoon(sc(u))` reads the site at once after the response (a forced `syncSiteIfDue`, step 7), so the next report and the site card carry the new property or site; a seventh choice in the hour is saved and read by the daily run or a later Refresh. Until a read lands, `sitestats` still holds the previous read, labelled with its own property and site. The response is `{ ok: true, account, read }`, `read` false when the hour's six were spent. The Reports page's selector for this choice comes with the Reports page work (spec section 8).
9. The owner's refresh of the site and social figures, and Dana's gate (rules 735, 736). `POST /api/connect/google {action:"sync_site"}` and `POST /api/connect/{meta|linkedin|youtube|tiktok} {action:"sync_social"}` are role `read` (every login of the workspace: owner, admin and member) and free: no credit is held or taken. The route checks the session, the role, then `getFreshConnection` (no record: 404 `not_connected`; a record whose token was refused: 502 `reconnect_required`). `sync_site` exists only on the google path and `sync_social` only on the four social paths; on any other path the action is 400 `bad_action`. The path's record must hold a part the readers read, else 404 `not_connected`: `holdsSite` (a Composio Google record with `google_analytics` or `google_search_console`; `syncSite` uses the same test) and `holdsSocial(provider, c)`: true exactly when some `READERS` entry whose provider is this path's holds the record (step 10), so meta answers for the Composio `instagram` part with `meta.igId` or the Composio `facebook` part with a `meta.pageId` of 1 to 30 digits (rule 739, step 13), linkedin for a Composio record with `meta.liOrg`, youtube for a Composio `youtube` record with a UC `meta.channelId` (rule 740, step 14), and tiktok for a Composio record with its `tiktok` part (rule 742, step 15). Then six clicks an hour per workspace, the two actions counted apart and every social path sharing one bucket (`allow("sitesync:{scope}", 6, 3600)`, `allow("socialsync:{scope}", 6, 3600)`, scope from `sc(u)`, so the owner and the members share it and another brand has its own); the seventh is 429 `slow_down` with `Retry-After: 3600` (rule 680) and reads nothing. Amended 2026-10-09: `sync_ads` (section 21, every ad path) has the same six an hour per workspace in its own bucket, every ad platform together (`allow("adrefresh:{scope}", 6, 3600)`, not the `adsync:{scope}:{provider}` time of the last ad read), and `sync_x` its own (`xsync:{scope}`, step 17); the seventh of each is 429 `slow_down` with `Retry-After: 3600` and asks no platform. So a 400, a 404 or a reconnect answer never spends one of the six. An allowed click runs `syncSiteIfDue(sc(u), now, true)` or `syncSocialIfDue(sc(u), now, true)` (a forced read of every held part at once, whatever the last read's age, step 7 and section 21b) beside `markOwnerSync(sc(u))` in one `Promise.allSettled`: the mark `ownersync:{scope}` (the click's ISO time, 8 days) is written even when the platform fails, and a failed mark is only logged. The answer is `{ ok: true, read }` (`read` false only when every held part waits for a reconnect, so nothing was read). A social read that saved the snapshot while a part's token was refused (another part was read) answers 200 `{ ok: true, read: true, reconnect: "<provider>" }` (`PartlyRead`, the first refused part's provider in `PLATFORMS` order), with the record of every refused part marked for reconnect: `syncSocial` puts the providers of all refused parts on the token it throws (`SocialReadError.refusedBy`), and `syncSocialIfDue` runs `refusedToken` for each in one `Promise.all`, so Instagram and YouTube refused together mark the Meta and the YouTube records, and a part that was read marks nothing. Any other failure is 502 with the error's message, answered by the refresh itself: the readers convert a refused token with the provider of the part that was refused (`syncSiteIfDue` with `google`, `syncSocialIfDue` with each refused part's provider: `meta`, `linkedin`, `youtube` or `tiktok`), and only the adapter's 401 (`refused`, ` 401:`) is a refused token: any other social failure, a 403 quota or permission answer included (YouTube's `HttpError 403 quotaExceeded`, a Facebook 403 not about `read_insights`), is thrown as it is, answered 502 with its message, and marks no record, since `refusedToken`'s own test (401 or 403 anywhere in the text) would ask the owner to reconnect for a quota day, and the route's own `refusedToken`, which would use the path's provider, never sees the read's error for `sync_site` or `sync_social`; so a refused Instagram token pressed on the LinkedIn path marks the Meta record, never LinkedIn's. The daily run sees a partial read as before: `social_failed:reconnect_required`. Dana's weekly-report gate (section 16 step 3) counts `ownersync:{scope}` newer than 7 days as activity, so a person who presses Refresh opens it; the daily cron's `syncSiteIfDue` and `syncSocialIfDue` write only `sitestats`/`socialstats` and never the mark, so the cron's reads alone never open it. `wipeAccount` deletes `ownersync:{scope}` for every brand (rule 747); the `rate:sitesync:` and `rate:socialsync:` counters expire within the hour. Phase B review (2026-10-08): the 200 with `reconnect` needs a part actually read. `syncSocial` sets `SocialReadError.saved` only when the snapshot it wrote holds a part, so a refused token beside known states alone (Instagram not professional, no figure read) writes those states and the owner's refresh still answers 502 `reconnect_required` (rule 735). Whole-phase review (2026-10-09): a part actually read is one with a follower count or a total (`nothingRead`, exported from social-stats.ts and the Not-read list's own test, step 18), so a Facebook Page whose posts were read but not its insights is written but is no read, and beside a refused token the refresh still answers 502 `reconnect_required`. `select_org` on a Composio LinkedIn record (rule 671; this amends section 21b step 9, where it read at once every time) reads the new company page as `select_page` reads a new Page (step 13): only while `allow("socialsync:{scope}", 6, 3600)` grants it, answering `{ ok: true, account, read }`, the choice saved either way; Integrations says its results come with the next read when `read` is false (`pageNote`, step 19) (rule 735).
10. The social snapshot v2 and its history (rules 737, 746; amended 664, 667, 668, 670; the expand step of rule 679). `socialstats:{scope}` is written as `{ v: 2, at, parts, errors, history }`: one `PlatformPart` per platform read (`platform`, `account` `clean(…, 80)`, `range`, `followers`, `totals`, an optional `prev`, at most 30 `posts`), a short error code per platform that failed, and the history. Every total is `number | null` (`EMPTY_TOTALS` all null), never 0 for a figure not read. Readers: every figure a reader keeps is a strict count (`count` in `social-read/common.ts`: a finite number of 0 or more, or digits sent as a string; a negative, an exponent, `"N/A"` or `{}` is null), Instagram and LinkedIn included. `READERS` maps each platform to its provider, `holds(c)` and `read(scope, c, now, deadline)`; Instagram (meta: the Composio `instagram` part with `meta.igId`) and LinkedIn (a Composio record with `meta.liOrg`; impressions are `views`, reactions `likes`, and `engagements` is clicks + reactions + comments + reposts only when impressions were read) read today, the Facebook Page reads a Composio `facebook` part with a `meta.pageId` of 1 to 30 digits (rule 739, step 13), the YouTube channel a Composio `youtube` record with a UC `meta.channelId` (rule 740, step 14), and the TikTok account a Composio record with its `tiktok` part (rule 742, step 15). `syncSocial(scope, { [provider]: record })` reads every held part at once with one shared deadline, beside one read of the stored snapshot, in one `Promise.all`, then writes one `set`. A refused token is still thrown after the write (`saved`), and a known state (`ig_not_professional`, `li_no_page_access`, `fb_no_insights`, `yt_no_analytics`, `tt_no_scope`) is written, not thrown; a reader may also answer a part with a known state beside it (`ReadPart.known`, YouTube's `yt_no_analytics`), which `syncSocial` moves into `errors` so the stored part carries no state of its own. History: `upsertHistory` adds one row per part read for the read's UTC month (`{ month, platform, followers, totals }`, the month's last read winning), keeps the rows of a part that failed or was not held, and keeps at most the newest 13 months per platform; a part read from another account than the stored part's (both names read and different: another company page chosen, another Instagram account connected) drops that platform's rows first, so no change is ever worked out across two accounts (a name not read keeps them, and a platform whose stored part is missing because its last read failed cannot be compared); it lives in the same key, so `wipeAccount` deletes it with `socialstats:{scope}` (rule 669). A snapshot from before v2 (no `v`) is read as not read yet: `socialDataBlock` returns "", the card shows "Social results have not been read yet." and none of its figures, `socialRead` is false, its history is not carried over, and `syncSocialIfDue` reads at once whatever its age (due when there is no snapshot, it is not v2, it is 20 hours old, or the read is forced); old code reading a v2 snapshot finds neither `ig` nor `li` and shows nothing. `syncSocialIfDue` reads the four social records and the snapshot in one `Promise.all` and refreshes the providers whose record holds a part and waits for no reconnect. Dana's block prints, per platform part, its account, from/to, followers, totals, `engagement_rate` (engagements over views as a fraction, worked out there and never stored), `prev` where it was read, `posts_read` and the 10 best posts by views then reach, or "not available (unknown, not zero)"; it tells Dana to compare only with `prev` and, where a part has none, to write [رقم] and say it needs a second read, and to give no percentage change for a prev figure of 0 or null. The history is read back (amended 2026-10-09): a part whose followers were read carries `last_month` (`{ month, followers }`), its platform's newest history row of an earlier UTC month than the read (X's from X's own history), only when that row's followers is a number above 0; the block says a change in followers may be given against it, never a change in totals. The card shows one block per part in the order of `PLATFORMS` (Instagram, Facebook, LinkedIn, YouTube, TikTok; the card keeps its own list so the client bundle imports only types, and a test checks it against `PLATFORMS`) and a failed part as not read yet. `publishOnce` adds the post id of a publish to Instagram, Facebook, LinkedIn, YouTube, TikTok or X to `socialpub:{scope}` (`OWN_POSTS`), so `fromThakeen` can mark those posts once their readers exist. Sources: `READS_SOCIAL` is meta, linkedin, youtube, tiktok and x; `connectedSources` counts a Composio Meta record with `igId` or `pageId`, Composio LinkedIn (with `liOrg`), a Composio YouTube record with a UC `channelId` (the same test as `holdsYt`, kept inline so `report-sources.ts` stays client-safe), Composio TikTok records, and X only on its direct record. A direct X record counts as a source because it is read on a member's paid click (step 17) and shown on the Reports card (step 19); the branch ships as one release, so the interim state before its reader landed was never released on its own, and `socialRead` keeps the followers tile shut until a snapshot part was read. Phase B review (2026-10-08, the expand step of rule 679): each stored part may carry `accountId`, taken in `syncSocial` only from the stored connection through `READERS[p].accountId` (Instagram's `igId`, the Facebook Page's `pageId`, LinkedIn's `liOrg`, YouTube's `channelId`; TikTok's connect stores no id, so none; kept only as 1 to 100 letters, digits or `_:.-`) and never displayed. History rows are dropped only when the stored and the new part both carry an `accountId` and they differ, so a renamed account keeps its history; when either has none (a part written before the id, or TikTok) the account names decide as before. `socialDataBlock` picks its fields and never prints the id, and the Reports page passes the snapshot through `socialForPage`, which drops it, before the browser sees it; code from before reads such a part and ignores the id (rule 746). Whole-phase review (2026-10-09): an Instagram username keeps its own characters (`handle` in `common.ts`: letters, digits, `_` and `.`, at most 80), since `clean` drops `_` and `reef_roastery` would read as another account `reefroastery` (rule 737); X's read carries as its `accountId` the user id X answered for the owner's own token, under the same rule, in `xstats:{scope}` (step 17, rule 746).
11. The social previous period and one shared deadline (rules 733, 741; amended 664, 665). `syncSocial` sets one deadline, `Date.now() + SOCIAL_DEADLINE_MS` (30 s on the real clock, whatever `now` it was given), and hands it to every reader; each reader calls `beforeDeadline(deadline)` before every Composio read, so past it nothing more is asked for. A read already asked lands (`CALL_TIMEOUT_MS`, 20 s, bounds it): one read's worst case is 30 + 20 s. Instagram's account read past the deadline fails its part with the code `deadline`; a later read past it leaves null figures, no `prev` or no posts; LinkedIn's two reads and every other part stand (`Promise.allSettled`). Instagram: a second `INSTAGRAM_GET_USER_INSIGHTS` for `[since − 28 days, since]`, the same arguments otherwise, beside the window's read and the post list in one `Promise.all`; its reach and views are the part's `prev` with that range, and a failed second read, one past the deadline or one with neither figure gives no `prev` (a refused token still ends the read). LinkedIn: the one `LINKEDIN_GET_SHARE_STATS` asks 56 whole UTC days ending at today's midnight, so each DAY row falls whole in one period; `splitDays(rows, window)` keeps the rows dated (by `timeRange.start`) in the window, the last 28 whole days ending yesterday, and in `rangeBefore(window)`, the 28 days before it; a row without a date or dated in neither counts in neither, and when none of the rows falls in either the read logs `li_no_day_rows` (the code alone) and its figures stay null. A previous period with no figure read gives no `prev`, so Dana's block and the card write no change for that part. `splitDays`, `rangeBefore`, `beforeDeadline` and the other helpers every reader shares live in `src/lib/social-read/common.ts`, which imports nothing at run time; social-stats.ts re-exports the public ones. A reader in its own file (the Facebook Page's, step 13; the YouTube channel's, step 14; the TikTok account's, step 15) imports them from there, never from social-stats.ts, which imports every reader for `READERS`, so no reader loads in a cycle. Phase B review (2026-10-08): every reader with a previous period (Instagram, the Facebook Page, LinkedIn, YouTube) keeps `prev` only when some figure read for the earlier period is above 0 (`anyAboveZero` in `common.ts`), so an earlier period whose figures were all 0 (28 zero daily rows on LinkedIn, 0 views and 0 reach on Instagram) gives no `prev` (rule 733). A reader called with a deadline already passed rejects with `deadline`, never a known state such as `ig_not_professional`, and asks Composio nothing (rule 741).
12. LinkedIn page views (rule 748; amended 663). Beside the share statistics and the followers, in the same `Promise.all` and under the same deadline, `readLi` asks `LINKEDIN_GET_ORG_PAGE_STATS` once with `organization` the connection's `meta.liOrg` (the URN checked against `^urn:li:organization:\d+$`, never from a request), `timeGranularityType: "DAY"` and the share statistics' 56 whole UTC days (`timeRangeStart`/`timeRangeEnd` in ms, ending at today's midnight). Each DAY row's `totalPageStatistics.views.allPageViews.pageViews` (views of every page of the company page) is parsed with the strict `count` (digits sent as a string parse; a negative, an exponent, `"N/A"`, `{}` or a missing figure is null) and split by `splitDays` like the share statistics: the window's days sum to `totals.pageViews`, the 28 before to `prev.totals.pageViews`, each null when no day of its period carried a figure, never 0 (page views read only for the earlier days are a `prev` on their own). The read needs the page's ADMINISTRATOR role and `rw_organization_admin`: a 403, any other failure or the deadline leaves `pageViews` null and keeps the rest of the part with no error code; a refused token still ends the read (thrown with `linkedin`, step 9). Page-view rows none of which falls in either period log `li_no_day_rows` once for the read. Dana's block carries `pageViews` in each LinkedIn period's totals and says they are visits to the page itself, unlike `views` (impressions). The catalog lists the slug as a read with exactly those four arguments (rule 663). Phase B review (2026-10-08): when the deadline stops the page-statistics read before it is asked, `pageViews` is null, the other totals and the followers stand and no `linkedin` error is written (rule 748).

13. The Facebook Page (rule 739; amended 663, 664, 673, 735). `READERS.facebook` (`src/lib/social-read/facebook.ts`) holds a Composio Meta record with the `facebook` part and a `meta.pageId` of 1 to 30 digits (`PAGE_ID`, `holdsFb`; the same check in `readFacebook`, the Not-read list and the connect's Page list, so a longer id is never asked about, offered or counted): the Page chosen for publishing (section 11a, `select_page`), never an id from a request. `readFacebook` asks, in one `Promise.all` with `smembers(socialpub:{scope})` and each call after `beforeDeadline`: `FACEBOOK_GET_PAGE_INSIGHTS` with the constant `metrics` (`page_media_view`, `page_total_media_view_unique`, `page_post_engagements`, `page_total_actions`, `page_follows`, `page_daily_follows_unique`, `page_daily_unfollows_unique`), `period: "day"`, `since` 56 days before today's UTC midnight and `until` today (YYYY-MM-DD), one call for both periods (Meta allows 90 days); and `FACEBOOK_GET_PAGE_POSTS` with the constant `fields` (`id,message,created_time,permalink_url,status_type,from,shares,reactions.summary(true),comments.summary(true)`, never `access_token`) and `limit: 30`. Each daily value is dated by Meta's `end_time` (the end of the Page's day) less 12 hours, then split by day (`splitDays`) into the window, the last 28 whole UTC days ending yesterday, and the 28 days before it; views, reach, engagements, clicks, followsGained and followsLost are the sums over the days read (null when no day carried one), followers the latest `page_follows` read, and `prev` is kept only when a figure was read there. Rows dated in neither period are not summed and log `fb_no_day_rows` once. Every value goes through a strict count: a finite number of zero or more, or one written in digits; `{}`, `""`, `"N/A"`, NaN, Infinity, a negative or a number too large is null, never NaN, a string or 0. The feed also returns visitors' and tagged posts and filters by updated time, so only posts with a Page post id (`\d+_\d+`), `from.id` equal to the Page and `created_time` inside the window are kept, at most 30: caption `clean(message, 80)`, type from `status_type` (photos IMAGE, video VIDEO, shared story LINK, else POST), likes, comments and shares from the post's own fields (a field Graph leaves out is null), the link only on https `facebook.com`/`www.facebook.com` with no port and no query string (`fbUrl`; a cut `photo.php?fbid=` link would open nothing), `fromThakeen` from the published ids. Each kept post's `FACEBOOK_GET_POST_INSIGHTS` (`post_media_view,post_total_media_view_unique`, `period: "lifetime"`, the only two Meta still serves) runs four at a time through `pmap`; a failed, rate-limited or late one leaves that post's views and reach null. Failures: a refused token on any of the calls ends the read with provider `meta` (thrown after the other parts are written, rule 664), and no further post read is asked; insights refused for want of the `read_insights` permission set the known code `fb_no_insights` (in `KNOWN`: written in `errors.facebook`, never thrown, so a Facebook-only workspace gets no daily failure line and the card shows the Page as not read yet; the fallback is the Meta app after its App Review, spec section 9); any other failure of one side leaves its figures null or its posts empty and the part stands; both failing is a failed read (`errors.facebook`, the last good part's history kept). The account is the stored `meta.pageName` through `clean(…, 80)`, or `Page {id}` through `clean(…, 80)` too. A Facebook-only Meta record with a Page is now read daily by `syncSocialIfDue` and answers `sync_social` on the Meta path (rules 673, 735); one with no Page still holds nothing. Dana's SOCIAL DATA block says the Page's reach adds up each day's unique viewers (one person seen on three days counts three times), engagements are engagements with its posts and clicks are actions on the Page. The connect callback starts a first read after the redirect for every Composio social connect (`isSocialProvider`: any Meta toolkit, LinkedIn, YouTube, TikTok; `readSocialSoon`, which reads only the parts held), so a Page connected alone shows its first figures at once. `select_page` on a Composio Meta record saves the new Page and reads it at once only while the workspace's six `socialsync:{scope}` reads an hour last (the `sync_social` bucket, step 9), answering `{ ok: true, account, read }` (`read` false and nothing read once they are spent, the choice saved either way); a direct Meta record has no social reader and answers `{ ok: true, account }` as before. Phase B review (2026-10-08): followers come only from `page_follows` rows dated with an ISO day, so an undated row is never the Page's count; Graph's permission codes `(#10)` and `(#200)` on the insights read are `fb_no_insights` too, even when the text does not name `read_insights`; an earlier period whose figures were all 0 gives no `prev`; the part's `accountId` is the stored `pageId` (rule 739).

14. The YouTube channel (rule 740; amended 663, 664, 673, 735). `READERS.youtube` (`src/lib/social-read/youtube.ts`) holds a Composio YouTube record with the `youtube` part and a `meta.channelId` of `UC` and 10 to 40 more letters, digits, `-` or `_` (`CHANNEL_ID`; `holdsYt`, the same check as connect, section 11b): the channel the login owns, listed at connect. No channel id is ever sent: `readYoutube` asks for the login's own channel (`channel==MINE`, `mine: true`) and uses the stored id only to pick the subscriber row. In one `Promise.all` with `smembers(socialpub:{scope})`, each call after `beforeDeadline`: `YOUTUBE_LIST_CHANNELS` `{ part: "statistics", mine: true, maxResults: 1 }` (followers: the stored channel's `subscriberCount`, null when the owner hides it or no row is that channel); `YOUTUBE_QUERY_ANALYTICS` `{ ids: "channel==MINE", startDate, endDate, metrics: "views,estimatedMinutesWatched,likes,comments,shares,subscribersGained,subscribersLost", dimensions: "day" }` from 56 days before today's UTC midnight to yesterday (YouTube's end date is inclusive; both dates built in code and checked as YYYY-MM-DD), one call for both periods; and `YOUTUBE_LIST_CHANNEL_VIDEOS` `{ mine: true, part: "snippet", maxResults: 30 }`. The analytics rows are read by their column names (in the asked order when no headers come, by name when a row is an object), split by day (`splitDays`) into the window and the 28 days before it, and each metric summed through the strict `count` over the days read: views, watchMinutes (`estimatedMinutesWatched`), likes, comments, shares, followsGained and followsLost (`subscribersGained`, `subscribersLost`), null when no day carried one; reach, engagements, clicks and pageViews stay null, `prev` only when a figure was read there, and rows dated in neither period are not summed and log `yt_no_day_rows` once. The upload list gives at most 30 unique video ids (`^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{11}$`), then one `YOUTUBE_GET_VIDEO_DETAILS_BATCH` `{ id: ids, parts: ["snippet", "statistics", "status"] }` (not asked past the deadline). Only videos the answer gives back for the ids asked, published inside the window and not private are posts (Thakeen's own uploads are private until the owner publishes them): type VIDEO, caption `clean(title, 80)`, views, likes and comments from the public counts through `count`, reach, shares and saved null, the link only `https://www.youtube.com/watch?v={id}` (`ytUrl`), `fromThakeen` from the published ids (the upload's video id, section 11b). Failures: a refused token on any call ends the read with provider `youtube`; analytics refused for want of their scope (Google's 403, forbidden, insufficient scopes, permission, or the Analytics API not enabled for the app; the adapter keeps the text and never makes 403 its status) keep the subscribers and the videos and add the known `yt_no_analytics` beside the part (`ReadPart.known`, moved into `errors.youtube` by `syncSocial`; in `KNOWN`, never thrown); any other failure of one side leaves its figures null or its videos empty and the part stands; all three failing is a failed read. The account is the stored `meta.account` (the channel title from connect) through `clean(…, 80)`, or `Channel {id}`. A YouTube record is read daily by `syncSocialIfDue` and answers `sync_social` on the YouTube path with `{ ok: true, read: true }` (rules 673, 735). Dana's SOCIAL DATA block says YouTube's followers are subscribers, followsGained and followsLost are subscribers gained and lost, watchMinutes are minutes watched and YouTube gives no reach. `count`, `settle` and `ISO_DAY` moved to `common.ts`, shared with the Facebook reader. Like the Page, connecting a YouTube channel starts a first read after the redirect, so it shows its first figures at once. Phase B review (2026-10-08): `CHANNEL_ID` (`youtube.ts`) is the one check wherever the id is checked (the connect in `via-composio.ts` uses it; `report-sources.ts` keeps the same pattern inline), and the `Channel {id}` fallback goes through `clean(…, 80)`. When `YOUTUBE_LIST_CHANNELS` answers rows and none is the stored channel, the login now answers for another channel, so its analytics and uploads are not the stored channel's: the read fails with the code `yt_other_channel` (not a known state), so alone it is thrown, nothing is written and the last snapshot stands; beside another part the YouTube part is dropped and its error code is written (its history kept); and the daily run's line (`social_failed:yt_other_channel`) names it; no record is marked. `yt_no_analytics` always stands beside the YouTube part. An earlier period whose figures were all 0 gives no `prev`; the part's `accountId` is the stored `channelId` (rule 740).

15. The TikTok account (rule 742; amended 663, 664, 673, 735). `READERS.tiktok` (`src/lib/social-read/tiktok.ts`) holds a Composio TikTok record with its `tiktok` part (`holdsTt`): the account connected with Thakeen's own TikTok app (section 11a), which also publishes the inbox drafts. No user or video id is ever sent: both reads act on the login's own account, and the catalog lists them as the toolkit's only reads (`TIKTOK_GET_USER_STATS` with `fields`, `TIKTOK_LIST_VIDEOS` with `max_count` and `cursor`). One `Promise.all`, each call after `beforeDeadline` (step 11): `TIKTOK_GET_USER_STATS` `{ fields: ["follower_count", "likes_count", "video_count"] }`, of which only `follower_count` is kept, as followers through the strict count; `TIKTOK_LIST_VIDEOS` `{ max_count: 20 }`; and `socialpub:{scope}`. A second page, `{ max_count: 20, cursor }`, is asked at most once: only when the first answered `has_more: true` with a cursor that is a whole number above zero, and its dated videos are none older than the window (TikTok lists the newest first). Posts are the videos with a digit id, unique across the pages, created (`create_time`, seconds, UTC) inside the window (the last 28 whole days, ending yesterday), at most 30: type VIDEO, caption `clean(title, or else video_description, 80)`, views, likes, comments and shares through the strict count, reach and saved null, and a link only on `https://tiktok.com` or `https://www.tiktok.com` without its query or hash (`ttUrl`). TikTok counts only over the account's lifetime, so the part's totals stay null and it has no `prev`; a change in followers comes only from the monthly history (step 10). A refused token on any call ends the read with provider `tiktok`. A refusal for want of a scope (`scope_not_authorized`, `scope_permission_missed`) on the app or the login is checked first, since the adapter may read its text as 401: it is the known `tt_no_scope`, beside the part when the other call answered, alone when neither did, written and never thrown, so the record, which also publishes, is not marked for reconnect. Any other failure of one side leaves followers null or the videos empty and the part stands; the second page failing or meeting the deadline keeps the first; both calls failing is a failed read. The account is the stored label `clean(…, 80)`, or `TikTok`. A TikTok record is read daily by `syncSocialIfDue` and answers `sync_social` on the TikTok path with `{ ok: true, read: true }` (rules 673, 735). Dana's SOCIAL DATA block says only TikTok's followers and posts are read, its totals are null and it has no prev. TikTok's publish id is not a video id, so `fromThakeen` stays false for TikTok until a published video's id is stored. Like the YouTube channel, connecting a TikTok account starts a first read after the redirect. Phase B review (2026-10-08): `tt_no_scope` alone needs every failed call to be a scope refusal; when both calls fail and one failed otherwise, that other error is thrown, a failed read: alone, nothing is written and the last snapshot stands; beside another part, the TikTok part is dropped and its error code is written (its history kept). When the Composio client throws a text naming a scope refusal (`scope_not_authorized`, `scope_permission_missed`, insufficient or missing scope: `SCOPE_REFUSED` in `composio.ts`, the one pattern the TikTok reader also reads), the adapter's catch keeps `scope_not_authorized` in the shaped message instead of `call_failed`, so such a throw is read as `tt_no_scope` and marks no record. A TikTok part has no `accountId`, so its history follows the account label (rule 742).

16. LinkedIn Ads (rule 743; amended 663, 733, 749). A new ad provider, `linkedin_ads` (`AD_PROVIDERS`, so `READS_ADS`, the `ads` source of `report-sources.ts`, `PerfSnapshot.source`, `AD_STATS_SOURCES`, the daily `syncAdsIfDue` and the wipe), read only and Composio only (`COMPOSIO_ONLY`: available when `COMPOSIO_PROVIDERS` names it and `COMPOSIO_AUTH_LINKEDIN_ADS` holds the auth config of Thakeen's own LinkedIn app with the Advertising API product, separate from the page toolkit's app). The `linkedin_ads` toolkit, pinned to `20260826_00`, lists four reads and no write: `LINKEDIN_ADS_SEARCH_AD_ACCOUNTS`, `LINKEDIN_ADS_GET_AD_ACCOUNT` (allowlisted, not called), `LINKEDIN_ADS_GET_AD_ANALYTICS` and `LINKEDIN_ADS_SEARCH_CAMPAIGNS`, each with the spec's argument names only. Connect (section 11a, `connectViaComposio`) asks `LINKEDIN_ADS_SEARCH_AD_ACCOUNTS` once, `{ page_size: 100 }`, and keeps the accounts whose id is digits (a plain id or its `urn:li:sponsoredAccount:` URN, at most 15 digits) and whose status is `ACTIVE` or absent, names `clean(…, 80)`; none refuses the connect (`no_ad_account`); the first is read until an owner or admin picks another listed one with `select_ad_account` (section 21, step 5; any other id is 400 `unknown_account`). A read (`linkedinAdsVia`, through `adRowsVia`) checks `meta.adAccount` against `^\d{1,15}$` before building `urn:li:sponsoredAccount:<id>` (else `no_ad_account`, with no call), then asks in one `Promise.all` `LINKEDIN_ADS_GET_AD_ANALYTICS` with the constant `{ q: "analytics", pivot: "CAMPAIGN", timeGranularity: "ALL", accounts: [<urn>], dateRange: { start: { year, month, day }, end: { … } }, fields: LI_ADS_FIELDS }` (`impressions,clicks,costInLocalCurrency,landingPageClicks,externalWebsiteConversions,pivotValues,dateRange`; the window's first and last day, LinkedIn's end date being inclusive) and `LINKEDIN_ADS_SEARCH_CAMPAIGNS` `{ adAccountId: <the id as an integer>, pageSize: 100 }` for the names. One row per campaign, its id read from `pivotValues` (`urn:li:sponsoredCampaign:<digits>`), named by the names call (`clean`) or `Campaign <id>` when that call failed or did not list it; spend is `costInLocalCurrency`, clicks `clicks`, conversions `externalWebsiteConversions`, each through `num` (missing or junk is null), and revenue null (LinkedIn reports no conversion value); a campaign with no impressions and no spend is left out. The previous 30 days (step 6) is the same analytics call over the earlier `dateRange`, `totalsOnly`, so no second names call; results land as every ad read's do (steps 1 and 6, section 21). Linkedin-Version (spec section 9, item 6): LinkedIn's Marketing API version 202510 sunsets on 15 Oct 2026. The pin stays `20260826_00`; the founder checks with Composio which `Linkedin-Version` the pinned `linkedin_ads` toolkit sends (and the `linkedin` one) before `linkedin_ads` is named in `COMPOSIO_PROVIDERS`.

17. X refresh, paid by the owner (rules 744, 745, 747; spec C3). X bills every read, so nothing reads it on a schedule: the daily social sync never reads or writes its key. `POST /api/connect/x {action:"sync_x"}` is role `generate` (any member, `ACTION_ROLE.sync_x`). After `getFreshConnection` (none: 404 `not_connected`) and six clicks an hour per workspace (`allow("xsync:{scope}", 6, 3600)`, rule 735; the seventh 429 `slow_down` with `Retry-After: 3600` before any charge or call, so failing reads that each refund cannot ask X without end), `refreshX(u, c)` (`src/lib/x-stats.ts`) runs in this order: (1) a Composio-routed X record answers 409 `x_direct_only` before any charge or call; (2) `xstats:{scope}` dated today (UTC) is returned as it is, `charged: false`, no call to X; (3) an ended trial answers 402 `trial_over`; (4) a due month is renewed first (`renewalDue`, `renewIfDue`, rule 332); (5) `chargeOnce(u.email, X_READ_CREDITS, { scope, agent: "dana", cap: getGov(scope).agentCaps.dana }, "xread:{scope}:{UTC day}")` takes `X_READ_CREDITS` (2 since 2026-10-24, rule 4034; 1 before) of the account record (also for a member) under the ledger's lock: `agent_cap` answers 402 with spent and cap, `no_credits` 402 with need and credits, a claim already held (a second click while the first reads) reads with no new debit, and a fresh charge sends Dana's 80% warning (`warnAgentCap`); (6) `readX(c, now)` and `socialpub:{scope}` in one `Promise.all`; (7) one `set` of `xstats:{scope}` = the part with `at` and its monthly history (`upsertHistory`, rule 746: the X user ids decide when both reads carry one, so a renamed handle keeps its rows and another id starts them again; a read stored before the id, the expand step of rule 679, follows the handles, both read and different starting them again). `readX` (`src/lib/connectors/x.ts`) makes two GETs, each with the owner's bearer token and `AbortSignal.timeout(15_000)`: `https://api.x.com/2/users/me?user.fields=public_metrics`, then, only for an id of 1 to 19 digits, `https://api.x.com/2/users/{id}/tweets` with `max_results=10`, `start_time` (now less 30 days, RFC 3339 without milliseconds), `exclude=retweets` and `tweet.fields=created_at,public_metrics,non_public_metrics,organic_metrics`; one page, never `pagination_token` or `expansions`. A post is kept with a digit id, a date inside the window, once, at most 10: type `POST`, `clean(text, 80)`, `https://x.com/i/status/{id}`, views from `impression_count` (public, then non-public, then organic), likes, replies as comments, `repost_count ?? retweet_count` as shares, bookmarks as saved, reach null, a missing number null. Followers are `followers_count`; the account is `@username` with X's own characters (`handle`: letters, digits, `_` and `.`, at most 80; `clean` would drop `_`), else the connection's stored `@handle` through the same rule or its stored label through `clean`, else `X`; `accountId` is the id X answered on `users/me`, never displayed (the `sync_x` answer and the Reports page drop it through `xForPage`, and Dana's block never prints it); totals stay null and there is no prev (post figures are lifetime totals, like TikTok's); X's post count has no field in the part and is not kept. No posts in 30 days (X answers no `data`) is a success that keeps the charge. Errors: HTTP 401 throws `x 401: unauthorized` (X's text is never kept), so the route's catch runs `refusedToken` and asks the owner to reconnect; 402, 403 and 429 throw `XUnavailable` (`x_unavailable`, no 401 or 403 digits), which never marks the record and answers 502, or 429 with X's `retry-after`, else the wait to `x-rate-limit-reset`, else 900 s (rule 680); another status throws `x_status_{n}`, an answer that is not JSON or not X's shape `x_malformed`. Only the click that took the charge refunds: every failure after it, the save included, runs `refundOnce` once, unless `xstats:{scope}` is dated today by then (another click's read landed, and its charge stands). A click that read on the hold never refunds; once its read is saved it calls `chargeOnce` again on the same claim, a no-op while the hold stands and one debit when the charging click failed first and gave the credit back, so one credit stands for the day's read in either order (a refused re-charge, no credits or Dana's cap, leaves that read free, and so does a read landing in the moment between the charging click's check and its refund). A click stopped after its charge with no catch run leaves its hold and the credit stands, and so does a refund that fails (`refundOnce` throws when the user lock times out): it is logged `x_refund_failed` and the click still answers X's own failure, so X's 401 still reaches `refusedToken` and X's 429 keeps its wait. The hold lives on `User.paidOnce` for 8 days and no sweep covers `xread:` (`sweepWeeklyHolds` matches `weekly:` only). Dana's SOCIAL DATA (`socialDataBlock`) reads `xstats:{scope}` beside the snapshot in one `Promise.all` and shows `x` with its own `read` and `read_days_ago` (not `synced`), also when no other platform was read; `synced` appears only with a v2 snapshot part. Cost (costs.md 3.2): $0.010 the user plus $0.005 a post, $0.060 a refresh at most, under one credit's ceiling with and without VAT; X deduplicates within a UTC day. `wipeAccount` deletes `xstats:{scope}` of every brand (rule 747), and `DELETE /api/connect/x` deletes it beside the connection (`dropXStats`, one `Promise.all`), so another account connected later never shows this one's read or history; connected the same UTC day, it is read at once on the day's hold, with no second credit. The Reports card's X row and its "Refresh X (1 credit)" button are step 19.

18. Not read, said in code (rule 738; spec A6). Dana's reports and Majed's strategy review never leave the list of what was not read to the model. `notRead(scope)` (`src/lib/not-read.ts`) reads in one `Promise.all` `listConnections(scope)`, the social snapshot, `xstats:{scope}` (`getXStats`), the ad rows (`getAdStats`) and the site snapshot, and walks the 15 platforms the founder named (`NOT_READ_PLATFORMS`) in a fixed order. A platform is read when its part is in a v2 social snapshot (a v1 snapshot holds none, rule 737), its X read is one Dana's block prints (`xReadOf`, shared with `socialDataBlock`; an earlier day's read counts, the block gives its date), it has an ad row in `adstats:{scope}:{provider}` (the CSV row names no platform), or the site snapshot holds its `ga` or `gsc` part; a YouTube part beside `yt_no_analytics` (public counts only) and a Facebook part with no figure read are not reads. Every other platform gets one reason, first match wins: `closed_to_apps` (Snapchat organic) and `waiting_approval` (Google Business Profile) always; `reconnect` when its record carries it and needs reconnecting; `choose_page` for a LinkedIn record with no company page (`liNeedsPage`); for X with a record, `x_direct_only` through Composio, else `refresh_x`; `publish_only` (amended 2026-10-09) when a record that publishes carries the social platform but no reader reads it: a direct Meta record's Instagram account (`igId`) or Page (`pageId`), production's Meta while Composio is off, or a Composio social record whose stored id no reader accepts, so such a workspace is never told the platform is not connected; `not_connected` when no record one of Thakeen's readers reads holds it (each reader's own meta test, so a direct LinkedIn, YouTube, TikTok or Google record, which neither publishes nor is read, a direct Meta record with no Page for Facebook, and a stale stored error name nothing); `analytics_scope` for `fb_no_insights`, `yt_no_analytics` and a `tt_no_scope` with no TikTok part; `read_failed` for anything else a held platform did not read (another error code such as `ig_not_professional`, `li_no_page_access` or `yt_other_channel`, a Facebook part with every figure null, or a first read not landed yet). A LinkedIn page-stats read that was refused adds no reason: page views stay null in SOCIAL DATA. `statsFor` reads `notRead` beside the other blocks in its one `Promise.all` and appends `notReadBlock` last: a NOT READ header, the items as data in `<not_read>` (display names and reason codes, `asData`), the instruction to list exactly these in the report's language and never add or drop one, and the meaning of only the reasons listed; nothing when every platform was read. No platform text, request field or id reaches the block: every name and reason is a constant.

19. The Reports page (spec section 8; amended 670, 734, 735, 738, 744). `reports/page.tsx` reads, in its one `Promise.all` and all under `sc(u)`, `getXStats` and the ad rows (`getAdStats`) beside the connections and the site and social snapshots, and builds the not-read items with `notReadOf(connections, { social, x, ads, site })`, the pure half of `notRead`, so each key is read once and the card and the list come from the same reads (amended 2026-10-09; no read in a loop). It hands the panel X's read through `xReadOf` and `xForPage`, which drops its `accountId`, the social snapshot through `socialForPage`, which drops every part's, the not-read items, and from the connection records only: `socialRefreshPath` (the first of `SOCIAL_PATHS`, the order of `SOCIAL_PROVIDERS`, that `connectedSources` counts and that waits for no reconnect), `xDirect` (a direct X record waiting for no reconnect) and `siteChoices` (the Composio Google record's `gaProperties` and `gscSites`, a malformed list read as empty, a name cut to 80, at most 50 of each, with the ones chosen); and three role flags, `can(u, "read")`, `can(u, "generate")` and `can(u, "connectors")`. The flags only decide what is shown: the connect route checks each action (steps 8, 9 and 17). The social card (`SocialStats`, a client component) shows one block per v2 part in the order of `PLATFORMS`, then X's own row (its account as stored, its own read date, followers and posts; the reader's fallback account "X" is not repeated after the name); a tile or figure carries its change against the part's previous window only where both figures are numbers and the previous one is above 0 (`changeText`: `Math.round(pctChange(cur, prev))` with its sign, "vs the previous period"), never otherwise, and a part with `prev` names that window. An empty account shows the platform name alone. A post link renders only over https on its platform's own host with no port or credentials (`postLink`, the readers' hosts) and opens with `rel="noopener noreferrer"`. The Not read list renders the items as React text from the card's own constants in both languages (the unconnected ones in one line, and `publish_only` as its own line, "connected for publishing only", never in it); a failed part's generic line gives way to it when the list names the platform, and the old LinkedIn line gives way to a `choose_page` item. A part whose own line says why it has no results (`ig_not_professional`, `li_no_page_access`) keeps that line, and the card leaves out its `read_failed` item, whose "the next read tries again" a read cannot bring about; Dana's NOT READ block keeps it. "Refresh" (`sync_social` on `socialRefreshPath`, free) shows with `read` when there is a path; "Refresh X (1 credit)" (`sync_x`) with `generate` on a direct X record only; the site card's Refresh (`sync_site` on Google) with `read`; and the GA4 and Search Console selectors (`select_property`) with `connectors` (owner, admin) only when two or more are listed. Each button (`RefreshButton`) posts its action, raises the limit modal for `no_credits`, `trial_over` and `agent_cap` (`limitFromError`), shows a fixed message for the answer's code (`refreshNote`: `slow_down`, `trial_over`, the role's out-of-credits line, `x_unavailable`, `x_direct_only`, `not_connected`, `reconnect_required`, a part to reconnect named from the 200 answer's `reconnect`, nothing read, X already read today without a charge), and calls `router.refresh()` after an answer that is ok. A choice whose read waits (`read: false`) says its figures come with the next read (`selectNote`), and so do Meta's Page choice and LinkedIn's company page choice on Integrations (`pageNote`, `integrations.readLater`). The panel opens the social card when there is a snapshot, an X read, a path to refresh, a direct X record, a LinkedIn page to choose or a not-read item that asks something of the owner (any reason outside `QUIET`: `not_connected`, `publish_only`, `closed_to_apps`, `waiting_approval`); it opens the site card when either part was read or a Composio Google record exists (`siteChoices`), so before the first read, or after a read that failed for both parts and wrote nothing, the card still shows both parts as not read, without a date, with its Refresh and its selectors. Both cards set `dir` by language. The social card's footer says the figures are refreshed daily; when X's row or its Refresh X button is shown it says X is read only when someone presses Refresh X (rule 744). The page shows no model text.

20. The report prompts and the golden set (rules 730, 738; amended 668; spec A5). The task specs of Dana's weekly report, board deck and dashboard spec and Majed's strategy review (`SPECS`, `src/lib/noor.ts`) end with one shared sentence, `reportData(where)`: use WORKSPACE DATA and, when present, STORE, SITE, SOCIAL and AD DATA, each figure with its own dates; take ad totals only from AD DATA (`performance-learnings.md` keeps the latest platform's findings, never a total); compare with the previous period only where prev exists (in WORKSPACE DATA, the previous month), otherwise write [رقم] for the change and say it needs a second read; and, when NOT READ is attached, name its platforms exactly as listed, each with its reason in plain words, never adding or dropping one (at the end of the report or document, on the deck's last slide, below the dashboard's tiles). Editing `SPECS` moves the four types' `codeHash` (`prompt-stamp.ts`), so items made after the change carry a new stamp. The golden set is version 6: `dana-report-roastery` and the new `dana-board-deck` (the same fictional roastery and placeholder check) read one fixture, written in the wording the real builders gave on 2026-10-08 (`statsText`, `siteDataBlock`, `socialDataBlock`, `adDataBlock`, `notReadBlock`) and kept as text so the input stays fixed until the version moves: no store, so sales and revenue are said to be absent; GA4 with `prev` and Search Console without; Instagram with `prev` and TikTok without; Snapchat Ads with `prev` and a new Meta Ads account without; and nine platforms not read. Every figure, name and date in it is a fixture value. Runs of version 5 and 6 never compare (`summarize` and a single-agent rerun's carried rows read only runs of the same version), so the first version 6 run has no deltas. The runs on Preview (`runGolden("manual", { agent: "dana" })` and `{ agent: "majed" }`) are plan Task 19.

## 51. Staging access (2026-10-08)

Spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-08-staging-smooth-releases-design.md` sections 5.3 and 5.4. Files: `src/lib/staging-access.ts` (cookie name, token, `hasAccess`), `src/proxy.ts` (the lock), `src/app/api/staging-access/route.ts`, `src/app/[lang]/staging-access/page.tsx`. Rules 674 and 675, `tests/staging.test.ts`. All of it exists only when `APP_ENV=staging`; on the live site the proxy checks nothing and the page and the route answer 404.

1. The lock. Without a valid `thakeen_staging` cookie the proxy answers every path but four with a bare 404 and `X-Robots-Tag: noindex, nofollow`: `/ar/staging-access`, `/en/staging-access`, `/api/staging-access` and `/robots.txt` (exact match). With `STAGING_ACCESS_KEY` missing or empty nobody gets in, not even with a cookie. The manifest (`/manifest.webmanifest`) is behind the lock too, and the browser fetches it without the cookie, so installing the app to the home screen is tested on a Vercel preview, not on staging.
2. The page. A visitor opens `/ar/staging-access` (or `/en/...`). It is a plain `<form method="post" action="/api/staging-access">`, so it works without JavaScript: a hidden `lang`, one password field `code` and one button («دخول» / "Enter"), under the heading «موقع التجربة» / "Staging". `?e=1` adds «الرمز غير صحيح» / "Wrong code". The page is `noindex, nofollow`.
3. The route. `POST /api/staging-access` is 404 off staging, and every other method is a bare 404 on staging and off it (no 405, no `Allow`), so live shows nothing of the route. It counts the try first, per IP, 5 per 15 minutes, and a sixth is 429 `slow_down` with `Retry-After: 900` even with the right code. Then it reads `code` and `lang` from the form body (never the URL) and compares the code with `STAGING_ACCESS_KEY` through `sameSecret`. The code is not logged and appears in no URL, cookie or redirect.
4. Wrong code, no key, or a body that is not a form: 303 to `{APP_ORIGIN}/{lang}/staging-access?e=1`, no cookie. Right code: 303 to `{APP_ORIGIN}/{lang}` with the cookie `thakeen_staging` = HMAC-SHA256 of the label `staging-access-v1` keyed by `STAGING_ACCESS_KEY`, in hex, never the code. It is `HttpOnly`, `SameSite=Lax`, `Path=/`, 30 days, and `Secure` whenever `APP_ORIGIN` is https, whatever `Host` and `x-forwarded-proto` the proxy forwarded: `sessionCookie` alone would drop the flag for a loopback `Host` without the proto header, which a proxy can send (over an http `APP_ORIGIN`, local development, its own rule stays: Secure except plain http to loopback). `lang` is `en` when the form says so, else `ar`.
5. Redirect host. Both redirects are built from `appOrigin()`, never from `req.url`: behind Hostinger the request URL can be the internal host. So `APP_ORIGIN` on staging must be the staging site's own origin, which also keeps the redirect inside the site's CSP `form-action 'self'`, applied to redirects by browsers that follow the CSP specification.
6. Signing everyone out: change `STAGING_ACCESS_KEY`. The cookie is an HMAC of the key, so every cookie minted under the old key stops matching at once; a copied cookie cannot be turned back into the code. The cookie only opens the door: it is not a login, and every handler behind it still enforces its own rule.
7. Two facts only the host can settle, checked once on staging after its first build (the plan's Task 13, step 4):
   - The cookie flags and the redirect host, from one answer: `answer=$(curl -si -X POST --data-urlencode "code=$STAGING_ACCESS_CODE" -d lang=en https://staging.thakeen.com/api/staging-access | tr -d '\r')`. Then `printf '%s\n' "$answer" | grep -i '^set-cookie:' | grep -oiE 'httponly|secure|samesite=[a-z]+|max-age=[0-9]+'` must list all four, and `printf '%s\n' "$answer" | grep -i '^location:'` must print a URL that starts with `https://staging.thakeen.com/`. The flags check the cookie and nothing more: behind Hostinger's proxy `sessionCookie` already sets `Secure` for any host that is not loopback, so all four show whatever `APP_ORIGIN` holds. The `Location` is what checks `APP_ORIGIN`, because the route builds it from `appOrigin()`: a staging app that kept the live value `https://thakeen.com` answers with a `Location` on live, and every link and OAuth callback built from `appOrigin()` would point at live too. Fix `APP_ORIGIN` in hPanel and rebuild before anything else. The commands print flag names and a URL only, never the cookie value or the code.
   - The client address. The 5-per-15-minutes bucket is per `clientIp(req)`: off Vercel only the last `x-forwarded-for` hop, the one the proxy nearest the app appended, else the shared `unknown` (rule 773; `x-vercel-forwarded-for` and `x-real-ip` count only on Vercel, because on Hostinger a client writes them, which on live on 2026-10-08 let a caller rotate its own bucket). Which proxy appends that hop on Hostinger, and whether it is the client or Hostinger's CDN edge, is unverified. If Hostinger forwards no address, or the last hop is the CDN's edge, five tries from anyone (behind that edge) lock the team out for 15 minutes. Send wrong codes from one network until one is refused (the sixth try of the window, counting the flags check above, must be 429 with `Retry-After: 900`), then one wrong code from a second network, such as a phone on mobile data: it must be a 303, not a 429. A 429 there means the bucket is not per client; fix `clientIp` (shared by every public route), not this route, and say so before relying on staging. Then the spoof check, from the first network while it is still refused: one more wrong code sent with `-H 'x-forwarded-for: 198.51.100.7' -H 'x-real-ip: 198.51.100.8' -H 'x-vercel-forwarded-for: 198.51.100.9'` must still be 429. A 303 means a client can choose its bucket: the proxy passed the caller's `x-forwarded-for` through without appending, and `clientIp` must read another header. The check uses up that first network's tries for 15 minutes, so run it last and wait before the probe and smoke scripts, which post the form once each. Since 2026-10-11 the list itself can be read first, without spending tries: `GET /api/admin/client-ip` with the admin key (rule 908; `curl -s -H "x-admin-key: $WAITLIST_ADMIN_KEY" https://<host>/api/admin/client-ip`, the key from the founder's own shell, never pasted anywhere; on staging add the lock cookie). Read `forwardedFor` and `bucket` from two networks, and once more with `-H 'x-forwarded-for: 198.51.100.7'`. One entry that is the network's own public address, unchanged by the spoof except for `198.51.100.7` added to its LEFT: the default `TRUSTED_PROXY_HOPS=1` is right. Two or more entries whose last is the same on both networks (an edge, often a private or Hostinger address) and whose second-to-last is the network's own: set `TRUSTED_PROXY_HOPS=2` in hPanel and read again until `bucket` is the network's own address and the spoofed value never is. The spoofed value as the LAST entry (the proxy did not append): no hop count is safe, and `clientIp` must read another header; stop and report (rule 979).
   - The same address decides the health bucket. `GET /api/health` keys `health:{ip}` (60 a minute) with the same `clientIp`, so a host that forwards no client address puts every caller of the probe, the smoke script, a monitor and a visitor into one bucket, and the probe alone (about 50 a minute) fills most of it. This check settles both: do not run the probe or the smoke script before it passes.
8. Running the scripts. `scripts/smoke.mjs` reads `STAGING_ACCESS_CODE`, `SMOKE_EMAIL`, `SMOKE_PASSWORD` and `SMOKE_CRON_SECRET`, and `scripts/probe.mjs` reads `STAGING_ACCESS_CODE`, each from the environment or, when that is empty, from the gitignored `.smoke.local` at the root of the checkout that runs them (one reader for both, `scripts/staging-cookie.mjs`); on staging the probe with no code from either place stops at once with exit 2 instead of polling the lock's 404s. Both accept only `https://thakeen.com` (anonymous checks, nothing secret sent), `https://staging.thakeen.com` and `http://localhost:<port>` or `http://127.0.0.1:<port>`: any other origin (a typo, `www`, plain `http`, a path) exits 2 before a request, so the access code, the test password and the cron secret reach no other host; `docs/system/variables.md` section 7 lists each and says what the test account needs: no brand (the app home check accepts `/ar/app/start`), a paid plan (a trial ends after 3 days and 30 chat messages, and the chat check then fails with 402 `trial_over` or `chat_allowance`), and a store of staging's own. Every staging run sends one real chat message on the staging Anthropic key. Neither script runs against staging before item 7 has passed; both say so in their headers but cannot check it.

## 52. Releases: staging, release pull request, rollback (2026-10-08)

Spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-08-staging-smooth-releases-design.md` sections 3, 6 and 7. Rule 679 (expand and contract; a process rule, not pinned by a test). The lock that keeps staging private is section 51 ("Staging access"); the two sites are described in `docs/system/architecture.md` ("Environments"); the checks below are run by `scripts/smoke.mjs` and `scripts/probe.mjs`.

Status on 2026-10-13 (the 2026-10-08 line said the flow was not switched on: the code and git show it is): the `production` branch exists on origin (tip `dff95f25`, release pull request #336, which carries `main` at `07ac4684`; release tags `live-2026-10-08-1`, `live-2026-10-09-1`, `live-2026-10-09-2`, `live-2026-10-12-1`), thakeen.com deploys from it (since 2026-10-08, `docs/system/architecture.md` "Environments"), and a merge to `main` does not reach customers until a release pull request merges `main` into `production`. The staging app is unverified here: nothing in the repository shows whether `staging.thakeen.com` builds from `main` yet (the 2026-10-11 study found it answering 404). Each step that changes something outside the repository (the live app, the staging app) is done only after the founder says yes to that step (spec section 11). A build of a `main` without the lock, made by any other merge in the gap, would put an open copy of the site on staging, so staging's auto-deploy (branch `main`) is turned on, and its first build started, only after the pull request that carries the lock is on `main` and `APP_ENV=staging` and the other staging variables are set (the plan's Tasks 12 and 13).

1. The path. Feature branch, pull request into `main`, which auto-deploys staging.thakeen.com. Then a release pull request `main` to `production`, which the founder merges, and `production` auto-deploys thakeen.com. That merge is the only way to the live site; merging to `main` never reaches customers. `production` is created at the commit live when it is made (`02934d2` on 2026-10-08), so switching the live auto-deploy to it changes nothing customers see, and nothing is pushed to it except through a merged pull request. If the GitHub plan allows a branch rule on a private repository it gets one (pull requests only, no force push, no deletion); if not, this section is the rule.
2. Who does what. Claude opens the release pull request from `main` (or from a tested commit of `main`), verifies the release, tags it, and opens the rollback pull requests. The founder merges, in GitHub, with "Create a merge commit": never squash or rebase, because a squashed release gives `production` commits `main` does not have and every later release pull request then conflicts. Claude never merges a release pull request, never pushes to `production` (the tag of item 7 is the only push) and never changes the live Hostinger app without the founder's yes for that step.
3. The release pull request's description has five fields:
