   - the pull requests merged into `main` since the last release (the newest `live-*` tag is the starting point; before the first tag, the commit `production` was created at);
   - the staging commit that passed;
   - the smoke results (item 4);
   - whether stored data changes shape, and if it does, the step: expand or contract (item 9);
   - whether any environment variable is added, changed or removed (item 10), by name and never by value.
4. What "passed staging" means. All of these, written into the release pull request:
   - `npm run check` and `npx next build` pass locally on the release commit.
   - The staging build of that commit completed (`hosting_nodejs_list-builds`) and staging's `/api/health` shows the commit. While `commit` is `unknown`, the build record's `options.source_options.commit.hash` is that commit.
   - `node scripts/smoke.mjs https://staging.thakeen.com` passes: `/` is 307 to `/ar`; `/ar` and `/en` are 200; `/robots.txt` disallows everything; `/api/tasks` without a session is 401; the staging test account signs in and the app home renders; one chat message gets an answer (real model, staging key); one cron route called by hand with the staging `CRON_SECRET` answers 200.
   - Any page or flow the release changes, opened by hand on staging.

   `.github/workflows/ci.yml` triggers on every pull request, so it also runs on pull requests into `production`. GitHub Actions has not run since 2026-10-05 (billing); once it runs again, a red run means the release is not merged.
5. Hotfix. The same path, only faster: fix on a branch, pull request into `main`, staging check, release pull request. About 5 minutes from merge to live. No shortcut around staging.
6. The window. A release starts at night in Saudi Arabia, between 22:00 and 00:40 UTC (01:00 to 03:40 Riyadh), unless the live site is broken. Inside that window the merge happens at minute :05 to :10, :20 to :25, :35 to :40 or :50 to :55 (so the last slot is 00:35 to 00:40), so the build of about 2 minutes finishes before the next quarter-hour tick of `/api/cron/secretary` (`architecture.md` 2.6). It also stays at least 10 minutes clear of every schedule in `crons.json` (`vercel.json` until 2026-10-10): none falls inside the window (the nearest after it are `golden` at 01:00 on Saturdays, 20 minutes after the last slot, and `stores` at 02:00). The hPanel crons on the live app follow the same rule: a read-only list of them on 2026-10-08 shows the same eight schedules as `vercel.json` plus `*/15 * * * *` for `/api/cron/secretary`, each a `curl` of the live route, so the clearance above holds for them too. The list shows no time zone; the window assumes the server clock is UTC, as `vercel.json` is, so a change of cron or server clock is a reason to recheck it.
7. After the merge. Claude reads the live build (`hosting_nodejs_list-builds`) until it completes, then, in this order:
   - Warm-up: one call each to `/ar`, `/en`, `/ar/app` and `/api/health`, so the new process is warm before a customer arrives.
   - Verify: `/api/health` on thakeen.com shows the release commit. While `commit` is `unknown`, `built` is later than before and the completed build whose `options.source_options.commit.hash` is the release commit. Then `node scripts/smoke.mjs https://thakeen.com` passes (the anonymous checks only; sign-in, chat and cron run on staging).
   - Tag: the `production` commit gets `live-YYYY-MM-DD-N` (N counts that day's releases from 1) and Claude pushes the tag. That a pushed tag starts no Hostinger build is a fact the first release confirms (spec section 12).
   - A wrong commit in `/api/health` after 10 minutes means the build failed or is stuck: read `hosting_nodejs_list-builds`, then the build log.
8. Before the first release. Step 0 (spec section 8) measures on staging what Hostinger's documentation leaves open: how long the restart after the switch takes, and whether a request in flight (a streaming chat answer, a cron run of up to 310 s) survives it. It runs `node scripts/probe.mjs https://staging.thakeen.com 300` while staging rebuilds, starts a build that fails on purpose to see the old commit keep serving, and compares `BUILD_COMMIT` with the build record. If the gap is longer than a second or two, the founder decides between living with it in the night window and a VPS with blue-green switching (spec approach C). Neither script runs against staging before the client-address check of section 51, item 7.
9. Data that changes shape (rule 679). For a few seconds two versions may run, and a rollback runs old code on new data, so every change to a stored shape (a Redis key, a field, a JSON layout) goes out in two releases:
   - Expand: the new code reads the old and the new shape and writes the new one. Old code must still read what the new code writes, or ignore it safely.
   - Contract: only in a later release, once rollback past the expand release is no longer wanted, the old reading goes.

   The release pull request that changes a stored shape says so and names its step. A one-time conversion of existing data is an idempotent script run after the expand release is live, never at build time. No test can tell whether old code survives new data, so the check is the reviewer reading that field (`tests.md` row 679: not pinned).

   Worked case, `User.grants` (rule 783, the founder's video grant; an expand step, so the release pull request that carries it says "expand"). The gates of older builds ignore the field and `saveUser` keeps it, but the `GET /api/profile` of builds before Part A returns every field of the record that it does not remove, so after a grant is written, a rollback past Part A would send the owner the founder's email and private note. Before rolling back past Part A, either write no grant until the release is past its rollback window, or run an idempotent cleanup that deletes `grants` from every user record first. Since 2026-10-09 (rule 787) that route lists the fields it sends instead of the ones it removes, so for it a new field on `user:{email}` is safe by default: a build with rule 787 never sends a field it does not name, including one a later release writes and a rollback leaves behind. Rolling back to a build before rule 787 still sends whatever that build does not remove: past Part A that is `grants` (the step above), and past rule 787 any field added to `User` after it, so a release that adds a private field to the record says so in its description.
   Worked case, the `thakeen_*` cookie and token names (rules 1810-1813, 2026-10-13; an expand step). The new code reads `thakeen_session` then `nashr_session` (and the same for the return, impersonation, language, theme, partner and OAuth-state cookies and the three storage keys), so nobody is signed out. Old code ignores the new names: a rollback signs out only visitors who signed in after the release, and changes no data. MCP and OAuth tokens minted after it start `thakeen_`; tokens are looked up by hash, so old `nashr_` tokens keep working on both sides. Contract later: drop `LEGACY` in `src/lib/cookie-names.ts` once 30 days have passed. `BEARER` in `src/lib/oauth.ts` keeps accepting `nashr_`: a personal MCP token never expires, so dropping it would cut off a connected client.
   Worked case, the multi-workspace releases (rules 2110-2139, 2026-10-19; section 91.1): a rollback floor. M1 is an expand step: it writes persons, memberships and per-(person, account) keys beside every legacy record, so a revert of M1 to any earlier build is safe. M2 is the first release that makes data an older build cannot read (a `p_` person with no legacy record, a membership held only in `mbr:`). Reverting M2 to M1 is safe, because M1 reads both shapes. Reverting past M1 is forbidden: the person would not sign in and the membership would vanish (fail closed, but the customer is locked out). So the M2 release pull request says "enable, rollback floor: M1", and while M2 is live a rollback pull request (item 11) never reverts past M1's release merge. The floor moves up with each contract step: M4 refuses sessions without `pid`, so after M4 the floor is M3.
10. Environment variables. A variable changes on staging first. On the live site a change needs a rebuild, so it counts as a release: it is named in a release pull request's description and the rebuild is done in the window of item 6.
11. Rollback (step by step, rehearsed locally on 2026-10-12: `docs/release/rollback.md`). A revert pull request into `production`, reverting the release merge, opened by Claude and merged by the founder. The live site is on the old code again about 2 to 3 minutes after the merge (one build). The `live-*` tags make the last good commit obvious. Rolling back a contract release lands on the expand code, which still reads both shapes; going further back than the expand release is what the contract step gave up (item 9).
12. After a rollback. Once a merge is reverted, merging the same commits again adds nothing back. So right after the rollback Claude opens a pull request `production` to `main` that brings the revert into `main`, and the founder merges it. The fix on `main` starts by reverting that revert. The fixed change then comes forward through staging and a normal release pull request (items 3 and 4).

### 52.1 Release checklist: model routing and the Straitly gateway (2026-10-09)

Spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-08-straitly-model-routing-design.md` sections 9 to 11, plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-08-straitly-model-routing.md`; rules 810-866 in `tests.md`. Each line is ticked in the release pull request that carries this work, and the release pull request names the step. Keys are set by the founder in the environment or the host's settings, by name only: never in chat, a command line that is saved, a commit or a pull request.

- [ ] Rule 679 step: EXPAND. The new stored fields are optional and additive: `Conversation.model`, `Brand.model` and `User.chinaHostingOk` (section 8 step 15.6). Old code ignores them. No contract release is needed, except the one that removed the `RATES` fallback from `src/lib/usage.ts`, done on 2026-10-10 with Opus 5 and Sonnet 5 (rule 970): no stored value is a rate, and a stored pick of a removed model reads as `unknown` and runs on the default, so no data conversion.
- [ ] Spike (plan Task 1): the founder sets `STRAITLY_API_KEY` in the shell that runs it, as in the next item. Record the result table in the spec's section 2 for the five unknowns:
  - strict tool schemas (`strict: true`);
  - adaptive thinking with `effort`;
  - the cache counts in `usage` (`cache_read_input_tokens` above 0 on the second identical call);
  - a non-Claude model on `/v1/messages`;
  - the rate limits and the auth header (`x-api-key` or `Authorization: Bearer`).

  The SDK adds `/v1/messages` itself, so the base it is given is `https://api.straitly.ai`, without `/v1`. `STRAITLY_BASE_URL`, when set, is an origin only: the app and the script refuse a `/v1` or any other path, a query, a fragment or user info, and whitespace, a control character or port 0 inside it (rule 810).
- [ ] Catalog ids: in the shell that runs the check, the founder types `read -rs STRAITLY_API_KEY; export STRAITLY_API_KEY` and pastes the key at the silent prompt, so it is neither shown nor saved in the shell history; then run `npx --no-install jiti scripts/check-models.ts`. This reads `GET /v1/models` (rule 812). Exit 0: every catalog id is listed. Exit 1: it prints the ids the list lacks. Exit 2: the check could not be made.
- [ ] Staging first, in this order:
  1. Direct run: while staging.thakeen.com still has `LLM_PROVIDER` unset (direct Anthropic), run the golden set once (next item).
  2. Switch: set `LLM_PROVIDER=straitly` and `STRAITLY_API_KEY` on staging only, keeping `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` for the direct fallback, and rebuild (item 10). Production keeps `LLM_PROVIDER` unset.
  3. Straitly run: run the golden set again.

  To go back to direct Anthropic on staging, unset `LLM_PROVIDER` there and rebuild.
- [ ] Golden set on staging: in the founder console's Golden set panel, press "Run" (`POST /api/admin/golden`; `src/lib/golden.ts`).
  - The two runs of the item above, direct first and Straitly second, at the same `GOLDEN_VERSION` (a constant in `src/lib/golden.ts`: nothing that changes it reaches staging between the runs).
  - Compare the overall score and each agent's judge score. The panel's own note says a move under ±5 is noise.
  - A drop beyond that stops the release.
  - The judge's scores on real work come from `src/lib/evals.ts` (`GET /api/admin/evals?days=N`, the last N days; the console's Quality tab shows 30). Read each agent's average judge score just before the switch and again N days after it, so the two windows do not overlap.
- [ ] Cost and refusals: the founder console's Billing tab, model usage block (rules 855-865):
  - each model's calls and USD;
  - each provider's USD (`anthropic`, `straitly`) and the fallback count (requests re-sent to Anthropic);
  - the refused picks.

  Then check the rates against the first Straitly invoice. This step lowers no Claude rate:
  - The Claude entries in `src/lib/models/catalog.ts` (the only rate table since rule 970) stay at the Anthropic LIST price while any route can reach Anthropic directly: the fallback (`withDirectFallback` in `src/lib/model.ts`), and all of production while `LLM_PROVIDER` is unset. Pricing the dearest route keeps the margin floor honest (the comment above `MODELS` in `catalog.ts`).
  - The invoice confirms the gateway's 40% below list (spec section 2), or corrects it. It sets or verifies the gateway-only entries (provider list price x 0.6). It is also the real figure behind the console's caveat: the provider USD there is at catalog (list) rates, so the `straitly` line for a Claude model reads above the invoice.
  - Changing a Claude rate is a separate founder decision, made with `docs/system/costs.md` updated in the same change and a full `npm run check`.
  - Any rate that does change, of either kind, is followed by `npx vitest run tests/pricing-floor.test.ts tests/model-catalog.test.ts` (part of `npm run check` as well).
- [ ] Opus by hand: use an environment with a model key (staging), because without a key chat runs in mock mode and gives the message back.
  - Pick Opus in the chat picker and send one message.
  - The allowance line drops by 2 messages (`chatUnitsFor`, rule 818).
  - The overflow hold is 5 credits (`chatHoldFor`, rule 817; 4 until yearly billing lowered the ceiling on 2026-10-24, rule 4033). It shows as `chat.overflow.hold` in `GET /api/chat?conv={id}` for that conversation (and in the `chat` field of the reply). On screen it shows only with the allowance used up: in the line under the box when "continue on credits" is on, or in the `chat_credits` limit dialog.
- [ ] Before any economy model becomes pickable (`pick: true` on an entry whose chat multiplier is below 1), both of these must be done:
  - Chat says that a message "counts as 1 message". Today the line appears only when `chatUnits` is above 1 (rule 846).
  - `POST /api/conversations` answers the new conversation's `chat` status. Today the allowance line keeps the previous conversation's ceiling until the first answer (rule 853, "Not pinned").
- [ ] Production: `LLM_PROVIDER=straitly` reaches production only after the founder's explicit yes in chat for that change, and in a release pull request that names it (items 3 and 10). Without that yes, production stays on direct Anthropic.

## 53. Email verification: signup, login, existing accounts, change email (2026-10-08)

Spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-08-email-verification-design.md` (the code is the truth where it differs: the bucket names in item 5 and the session window in item 9). Files: `src/lib/auth.ts` (`emailVerified`, `currentUserUnverified`, the gate in `resolveSession`), `src/lib/verify-email.ts` (code, limits, `markVerified`, `moveUnverifiedAccount`), `src/app/api/auth/verify/send|check|email/route.ts`, `src/app/api/auth/signup/route.ts`, `src/app/[lang]/app/verify/page.tsx`, `src/components/app/VerifyForm.tsx`, `src/lib/verify-client.ts`, `src/app/[lang]/app/layout.tsx`, `src/lib/mcp.ts`, `src/lib/schedule.ts`, `src/lib/secretary-remind.ts`, `src/lib/secretary-feed.ts`, `src/app/api/auth/login/route.ts`, `src/app/api/auth/reset/route.ts`, `src/components/app/AuthForm.tsx`. Rules 751-769, `tests/email-verify-*.test.ts`. A follow-up spec, not this one, adds WhatsApp phone verification.

1. Who is gated. Every owner login (`user:{email}`) whose record has no `emailVerifiedAt`. A member login is gated by its owner's state (rule 767): while the owner has not verified, the account spends nothing, so its members are signed out too, in both resolvers, and come back on their own once the owner verifies. A founder impersonation passes (support must reach a stuck account), the console key's `imp: "key"` included. Existing accounts are not grandfathered: on release day none has the stamp, so each owner, the founder included, meets the gate on the next request. `createUser` sets `gatedSignup: true` from release day on; it marks an account created under the gate and is never cleared.
2. Signup. After the account and its session exist, the route mails the first code and, only when that mail went out, counts it in `verify:gap:{email}`, `verify:hour:{email}` and `verify:day:{email}` (a failed mail is not counted, so the verify page's own first send goes through), and answers `{ok, referred, partner, verify: true, mail: boolean}`. A mail that cannot go out (mail off, the provider refused, the call threw) never fails the signup; `mail: false` sends the form to `/{lang}/app/verify?next=...&sent=0`, and the page then offers a resend instead of saying a mail went. The form always goes to the verify page with the old destination (the start page, or `next`) in `next`.
3. Login. The password still creates an owner's session. The answer is `{ok: true, verify: boolean}`: `verify: true` for an owner who has not verified, and `LoginForm` then goes to `/{lang}/app/verify?next=` with the `safeNext`'d destination (or `/{lang}/app`) instead of the destination, so a `next` outside `/[lang]/app` cannot loop back to login. The OAuth consent page (`/oauth/authorize`) sends a signed-out visitor to `/ar/app/login?next=/oauth/authorize?...`; `safeNext` keeps that path, so after the code the verify page returns to the consent page. An owner who opens any app page with an old session lands on the verify page through the layout (item 4). A member of an unverified owner gets 403 `owner_unverified` and no session (the form says «صاحب الحساب لم يؤكد بريده بعد»); a member's password reset saves the password and answers the same 403. An owner's password reset verifies the address (rule 769): the single-use link was mailed to that address, so `/api/auth/reset` stamps `emailVerifiedAt` when unset, inside the lock that saves the new hash.
4. The gate. `currentUser()` returns `null` for an unverified owner, so every page and route that calls it (also the ones added later) treats the owner as signed out. `currentUserUnverified()` resolves the same session and also returns that owner; only the app layout, the verify page and the three verify routes call it (rule 752, a source scan). The app layout, when `currentUser()` is null and the path is not public, asks `currentUserUnverified()` and, for an owner, redirects to `/{lang}/app/verify`, keeping the requested page in a `safeNext`'d `?next=`; otherwise to login. `/verify` is in the layout's public list. The verify page sends a signed-out visitor to login and a member, a founder session or a verified owner to `?next=` or the app. A member of an unverified owner resolves in neither resolver, so the layout and the verify page send them to login. The MCP bearer (`userFromBearer`) refuses an unverified owner (rule 753), tokens minted before the gate included.
5. The code. `sendVerifyCode` issues 6 digits from `crypto.randomInt` (`issueVerifyCode`), stores `{hash, sentAt}` under `verify:code:{email}` with a 900 s TTL (SHA-256 salted with the address, never the code: that keeps the code out of storage and logs, but 10^6 codes reverse offline for anyone who can read Redis, hence the 15-minute TTL and the attempt cap), and mails it in the account's language (plain text through `sendEmail`). A new code replaces the old one and deletes `verify:tries:{email}`. Send is `POST /api/auth/verify/send`, for the session's address only. Refusals, in order, the refusing one names the wait with `Retry-After`: 503 `mail_unavailable` when `mailReady()` is false (unless a test deploy, item 10); 429 `slow_down` from `verify:gap:{email}` (1 per 60 s), `verify:hour:{email}` (5 per hour), `verify:day:{email}` (10 per day, rule 768, `Retry-After: 86400`) and `verify:ip:{ip}` (20 per hour). 401 without a session (and for a member of an unverified owner); 409 `already_verified` for a member of a verified owner or a verified owner; 502 `send_failed` when the provider refuses the mail, and the code is dropped then (a code nobody received must not be left to guess against). The page shows a 60 s resend countdown, taken from the answer's `resendIn` or the `Retry-After`.
6. Check. `POST /api/auth/verify/check {code}`: 401 without a session or for a member of an unverified owner, ok for a member of a verified owner or a verified owner (a double submit lands here), 429 `slow_down` past 60 per IP per hour (`verifycheck:ip:{ip}`). Arabic-Indic and Persian digits are turned into Latin; input that is not exactly 6 digits then is 400 `wrong_code` and uses no attempt. Otherwise `checkVerifyCode` reads the code (`expired` when gone), takes an attempt with `INCRBY verify:tries:{email}` (TTL 900 s, same as the code) before comparing with `sameSecret`, so parallel guesses each get their own count: a wrong answer is 400 `wrong_code` with `attemptsLeft`, the fifth deletes the code and the counter (`locked`), and a request that finds the counter already past five also answers `locked`. A correct code calls `markVerified`: under the account lock it sets `emailVerifiedAt` only if unset, then deletes the code and the counter, and the page goes to `next` or `/{lang}/app` (onboarding for a new account, as before). A parallel submit that lost the race and finds the account verified answers ok.
7. The verify page. `/{lang}/app/verify`, Arabic first, quiet-D (rule 519). One numeric input (`inputmode="numeric"`, `autocomplete="one-time-code"`, left to right, wide enough for Arabic digits), a resend button disabled while the countdown runs, the attempts left after a wrong code (Arabic special-cases one and two: «باقي محاولة واحدة», «باقي محاولتان»), and Log out. The address shown is the session's, never one from the URL. It says "We sent a 6-digit code to {email}" only while a mail is known to have gone; after a signup with `sent=0`, a refused send or a change-email answer with `mail: false` it says «بريدك: {email}» / "Your email: {email}" and offers a resend at once. A first visit with no code waiting sends one when the page opens (`autoSend`, once). When this deploy cannot send mail (`mailReady()` false, and not a test deploy) the page shows the support link at once: WhatsApp (`SUPPORT_WHATSAPP`), or a neutral "Contact us" link to the public help page when no number is set. The code is never in a URL or a log.
8. Change a mistyped address. "Wrong email? Change it" shows only for an owner with `gatedSignup` and no `emailVerifiedAt`; `POST /api/auth/verify/email {email}` enforces it (403 `not_allowed` for any other account, a member session or a founder impersonating; 401 without a session or for a member of an unverified owner). Order: `verifymove:ip:{ip}` 20 per hour (429), `bad_email` and `same_email` (400), `verifymove:acct:{session email}` 10 per day, counted before the lookup so a taken address is no oracle (429), then `moveUnverifiedAccount`: the login locks of both addresses in alphabetical order, then the old account's user lock; 3 moves per account per UTC day (429 with `Retry-After` to the next UTC midnight; the counter is `emailMoves` and travels with the record); the target must be free in `getLogin` (owner or member), else 409 `exists`. Inside: write the record at the new address (name, phone, password hash, lang, plan, credits, trial end, `createdAt`, `gatedSignup`), register its referral code, carry the referral applied at signup and the partner attribution, drop the old address's pending code, and only then `wipeAccount(old)`. An account that is still gated and unverified holds no brand data (the gate blocked every route), which is why this small move is enough; an older account (no `gatedSignup`) never sees the option, because its data is keyed by its address. A move that stopped before the wipe leaves two records; the retry recognises its own copy (same `createdAt`, same password hash, still gated and unverified) and finishes instead of answering `exists`. On success the route ends the old session, opens one for the new address and sends a code to it through the same refusals as item 5; it answers 200 `{ok, email, mail?}` even when that send is refused or fails (the account has moved; `mail: false` and the page offers a resend), 429 `slow_down` when a bucket is full.
9. What a move leaves behind. The moved record gets a fresh `sessionsAfter` (the spec listed it as carried; it is not): a session issued earlier for the target address (a deleted former owner, a removed member, this owner's own devices after A to B to A) must never resolve to the moved account, and the session the route opens right after is not older than it. `wipeAccount(old)` does what any account deletion does, so it appends an entry to the `deleted-accounts` list and drops the old address's `uev:` signup counters: the founder's funnel counts the signup of a moved account under neither address (it undercounts). The referrer's list and the partner's set may name both addresses after a stopped move, until the retry.
10. `devCode`. Only when `APP_ENV=test` and the deploy is not a production deploy (`devCodeExposed`, rule 766) the send, signup and change-email answers carry the code as `devCode`, and they do so even when mail is off or refused, so the e2e suite verifies an account without a mailbox (`signUpVerified` in `e2e/fixtures/accounts.ts`). No other answer, log or URL ever carries a code. Hostinger and Vercel production must never set `APP_ENV=test` (`variables.md`).
11. What pauses until the owner verifies (rules 755 and 756). `dailyRoster()` puts an unverified owner's scopes in `idle`: the `daily`, `evening`, `weekly`, `routines` and `stores` crons skip them (no brief, lifecycle email, scheduled task or credit spend), and so does everything in `dailyFor` for them: renewal, top-up settlement and hold refunds wait too, and only Reem's purge of items deleted over 30 days ago still runs for the idle scopes. The reminder sweep (`cron/secretary`) leaves their reminders in `sec:due` and delivers them late, once the owner is verified. `cron/media` keeps finishing and refunding jobs already started and paid (a paid result is not thrown away) and `cron/golden` is the founder's evaluation; neither changed. Calendar feed and booking links answer as for an unknown owner, a member's link too while the owner behind it is unverified. Consequence the founder accepted: an existing customer's automations and booking page pause until they verify, then resume on their own.
12. The founder console. The founder's cookie access goes through `currentUser`, so after release the founder must verify their own email first; `WAITLIST_ADMIN_KEY` access (header or key session) does not depend on a login and still works. Impersonating an unverified owner works. Since 2026-10-09 (rules 776-777) the Users list marks an owner without `emailVerifiedAt` "Unverified" («غير موثّق»), the account panel shows the day the address was proved, and an unverified owner's panel offers "Resend verification code": `POST /api/admin/users/{email} {action: "resendVerification"}` mails a fresh code to the account's own address through the same `verifySendRefusal` as item 5 (the per-address buckets are shared with the owner's own resend), never returns the code (not even on a test deploy), answers 409 `already_verified` for a verified owner and 502 `send_failed` when the mail did not go out (the code is then dropped), and logs `resendVerification` with `sent` to `admin:log`. The founder never sees or types the code; the owner still enters it on the verify page.
13. Release. Resend must stay configured (`RESEND_API_KEY`, `MAIL_FROM`) on Hostinger: without it nobody can verify and the page shows only the support link. Run the staging smoke before the release pull request (section 52).

## 54. Agent scope: four layers (2026-10-10)

Files: `src/lib/agent-scope.ts`, `src/lib/agent-scope-classify.ts`, `src/lib/agent-scope-verdicts.ts`, `src/lib/notes-read.ts`, `src/lib/scope-refusal.ts`, `src/lib/rate.ts`, `src/lib/chat.ts`, `src/lib/generate-batch.ts`, `src/lib/external-drafts.ts`, `src/lib/parallel.ts`, `src/lib/noor.ts`, `src/lib/app-i18n.ts`, `src/app/api/chat/route.ts`, `src/app/api/generate/route.ts`, `src/app/api/publish/[id]/route.ts`, `src/app/api/admin/agent-scope/release/route.ts`, `src/components/app/Inbox.tsx`, `src/lib/agent-scope-eval.ts`, `src/lib/agent-scope-set.json`, `src/app/api/admin/agent-scope/route.ts`, `src/app/api/admin/agent-scope/modes/route.ts`, `src/app/api/admin/agent-scope/eval/route.ts`, `src/components/admin/AgentScopePanel.tsx`, `src/app/api/mcp/route.ts`, `src/components/app/GeneratePanel.tsx`, `src/components/app/ReportsPanel.tsx`, `src/components/app/ChatPanel.tsx`. Spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-08-entitlements-agent-scope-design.md` section 6. Branch `claude/entitlements-part-b`; the other layers are later tasks on it.

The founder sets each layer's mode in `agent-scope:modes` (`prompt` on or off; `input`, `role`, `output` off, shadow or enforce; default on, shadow, shadow, shadow, rule 790). A layer in `shadow` asks the classifier and logs what it would have refused without changing the answer; in `enforce` a refusal replaces the answer. The classifier (`classify`, Haiku through `route("agentScope")`, 3 s, rule 791) answers with one of six verdicts; every verdict but `in` is logged to `agent-scope:log:{UTC day}` (rule 793).

1. Chat turn: input gate (rules 794, 795). The web chat (`POST /api/chat`) and MCP `ask_noor` read the modes and the brand scope's ceiling of refused messages with the burst ceiling, in one round (`agentScopePreflight(u)`). In `enforce`, a scope that has had 20 refused messages in the hour or 60 in the day (`rate:agent-scope:h|d:{scope}`, the brand scope `sc(u)`) is answered first: 429 `slow_down` with `retry` and `Retry-After` (MCP: the slow text with `isError`), before the allowance is touched and with no classifier call. Otherwise `chat()` gets the modes and, after the paused reply and the brand reads and before the first model call, builds the context: the agent, `where` (`chat` or `mcp_chat`), the active brand's name and sector, the owner's other brands (`otherBrandNames`, `[]` when the store fails) and the request's deadline. `off`: nothing. `shadow`: the classifier runs alongside the turn, is awaited before every return, and a refusal is only logged; the turn is counted as usual. `enforce`: the classifier is asked first; `in` runs the turn; any other verdict saves the owner's message and the refusal (written from the owner's own words, rule 792) to the thread and returns it with no model call and no `usd`, so `runChatTurn` gives the message back to the allowance, and the refusal is counted toward the ceiling (only enforced input refusals count). A classifier error or a timeout lets the turn through (input fails open), and so does a ceiling that cannot be read.
2. Generation notes: input gate (rule 796). `POST /api/generate` and MCP `generate_content` hand the owner's notes to `generateBatch` as `notesGate`, and the gate runs inside it, so a refusal charges nothing and saves nothing and an owner who is refused for a real reason reads that reason. Order: auth; the model-paused 503 (web); the type check (400 `bad_type`; MCP "Unknown type."); `agentScopePreflight`; then `generateBatch(…, { agentScope: modes, notesGate })`, which checks the model switch (paused), the brand (`no_brand`), the trial (`trial_over`) and the missing data (`missing_data`), and only then the gate, before the renewal, the owning agent's cap and the charge. A trial that has ended, a missing brand file or a missing cart store therefore make no classifier call, count nothing and read their own answer in every mode. Notes are a string: the web route drops any other value, so an array that the generator would read as its text never passes unread. The gate asks, counts and refuses nothing when `input` is `off` or the notes are empty after trimming, so a spent refusal ceiling (below) never holds a request without notes. Otherwise: (a) the preflight's spent ceiling (item 1: 20 refused requests in the hour or 60 in the day, only in `enforce`) is 429 `slow_down` with `retry`, `Retry-After` 3600 or 86400 and the slow text as `message` (MCP: the slow text with `isError`), with no classifier call; (b) the ask is counted against the scope's bound on classifier calls for generation, 60 an hour and 200 a day (`rate:agent-scope-gen:h|d:{scope}`, `agentScopeGenRefusal`), which the free refusals cannot reach but `no_credits` and `agent_cap` still can: over the bound, `enforce` answers the same 429 `slow_down` (the window that is spent, the day when both are) and `shadow` skips the classifier, logs nothing and lets the batch go on to its own answer; (c) the classifier reads the notes trimmed and cut at the first 2000 characters, the ones the generator and the reviewer read (`GENERATE_NOTES_READ`, one constant), with the owning agent of the type (`agentForTask`: Dana for a report, Razan for finance and so on), the brand's name and sector, the names of the other brands (`[]` when that read fails, printing only the error's name) and the request's deadline; `where` is `generate` or `mcp_generate`. The reply language is the request's `x-lang` else the account's on the web, the account's over MCP. `shadow`: a refusal is logged; the check runs beside the batch, which is charged as usual, and `generateBatch` awaits it before it returns on every path (a saved batch, a refusal at the charge, a failure). `enforce`: the check is awaited before the charge; a verdict other than `in` is 422 `{ok:false, error:"agent_scope", message}` on the web or a tool error with the same text over MCP, and counts toward the ceiling of item 1; credits, tasks and the activity log are untouched. A classifier error or timeout, or a bound counter that cannot be read, lets the batch run (input fails open); a paused or mocked model asks nothing. The Generate page and the Reports page (the report buttons and the cart-recovery draft) show `message` of an `agent_scope` or `slow_down` answer as text, through `scopeRefusalText` (`src/lib/scope-refusal.ts`). The classifier's cost goes to the global meter only, never to the owner.
3. The prompt, the roles and the offered tools (rules 797-799; spec section 6, Layers 1 and 3).
    1. Layer 1, the scope paragraph (`AGENT_SCOPE_PARAGRAPH`, `src/lib/agent-scope.ts`; the founder's `prompt` switch, on by default). Chat: `system()` ends the cached `fixed` block with it, as static text, so every account shares one cached prefix; the brand's sector goes in the uncached `now` block as `SECTOR: ` + `asData(brand.industry)` right after `BUSINESS:` (not when there is no brand file, and since rule 800 not when the brand has no sector, which used to throw), because the sector is owner or crawl data. `chat()` passes `{ prompt: modes.prompt === "on", roleEnforced: modes.role === "enforce" }`; with the role layer in `enforce`, the intro of the agents whose intro said it (Reem and the generic one) stops saying "and call generate_content for them" when the owner asks for another agent's content, because the code now refuses that call. Generation: `systemPrompt` (`noor.ts`) ends its cached fixed block with the paragraph unless `GenOptions.agentScope` is `false`; it is added there and not in `fixedPrompt`, which the prompt stamp hashes. `generateBatch` passes `agentScope: modes.prompt === "on"`, taking the modes from its caller (`BatchOpts.agentScope`) or, when the caller passed none (routines, the weekly plan, handoffs, an admin run), from one `readAgentScopeModes()` in the `Promise.all` where it reads the brand and governance. MCP: the `ask_noor` and `generate_content` tool descriptions each end with "Scope: only marketing, sales, growth, finance and analytics work for this account's brands; anything else is refused." `prompt: off` removes the paragraph and the sector line from every one of these.
    2. Layer 3, roles for every agent (`roleOwner(agent, type)`: null for no agent or a type in the agent's `tasks`, else the owner from `agentForTask`; Reem has no work type, so every type has an owner for her). `runTool`'s `generate_content` and `draft_items` call `roleGate` after the type checks and before `generateBatch`. Majed (the lead) is always refused with `roleRefusalText("majed", type)` (propose it with `propose_handoff`), in every mode, with no switch card, no log and no mode read (R7). Every other agent follows the founder's `role` mode (the turn's copy in `ToolCtx.modes`, else one read, and only for a type that is somebody else's): `off` runs the batch; `shadow` runs it and writes one `wrong_agent` line to the agent-scope log (`layer: role`, `where: tool`, `mode: shadow`, the agent, `text: type: notes`, the owner's words cut to 200 characters); `enforce` pushes the action `switch:{owner}` and answers with `roleRefusalText` (which tells the agent to say in one sentence who makes it and that the button under its reply switches to that agent), without calling `generateBatch`, so nothing is created and nothing is charged. `turnParts` turns the last `switch:` action whose id passes `isAgentId` into `ChatMsg.switchTo` (an optional field: expand-only, rule 679, an old message has none); the reply and the stored message carry it. `ChatPanel` shows under such an assistant message a button, `chat.switchTo` ("Talk to {name}" / "تحدّث مع {name}") with the agent's display name as plain text, which calls `pick(m.switchTo)` and so opens a new conversation with that agent; it is shown only when `switchTo` is a known agent other than the one the owner is talking to.
    3. The offered-tool check (rule 799, R6). The first thing `runTool` does, when the turn has an agent, is to look the tool up in `toolsFor(agent)`, the list the model was offered in that turn; a name that is not in it (a tool of another agent's turn, such as `propose_handoff` or `contact_away`, or an invented one) is answered `Unknown tool.` before the role check, any other check or any write. It does not depend on any mode. A call with no agent (a test, a caller outside a chat turn) keeps each tool's own answer.
4. Replies and items: output gate (rules 800, 801). Chat (`chat()`, web and MCP `ask_noor`): the model's final answer, when it has text and the `output` layer is not `off`, goes to `scopeCheck("output", …)` with the turn's context. `enforce` awaits it and, on a refusal, saves and returns `agentScopeRefusalText` in place of the text (the cards and the turn's `usd` stay; the classifier's cost goes to the global meter only, never to `ChatTurn.usd`); `shadow` starts it, saves the reply at once and awaits the check in the turn's `finally` (with the input gate's shadow check: both are in one `late` list, awaited on every path, a throw included). A reply cut at its cap is the model's text and is checked the same way (`enforce`: the refusal replaces the text and the cut note). Only the replies the code writes are not checked: paused and mock, out of time, the model's refusal, the tool-round limit, the ceiling, and the capped reply of an empty cut. Generation (`generateBatch` step 5a, section 8.13): every item is checked before it is saved; `enforce` keeps the items inside the team's work and refunds the withheld share; none kept, or a once-charged batch with any withheld, is `AgentScopeRefused`, the whole charge refunded: `/api/generate` 422 `{ok:false, error:"agent_scope", message, credits}`, MCP `generate_content` a tool error with the refusal, the chat tools `Not done: {refusal} Nothing was saved and the credits were refunded.`; a partial hold adds `held` and `refunded` (what landed) to the web answer and, in MCP and the chat tools, "{n} item(s) were withheld as outside the team's work, and their credits refunded.", the last clause only when the refund landed (`withheldNote`) Each refused item is logged with its id (`itemId`).
5. MCP `save_draft`: output gate (rule 802). `saveExternalDraft` checks the hourly and daily draft allowance (`draftAllowed`) first, so a refused draft still counts in both buckets (accepted: it bounds the classifier's spend), then reads the brand and the modes together, then (layer not `off`) checks the draft's title and body (`where: "save_draft"`, the agent that owns the type, the draft's language). `enforce`: a refusal saves nothing and returns `agent_scope` with the refusal, which MCP answers as a tool error. `shadow`: the draft is saved and the check is awaited with the save. A classifier error saves the draft. The check is the only model call of `save_draft` and is metered to Thakeen, not to the account.
6. Publish: output gate and the founder's release (rules 1410, 1411; 2026-10-12). `POST /api/publish/{id}` and the chat tool `publish_item` call `publishScopeCheck(u, t, where, lang, modes)` after their own checks (the publish route: auth, version, target, connection; chat: the agent's tool check, role and approval gates) and before `publishOnce`, so a held item is never sent and never changed. With the `output` layer `off` there is no call. A text the founder released makes no call either: `agentScopeRelease.hash` equals `itemHash(t)` (sha256 of the title, a blank line and `editedBody ?? body`), so an edit after the release is checked again. Otherwise `scopeCheck("output", …, {failClosed: true})` runs with the item's agent (`agentForTask(t.type)`), the brand's name and sector and the owner's other brands (`where` is `publish` or `chat_publish`, `itemId` the task's id, the route's `deadline`). `enforce`: a refusal answers 409 `agent_scope_held` with `message` (the refusal in the item's language; chat: `Not sent: ` and the refusal), a classifier error 503 `agent_scope_unavailable` with the retry sentence (the Inbox shows `message` for both, not the unknown-result notice). `shadow`: the line is logged and the item publishes. Chat `publish_item` sends only a copy whose `itemHash` still equals the checked text (an approved item can be edited and stay approved), else `Not published: the owner edited this item a moment ago.`; the publish route has the same hold through `taskVersion`. The founder releases one held item with `POST /api/admin/agent-scope/release {email, brandId?, itemId}` (founder only; the brand must be one of the account's own; one `admin:log` row); the owner still presses Publish. `Task.agentScopeRelease` is a new optional field (rule 679 expand: old code ignores it).
7. Switches and the test set (rules 1412, 1413; 2026-10-12). Founder console, Quality section, under the golden set: `AgentScopePanel` (`src/components/admin/AgentScopePanel.tsx`) reads `GET /api/admin/agent-scope?day=` (the modes, the last eval, the day's newest 500 log lines, this month's classifier calls and dollars from the global meter) and offers four selects (`POST /api/admin/agent-scope/modes`; since rules 1500-1503 each value is named in the founder's language, on «مفعّلة» / On, off «متوقفة» / Off, shadow «مراقبة فقط» / Watch only, enforce «ترفض فعلاً» / Refuse, with a line under each select saying what the stored value does; the stored values stay on/off/shadow/enforce and the defaults stay `AGENT_SCOPE_DEFAULT_MODES`; picking a value saves nothing and opens the console's reason form (`useAdminForm`, rule 1176) with "from → to" and what the new value does, Cancel or Escape puts the select back, Apply posts the one field with its reason, which the route checks (400 `reason_short` / `reason_long`) and writes into the `admin:log` row), a Run button with a confirm that names the paid calls (`POST /api/admin/agent-scope/eval`), a day picker, and on log lines of `publish` or `chat_publish` with an `itemId` a Release button (item 6). The set is `src/lib/agent-scope-set.json`, written by hand (invented brands, Arabic and English, every agent, owner requests and agent texts, greetings and help, code requests, injected instructions); `scoreAgentScope` passes at 95% or more of refusal-labelled cases refused (any refusal verdict) and 2% or fewer of `in` cases refused, a case with no answer counting as not refused. `input` and `output` reach `enforce` only after a passed eval on the model the classifier uses now (409 `eval_not_passed`), so changing the model in `route("agentScope")` needs a new run first. Rollout (spec section 11): Part B ships with prompt on and the other three layers in shadow; after the founder approves the merge and the deploy, two weeks in shadow while the founder reads the log and runs the set (on staging first); then the founder switches input and output to enforce from the panel; roles follow once the shadow log shows the refusals are acceptable.

## 60. Agent instructions (agent skills portal) (2026-10-10)

Spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-10-agent-skills-portal-design.md` (the code is the truth where it differs: the edit counter in item 1, the plan gate on the Google import in item 8, the activity rows of the test in item 5). Rules 930-959, routes in `permissions.md` rows 190-203. Files: `src/lib/instructions-safety.ts` (caps, plan table, `normalizeInstruction`, `screenInstruction`), `src/lib/instructions-store.ts` (layers, versions, history, feed, pause), `src/lib/agent-instructions.ts` (`loadLayers`, `instructionBlocks`, the one place stored instructions become prompt text), `src/lib/instructions-diff.ts`, `src/lib/instructions-http.ts`, `src/lib/instructions-test.ts`, `src/lib/instruction-proposals.ts`, `src/lib/instruction-refs.ts`, `src/lib/skills-admin.ts`, `src/app/api/instructions/*`, `src/app/api/admin/skills/route.ts`, `src/app/[lang]/app/instructions/page.tsx`, `src/app/[lang]/admin/skills/page.tsx`. Two layers feed an agent's prompt: the **platform** layer (the founder, trusted, in Thakeen's voice) and one **workspace** layer per brand (the owner, untrusted, fenced as data). Instructions are prompt text only: no route, gate, tool list, price or approval path reads them (item 11).

1. Write a draft. `PUT /api/instructions {items, base?}` (role `instructions.write`, owner or admin member; 60 writes an hour per workspace, shared with activate, discard, restore and a proposal's answer). `saveDraft` runs under `skillslock:{scope}` (`db.lockOwned`, 10 s, up to 40 tries 50 ms apart, else 409 `busy`), normalizes every item (control characters, bidi and zero-width characters dropped, whitespace collapsed), refuses what is over 600 characters (platform 2000), over 120 stored items (platform 100), over 3 reference files, a target that is not one of the eight agents or `media` (`reviewer` and `judge` only on the platform layer; `lock` only on the platform) and a text the screen refuses (`injection`, `authority`, `secret`; Arabic and English), with the index of the first refused row; an empty row is dropped. The whole draft is replaced; the live set is untouched. An item keeps its source and edit time when its id is the same and nothing in it changed. Stale-save check (rule 954): every write of the layer (a draft save, a discard, an activation, a restore, a chat apply) moves its edit counter `seq`; the page sends the counter it loaded (`editBase`: `seq`, or for a layer stored before the counter the draft's version, the live set's with no draft) as `base`, and a different stored value is 409 `stale` with nothing saved, so two tabs, or a tab and a chat apply, cannot overwrite one another. A PUT without `base` is still accepted (expand step); the founder's route requires it. Live versions stay the plain numbering of the history (v1, v2, ...); the counter is separate.
2. Activate, discard, history, compare, restore. `POST /api/instructions/activate` makes the draft the next live version (`before.v + 1`) under the lock, checks the stored draft again against today's caps and the plan (`checkLimits`, counted on the items that are on: `instructions_limit`, `per_agent_plan` on a plan without per-agent targets, `refs_plan` on a plan without reference files; all 409, also `no_draft` when a second activation finds nothing) and writes the layer, one `Revision {id, v, at, by, before, after, rollbackOf?}` pushed to `skillshist:{scope}` (cap 50; the platform's 100), an owner activity row (`instructions_changed`, count of items on, note `v<n>`) and a row in the founder feed `skills:feed` (cap 500: time, scope, account email, action `activate|restore|apply`, version, count; never an instruction's text) in one `Promise.all`; a failed feed write is logged and never undoes the change. `POST /api/instructions/discard` drops the draft. `GET /api/instructions/history` lists the newest versions; `?compare=<revisionId>` is matched against this workspace's own list and answers `diffSets(revision.after, live set)` (by id: added, removed, changed with old and new text; an on/off flip is a change, a reordered agent list is not). `POST /api/instructions/restore {revisionId}` looks the id up only in `skillshist:{sc(u)}` (404 otherwise), puts that version's items back as a new version with `rollbackOf` through today's checks (a limit or `busy` is 409), keeps a draft in progress, and writes the same activity and feed rows with action `restore`. Nothing is ever deleted from the history. Every `by` goes out through `publicBy`: a member never reads who impersonated.
3. Render: what reaches each prompt. `loadLayers(scope, limits, which, names)` reads `skills:platform`, `skills:paused` and `skills:{scope}` in one `mget`, then the reference files of the applied items that reach `names` in a second `mget` (`names` are the targets the caller renders for; with none no file is read). `instructionBlocks(layers, target, also?)` is pure and returns the platform block (`THAKEEN STANDARDS`, one line per item for the target, `[locked]` items first), the workspace block (`OWNER INSTRUCTIONS`, `{for_all_agents, for_you}` as `asData` JSON inside `<owner_instructions>`, then the how-to-use lines that put the owner's instruction below `RULES`, `BRAND RULES`, a `[locked]` standard, approval, publishing, credits, the brand file and the tool list, and optionally `<reference_files>`) and the stamp hashes `plat` and `ins`. Where each lands: batch generation (`systemPrompt`, `generate-batch.ts` loads the layers in its existing `Promise.all` with `names=[agentForTask(type).id]`): platform text appended to block 1 after `fixedPrompt(agent)`, so block 1 stays identical across brands; workspace text in block 2 after the industry playbook and before the workspace files, followed by the line that an explicit owner instruction beats a learned preference. Chat (`system()` in `chat.ts`, all eight agents): platform in block 1; workspace in block 2 after the brand file and `OWNER PREFERENCES`, before the memory files, with the same line. Reviewer and cross-review (`noor.ts`): the platform block for target `reviewer` appended to the system text; the workspace block for the agent whose drafts are checked (`also`) goes in the user message as data followed by the line that an item following an owner instruction is not corrected for it, so the cached system text is the same for every brand. Judge (`judge.ts`): the `judge` platform block in the system text, the owner's instructions in the user message as data with the line that following one is not marked down (the judge can only move an auto-approved item back to pending, never the other way). Image and video prompt writer (`media/prompts.ts`, layers loaded in `media/start.ts` with `names=["media", the item's agent]`): the `media` platform block in the system text, the items for `media`, `all` and the item's agent in the user message as data; the fixed template used when the model is off gets none. Golden set (`golden.ts`): `loadLayers(null, null)`, the platform layer only, no workspace. Not covered: Reem's meeting extraction, onboarding and the free tools (stated on the customer page). Caps at render: at most 15,000 characters of instruction text per prompt (platform first, then the workspace, cut in list order), 8,000 characters of reference text (each file cut at 4,000; one that does not fit what is left is skipped, never a fragment). Render checks again what the store checked: text normalized and screened, a workspace item naming the reviewer or the judge dropped, a workspace `lock` ignored, and a refused item takes no slot of the plan limit. Pause: while `skills:paused` is `1`, `instructionBlocks` renders no workspace block anywhere; the platform block is unchanged. Plan limits at render (an account that moves down keeps its set): only the first `max` items that are on and clean, in list order, apply; per-agent items are skipped on a plan without them; references are skipped where the plan has none; the page says how many are not applied (`skippedCount`). A workspace scope with no limits applies nothing (fail closed). Prompt stamp: `Task.pb` gains `plat` and `ins` (hash of what the agent received, `none` when empty); golden's `promptChangeOf` treats a different `plat` as a prompt change and never compares `ins`; old tasks without them read as before.
4. Hard safety. Every write and every render calls the same functions: `normalizeInstruction` then `screenInstruction` (refused with a named reason, never silently changed). `injection`: "ignore the instructions", "system prompt", "you are now", a tag, a code fence, and the Arabic equivalents. `authority`: publish, send or approve without the owner, auto-approve, spend or move credits, change the plan, edit the brand file, call a tool, a discount or price not in the brand file. `secret`: reveal or repeat the prompt, keys or another customer's data. A protective rule ("never publish without my approval") passes. The screen is defence in depth: the real protection is that no gate reads an instruction (item 11) and that workspace text is fenced as data. Known limits of the screen (pinned by the fence, not by the regexes): a negation within three words can let a rewording such as "do not hesitate to publish without asking" pass; some invisible code points (U+2061-2064, U+034F, variation selectors) are not stripped; close variants such as "just publish it" are not matched.
5. Test this instruction. `POST /api/instructions/test {agent, type, brief, confirm: true, nonce}` (role `instructions.test`; 10 an hour and 30 a day per workspace, counted before the body is read; a caller refused by role is not counted). The price is `2 x creditsFor(countCost(type, 1), model, "generate", type)` with the model `generateRouteFor` picks for the brand and type (`testQuote` in `instructions-test.ts`; the customer page prints the same number on the button, so the two cannot disagree). Order: 401; 403; the two rate buckets (429 with `Retry-After`); the body (400 `confirm_required` unless `confirm` is the literal `true`, `bad_type` when the type is not a `draft_items` type or its owner is not `agent`, Reem never, `brief_too_long` over 500 characters, `bad_nonce`, `no_draft`); the founder's pause (`skills:paused`) is 409 `paused` and a draft equal to the live set (same items in the same order by text, targets, switch, lock and files; ids and times ignored) is 409 `no_change`, both before anything is claimed or charged and with no activity row (with the pause on both generations would run without the workspace layer, and an equal draft writes the same item twice; the page disables Test with a hint in both cases); a paused model is 503 before anything is claimed; `no_brand` 400; `trial_over` 402; `model_unavailable` 503. Then the nonce claim `db.setNx("skilltest:{scope}:{nonce}", 600 s)` (a second request with it is 409 `duplicate`, in sequence or at the same instant), then `charge(email, cost, {scope, agent, cap})` once (not `chargeOnce`, whose holds stay on the user for 8 days); no credits or the agent's monthly cap is 402 and releases the claim so the same click may be sent again after a top-up. Two `generate()` calls with `count: 1` in `Promise.all`, one with the live set and one with the draft set (the reference files load for both), the same model, the reviewer as governance sets it, cross-review and judge off, metered to the account; the brief is only ever the owner's-notes argument of `generate` (the user message), never system text; nothing goes to the inbox and nothing is learned. The answer is `{before, after, spent}`. Any throw after the charge calls `refund(email, cost, receipt)` (which also lowers the agent's counter) and answers 502 `generation_failed` (503 `model_paused`); the model's error text is never sent; a failed refund is 500 `refund_failed` and the log carries the amount, the agent, the month and a hash of the scope, never the email. A failed test keeps its claim (a retry is a new click with a new nonce). Activity rows (spec change): a successful test is one `instructions_tested` row (`credits` = what was taken, `count` 2), and a refusal at the charge is a `no_credits` or `cap_reached` row with the price the test would have had, as `generateBatch` writes them; a refunded failure leaves none.
6. Chat proposal. The tool `propose_instruction {text, agents?}` is offered to all eight agents, free. Its handler is a code gate: refused for an MCP call and for a token actor (what another assistant says is not the owner's rule), at most 2 cards a reply (`INSTRUCTION_CARDS_PER_REPLY`), at most 20 stored proposals a day per workspace (`rate:instr:p:{scope}`, a refused text costs none of it). `proposeInstruction` normalizes and screens the text exactly as a saved one (a refused text makes no card; the tool answer states the reason for the model to relay), accepts `agents` only as `"all"` or a list of the eight agents (stored as `"all"` below a plan with per-agent targets, and the tool says so), and stores `skillprop:{scope}:{id}` (16 hex, 7 days) plus the id in `skillprops:{scope}`. The reply carries the card ("Add to your agent instructions": text, Apply, Dismiss; `ChatMsg.instructionProposals`, optional, the card without `by`), whose state (open, applied, dismissed, expired) is read when the thread is loaded. Apply and Dismiss are `POST /api/instructions/proposals/{id} {decision}` (role `instructions.write`, the writes bucket). `decideInstructionProposal(sc(u), id, ...)` takes `skillprop-claim:` (`setNx`, 60 s; a parallel answer is 409 `claim_busy`), reads the proposal only from the caller's scope (404 `not_found`), answers 410 `gone` when `skillprop-done:` already holds an outcome, and for Apply runs `appendActive` under the workspace lock: the item (id = the first 12 hex of the proposal's, so a retry after a failed outcome write finds its item and adds nothing twice; `src: "chat"`) joins the live set as the next version and the draft in progress too (so the next Activate keeps it), through today's text, count (120) and plan checks; a refusal is 409 with its reason and leaves the proposal open. The outcome record is written before the proposal is deleted. The activity row and the feed row carry action `apply`, `via: chat`.
7. Where the customer works. `/{lang}/app/instructions` (sidebar row for every role; read-only for a member who cannot `instructions.write`) shows the draft or the live set, the plan's limits and the "N are not applied" note, the history with compare and restore (Restore is disabled while the form has unsaved edits, and the form resets to the server's layer after every write), and the test with its price and a confirm dialog (the app's own dialog); the page never reads the platform layer.
8. Google Docs and Sheets as reference files (rules 955-956). Off until the founder sets `COMPOSIO_AUTH_GOOGLEDOCS`, `COMPOSIO_AUTH_GOOGLESHEETS` and `google_workspace` in `COMPOSIO_PROVIDERS` (`variables.md`); until then `refsAvailable` is false, the page hides Import and the routes answer 409 `not_connected`. Read only by construction: the catalog lists for `googledocs` the tools `GOOGLEDOCS_SEARCH_DOCUMENTS` and `GOOGLEDOCS_GET_DOCUMENT_PLAINTEXT` and for `googlesheets` `GOOGLESHEETS_SEARCH_SPREADSHEETS` and `GOOGLESHEETS_VALUES_GET`, `write: {}` for both. Plan gate (spec change): `POST /api/instructions/refs/search` and `refs/import` need `instructions.write` and a plan with reference files (403 `refs_plan` on trial), checked before the rate limit and the body; 30 searches and 30 imports an hour per workspace in two buckets. The owner connects Google Workspace on the Integrations page (the Composio connect flow), searches by name (at most 10 results, `{id, title, kind, modified?}`; an id that is not `[A-Za-z0-9_-]{10,100}` is dropped), picks one and presses Import. The connection read is the workspace's own (`getConnection(sc(u), "google_workspace")`); the query and the document id are tool arguments and in no key. A Doc is imported as plain text with tables, a Sheet as CSV of `A1:Z200` (each cell through `csvSafe`, whole rows only), at most 20,000 characters, written with `writeFile(sc(u), "ref-<slug>.md", ..., "user")` and recorded in the hash `skillrefs:{scope}` (`{source: "gdocs"|"gsheets", id, title, at}`). The same source id always lands in its own file: pressing Refresh is an explicit overwrite of the imported file (edits the owner made to it are replaced); when the recorded file is gone the record is dropped and the import is named again; another source with the same slug gets `-2`, `-3`; a different source never takes a name already in use, and a Refresh overwrites whatever file is at the name recorded for that source (an owner's own file written there after deleting the imported one included); 409 `files_full` at the Files page's limit. `GET /api/instructions/refs` lists the records (`exists` says whether the file is still there) and `available`. The imported text is untrusted: it is a workspace file like any other and reaches a model only inside `<reference_files>` as `asData`, for the agents the instruction targets (item 3).
9. Founder console. `/{lang}/admin/skills` and `GET|PUT|POST /api/admin/skills` authenticate with one `adminActor(req)` call first (the console key session or the founder signed in; 401 otherwise, so a customer session never reaches it). The layer written is the constant `PLATFORM`, never a request value; who wrote it comes from the session. PUT needs `base` (400 `bad_base`, 409 `stale`) and allows the eight agents, `media`, `reviewer`, `judge`, the `lock` flag and 2000 characters, with the same screens. POST `{action}`: `activate`, `discard`, `restore {revisionId}`, `pause {on}` and `test {agent, type, brief?}`. Reading a customer's set: the feed lists changes (account, brand name or id, action, version, count; no text); `?workspace=<scope>` is 404 unless the scope string is one the server's own `listFeed()` holds, and then the key is built from that row, answering the live set read only (no draft, no `by.imp`). Pause: the switch asks through the app's dialog, `setPaused` stores `skills:paused = "1"` (or deletes it); the client returns a stored `"1"` as the number 1, so every read compares as text. Admin test: the same two generations on the fictional `SAMPLE_BRAND` with the platform layer only (live set against draft), no `charge` (the route imports none), metered under the fixed label `skills-test@nashr.internal`, 30 an hour on one constant bucket `admin:skills:test`. Every founder write goes to `admin:log` through `adminAudit` (section `quality`, target `platform-skills`, rule 1172) with `what: skills.<action>` and small before and after values (versions and counts, never text). Platform writes add no activity row and no feed row.
10. Account deletion. `wipeAccount` removes, for every brand scope of the account, `skills:{scope}`, `skillshist:{scope}`, `skillslock:{scope}`, `skillrefs:{scope}` (`instructionKeys`) and every chat proposal key (`skillprop:`, `skillprop-done:`, `skillprop-claim:`, the index `skillprops:{scope}` and the day counter `rate:instr:p:{scope}`, `instructionProposalKeys`); the imported `ref-*.md` files go with the other workspace files; then `removeFeedRowsFor(email)` takes the account's rows out of `skills:feed` under the feed's own lock (each row by its exact text; a lock that stays taken stops the wipe and a retry runs it again). `skilltest:{scope}:{nonce}` claims expire by themselves in 10 minutes.
11. Not a gate (rule 951). The code that approves, publishes, charges credits or applies governance caps (`publish/[id]`, `tasks/[id]`, `secretary/meeting/[id]/approve`, `publish-once.ts`, `connectors/publish.ts`, `brand-rules.ts`, `decisions.ts`, `task-version.ts`, `secretary-meeting.ts`, `credits.ts`, `governance.ts`, `roles.ts`, `mcp.ts`, and the spend and brand-change code: `media/jobs.ts`, `x-stats.ts`, `brand-proposals.ts`, `api/brand/route.ts`, `api/brand/proposals/[id]/route.ts`, `chat-allowance.ts`, `chat-budget.ts`, `schedule.ts`) imports none of `agent-instructions`, `instructions-store`, `instructions-safety`, `instruction-proposals`, `instruction-refs`, `instructions-http`; `tests/instructions-no-gate.test.ts` scans them. The prompt modules that do import them (`noor.ts`, `chat.ts`, `judge.ts`, `golden.ts`, `generate-batch.ts`, `media/*`) only build prompts: `generate-batch.ts`, `media/start.ts`, `noor.ts`, `judge.ts` and `golden.ts` import `agent-instructions` and nothing else of the set, and the test pins that (accepted deviation from spec 6 item 5: a spend path imports the renderer for prompt text only, never to decide).
12. Known gaps, accepted for this release. (a) The golden set's prompt-change stamp (`plat`) is the platform text the batch writer agent receives; an edit that changes only the `reviewer` or `judge` platform block does not move it, so the golden compare does not flag that edit as a prompt change. (b) The platform `lock` is enforced by prompt order and wording only (`THAKEEN STANDARDS`, `[locked]` first, "both win over a standard not marked [locked]"), so a customer instruction may still sway style against a locked standard; the hard limits (approval, publish, credits, brand file) are code. (c) Imports take no lock: two different sources whose titles make the same slug, imported at the same instant, can both pick the same free name; the second overwrites the first file and its record (the record is one field per file name), so the first source is not listed any more and importing it again gives it a new name. (d) The Composio response shapes for the four Google tools were read from `composio execute <slug> --get-schema` and are not verified against a real login until the founder connects one; the parsers read several key names (`findKey`) and an unreadable answer is `upstream` or `empty`, never a crash. (e) The Integrations card for Google Workspace is English-only. (f) A concurrent propose can leave an index entry the wipe misses for up to 7 days (the proposal's TTL).

## 61. Model cost metering: every call Anthropic bills (2026-10-10)

Rules 980-986, `docs/system/tests/0980-meter-billed-gaps.md`. Files: `src/lib/metered.ts` (the wrapper), `src/lib/usage.ts` (`meter`, `meterBilled`, `billedParts`), every caller of the Messages API (`chat.ts`, `noor.ts` generate/critique/crossReview, `onboard.ts`, `judge.ts`, `media/prompts.ts`, `secretary-meeting.ts`, `api/tools/[tool]/route.ts`). Numbered 61 so it does not collide with sections 54-60 on open branches. Why: the Anthropic Console (2026-09-11..10-10) billed more than the founder console showed at the same rates; the gap was calls `meter()` never saw.

1. Every call is a stream (rule 980). No file in `src` calls `messages.create`; each `messages.stream(...)` is passed to `metered(stream, {email, kind, agent?, requested?})`, and only `metered.ts` awaits `finalMessage()`. Calls that relied on the SDK `timeout` alone (judge, cross-review, image prompts) now also carry an `until()` signal: a stream's `timeout` covers only the wait for the response headers, the signal bounds the whole body.
2. A reply is metered as billed (rule 981). `meterBilled` reads `usage.iterations` when the reply has it (a server-side fallback: `fallbacks: "default"` in `generate`): each iteration at its own model (`message` with no model = the requested model, `fallback_message`/`advisor_message` = theirs, `compaction` = the requested model), summed per model. Top-level usage is ignored then (it covers only the serving attempt). The account, kind, agent and provider counters count ONE call; each model's `_all` counter counts its own attempt; USD is the sum at each model's rate. Without iterations it is `meter()` exactly as before.
3. A stream that fails after its first event is metered from its snapshot (rule 982): `stream.currentMessage`, or the last of `receivedMessages` when the SDK had completed a message and cleared the snapshot. Input and cache counts are exact (`message_start` carries them). The output count is raised to an estimate of what had streamed (rule 983: text, thinking and tool input; about 4 ASCII or 2 other characters a token; a lower bound, hidden thinking is billed and not seen). The same error is rethrown, so refunds, fallbacks and "out of time" are unchanged.
4. A failure before the first event (4xx, 429, 5xx, connection error, timeout before headers) meters nothing (rule 984). Those are the only attempts the SDK's `maxRetries` repeats; it never retries a started stream, so a retry cannot hide a metered attempt.
5. Chat (rule 985): a turn cut by the request's deadline is metered as `chat` for its agent with what it had streamed, and the owner still gets `OUT_OF_TIME_REPLY`. The turn's `spent` ceiling counts only completed calls, as before.
6. The judge (rule 986): bounded by `MODEL_BUDGET.JUDGE_MS` through a signal; the SDK `timeout` stays as the header bound.
7. Nothing stored changes shape: the same `usage:` counters get more increments. Release step: none (rule 679 not involved). Months before the release keep the gap; compare the Anthropic Console with the founder console again a week after it is live.

## 62. Monitoring: uptime, the store, the crons, and CI parity (2026-10-11)

Rules 1011-1019. Nothing watched production before (360 study F-004, F-017, B-001): `/api/health` said the process was up and never whether Redis answered, the eight cron entries live in hPanel and QStash outside the repository, and CI built with Turbopack on Node 24 while thakeen.com builds with webpack on Node 22.

1. The shallow check. `GET /api/health` is unchanged: `{ ok, commit, built }`, public, 60 a minute per IP (rule 676). The release probe and the smoke script keep using it.
2. The store check. `GET /api/health?store=1` is public and adds `store: { ok, ms }` from one `GET` of a key nobody writes. It is 503 with `ok: false` when the read fails or takes over 2 s. It never names the key, the host or the error (rule 1011).
3. The cron check. `GET /api/health?crons=1` with `Authorization: Bearer ${CRON_SECRET}` adds `crons: { stale, waiting, jobs }`. 401 without the bearer and when `CRON_SECRET` is unset; 503 when the store is down or a job is late (rules 1012-1013). A job with no heartbeat yet is `waiting`, not late, so a fresh deploy or an emptied store raises no alarm; the first run of each job (a day, a week for `weekly` and `golden`) fills it in.
4. The heartbeats. Each cron route writes `cronbeat:{job}` when it finishes (rule 1014); `GET /api/admin/overview` carries them as `crons` and the founder's Usage page shows each job with its last run and "late" in red (rule 1015). The self-check is these writes read by 3 and by the page: no new cron entry, so hPanel and QStash need no new schedule. What it does not do yet: send an email when a job is late. If the host stops calling every cron, the page and the check say so, but nobody is told unless the external monitor in 5 uses the bearer form.
5. The external uptime monitor (founder steps; the code holds no vendor, key or address, rule 1016; no account was created by Claude):
   - Pick any free uptime service that checks a URL every 5 minutes, accepts a keyword and emails an address. UptimeRobot and Better Stack are examples, not a recommendation: check that the current free plan has a 5-minute interval, keyword checks and email alerts. The choice and the sign-up are the founder's.
   - Monitor 1 (works on any free tier): URL `https://thakeen.com/api/health?store=1`, every 5 minutes, "up" only on HTTP 200 and the text `"ok":true`. Alert email: hello@thakeen.com. It goes down when the site is down, Redis does not answer within 2 s, or the 60-a-minute limit is hit by someone else (rare at one call per 5 minutes).
   - Monitor 2 (needs a service that can send a request header, a paid tier on some): URL `https://thakeen.com/api/health?crons=1`, header `Authorization: Bearer <the live CRON_SECRET>`, the same keyword and interval. The secret is typed into the service's own header field by the founder, never pasted into chat or into the repository. Without header support, the Usage page is the cron check.
   - Optional: add the same two monitors for `https://staging.thakeen.com/api/health?store=1` only if the staging cookie can be sent; otherwise skip staging (the lock answers 404).
   - Test it once with the service's test-alert button, then read the email at hello@thakeen.com.
6. CI parity (rule 1017). `.github/workflows/ci.yml` uses Node 22 and runs `npm run build:hostinger` (`next build --webpack`) in the existing Build step; `playwright.config.ts` builds its server with the same script. No job, step or matrix was added, so a push costs the same minutes; the webpack build is slower than Turbopack's, a minute or two on a cold cache (read it off the first run). The Vercel project's own build (Turbopack, being retired) is not changed.
6a. Both person shapes (rules 2510-2511, 2026-10-20). Since multi-workspace M1 the test helper makes `p_` persons by default (section 93 item 14), the shape M2 will sign up, while every production login keeps the legacy shape until M2. On a ready pull request (`steps.scope.outputs.e2e`) CI therefore runs the unit tests twice: the usual step, then "Unit tests (production person shape)" with `TEST_PERSONS=legacy`, before the end-to-end suite. A draft push runs the first pass only, as before. The job's cap went from 35 to 40 minutes (rule 2511, raising rule 1211) for the about 7 minutes the second pass adds. Found by the integration check of 2026-10-20, which ran the whole suite in both shapes by hand.
   - Build once (rule 1207, 2026-10-12). The first CI runs measured the cost: the Build step went from 15 s to 1 m 46 s cold, and the end-to-end server built a second time before `next start` (60 s to the first test, was 19 s), so a run reached about 24.5 of the 25-minute `timeout-minutes` and one was cancelled. Now, under `CI` and with `.next/BUILD_ID` present, the server only runs `next start` on the Build step's `.next` (`webServerCommand` in `e2e/web-server.ts`); without `CI`, or without a build, it still builds first, so a local run never tests a stale build. The build is made without `APP_ENV`, like thakeen.com's, and the server gets `APP_ENV=test` at runtime: every reader of `APP_ENV` (`isProductionDeploy`, `isStaging`, `devCodeExposed`) runs at call time, and the only build-time readers, `robots.ts` and `sitemap.ts`, ask `isStaging()`, which is false both unset and under `test` (rule 1209). A local `next build --webpack` with `APP_ENV` unset and one with `APP_ENV=test` gave the same 127 prerendered routes and the same 734 static files, byte for byte once the build id is set aside (`docs/system/tests/1207-ci-build-once.md`). The timeout stayed 25 minutes until rule 1211 (2026-10-12) raised it to 35: with one build a passing run still took about 24 minutes (unit tests about 7, the suite about 14), and PR #331's run was cancelled at 25 minutes with 1,042 suite tests passed and a handful left. A run that passes costs the same; only a hung run can now cost up to 10 more minutes.
   - The Next build cache (rule 1208). It is restored before the Build step and saved by its own step straight after it (`actions/cache/restore` and `actions/cache/save`), no longer by the combined `actions/cache` step, whose save runs after the job and only when the whole job passed: both measured runs (one red, one cancelled) threw their cache away, so every push of that PR built cold. A cache saved by a pull request serves only that PR's later runs; a base branch's cache would serve every PR, but main runs no CI (rule 988), and a push run on main only to seed the cache would cost a full install and build per merge to save about a minute once per PR, so there is none. restore-keys fall back to the same lockfile, then to any Next cache on the runner's OS.
   - Green reuse and affected unit tests (rules 2580-2586, 2026-10-20, `docs/system/tests/2580-ci-reuse.md`). A run that passed everything (Build, Type-check, Lint, every unit test and the end-to-end suite, so a ready PR) records its merge tree in the Actions cache under `ci-green-v1-{OS}-{tree}-{hash of ci.yml and scripts/ci-affected.mjs}` (rule 2582). A later run of the same PR whose checked-out tree has that exact key, and whose record names the same tree, skips Node, the install, the build, the checks and the suite and passes in about a minute; the secret scan still runs (rules 2580-2581). It fires on a re-run, `gh pr ready` on a tree that already passed, and the "merge main right before the merge" push when main has not moved; any changed file gives a new tree and a full run. On a draft PR into `main`, Unit tests runs only the test files whose import graph, path mentions or folder reads reach a changed file (`scripts/ci-affected.mjs`, rules 2583 and 2585); a ready PR, the release PR into `production`, an empty diff, a shared file (the install, the vitest and TypeScript config, `tests/helpers/`, `tests/fixtures/`, `src/lib/db.ts`, `src/lib/keys.ts`, CI itself, rule 2584) or `content/` runs every test. Type-check and lint stay whole on drafts. The end-to-end trigger (ready PRs only, rule 772) and the deploy path are unchanged.
7. Not covered. Backups of the Upstash store (F-005), the DMARC and certificate findings, and the daytime rebuild question are separate findings. The Hostinger 300 s route limit is still unmeasured (B-004); the heartbeat would show a `daily` that is killed mid-run as late, not why.

## 63. Settings, notification preferences and the billing return (UI/UX phase 3, 2026-10-11, merged in #329)

Branch `claude/uiux-phase3` (rules 1140-1158, `docs/system/tests/1140-uiux-phase3.md`). Numbered 63 because the monitoring change took 62 on main first.

1.
