Old links. `src/proxy.ts` answers `/{lang}/app/{profile,team,brands,billing,onboarding,integrations,governance}` (the exact page path, with or without a trailing slash) with a 307 to `/{lang}/app/settings/{section}`, keeping the query string, so a Paymob return (`?paymob=return`), an integration error, an email already sent, a bell entry already stored and a bookmark all land. 307, not 308: no browser caches the move for good, and a rollback finds the old pages again. The proxy imports only the pure map (`src/lib/settings-nav.ts`, a type-only import), never the store (`tests/staging.test.ts`).
2. Notification preferences, expand step (rule 679). The record stays `profile:{scope}` (the brand's; the account's `profile:{account}`, `acct(u)`, when the brand's says nothing: the settings page, the evening wrap and Reem's reminders all pass the account id, rule 3032). `PUT /api/notifications/prefs` writes the new `notify` (v1) and the old `emails` level derived from it (`legacyEmails`: "none" when no email is on, "all" when every one is, else "important"), so code from before this release reads the nearest old level. Every reader (`readPrefs`, `readPrefsMany`) reads the new shape when present, else the old level with its old meaning (`prefsFromLevel`: "all" sends everything; "important" sends Noor's brief only when something waits and still sends reminders, never the evening wrap; "none" sends no email). Bell entries the person switched off are claimed (their fingerprint is set) but not written (`pushNotifs`); credits and trial notices always arrive. One behaviour moved: a brand record that exists but holds no choice now falls back to the account's choice in the brief and the evening wrap too (the reminders already did).
3. Notification preferences, contract step (a later release): run an idempotent script that writes `notify` from `emails` wherever it is missing, then drop the `emails` fallback in `prefsFrom` and stop writing `emails`. `PUT /api/profile` `emails` (the old form's field) is not used by the new pages; it goes in the same step.
4. Sign out other devices (`POST /api/account/sessions`, row 161). The wrong-password counter first (`allow` takes the hit before the compare, atomic so parallel tries cannot race it, rule 1183; 429 once 10 failures landed this hour), then the login's own password (a correct one gives its hit back with `giveBack`, so it never counts, rule 1170), then `rotateSession` (rule 1171): this device's fresh session is written, then the stamp `devicesAfter` (on the member record or the account, a stamp only `resolveSession` reads), then the old session record is deleted, so a failure at any step leaves this device signed in. The calendar feed, booking links, MCP tokens and connected apps stay: they follow `sessionsAfter`, which only a password change or reset moves (since 2026-10-11, UI/UX phase 4; phase 3 moved `sessionsAfter` and so ended the feed and booking links too). A member's sign-out never touches the owner's or another member's sessions. Legacy bare-string sessions (read as issued at 0) end too. Stored shape: `devicesAfter` is a new optional field on `user:` and `member:` (expand; a record without it signs nothing out, so no contract step). The list of signed-in devices waits for a session index (Phase 3b).
5. The Paymob return. `startCheckout` writes `paymob:last:{email}` (24 h) next to the intent. The return page (`/app/settings/billing?paymob=return`, reached through the redirect while the redirection URL still names `/app/billing`) polls `GET /api/billing/status` every 3 s for at most 20 tries; it stops on paid or failed and refreshes the page once on paid. It never applies a payment: the webhook does, as before. While Paymob is off (production today) the page shows no poll and the route answers `enabled: false`.
6. Contract steps for the settings move (a later release, after the expand release is live): change the two Paymob `redirectionUrl`s in `src/lib/billing.ts` (`startCheckout`'s return and the cron renewal's) to `/app/settings/billing`; change the server-side links still naming old pages (`src/lib/chat.ts` page list and billing link, `src/lib/notifications.ts` `href: "governance"` and the credits and trial links, `src/lib/lifecycle.ts` billing email link, `api/connect/[provider]/start` error redirect to `/app/integrations`); then drop entries from `LEGACY_SETTINGS` (keep `billing` and `profile` longest, since lifecycle and billing emails named them).

## 64. Founder console second factor (2026-10-12, branch only, rules 1370-1384)

360 study C-004 and leftovers L055: the console that changes money, plans, credits, impersonation and every tenant's agent instructions opened behind one key or one password. Code in `src/lib/admin-2fa.ts`, `src/lib/totp.ts`, `src/app/api/admin/2fa/route.ts`, `src/components/admin/SecondFactor.tsx`; tests in `tests/admin-2fa.test.ts`; coverage in `docs/system/tests/1370-admin-second-factor.md`.

1. Before enrolment. Nothing is enforced: every console write works on the key or the founder sign-in alone, exactly as before this change, so shipping it cannot lock the founder out. Every console page shows a banner with «إعداد التحقق بخطوتين» / "Set up two-step verification".
2. Setup. "Set up" posts `start`: a new 160-bit secret, sealed in `admin:2fa:pending` for 15 minutes and tied to the actor. The dialog shows a QR code of the `otpauth://` URI (item 10), then the secret in groups of four and the `otpauth://` link (opens the authenticator app on a phone) as the fallback. The founder types the app's current code; `confirm` checks it (rate limited, step burned), writes `admin:2fa` with the sealed secret and the recovery code's SHA-256, opens a step-up, logs `2fa.enrol`, and answers the recovery code, which the next dialog shows once («احفظ رمز الاسترداد الآن»). Closing it reloads the console without the banner.
3. After enrolment. Every founder write (permissions.md section 4 item 10a) answers 403 `second_factor` without a live step-up. `adminFetch` catches it, the prompt asks for the code, `verify` opens a step-up (30 idle minutes, 12 hours at most, tied to the actor and to the session cookie it was opened in), and the write is sent again by itself. Cancel leaves the 403 with the caller. Reads never ask.
4. Scripts on the `x-admin-key` header send the current 6-digit code in `x-admin-otp`; each code works once.
5. A lost phone. The prompt takes the recovery code too: it removes the enrolment (logged `2fa.recovery`), the console is back to step 1, and the founder sets up the new phone (a new secret and a new recovery code). A recovery code in `x-admin-otp` is refused.
6. A new phone while the old one still works: «استبدال تطبيق المصادقة» / "Replace the authenticator app" asks for a code first (a leaked key alone cannot swap the phone), then runs step 2.
7. Break glass, both lost: delete the `admin:2fa` key in the Upstash console (production database). The console is back to step 1.
8. `CONNECT_SECRET` seals the secret (variables.md). Rotating it makes the enrolled secret unreadable: writes are refused until the recovery code is used, then step 2 again. Production without `CONNECT_SECRET` answers `start` with 503 `no_secret_key` and enrols nothing.
9. Stored shapes: `admin:2fa`, `admin:2fa:pending`, `admin2fa:step:*` and `admin2fa:used:*` are new keys that no older code reads, so this is a single release with no contract step (rule 679).
10. QR code (2026-10-13, rules 1620-1624, `docs/system/tests/1620-admin-2fa-qr.md`). The setup dialog says «امسح الرمز بتطبيق المصادقة، أو أضف المفتاح يدويًا» / "Scan the code with your authenticator app, or add the key by hand" and draws `setup.uri` as a QR code (`src/components/admin/QrCode.tsx`, about 200 pixels, `role="img"` named «رمز QR لإعداد التحقق بخطوتين في تطبيق المصادقة» / "QR code to set up two-step verification in your authenticator app"). The code is computed in the browser by `qrPath` (`src/lib/qr.ts`, the `uqr` package, error correction M, a quiet zone of four modules) and drawn as SVG elements: no image service, no request, nothing stored or logged, no markup injected; the URI reaches the page only in the `start` answer's JSON body, as before. Dark modules on a white tile in both themes (the palette primitives `ink` and `paper`, which the dark theme does not re-point), because scanners read dark on light best. The grouped key and the `otpauth://` link stay under it for typing by hand. No route, permission, stored shape or environment variable changes.
## 65. The owner's own data: download and consent record (2026-10-12, rules 1320-1330, branch only)

Files: `src/app/api/account/export/route.ts`, `src/lib/privacy-export.ts`, `src/lib/consent.ts`, `src/lib/legal.ts`, `src/lib/help.ts`, `src/components/app/settings/DangerZone.tsx`, `src/lib/roles.ts`.

1. Download. Settings, Danger zone shows the owner a "Download my data" link (`<a href="/api/account/export" download>`); a member never sees the card, and a founder viewing the account sees a note instead of the link. `GET /api/account/export` runs `currentUser` (401 `auth`), `forbid(u, "account.export")` (owner only, in `IMPERSONATION_BLOCKED`), then `allow("accountexport:h:{email}", 3, 3600)` and `allow("accountexport:d:{email}", 10, 86400)` (429 `slow_down` with `Retry-After`), then `buildExport(u)`. Nothing is read from the request.
2. What it reads. Every key comes from the session's owner email: `listBrands(email)`, then per brand `scopeFor(email, id)` (as `wipeAccount` does); the brand that `sc(u)` names is marked `active`. Per scope one `Promise.all` reads `brand:`, `tasks:` (all ids), `files:`, `convs:`, `conns:` and the legacy `chat:{scope}` thread, then a second one `mget`s the task, file, conversation and connection records in chunks of 100 (`pmap`, 6 at a time) and reads each thread (`pmap`). No `await db.*` in a loop.
3. What it holds (`thakeen-export/1`, pretty JSON, `Content-Disposition: attachment; filename="thakeen-data-YYYY-MM-DD.json"`, `Cache-Control: no-store`): the account (email, name, phone, language, created, verified), the consent record (`consentOf`), plan and credits (plan, credits, trial end, renewal, seats, top-ups, the subscription's plan, status, paid-through, auto-renew and card brand and last four), the partner referral date and channel, and per brand its profile, tasks, files, conversations with their messages (role, text, time, agent; oldest first, up to `CAPS.chat` each) and connections (`connectionView`: provider, dates, `needsReconnect`, and `meta` without any key matching `SECRETISH`). Left out: the password hash, session stamps, the encrypted `enc` connection blob (never decrypted), MCP tokens and OAuth grants, the Paymob card token and transaction references, and internal locks and counters. JSON, not CSV, so `csvSafe` has no cell to guard.
4. Consent record, expand step (rule 679). `createUser` writes `User.consent` (section 1, step 5). The field is optional: accounts created before 2026-10-12 have none, and `consentOf` reads them as `{ recorded: false, note }` without inventing a date; older code ignores the field and `saveUser` keeps it. No data conversion runs, now or at build time: an old account has no record to convert. A contract step is not planned; if the lawyer asks for an explicit checkbox, its wording and a new `via` value come in a later change.
5. Versions. `PRIVACY_VERSION` and `TERMS_VERSION` are each document's own "last updated" day in `src/lib/legal.ts`; a change to either document changes the version new signups record.
6. Processor list (rule 1327). The privacy policy names the services the code calls, in Arabic and English, and the help answer "Who processes my data?" names the same ones except the model maker (rule 514: help never names it; it points to the policy): Hostinger (hosting), Upstash (data and timed jobs), Anthropic (text, inside the allowed "Anthropic for text" disclosure phrase), the Straitly gateway (off by default, may carry text-model requests to the text provider when switched on), fal.ai, kie.ai and Higgsfield (images and video), Vercel Blob (media storage), Resend (email), Composio (accounts connected through it), Cloudflare Turnstile (signup and password reset, when switched on) and Paymob (card payments, when online payment is offered). Vendor names stay out of marketing copy (rule 1328). The two new help answers ("How do I get a copy of my data?" and "Who processes my data?") carry `chat: false`, so `src/lib/chat.ts` leaves them out of the chat prompt (its size is pinned); the delete answer the prompt keeps names the download.

## 66. Security hardening: headers, pinned fetches, hashed sessions, MCP tokens (2026-10-12, branch only)

Branch `claude/security-hardening-a50eea` (rules 1340-1356, `docs/system/tests/1340-security-hardening.md`; 360 study C-006, C-007, C-013, C-014, C-015, C-016, C-018, F-019, J-004, leftovers L065, L066, L089). Numbered 66 because 64 and 65 are claimed by parallel branches. Customers see no change.

1. Headers (rules 1340-1341). `next.config.ts` drops `X-Powered-By` and adds `Content-Security-Policy-Report-Only` with the full policy; the enforced CSP stays the four directives of rule 428. The browser only reports what the full policy would block, in its console. Moving a directive into the enforced header is a later change, after the console is clean on every page with Turnstile on. Since section 90 (rules 2320-2329) the proxy sends both policies for pages and enforces the nonce-based script directive. `/.well-known/security.txt` is a static file.
2. Outside calls (rules 1342-1344). `safeFetch` checks the URL as before, then connects through `pinnedAgent()`, whose lookup refuses any answer with a private address, so DNS rebinding between the check and the connect fails. A call without its own signal gets 30 seconds. The connectors' own calls to fixed provider hosts (Salla, Zid, Meta, Meta Ads, Telegram, Brevo, Shopify, X and the OAuth token exchange) carry the same 30-second deadline, or their own shorter one where they had it.
3. Sessions, expand step (rules 1345-1346, rule 679). New sessions are stored under `session:h:{sha256(token)}`; reads try that key, then the raw key for a 64-hex token, so every session written before still works until its 30 days end. Sign-out, rotation, impersonation stop and the founder's restore go through `dropSession`/`sessionRecord`. Contract step (a later release, 30 days after this one is live): drop the raw read in `sessionKeys`; no data script is needed, the raw records expire.
4. MCP OAuth (rules 1347-1349). Access tokens live 1 hour (a refresh mints a new pair, as before); tokens issued before keep their 30 days. Authorising a client again replaces its earlier grant for this account. A registered client expires after 90 days without a code or token. Not changed here: the single `noor` scope, an Arabic consent page, revoking the grant when a used refresh token comes back (concurrent refreshes would break), and the Connected apps panel's silent revoke failure.
5. Impersonation and connector keys (rules 1350-1351). One impersonation per browser at a time (section 14, item 5). Connector records carry `kid`; rotating `CONNECT_SECRET` means setting the new value and `CONNECT_SECRET_PREVIOUS` to the old one, so every connection keeps working and is re-sealed on its next save. `CONNECT_SECRET` also signs booking links (`booking-token.ts`) and Composio state (`composio.ts`), which do not read the previous key: rotating it still ends open booking links and in-flight Composio connects.
6. Integration requests (rule 1353). `POST /api/integrations` takes an id from the catalogue (or `other:` with free text, cut to 80 characters), counts the vote under that id (or `other`), and allows 30 a login an hour.
7. Logs (rule 1354). A refused OAuth token exchange throws `{provider} token {status} {code}` with the RFC 6749 code only (the calendar sync still reads `invalid_grant`); the connect callback logs `errorTag(e)`: the error's name and an HTTP status.
8. CI (rule 1355). The "Secret scan" step of "Nashr CI" runs gitleaks 8.30.1 (checksummed download, no third-party action) over the pull request's commits before anything is installed; `.gitleaks.toml` is the default rules with the generic-key rule relaxed on tests, study reports and `.archify`. Every action in both workflows is pinned to a commit SHA.

## 67. Small decided fixes (2026-10-12, branch only, rules 1470-1486; numbered 64 on its branch, renumbered in the 2026-10-12 build batch: 64-66 were taken)

1. Partner programme, Saudi Arabia only (rules 1470, 1471). See section on partners, step 1: the application form asks where the applicant lives and refuses another country before sending; `POST /api/affiliates` refuses `country_closed` and stores `country`; `PUT /api/partner` refuses a residence outside `PARTNER_COUNTRIES`; the partner page offers only open countries; the terms (version 2026-10-12) and the public page say so. Partners already saved with another country keep being paid and flagged (`taxFlags`).
2. Currency in task specs (rules 1472, 1473). `taskSpec(type, brand)` fills `{CURRENCY}` from the brand's zone (`currencyOf`), at generation, cross review and the judge; without a brand it is SAR, so the prompt stamp's spec text is unchanged.
3. Stalled sign-ups (rules 1474, 1475). The founder overview lists accounts at least 24 hours old, not suspended, with no brand file, newest first (50 shown, the count beside), each with where they stopped: email not verified, or verified with no brand file.
4. Provider faults (rules 1476-1478). When a chat turn, a generation or the onboarding run fails because Anthropic or the gateway rate-limits (429, `rate_limit_error`), is overloaded (529, `overloaded_error`) or the balance is spent (402, `billing_error`, a 4xx whose text names the credit balance), the customer gets `model_busy` or `model_unavailable` with its own Arabic or English message, never the provider's name or text; the charge is refunded as before. The onboarding stream no longer sends the error's own message at all (`failed` otherwise). The founder gets one audit row per fault and place an hour (`provider_{fault}`, section system). Media renders already classify their providers' errors (`ProviderError`, the breaker), unchanged.
5. Approvals per person (rules 1479, 1480). The team page shows, for the owner and admins, each person's approvals (publishes included), rejections, edits and approval rate over 30 days, from the decision records across every brand.
6. Markdown tables (rule 1481). A row without its closing pipe keeps its last cell.
7. Blog drafts (rule 1482, issue #144). WordPress and Shopify take the first H1 as the title wherever it sits and remove that line from the body.
8. Large workspace (rules 1483, 1484, D-019). When the first call had to leave out earlier messages or has less than a full answer's room, the reply carries `large: true` (expand: an optional field old readers ignore) and the chat shows a quiet hint with a link to the Files page. Not shown on the no-answer reply, which already says so.
9. Upstream pins (rule 1485, issues #263, #264). Reviewed: agency-agents changed only code samples in two tracked files (nothing to adopt); paperclip's budget and low-trust changes are ideas only (a budget reserve, blocking unpriced usage by default, mentions as context rather than assignment). Both pins bumped.
10. Routines on by default (rule 1486, A-011, L052; the founder's decision of 2026-10-12, "routines on by default within plan caps", added brands too). See section 16, Routines: a routine with no stored switch is on, the owner's stored `false` stays off; the Scheduled tasks text says so in both languages, and the routine's notification and agent note no longer say "a routine you turned on". Expand only: no stored shape changes, no conversion.

## 68. AI safety gates: paid work from chat, regulated sectors (2026-10-13, branch only, rules 1510-1529)

1. Paid work from chat needs the owner's own words (rules 1510-1513). The chat tools that spend credits are `generate_content`, `draft_items` and `generate_image`. Before each one charges, `spendGate` (src/lib/chat.ts) reads only `ctx.ownerMsg`, the owner's latest message that chat() saved for this turn, never a tool result, a file, a crawled page, connector data or the model's words. `spendIntent` (src/lib/spend-intent.ts) is deterministic: a request verb that no negation or question word cancels (Arabic and English lists, Arabic folded) with a deliverable noun in the same clause, or a clause that opens with a number and a deliverable; for an image, a drawing verb or a request verb with an image noun. A bare "yes", "تمام" or "go ahead" is not a request.
   - Explicit: the tool runs as before.
   - Not explicit, in the app: nothing runs and nothing is charged. The proposal (type, brief, count or item, estimated credits) is stored under `sc(u)` for 24 hours (`spendprop:`) and the reply carries a card, at most two a reply, showing what would run and its price. The owner's Run (`POST /api/chat/spend/{id}`, permissions row 206) is the approval: `decideSpend` claims it, runs `generateBatch` or `renderImage` with the stored fields only, and closes it as `ran`; Dismiss closes it as `dismissed`. A refused run (no credits, cap, trial, missing data) or a failure leaves the card open. A reloaded chat shows each card's state.
   - Not explicit, over MCP: no card can be shown, so the call is refused and the agent is told to ask the person politely, in their language, to say plainly what to make.
   - A call without an owner message (tests, other callers) is unchanged. The starter plan's Start button sends "ابدأ:" / "Start:" with the item, which counts as the request.
2. Regulated sectors never auto-approve (rules 1520-1523). `regulatedSector(brand.industry)` (src/lib/regulated.ts) recognises pharmacy, health (clinics, hospitals, dental, medical), real estate, legal (law firms, lawyers) and finance (investment, banks, fintech, insurance, lending, trading, crypto). `initialStatus` answers `pending` for such a brand whatever governance's auto-approve says, so every item, from the app, chat, a handoff or a routine, waits for a person in the inbox; every other industry keeps auto-approve. Customer-facing items of a regulated brand get one plain line at the end in the body's language (`withDisclaimer`, once): general information, not medical, legal, investment or financial advice, or final details as in the contract; no promise. Internal documents (plans, reports) get none. The chat tool tells the agent that auto-approve is off for regulated sectors, so it does not blame a brand rule.
3. Shapes (rule 679, expand only). `ChatMsg.spendProposals` is optional; older messages have none and older code ignores it. No stored field changed shape; the brand's existing `industry` is read as it is.
4. Not done here. The Governance page shows no hint that auto-approve is off for a regulated brand; external drafts from MCP get no disclaimer (they are always pending). Rules 1515-1519 and 1525-1529 are held for this change and not used.
5. Two clicks at once (rule 1514, batch review 2026-10-13). The claim `spendprop-claim:` (`setNx`, 330 s) lets one Run reach the charge; a second click while it runs gets 409 `claim_busy`, and one after it closed gets 410 `gone`. One charge per card.
6. The X cut keeps the disclaimer (rule 1524, batch review 2026-10-13). X takes 280 characters; for a post that carries a regulated brand's disclaimer line, `cutKeepingDisclaimer` (src/lib/regulated.ts) trims the body and keeps the line whole at the end, in both the direct X connector and the Composio one. Any other post is cut as before.

## 69. Founder margin alert and integrations page (2026-10-13, branch only, rules 1530-1549)

The PRD asks for a margin alarm before the first customer: heavy chat plus media can cost more than a plan earns. Both pieces are founder reads; nothing here changes a customer.

1. Per-account media cost (rule 1531). When an image or video render finishes or fails with a billed cost, `meterRender` adds its micro-dollars to `usage:{email}:{month}:media_usd_micro`, only while the job's account is still the same one (`sameAccount`, spec A32). The key is under `usage:` and in `usageMonthKeys`, so `wipeAccount` deletes it. Expand only: months before this release read 0.
2. The formula (rule 1530, `src/lib/margin.ts`). For one paying account (not suspended, not on the trial, a plan with a price) and one UTC calendar month: revenue = `netPriceSar(priceOf(u))` (the plan's monthly list price or the Agency deal's price, net of VAT; the list prices exclude VAT, `PRICES_INCLUDE_VAT = false`); cost = (`usd_micro` + `media_usd_micro`) / 1e6 USD; margin % = round((revenue − cost × `SAR_PER_USD`) / revenue × 100), `SAR_PER_USD = 3.75` from `src/lib/pricing.ts`, the rate the overview and the Users table already use. Cost is month to date against the whole month's price. Top-up packs are not plan revenue and are left out.
3. Console (rules 1532, 1533). The Users table's margin uses the same formula and now includes media cost (`mediaUsd`). The Usage page opens with a Margin section (`#margin`, Arabic and English): the paying accounts under 50% this month, live from the overview's `margin` field, and the last daily run. Read-only.
4. Daily run (rules 1534-1539). `GET /api/cron/daily`, first hop only, after the reminder sweep, runs `runMarginAlert`: every account read once (`pmap`), the counters in chunked MGETs, then a per-account per-day marker `margin:alerted:{day}:{sha256(email)[:24]}` taken with `setNx` (2 days) so an account is alerted at most once a day however often the cron runs. The accounts newly alerted go to every `ADMIN_EMAILS` founder as ONE bell item (kind `credits`, which no preference switches off; the channel the golden-set regressions use), in the founder's language, naming plans and percentages only, never an email or a name, linking to `/{lang}/admin/usage#margin`. The day's rows (with emails) are kept in `margin:day:{day}` for 8 days, the counts in `margin:last`. No new cron schedule (Hostinger crons are set by hand). A failure is one fixed log line and `{error}` in the cron's answer; it never stops the daily batch. No automatic action on any account.
5. Integrations page (rules 1540-1544). `/{lang}/admin/integrations` (nav: Integrations / التكاملات) reads `GET /api/admin/integrations` (`adminActor` first, 401; a read, so no second factor and no audit row). Each outside service is on, partial or off from whether its environment variable NAMES are present and non-empty; the page shows the names as set or missing and the code gates (`CLAUDE_PAUSED`; `PAYMOB_LIVE`, `MEDIA_MODELS_ON`, `LLM_PROVIDER`, `COMPOSIO_API_KEY` + `COMPOSIO_PROVIDERS` by presence). No value, length or prefix is read out or sent: any two values give the same answer. A set `LLM_PROVIDER` reads "set", not which provider; Paymob live and test keys are not told apart. To change one, set the variable in hPanel and redeploy. Below the services, Connections (rules 2177-2178): the stores cron's last count per platform (connected, must reconnect, through Composio) and the time of that run, or a note that no run has counted yet.
   Drift fix 2026-10-18 (rules 2160-2162): kie.ai and Higgsfield need `MEDIA_DIR` (the disk store `src/lib/media/config.ts` requires before it offers their models), no longer the retired `BLOB_READ_WRITE_TOKEN`; Sign in with Google has its own row (`GOOGLE_SIGNIN_CLIENT_ID` + `GOOGLE_SIGNIN_CLIENT_SECRET`, a separate client from the calendar's `GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID`); a test fails when a name on the page is no longer read anywhere in `src/`. Rule 2165 (same day): the Composio row warns, by name, when a two-toolkit provider (Meta, Google, Google Docs and Sheets) has one `COMPOSIO_AUTH_*` set and the other missing, since `composioReady` then hides its Connect button; which providers `COMPOSIO_PROVIDERS` lists stays unread. Each direct-app row lists the callback paths its platform app must hold (from `registry.ts` by client id name, plus `/api/auth/google/callback` for Google sign-in), as paths after the site's address, so the same row serves thakeen.com and staging. Rules 2172-2173 (same day): the Shopify row also lists its webhook path, `/api/shopify/compliance` (all three compliance fields; section 11 step 14d), and under it the Shopify compliance requests: the stores still waiting for the next stores run and the 10 newest receipts (topic, store, time, and the workspace count of a finished erasure), read from the two fixed keys by `complianceSummary`. A failed read leaves that block out; the rest of the page loads.

## 71. Staff identity: sign-in, enrolment, recovery, bootstrap, break-glass (2026-10-13, branch `claude/staff-identity`, rules 1630-1654 and 1659, phase 1 = expand)

The founder console now has its own login, separate from every customer account (spec `docs/specs/2026-10-13-staff-identity.md`). A staff login is a `staff:{email}` record with its own password, its own authenticator app and its own short session; a customer session, the founder's own included, never becomes one (rule 1630). Phase 1 is the expand step of rule 679: the founder's customer-account path and the key form keep working, with a banner, until a staff owner closes the first and a later contract release removes it. No new environment variable.

1. Bootstrap, the first staff login (rule 1639). Only while no owner exists, and only from a key session (the key form; the header cannot do it). The console banner of the key session offers «إنشاء حساب الموظف» / "Create your staff login". A founder in their own customer session gets the link «إنشاء حساب الموظف من جلسة المفتاح» to `/{lang}/admin?login=1`, which shows the key form (rules 2230-2231, 2026-10-18: it used to loop back to the overview); after the key and the console code the same URL renders the console with the key-session banner and this button (rule 2232). The form asks for the email, the name and a reason (the console code too once `admin:2fa` is enrolled). The answer shows a setup code once (30 minutes, single use, `Cache-Control: no-store`), never mailed and never in a URL, and one `staff.bootstrap` row is written. The founder opens `/{lang}/admin/login/setup`, types the email, the setup code and a new password (12 characters to 72 bytes, the most bcrypt reads, about 72 Latin or 36 Arabic letters; not a common password, not the customer password of the same address, rule 1641), and goes straight to the enrolment. Once an ACTIVE owner exists (enabled, with a password and an authenticator, rule 1659) the bootstrap route answers 409; an owner who never finished the setup or is disabled does not block it. The bootstrap, like every owner change on the Staff page, decides and writes under `lock:staff:owners`, so two writes at once can never leave no active owner. Since 2026-10-23 (rules 3700, 3701, 3704, branch `claude/m0-staff-door-653d30`, founder approval of the change to rule 1639): the founder signed in to their own customer account opens `/{lang}/admin` and the one banner «لوحة المؤسس تحتاج دخول موظف خاصًا بك» offers «أنشئ دخول الموظف». The dialog fills the founder's email (editable) and a reason, and asks for the 6-digit code of the console authenticator or of the founder's own two-step sign-in, or a passkey; the proof is made for this request, never a step-up still open. The answer carries no code: the code goes into the setup hand-off cookie (`__Host-thakeen_staff_setup`, HttpOnly, SameSite=Strict, 30 minutes) and the browser goes to `/{lang}/admin/login/setup`, which shows the address and asks only for the new password. A founder with no authenticator and no passkey is told how to get one; the key session (item 7) stays as break-glass and still answers the code once. The banner no longer links to `?login=1`. The customer session never becomes a staff session (rule 1630).
2. Daily sign-in (rules 1631, 1632). `/{lang}/admin/login`: email and password. Every refusal looks the same (401 `invalid`) and takes the same time (one bcrypt compare always runs). A right password opens a 5-minute pre-auth step (`__Host-thakeen_staff_pre`), then the 6-digit code from the person's own authenticator opens the staff session (`__Host-thakeen_staff`: `HttpOnly`, `Secure`, `SameSite=Strict`, `Path=/`; 15 minutes idle, 12 hours at most, rules 1633-1634) and a step-up tied to it, so the first console writes after sign-in need no second prompt. The step-up lasts 30 idle minutes; after that a write asks for the person's own code again (the console's code prompt). Staff management, the customer-path switch and own credential changes always ask for a fresh code in the form (rule 1636).
3. First sign-in enrolment (rule 1632). The enrolment opens only right after a setup or reset code (item 1, item 5): a record with no authenticator is refused at the daily sign-in with the same `invalid`, so a password alone can never enrol an authenticator. The enrolment shows a QR code (drawn in the page, nothing fetched), the key in groups of four and an `otpauth://` link. A code from the new app confirms it; 8 recovery codes are shown once («احفظ رموز الاسترداد الآن» / "Save your recovery codes now") and stored hashed (rule 1642). There is no skip: no session opens before the confirm. The secret is sealed under a key derived from `CONNECT_SECRET` with its own label and the key's `kid` (rule 1654).
4. A lost phone (rule 1642). At the code step the person types a recovery code instead. It is spent, and the step moves to the enrolment: a new authenticator must be confirmed before any session opens (the old one stays until then, so the password alone can never enrol one). Confirming it ends every earlier session of that person (rule 1635). The account page can also issue 8 new recovery codes with a fresh code.
5. A lost password (rule 1643). There is no email path for staff, on purpose: a mailbox takeover must never open the console. Another staff owner (Staff page, "Reset", reason and a fresh code) or the break-glass key session (the same button, the console code once enrolled) issues a reset code, shown once for 30 minutes. At that moment, inside the record's lock, the old password, the authenticator and the recovery codes are cleared and every session of the person ends, so a stolen password stops working before the code is even redeemed. The person types the code at `/{lang}/admin/login/setup` with a new password, and the enrolment follows. Nobody resets their own login from the Staff page (`not_self`; the account page changes one's own password). The key session can reset a login only when `admin:2fa` is enrolled and a fresh console code comes with the request (else 403 `console_code_required`), and only while no other active owner exists (else 409 `owner_active`: that owner does the reset). Each key reset is mailed to every `ADMIN_EMAILS` address through the normal mailer (action, login and time only, never the code or the reason; nothing is sent when mail is not set up).
6. A locked address (rule 1644). 10 failed sign-ins for one address within 15 minutes lock it for 15 minutes: during the lock the password alone answers the same `invalid`, so a guesser learns nothing. The real person signs in anyway by typing the password AND the authenticator code together («رمز التطبيق» on the same form, shown after a refusal): both right open the session at once (the row says `locked: true`) and the lock stays for the guesser; any wrong part is the same `invalid`, and those codes are limited to 5 per address and per IP per 15 minutes. One `staff.locked` row is written per lock (actor `system`), after the answer so the 10th failure takes no longer; the counts themselves live outside `admin:log`. Changing one's own password on the account page checks the current password the same way: refused while locked, a wrong one counts toward the lock, 5 per IP per 15 minutes. The key form is never affected by a staff lock. Since 2026-10-23 (rule 3702): the sign-in form shows the code field only after the server answers `locked: true`, which it does for a locked address, known or not (unknown addresses are counted and locked alike), so the flag never tells which addresses exist; every other refusal stays the plain `invalid`.
7. Break-glass: the key form (rules 1645, 1646). `/{lang}/admin?login=1` still opens a key session, now as the emergency door: once `admin:2fa` is enrolled it needs the console code (or its recovery code) with the key, a wrong or missing code looks like a wrong key, and every key sign-in writes a `key.login` row. A key session shows the banner «جلسة طوارئ بالمفتاح، ادخل بحساب الموظف» / "Break-glass key session: sign in with your staff login". It can create the first owner (item 1), list the staff, and reset or enable again a login while no other active owner exists, always with a fresh console code and a mail to every `ADMIN_EMAILS` address (item 5); nothing else on the Staff page. The `x-admin-key` header stays for scripts (rate limited, `x-admin-otp` for writes once enrolled) and never opens a browser session. Such a script carries the key and no cookie, so the console write check (rule 1647) skips its `Origin` and JSON parts for it; a `Sec-Fetch-Site: cross-site` request is still refused. Every other console write, every `/api/admin/2fa` action and marking leads seen included, runs the full check. Since 2026-10-23 (rule 3705): no screen links to the key form (the sign-in page lost its three bottom links); its key field has its own name, `autocomplete="new-password"` and the password managers' ignore attributes, so a saved customer password is no longer filled into it, plus a show toggle; the server trims spaces around the key.
8. Closing the customer path (rules 1649, 1650). While open, the founder's own customer session (an `ADMIN_EMAILS` owner signed in normally) still opens the console with the banner «انقل دخولك إلى حساب الموظف» / "Move to your staff login" and audit rows say `via: "founder-session"`. A staff owner with an authenticator closes it from the Staff page ("Close this way in", a reason and a fresh code): `staff:config.customerPathOff` is set, one `console.customerPathOff` row is written, and from that moment `adminActor`, `isAdminCookie` and the console pages ignore customer sessions. It cannot be reopened from the console (only by deleting `staff:config` in the store, a deliberate act). The key path is untouched. Since 2026-10-23 (rule 3704) the banner reads «لوحة المؤسس تحتاج دخول موظف خاصًا بك» / "The founder console needs your own staff login" while no owner exists (with the create button of item 1), and «الدخول بحساب الموظف» / "Sign in with your staff login" once one does.
9. Signed-out links (rule 1652). The staff cookie is `SameSite=Strict`, so a console link opened from mail or chat arrives without it. A console page with no live staff, key or founder session therefore redirects to `/{lang}/admin/login?next=` (the page's own path through `safeNext`, a console path only) instead of the old 404; after the sign-in the person lands on that page. A key session that ended still shows the ended notice with the link to the key form. Console pages and staff routes are `noindex` and `no-store` (rule 1653).
10. Customer view from a staff session (rule 1648). "View as" opened by a staff member writes `imp` = the staff email and `ss` = a hash of the staff session id on the customer session; the view ends as soon as that staff session is no longer live (signed out, 15 idle minutes, 12 hours, a reset or disable).
11. The contract release (rule 1651, NOT built). After the founder has signed in with the staff login for a while and closed the customer path, a later release removes the `ADMIN_EMAILS` session path from `adminActor`, `isAdminCookie`, `consoleIdentity` and the banners. `ADMIN_EMAILS` then names alert recipients only. That release pull request names itself the contract step.
12. Rotating `CONNECT_SECRET` (rule 1654). Set the new value and keep the old one as `CONNECT_SECRET_PREVIOUS`. Staff secrets sealed with the previous key still open (by their `kid`) and are sealed again with the current key the next time a code from them is accepted. A rotation WITHOUT `CONNECT_SECRET_PREVIOUS` makes every staff secret unreadable: staff then sign in with a recovery code and enrol again, or the break-glass key session issues reset codes.
13. Stored shapes (rule 679, expand). Only new keys: `staff:{email}`, `staff:index`, `staff:config`, `staffsession:`, `staffpre:`, `staffenrol:`, `staffsetup:`, `stafffail:`, `staffotp:used:`, the lock `lock:staff:owners`. The customer `Session` gains an optional `ss`, which old code ignores; old sessions without it read as before. No conversion script. Since 2026-10-23 (rules 3700-3707): `staffsetup:` records may have `kind: "invite"` (TTL 24 hours) and `createdBy`/`by` may be the founder's email; new keys `staffbootpk:{sha256}` (the founder's passkey challenge, 300 s) and `staffinvite:` (rate buckets); the cookie `__Host-thakeen_staff_setup` (`thakeen_staff_setup_dev` on loopback). Old code reads an `invite` record as unknown and refuses it (`invalid`), which is safe. No conversion script.
14. Inviting staff (rules 3706, 3707, 2026-10-23). A staff owner opens the Staff page, «دعوة بالبريد» / "Invite by email": email, name, role (owner or support, read-only), reason and a fresh code. The record is created without a password and an invite mail goes out with a link `/{lang}/admin/login/setup#invite={code}`: the code sits in the fragment, which no browser sends to a server; the page reads it, removes it from the address bar and hides the code field. The code works once within 24 hours and only its hash is stored. When mail is not set up or the send fails, the owner sees the link once to hand over. 10 invites per owner and 20 per IP per hour. The key session cannot invite; setup and reset codes are still never mailed (rule 1643).

## 72. Founding offer and price lock (2026-10-13, branch only, rules 1700-1712; 1713-1719 held)

Founder decision 2026-10-13: build the founding offer in code. Source: PRD `docs/PRD-Thakeen.md` 11.2-11.3, spec `docs/specs/2026-10-13-founding-20-seats-price-lock.md`, `src/lib/founding.ts`. Everything automatic is off while Paymob is off (`paymobConfig()`, section 4a of `variables.md`): no checkout, no callback, and the public counter answers closed. No new environment variable; the numbers are constants in `src/lib/founding.ts`.

1. Numbers (rule 1701). 20 founding seats, a 12-month price lock for a seat, then a 6-month lock for the paying accounts after them up to the first 100 paying accounts (the 6 is PRD 11.2; the 100 is the 2026-09-29 pricing design and is unverified against the PRD), a 7-day refund window, sale from `SALE_START` = 2026-11-01 (Riyadh day).
2. Checkout (rule 1702). `POST /api/billing/checkout` with `founding: true` on a first plan payment: the server sets `founding` on the stored intent only when the sale has started, the account has no seat, no lock and no online subscription, and a seat is free. The browser's flag is a request, never the decision.
3. Payment (rules 1700, 1703-1705). `applyPaid`, under the account's lock, on the account's first online subscription only (no `billing` yet) and after `SALE_START`: an intent with `founding` claims a seat with `db.zaddCapped("founding:seats", …, 20)` (member: the account id `acct(u)` since rule 2982; freeing a place removes the id and the address, so rows written under the address before also leave), one Lua step (ZSCORE, ZCARD, ZADD), so two payments can never take the last seat; a repeated callback answers `exists` and takes nothing. A founding seat then takes an uncapped lock of 12 months; a payment that found no seat, or never asked, takes a 6-month lock if `pricelock:accounts` holds fewer than 100. The lock stores the plan's list price before VAT (`baseHalalas`), `from` (the payment's Riyadh day), `until` (`from` + months) and what was paid. One console line `founding txn:… seat:claimed|full price_lock_months:N`.
4. Renewal (rule 1706). `chargeSavedCard` reads `lockedBaseHalalas(u, plan, paidThrough)`: while the lock is live, on the same plan, and the period being paid starts before `until`, the charge is the lower of the locked and the current list price, plus VAT. The intent stores `lockedBaseHalalas`, and `applyTransaction` checks the callback's amount against that same figure. Another plan, an ended lock or no lock: the list price, exactly as before.
5. Refund proposal (rule 1707). For the founder only: paid minus credits used × (price before VAT ÷ the plan's monthly credits), never below 0 or above the payment, while the Riyadh day is within 7 days of the payment day. The refund button stays the full refund (rule 1300, which ends the plan); a partial refund through Paymob waits for test mode.
6. Refund ends the seat (rule 1708). `endPlanOnRefund` marks the seat and the lock ended and frees both places; a later payment is an ordinary subscription (the account keeps the ended record, so it never claims again on its own).
7. Founder console (rules 1709, 1710). Billing section, panel "Founding seats and price locks": counts, every account in either set with its seat, lock and refund proposal. `GET /api/admin/founding` (`isAdmin`). `POST /api/admin/founding` (`adminActor`, second factor, a reason, `grant_seat` / `end_seat` / `grant_lock` / `end_lock`, under the account's lock, one `adminAudit` row in section billing). A hand seat is still capped at 20; a hand lock is not capped, takes a plan (default the subscription's) and a first paid day not in the future and at most 400 days back.
8. Public counter (rule 1711). `GET /api/founding/seats`, no session, `allow("foundingseats:ip:{ip}", 60, 60)`: `{open:false}` while payment is off, before the sale or with no seat left; otherwise `{open:true, left, total}`. Never an email. Cached 30 s. No page reads it yet: the pricing-page line waits for the founder's wording.
9. Leaving (rule 1712). `wipeAccount` frees both places; the data download (`buildExport`) shows the seat and the lock.

Stored shape (expand step, rule 679): two optional fields on the account, `founding` and `priceLock`, and two optional fields on a Paymob intent, `founding` and `lockedBaseHalalas`. Old code ignores them and keeps them on save; no conversion script.

## 73. Fair-use stop (2026-10-13, branch `claude/fair-use-stop-4fd43a`, rules 1720-1737; 1738-1739 held)

Founder decision of 2026-10-13: "no need to lawyer, just stop the service". Instead of a fair-use clause, the code stops paid AI usage of an account whose cost this month reached 95% of what it paid this month (founder decision of 2026-10-13, checklist job fair-use-margin: 5% stays as margin). Section 72 is left for the founding-mechanics change (rules 1700-1719) that runs in parallel.

1. The limit (rules 1720, 1721, 1728, `src/lib/fair-use.ts`). For one paying account (`isPaying`: not suspended, not on the trial, a plan with a price) and one UTC calendar month (`monthKey()`): cost = the margin alert's own two counters (`usage:{email}:{month}:usd_micro` + `media_usd_micro`) / 1e6 × `SAR_PER_USD`, read in one MGET; limit = (`netPriceSar(priceOf(u))` + the top-up packs paid this month net of VAT, `topupRevenueSar`) × `FAIR_USE_PCT` / 100, with `FAIR_USE_PCT = 95`. Paused when cost ≥ limit, unless the founder lifted it for this month. No second counter. A top-up pack is money paid, so buying one raises the limit by what was paid and ends the pause. The trial keeps its own limits (30 messages, its credits, one video) and is never read here.
2. Where it stops (rules 1722-1725, 1732, 1734). `fairUseGate(u)` runs before any provider call, before anything is charged or counted, next to the gate already there: `reserveChatTurn` (web chat `POST /api/chat` and MCP `ask_noor`; checked before the allowance is counted), `generateBatch` (generate, routines, the weekly plan, handoffs, chat tools, spend cards, MCP `generate_content`; after the trial check, before the classifier and the charge), `renderImage` and `renderVideo` (the free placeholder card too), `POST /api/onboard` and `/api/onboard/confirm`, `POST /api/instructions/test` and a meeting's notes and transcript (`proposeFromMeeting`). Every one answers 402 `fair_use` (the chat route `{error:"fair_use", chat, credits}`, generate `{error:"fair_use", resumes}`); MCP and chat tools get one English sentence (`fairUseText`). A routine run that is refused records the outcome `fair_use`, which counts as skipped. Not gated: X reads (`x-stats.ts`, X API cost, not model or media cost) and model calls that charge no credits (Reem's chat, crawls) (rule 1737).
3. If the counters cannot be read (rule 1726), the gate lets the request through and logs `fair_use_read_failed`: a store outage must not stop every paying customer, and the margin alert still tells the founder.
4. The owner's message (rule 1727). `LimitModal` kind `fair_use`: the title "Usage paused until next month" / «توقف الاستخدام مؤقتاً حتى الشهر القادم»; the owner reads that chat, generation, scheduled tasks and media are paused, nothing was sent or charged, and usage resumes on the 1st of next month or sooner with a top-up pack (the button goes to the Subscription page's top-up section); a member is told to ask the owner.
5. The founder (rules 1729-1731, 1733). On the first refusal of the month, every `ADMIN_EMAILS` founder gets one bell item (kind `credits`, plan only, no email or name, linking to `/{lang}/admin/usage#margin`), once per account per month (`setNx` marker `fairuse:alerted:{month}:{sha256(email)[:24]}`, 40 days). The Usage page's Margin table marks each row Paused or Lifted this month, with Lift pause / Re-apply buttons: `POST /api/admin/fair-use {email, action: "lift" | "clear", reason}` (`adminActor`, then the second factor, then a reason; under the account's lock; one `admin:log` row `fair_use_lift` / `fair_use_clear`). A lift is `User.fairUseLift = {month, at, by}` and counts for that month only, so the stop applies again on the 1st without anyone clearing it. Expand only (rule 679): an older reader ignores the field.
6. Terms (rule 1736). One sentence in "Subscription and credits" (both languages): usage may pause when an account's monthly usage exceeds its plan's fair-use limit. The terms' date moves to 2026-10-13 (`TERMS_VERSION`, stored with a new signup's consent; no re-acceptance for existing accounts).


## 74. Media storage on the Hostinger disk (2026-10-13, branch only, rules 1750-1774; numbered 70 on its branch, renumbered in the 2026-10-13d batch: 70 is held for brand limits Part C, 71-73 were taken)

1. Where (rule 1750). `MEDIA_DIR` names an absolute folder outside the app folder (a build replaces the app folder): live `domains/thakeen.com/media`, staging `domains/staging.thakeen.com/media`. Unset, relative or inside the app folder means no store: kie and Higgsfield are not offered, fal is hot-linked as before.
2. Writing (rules 1751-1753). A finished render is downloaded through `safeFetch` (https on every hop), capped, sniffed and converted as before. `writeToStore` refuses a file over `MEDIA_MAX_FILE_BYTES` (`too_large`), refuses when the store would pass 95 % of `MEDIA_MAX_BYTES` or `MEDIA_MAX_FILES` or leave under 2 GiB free on the disk (`full`), then writes the index (`mfile:{scope}:{name}` and `mfiles:{scope}`, the job's own scope), then the bytes to `.tmp/{name}.part` (`wx`, fsync) and hard-links them to `{2 hex}/{name}`. The URL is `{mediaOrigin}/m/{name}`: `APP_ORIGIN` when it is thakeen.com or staging.thakeen.com, else thakeen.com. The same room check runs before a render is charged (`storeReady`).
3. Reading (rules 1754-1758). `GET /m/{name}` (and HEAD) serves a signed-in member of the owning workspace, or a 24-hour signed link; everything else is one bare 404. Ranges (206, 416, several ranges answered whole), ETag 304, `?download=1` attachment, `Content-Security-Policy: sandbox`, `nosniff`, `noindex`, `private, max-age=86400` for a member and `no-store` for a signed link. 1200 requests an hour per IP; at most 6 full-body streams over 10 MB at once (503 after).
4. Handing a file over (rule 1755). The task always keeps the plain URL. A signed link is minted only at the hand-off: a reference image sent to kie or Higgsfield (`registry.ts`), a Facebook, Instagram, TikTok or WordPress publish (`publish.ts`), and a Composio staged file (`composio.ts`). Old Blob URLs pass through unchanged.
5. Deleting (rule 1761). `wipeAccount` calls `forgetScopeMedia` for every brand scope: each file is unlinked (a missing one is fine, any other error stops the wipe for a retry), the index keys are deleted with the rest, and the counters give the space back.
6. Checking (rule 1762). The console's media check runs `probeStore`: write, read, delete a check file, and keep `.sentinel` naming every build that found the folder. If a deploy wipes the folder, "survived builds" falls back to 0: then the folder is not safe from builds and must move.
7. Releases (rule 679, expand then contract). Expand (this change): new renders go to the disk; `isOurMediaUrl` still accepts `*.public.blob.vercel-storage.com`, so old posts keep their images. After the expand release is live and the founder has set `MEDIA_DIR` and seen "survived builds" at 1 or more after a redeploy: run `npx --no-install jiti scripts/media-blob-move.ts` (scan, counts only), then the same with `--copy` on the Hostinger app's shell with the app's `KV_REST_API_URL`, `KV_REST_API_TOKEN` and `MEDIA_DIR` (rule 1763; idempotent, run again until it reports nothing left or only failures to look at). Contract (a later release, once the scan reports 0 on live): drop the Blob host from `isOurMediaUrl`, the `BLOB_READ_WRITE_TOKEN` row and the `@vercel/blob` dependency, and the "older copies on Vercel Blob" sentences in the privacy policy and help (rule 1764).
8. Staging (rule 1760). `/m/` passes the staging lock, because the route checks its own session or signature. Staging needs its own `MEDIA_DIR` and `APP_ORIGIN=https://staging.thakeen.com`, or its URLs name the live host, whose disk does not hold the file.

## 75. Customer cloud drives and the media library (2026-10-13, branch only, rules 1775-1799)

Numbered 72 on its branch, renumbered 75 in the 2026-10-13d batch (70 is held for brand limits Part C, 71-74 were taken). It builds on section 74 (media storage).

1. Connecting (rules 1775-1777, 1786). Google Drive, Dropbox and OneDrive are Composio-only providers (`google_drive`, `dropbox`, `onedrive`; toolkits `googledrive`, `dropbox`, `one_drive`, pinned). The Integrations card shows Connect only when Composio is on for the provider (`COMPOSIO_PROVIDERS`) and its auth config is set (`COMPOSIO_AUTH_GOOGLEDRIVE`, `COMPOSIO_AUTH_DROPBOX`, `COMPOSIO_AUTH_ONEDRIVE`). Connecting stores `conn:{scope}:{provider}` with the Composio account id; nothing is read at connect time.
2. Searching (rule 1778). Files page, Image and video library: each connected drive has a search box. `POST /api/drives {action: "search"}` makes one call with the person's words as an argument, a constant result cap (25) and a constant type filter (images, MP4, TXT, MD, CSV); folders and anything else are dropped.
3. Importing (rules 1779-1781). The person presses Import on one result. The id and extension are checked, the download tool runs, and its temporary `s3url` is fetched through `safeFetch` (https on every hop, capped: 25 MB image, 100 MB video, 120,000 bytes text) or its inline base64 is measured before it is decoded. An image is re-encoded as one of our JPEGs and an MP4 kept as is, both as library files (`taskId: ""`, `source` = the drive) in the private store under the workspace's scope; a text file becomes the workspace file `drive-<name>.md` or `.csv`, never a core memory file. At most 300 library files per workspace.
4. Uploading from the device (rule 1783). "Upload an image" posts one image of at most 5 MB to `POST /api/media/upload` (the proxy buffers 6 MB of a body, rule 774, so no video this way); "Upload a text file" reads a TXT, MD or CSV file in the browser and saves it with `PUT /api/files` as `upload-<name>`.
5. iCloud Drive (rule 1787). Apple offers no public API for a third-party web app to read or write a person's iCloud Drive, so there is no iCloud connector and none will be built on an unofficial route. On iPhone and iPad both upload buttons open the Files picker, which includes iCloud Drive; the library copy, the integrations catalog ("planned", with that note) and the help answer say so.
6. Exporting (rule 1782). Under each library file, and under each post's image or clip in the inbox, "Save to <drive>" appears for each connected drive. `POST /api/drives {action: "export"}` accepts only a URL whose index entry is in the caller's scope, stages it with Composio through a 24-hour signed link and uploads it as `thakeen-<8 hex>.jpg|mp4`: Google creates a new file (plain upload for an image, resumable for a video), Dropbox adds under `/Thakeen` with `autorename`, OneDrive renames in `/Thakeen` on a clash. Nothing at the drive is overwritten, moved, shared or deleted.
7. Deleting (rule 1784). Delete under a library file removes that file (index, disk, counters); a post's media is never deleted here. Deleting the account deletes the library with the rest (rule 1761). Files already saved to a drive stay there: they are the customer's.
8. Who (rule 1785). Any member may search, import, upload, delete and export (`library.write`, `library.export`); a founder impersonating may not export. Hourly per workspace: search 60, import 30, export 30, delete 120, upload 30.
9. Releases. Expand only: the index entry gains an optional `source`; entries without it read as a post's media, as before. Dormant until the founder creates the three Composio auth configs and sets the variables above.

## 76. One site menu, language redirects and the global 404 (2026-10-14, branch only, rules 1854-1859 and 1873-1876; site critique 2026-10-14)

Files: `src/components/Nav.tsx`, `src/components/NavDrop.tsx`, `src/components/LangToggle.tsx`, `src/components/Footer.tsx`, `src/components/home/SectionChips.tsx`, `src/lib/home.ts` (`HOME_NAV`), `src/lib/home-ld.ts`, `src/app/[lang]/page.tsx`, `src/proxy.ts`, `src/app/global-not-found.tsx`, `next.config.ts`, `src/app/sitemap.ts`. Tests: `tests/global-nav.test.ts` (`docs/system/tests/1854-global-nav.md`).

1. The menu (rule 1854). Every public page renders the same `Nav`: Team (`/{lang}/agents`, with the agents in a dropdown), How it works (`/{lang}#steps`, the homepage's seven steps), Pricing, Help, then the call to action «ابدأ مجاناً» / "Start free" to `/{lang}/app/signup`. Log in and the language toggle are small utilities beside it. Customers, agencies, sectors and resources left the menu; the footer lists every one of them and now Help too. Under `lg` the four links and log in fold into a native `<details>` menu with a 44px button (rule 1856); under `sm` the language toggle moves into it as well.
2. The dropdown (rule 1855). Opens on hover and keyboard focus with CSS. Escape closes it and focuses the tab's link; it stays shut until the pointer leaves or focus leaves the menu. ArrowDown on the tab opens it and focuses the first item. The tab carries no `aria-haspopup`.
3. The homepage's anchors (rule 1854). `HOME_NAV` (rules, your day, how it works, plans, questions) renders as a row of 44px hand-drawn pills right under the cover, a list labelled «في هذه الصفحة» / "On this page", not a second `<nav>`. The row scrolls inside itself on a phone and wraps from 700px.
4. Skip link (rule 1858). `Nav` renders «تخطَّ إلى المحتوى» / "Skip to content" to `#main` first, visible on focus. The homepage's `<main>` is `id="main"`; the other public pages render their own `<main>` without the id yet (listed in `docs/system/tests/1854-global-nav.md`).
5. Unprefixed addresses (rules 1873, 1874). `/pricing?plan=pro` answers 307 to `/ar/pricing?plan=pro` or `/en/...` by the visitor's cookie, then Accept-Language (Gulf English locales read as Arabic), else Arabic. Only the public folders under `src/app/[lang]` move; reserved root paths never do.
6. The 404 outside the language tree (rules 1875, 1891). `/xyz`, `/ar-sa/pricing` or `/admin` (a first segment that is not a language, a root folder, a file or a framework path) is rewritten by the proxy to `/{lang}/__missing`, so the catch-all answers 404 with `src/app/[lang]/not-found.tsx` in the visitor's language; the address bar keeps what they typed. An address matching no route at all renders `src/app/global-not-found.tsx`, a full document, `lang="ar" dir="rtl"`, light theme, the global styles and the two fonts, Arabic first, English in its own left-to-right block, links to `/ar` and `/en`. Next adds `noindex` and answers 404.
7. Search data (rules 1859, 1876). `/ar` and `/en` set their canonical and hreflang (ar, en, x-default = Arabic). The homepage carries three JSON-LD blocks from `homeJsonLd`: Organization, SoftwareApplication with one offer per self-serve plan from `PLAN_PRICE_SAR` in SAR a month (VAT as `PRICES_INCLUDE_VAT`), and FAQPage from `HOME_FAQ`; no rating or review. Each sitemap URL lists its ar, en and x-default alternates.
8. Releases. No stored shape changes. No permission or environment variable changes.
