## 77. API route wrapper: one entry path for a request (2026-10-16, branch only, rules 1900-1906)

1. A request reaches a route exported as `handler(options, fn)` (`src/lib/api-handler.ts`, `architecture.md` 2.4 "API route wrapper").
2. Authentication first. `auth: "user"` reads the session with `currentUser()`; `"admin"` asks `isAdmin(req)`; `"cron"` compares the `Authorization: Bearer` value with `CRON_SECRET` through `sameSecret` (an unset secret refuses everyone); `"public"` skips this step. A failure answers 401 with the route's own body (`{ ok: false }`, or `{ ok: false, error: "auth" }` where the route already said so). Nothing else runs: the role, the rate counter, the query and the body are untouched.
3. Role. With `role`, `forbid(u, role)` from `src/lib/roles.ts` answers its usual 403 `{ error: "forbidden" }`. A role on any auth but `"user"` throws when the module loads.
4. Rate limit. With `rate`, one `allow` hit on `rate:<bucket>:<who>`: the account's email (default), `sc(u)` (`per: "scope"`), or the client IP (`per: "ip"`, and always without a user). Past `max` it answers 429 `{ ok: false, error: "slow_down" }` with `Retry-After` set to the window. A route that should count only well-formed requests (`wishlist/suggest`) keeps its `allow` inside `fn` instead.
5. Size. With `maxBytes`, a Content-Length above it answers 413 `too_large` before the body is read.
6. Query, then body. Each zod schema checks its part (the query as the first value of each name; a body that is not JSON as `{}`). A refusal is 400: the first failing field's message when it is an `ApiError` code (`bad_agent`, `too_short`), else the option `invalid`, else `{ ok: false }`.
7. `fn` runs with `{ req, u, scope, input, query, params }`. `scope` is `sc(u)` from the session. A scope, email or id in the body, the query, a header or the path is data to `fn` and never selects the tenant.
8. Releases. No stored shape changes, no permission change, no environment variable change (`CRON_SECRET` and `WAITLIST_ADMIN_KEY` are read as before). New dependency `zod`.

---
## 78. Mail, logs and tooling (2026-10-16, branch only, rules 1960-1979)

Supastarter quick wins (gap study 2026-10-16, standing order «the best of supastarter in all aspects»), built with our own code: no react-email, no new dependency. Files: `src/lib/mail-provider.ts`, `src/lib/notify.ts`, `src/emails/{index,verify-code,daily-brief,samples,copy}.ts`, `src/app/[lang]/admin/mail/page.tsx`, `src/lib/log.ts`, `src/lib/request-id.ts`, `src/proxy.ts`, `src/instrumentation.ts`, `src/lib/notifications.ts`, `src/app/api/notifications/route.ts`, `src/components/app/{NotificationCenter,NotificationsPanel}.tsx`, `.env.example`, `src/lib/blog.ts`, `src/lib/home-ld.ts`, `src/app/[lang]/blog/[slug]/page.tsx`. Tests: `tests/mail-logs-tooling.test.ts`; coverage `docs/system/tests/1960-mail-logs-tooling.md`.

1. Mail (rules 1960-1963). `sendEmail({ to, subject, text, template, context, locale })` renders the HTML part from `EMAIL_TEMPLATES` and hands the message to `mailProvider()`: Resend with `RESEND_API_KEY`, else the console provider off production (kept in memory, logged as domain and sizes only, answers not delivered), else nothing. The answer is unchanged: true only when Resend accepted it. The verify code (`verifyCode`), Noor's brief (`dailyBrief`), the signup nudge and the daily follow-ups (`notice`) now have HTML parts; their subjects and text parts are word for word what they were.
2. Mail preview (rules 1964-1965). `/{lang}/admin/mail`, a console section behind `requireAdmin`, shows every template in Arabic and English from fixed samples, each in a sandboxed frame (`sandbox=""`: no script, no same origin). It reads no customer data.
3. Logs (rules 1970-1976). Server code writes `log.error("what failed", { safe fields })`; `architecture.md` lists what the logger never writes. The proxy gives every request an `x-request-id` (kept when the client sent a well-formed one) on the request and the response; `withRequest(req)` adds it to a route's lines, and `onRequestError` logs Next's own request errors with it.
4. One bell entry read (rule 1966). Opening an unread entry in the bell or on the notifications page, or its "Mark as read" button there, posts `{ id }` to `/api/notifications`; the entry is marked read under the bell lock and the answer carries the unread count, which the workspace counter takes. "Mark all as read" is unchanged.
5. Tooling (rules 1977-1979). `.env.example` lists every name in `variables.md`, values empty (the build- and platform-set ones and the release-script inputs commented out); `.gitignore` keeps it. Every blog post page carries an Article (`articleJsonLd`, escaped by `ldJson`). `src/lib/blog.ts` checks every post when it reads them (five front matter fields, a real `YYYY-MM-DD` date, a hyphenated lowercase slug, an Arabic and an English body split by `---EN---`) and throws naming every problem, so `next build` fails on a bad post.
6. Releases. No stored shape changes (`read` on a bell entry already existed). One request body gains an optional field (`POST /api/notifications { id }`): a client that sends no body keeps working. No environment variable added.

---
## 80. Customer account security: two-step sign-in, devices, sign-in address, Google (2026-10-16, branch `claude/beautiful-blackburn-50e6d7`, rules 1930-1956)

Supastarter gap study 2026-10-16 (customer auth pack), built on Thakeen's own staff code. Permissions in docs/system/permissions.md rows 242-248 and "Account security"; tests in `docs/system/tests/1930-account-security.md`. Every record below is keyed by the ACTING login (`actingLogin` in `src/lib/own-password.ts`): a member manages its own login, never the owner's. Passkeys (2026-10-20) are section 99.

### 80.1 Customer two-step sign-in (rules 1930-1937)

Code in `src/lib/mfa.ts`, `src/lib/login-finish.ts`, `src/app/api/account/2fa/route.ts`, `src/app/api/auth/login/mfa/route.ts`, `src/components/app/settings/TwoStepSettings.tsx`.
1. Setup (Settings, Security, «الدخول بخطوتين» / "Two-step sign-in"; recommended to owners). The password starts it: a new secret sealed with `CONNECT_SECRET` under its own label (`mfa:pending:{login}`, 15 minutes), shown once as a QR code and as text. A code from the app turns it on (`mfa:{login}`) and shows eight recovery codes once; only their hashes are stored.
2. Sign-in. POST /api/auth/login checks the password as before, then `finishLogin`: with two-step on it answers 401 `mfa_required` and sets the short challenge cookie `thakeen_mfa` (5 minutes, 5 tries, the login kept server side). The form asks for the code; POST /api/auth/login/mfa takes the authenticator's 6 digits (each 30-second step works once) or one recovery code (spent), spends the challenge, runs every login refusal again and only then creates the session. The log-in route's own per-IP and per-login buckets apply to both steps.
3. New recovery codes need the password and an authenticator code; turning it off needs the password and a code or a recovery code. A founder impersonating can do none of it (`mfa.manage`).
4. A login record created after an enrolment (a deleted account's address registered again) ignores that older enrolment (`bornAt`). Since rule 2990 (2026-10-22) the birth is one rule for sign-in and for the session's own settings (`loginBornAt`, src/lib/own-password.ts): a legacy owner's account `createdAt`, a legacy member's record `acceptedAt`, else the PERSON's `createdAt`. Never the account or membership the session acts in: with multi-workspace on, a second workspace created after the enrolment, or one joined after it, would make the enrolment look older than the login, so a password alone would sign in and a new setup (or a passkey add, or an address change) would pass without the current code.

### 80.2 Signed-in devices (rules 1938-1941)

Code in `src/lib/devices.ts`, `src/lib/device-label.ts`, `src/app/api/account/sessions/route.ts`, `src/components/app/settings/DeviceList.tsx`.
1. `createSession` records the session in `devs:{login}` (a hash keyed by a device id derived from the session key; the value holds the session's Redis key, which is already a hash of the cookie, the browser string cut to 300 characters, the client address and the time; 50 at most, 30 days); impersonation sessions are never recorded. Signing out removes the entry; entries whose session has ended drop out on the next read.
2. Settings, Security lists them with a plain label («كروم على ماك» / "Chrome on Mac"), the last sign-in and «هذا الجهاز» / "This device". DELETE ends one other device with the password (the current one is refused: that is signing out). "Sign out other devices" (POST, rule 1150) stays.

### 80.3 Changing the sign-in address (rules 1942-1945)

Code in `src/lib/email-change.ts`, `moveMemberLogin` in `src/lib/team.ts`, `moveOwnerLogin` in `src/lib/verify-email.ts` (the core shared with the typo fix of rules 763-765), `src/app/api/account/email/route.ts`, `src/components/app/settings/EmailChange.tsx`.
1. Settings, Account, the email card: «غيّر البريد» / "Change email" asks for the new address and the password. A 6-digit code goes to the NEW address (15 minutes, 5 tries); nothing changes until it comes back. A taken address (owner or member) is refused.
2. The code moves the login under `lock:login` of both addresses: a member's record, roster entry, active brand, two-step enrolment and Google link; an owner's account record (marked proved, since the code proved the new address), referral code, partner link, two-step enrolment and Google link. Pending invites a member sent are dropped; nothing keeps naming the old login. Every device of the old login is signed out, this device gets a new session, and the OLD address gets a notice naming the new one.
3. Limit. An owner's data is keyed by the address today (`sc(u)` falls back to the email), so only an owner whose account holds no work yet (no brand record, tasks, chats, files, connections, members, paid plan, card or partner data, and none of Reem's items, agent instructions, settings, notification choices, working days or activity, decision, handoff and note lists; the daily run's own `schedule:` write does not count) can move here; any other gets «تغيير بريد المالك لحساب فيه شغل يمر عن طريق الدعم الحين» / "changed through support for now". Separating the account id from the address is the follow-up that lifts it.

### 80.4 Sign in with Google (rules 1946-1953)

Code in `src/lib/google-signin.ts`, `src/app/api/auth/google/start/route.ts`, `src/app/api/auth/google/callback/route.ts`, `src/app/api/account/google/route.ts`, `src/components/app/AuthForm.tsx`, `src/components/app/settings/GoogleLink.tsx`; spec `docs/specs/2026-10-13-google-sign-in.md`.
1. Off until `GOOGLE_SIGNIN_CLIENT_ID` and `GOOGLE_SIGNIN_CLIENT_SECRET` are both set (variables.md section 2): the routes answer 404 and no button is drawn.
2. «ادخل بحساب Google» / "Sign in with Google" on the login page and «سجّل بحساب Google» / "Sign up with Google" on the signup page goes to /api/auth/google/start: a state (cookie-bound, 10 minutes, used once), a PKCE S256 verifier and a nonce, then Google's consent page with the fixed callback `{APP_ORIGIN}/api/auth/google/callback` and the scopes `openid email profile`.
3. The callback trades the code at Google's fixed token address and verifies the ID token. A Google account already linked opens its login. Otherwise: an owner whose address is proved is linked and signed in; an unproved owner, a member, or an owner linked to another Google account is refused with a message to sign in with the password and link from settings; an unknown address is refused on the login page and, on the signup page, becomes a new owner whose address is already proved (trial, referral and partner as a password signup). Every sign-in passes `finishLogin`, so a suspended account is refused and two-step sign-in still asks for its code.
4. Settings, Security: «اربط حساب Google» / "Link a Google account" links the signed-in login (owner or member) to the Google account it picks; linking and unlinking both need the login's current password. Since 2026-10-19 (rule 2302) linking starts with `POST /api/account/google {action: "link", password, lang}`, which checks the password (the same 10-per-hour bucket as unlinking) and answers Google's address for the card to open; `GET /api/auth/google/start?mode=link` answers 405 `use_post`, so the password never rides in a URL. A password reset and a password change drop the login's link (rule 2301): a link someone added while they held the account does not outlive the password. An account opened with Google sets a password through "forgot password" before it can unlink.

### 80.5 Account deletion and Paymob (rules 1954-1956)

1. `wipeAccount` first calls `stopRenewals` (rule 1955): under the account lock, renewal turns off and the saved card token is deleted, before anything slow can fail. A renewal that read the card before that re-checks the account, renewal and card under the same lock right before it charges (`chargeSavedCard`, rule 1956) and stops with `renewal_off`.
2. The wipe also deletes every login's two-step, address-change, Google link and device records and ends every recorded session (rule 1954), and, since customer passkeys (section 100.4, rule 2425), the person's passkey set, the `pkcred:` entries that still name its pid and an open `pkreg:` registration.

### 80.6 Releases

All keys are new (`mfa:*`, `mfa:pending:*`, `mfacode:*`, `mfaotp:used:*`, `mfachal:*`, `devs:*`, `emailchange:*`, `gsi:state:*`, `google:*`, `googlesub:*`) and no older code reads them, so this is one release with no contract step (rule 679). Before Google sign-in is switched on, the founder creates the OAuth client and sets the two names in hPanel (the pull request lists the steps).

## 81. UX rollout wave 1b: toasts, empty states, skeletons, route progress, URL filters, settings groups (2026-10-17, branch `claude/ux-rollout-1b-db68f5`, rules 2000-2007)

Supastarter gap study 2026-10-16 (UX rollout, wave 1b), built with our own primitives and no new dependency; supastarter was read as a UX reference only. No route, permission, environment variable or stored shape changes. Tests in `docs/system/tests/2000-ux-rollout.md`.

1. Feedback (rules 2000-2001). No `alert()`, `confirm()` or `prompt()` in `src`: the inbox's two publish failures go to `toast.danger`. Across the workspace panels a success is a `toast.success` and a failure not tied to a field is a `toast.danger` (`useToast`, `src/components/ui/Toast.tsx`, mounted by the app layout only; the founder console keeps its inline lines). A field or validation error, a refusal the person must act on (limits, no credits, no permission, a link to follow) and a blocking error stay inline with `role="alert"`. `SettingsCard` sends an `ok` status to the toast once per status object and keeps an `error` status inline (`statusTarget`). No secret, code or token is ever put in a toast.
2. Empty states (rule 2002). Panels with nothing to show render `EmptyState` (`compact` inside a card or a table) with the copy in `src/lib/empty-copy.ts`: a title and one next step, Hijazi white Arabic and English, no numbers, customers or promised results. `appDict[lang].lang` names the dictionary's language, so a panel given only `t` picks the matching copy.
3. Skeletons (rule 2003). A panel waiting for its first data shows `PanelSkeleton` (rows, cards or one line) inside one polite `role="status"` region with the loading words for screen readers; the shapes are hidden from them and stop moving under reduced motion. A fetch that fails ends the skeleton.
4. Route progress (rule 2005). `src/instrumentation-client.ts` exports `onRouterTransitionStart`: a navigation to another path or query on this origin sends the window event `thakeen:route-start`. `RouteProgress` (in the language layout, after `ThemeScope`) runs `progressStore` from `src/lib/route-progress.ts`: nothing for 150 ms, then a 3px bar in `--color-primary` across the top (mirrored in Arabic, `aria-hidden`), finished when the path or the query changes, faded in 250 ms, ended by itself after 10 s. Under reduced motion it does not animate.
5. URL filters (rule 2007). `useUrlQuery(codec, typed)` in `src/components/admin/DataTable.tsx` reads a table's query from the address through a codec that validates every value (`src/lib/url-query.ts`; the users list uses `parseUserQuery`/`userQueryParams`). A filter, sort or page change is a history step (`router.push`), typed search text replaces the step, any change but a page goes back to page 1, and an address with a value the codec rejects (or an unknown parameter) is replaced by the canonical one. The routes read and check the query again as before (founder-only, `isAdmin`); nothing here widens what they return.
6. Settings groups (rule 2006). `SETTINGS_GROUPS` labels the same ten sections in two groups, «أنت» / "You" (account, security, notifications) and «مساحة العمل» / "Workspace" (brand, brands, members, billing, connections, governance, danger). The index list shows each group under an `h2`, the side column under a label that names its list. Paths and order are unchanged.

### 81.1 Releases

No stored shape changes: one release, no expand/contract step.

## 82. File splits, wave 2a: secretary, chat and the in-app dictionary (2026-10-17, branch `claude/wave2-file-splits-fa14bc`, rules 2010-2015)

Supastarter gap study 2026-10-16, wave 2a: three modules grew past what one person can hold in their head, so each was cut into cohesive modules. A pure move: every statement keeps its text, nothing is renamed, and the original path re-exports every public name it had. No route, permission, environment variable, Redis key or stored shape changes. Tests in `docs/system/tests/2010-file-splits.md`.

1. `src/lib/secretary.ts` (1800 lines) is a barrel of 37 lines over nine modules, in import order: `secretary-keys` (ids, `SEC_KEYS`, the per-scope limits; imports nothing local), `secretary-zone` (zone, work calendar, away map), `secretary-input` (cleaning, parsing, `fail`), `secretary-mutate` (`OWNER_ONLY`, `mutateItem`), `secretary-store` (item reads and writes, `withLock`, `listOpenItems`), `secretary-change` (`changeItem`, reminders, series spawn), `secretary-read` (agenda, pages, purge, daily sweep), `secretary-owner` (snooze presets, owner change parsing) and `secretary-tools` (the chat and MCP tool definitions and `runSecretaryTool`). `listOpenItems` sits in `secretary-store` so that `secretary-change` and `secretary-read` need not import each other.
2. `src/lib/chat.ts` (1101 lines) keeps the turn itself, `chat()`, at 215 lines and re-exports the rest from six modules: `chat-history` (message types and history lists), `chat-tools` (tool definitions), `chat-prompt` (the system prompt), `chat-reply` (reply parts and statuses), `chat-gates` (the role, plan and limit gates) and `chat-run-tool` (`runTool`, which executes a tool call). No part imports `chat.ts`.
3. `src/lib/app-i18n.ts` (1231 lines) is 26 lines: `appDict` joins five area parts in order, `app-i18n-shell`, `-workspace`, `-pages`, `-todo` and `-team`. Each part declares its Arabic object and types its English against it (`enX: typeof arX`), and the join types the whole English against the whole Arabic (`const en: typeof ar`), so a missing key in either language is still a compile error. The 37 top-level keys keep their order.
4. Layering. The modules form a one-way graph (rule 2013) and each stays under 450 lines (rule 2014). The next candidates are `secretary-agenda.ts` and `secretary-calendar.ts`.
5. Tests that read source text now read the module that holds the code (rule 2015); no assertion was weakened. `tests/file-splits.test.ts` pins the exported names of `@/lib/secretary` and `@/lib/chat` against the lists captured from main 50ff790b.

### 82.1 Releases

No stored shape changes: one release, no expand/contract step.

## 83. Typed Redis key registry and read validation (2026-10-17, branch `claude/wave2-key-registry-7ec6c2`, rules 2030-2039)

Supastarter gap study 2026-10-16, wave 2b. Files: new `src/lib/keys.ts`, new `src/lib/stored.ts`; key strings only in `src/lib/{auth,brands,brand,account,team,conversations,mcp,privacy-export,email-change,oauth,track,referral,schedule,usage,governance,perf-store,partners,files,evals,store-sales,rate,onboard-confirm,notifications,google-signin,stats,social-stats,site-stats,routines,margin,handoffs,feedback,decisions,admin-stats}.ts`, `src/lib/connectors/store.ts`, `src/lib/social-read/common.ts`, five API routes and the chat page. `src/lib/secretary*.ts`, `src/lib/chat.ts` and `src/lib/app-i18n.ts` are left for the wave 2a split. Tests: `tests/key-registry.test.ts`, `tests/key-registry-ratchet.test.ts`, `tests/stored-shapes.test.ts`; coverage `docs/system/tests/2030-key-registry.md`.

1. A request names its workspace through `sc(u)` only. `sc` returns a `Scope`, the branded string every `wsKey.*` builder requires (rule 2031). A body, query, header, path or cookie value is a plain `string` and does not type-check as a key, so a route cannot key a workspace record by what the caller sent.
2. Code that walks an account's own brands (`wipeAccount`, `buildExport`, `ownerHasData`) builds each scope with `workspaceScope(email, brandId)` over `listBrands(email)` for the signed-in owner (rule 2032). A scope read back from our own records uses `storedScope`. Neither appears under `src/app`.
3. Lib helpers whose scope parameter is still `string` (`getTask`, `saveConnection`, `readFile`, ...) turn it into a `Scope` with `scopeParam` where they build the key (rule 2033). Their callers already pass `sc(u)`; narrowing the parameters is a later pass, and the ratchet counts these calls (90 when this landed).
4. Every builder returns the bytes of the template it replaced (rule 2030): no key moves, nothing to migrate. A raw key template written outside `keys.ts` counts against its file's number in the ratchet test, which may only go down (rule 2034; 801 before, 524 after this change, in 179 files).
5. Reads of four shapes are checked (rules 2035-2039): the brand list (row by row; a row without an id is left out, a missing status is active), the active brand (missing or malformed is `default`; a numeric-looking id the Redis client read back as a number is text again), a connection (without `enc` it reads as not connected instead of failing in `decrypt`), a conversation (without an id it is absent; a missing count is 0). A record that does not fit is logged as `stored shape invalid` with the shape name, field paths and zod codes, never the key, the account or a value, and the reader gets the safe default. Nothing throws on old data.

### 83.1 Releases

No stored shape changes and no key changes: one release, no expand/contract step (rule 679). The read schemas accept every shape written so far and keep unknown fields (`looseObject`), so the next write of a record carries the fields a newer release added. A brand row without an id is dropped on the next brand-list write; such a row could not name a scope before either. No permission or environment variable change; `zod` is already a dependency.

## 84. Admin workspace view and suspension end dates (2026-10-17, branch `claude/wave2-admin-team-suspension-853778`, rules 2070-2083)

Supastarter gap study 2026-10-16 (wave 2d, admin console). Supastarter's admin organization page was read as a pattern only; no source is copied. One new route, no new Redis key, no new environment variable. Tests in `docs/system/tests/2070-admin-team-suspension.md`.

1. Suspend with an end date (rules 2070, 2074, 2076). In the account sheet, Suspend asks "For how long?": no end date, 7 days, 30 days, or until a date (a calendar day; the account opens at the start of that day, Riyadh time, at least tomorrow and at most 365 days away). The console sends `for` and `date`; `parseSuspendFor` turns them into the value stored in the account's existing `suspended` field: `true` (no end date) or `{ until: ISO }`. A broken choice is 400 `bad_until` and nothing changes. The audit row of `suspend` and `unsuspend` has `before` and `after` as `{suspended, until}`. The Users list, the account detail and the sheet show "Suspended · until …" from the API's `suspended` boolean and `suspendedUntil`.
2. Read-time expiry (rules 2071, 2072). `isSuspended(u, now)` in `src/lib/suspension.ts` is the only reader of the stored field (a test fails on any other `.suspended` read in `src/lib` or `src/app`). `{ until }` blocks only while `until` is in the future; `true` and any value this code does not know (a broken date included) block, so the check fails closed. Every place that used to read `u.suspended` (`currentUser`, sign-in, the two-step and Google sign-in finish, password reset, MCP tokens, the team checks, entitlements, the admin statistics, evals, margin, the daily schedule and Reem's reminders, series, calendar feed and Google calendar) reads it through `isSuspended`. No cron is needed for an account to open again.
3. Expiry tidy and audit (rule 2073). When a stored suspension has ended, the next sign-in of the account or of one of its members (`finishLogin`) and staff opening the account (`GET /api/admin/users/{email}`) call `endExpiredSuspension`: under the account lock it re-checks, writes `suspended = false` and one `suspension.expired` row (actor `system`, `before {suspended: true, until}`, `after {suspended: false}`). Two sign-ins at once write one row; a suspension renewed in between is never cleared. Best effort: a failure is logged and the sign-in goes on. Recommended follow-up, not built: the daily cron could call `endExpiredSuspension` for every account whose `until` passed, so the log shows the expiry within a day even when nobody signs in. Blocking does not depend on it.
4. The customer's message (rule 2075). A suspended sign-in answers 403 `{ ok: false, error: "suspended", until? }`; `until` is there only for a timed suspension. The sign-in, two-step and password-reset forms show `SUSPENDED_UNTIL` with the date and time in Riyadh: «حسابك موقوف لحد … بتوقيت الرياض، وبعدها يرجع يشتغل تلقائي. لو عندك سؤال كلّمنا على واتساب.» / "This account is suspended until …, Riyadh time. It opens again by itself after that. Questions? Contact us on WhatsApp." Without an end date the forms keep their old message.
5. Workspace view (rules 2077-2082). The account sheet has a sixth tab, Workspace. Opening it calls `GET /api/admin/users/{email}/workspace` once: the wrapper's console gate (401), `staffMay(consoleRole(req), "workspace.view")` (owner and support; anything else 403), the account found from the chosen address with `getUser`, or for a member's address `getMember` then the owner named on that stored record (404 when neither), one `workspace.view` audit row (`extra.openedFrom` for a member's address), then the answer. It shows the owner (verified, suspended until, last sign-in, last active, joined), the plan (credits, trial end, seats used of the limit, active brands of the cap), the members (role, invited by, joined, paused, last sign-in or "Not recorded"), the pending invites (role, invited by, sent, expires; no id or token) and the brands (active or archived). All reads run in one `Promise.all` and one `pmap`; `peekInvites` and `lastSignIn` read without tidying, so opening the view writes nothing but the audit row.
6. Console panel (rule 2083). `WorkspacePanel` uses the wave 1b parts: `PanelSkeleton` while loading, `EmptyState` for no members, invites or brands, `ErrorState` for a failed read (with Try again), a forbidden role or an unknown address. Text only. Its words live in `src/components/admin/users-copy.ts` (`ws`), not in `app-i18n.ts`, which a sibling branch is splitting.

### 84.1 Releases

Expand step of rule 679, and the only step needed. The new code reads `true`, `false`, absent and `{ until }`, and writes `{ until }` only for a timed suspension. Code from before this release reads `suspended` only for its truth, so a `{ until }` record still blocks there (it ends at the next unsuspend instead of at its date, never earlier), and every old write replaces the whole field with `true` or `false`, so an old end date can never outlive an old unsuspend. `true` stays the shape of an indefinite suspension, so there is no contract step and no data conversion.

---
## 85. Payment provider and storage adapter seams (2026-10-17, branch `claude/wave2-payment-storage-seam-6c1d1e`, rules 2050-2063)

Supastarter gap study 2026-10-16, wave 2c: two interfaces, no behaviour change. The provider type copies the idea of supastarter's payments package (one type, one implementation chosen in one place), not its code. Paymob stays off exactly as before, until its PAYMOB_* variables are set. No route, permission, environment variable, Redis key or stored shape changes. Tests in `docs/system/tests/2050-payment-storage-seams.md`.

1. Payment provider (rules 2050-2057). Billing (`src/lib/billing.ts`) reaches a card processor only through `PaymentProvider` (`src/lib/payments/provider.ts`): `createCheckout`, `savedCard.prepare` / `savedCard.charge` (null when the deployment cannot charge a saved card), `parseEvent`, `verifyWebhook`, `parseTransaction`, `parseCardToken` and `refund`. `paymentProvider()` in `src/lib/payments/index.ts` is the one place it is chosen; Paymob KSA (`paymobProvider()` in `src/lib/paymob.ts`) is the only implementation and answers null while `paymobConfig()` does. Routes and pages ask `paymentsEnabled()` instead of reading the Paymob gate. The keys stay in the provider's closures, never on the object. Errors are `PaymentError` (`PaymobError` extends it with the same messages).
2. What stays in billing, unchanged: the amount, currency and live check against our own `priceFor` (a mismatch applies nothing), the account taken from our intent record, never from a callback, and exactly-once handling per transaction (`paymob:done:{id}` and `paymob:txlock:{id}`). The webhook order is the same: no provider gives 404, a body that is not an envelope gives 400, a bad signature gives 401 before any field is used, then a card token is saved or a transaction applied. The signature is Paymob's HMAC-SHA512 over its 20 transaction fields (8 for a card token) in the documented order, compared with `sameSecret`.
3. Storage adapter (rules 2058-2062). The media store (`src/lib/media/store.ts`) keeps every decision (download through `safeFetch`, magic bytes, JPEG conversion, size cap, quota and free-space guard, the index `mfile:{scope}:{name}` under `sc(u)`'s scope) and reaches the bytes only through `StorageAdapter` (`src/lib/media/storage.ts`): `configured`, `put`, `get`, `stream`, `head`, `delete`, `url`, `freeBytes` and `probe`. `storage()` is the one place it is chosen; the private disk folder (`src/lib/media/disk.ts`, rules 1750-1763) is the only implementation, with the same write order (`.tmp/{name}.part` opened `wx` 0600, fsynced, hard-linked, never overwritten). An adapter sees a name that passed `MEDIA_NAME` and bytes, never a scope or a request. The `/m/` route checks the share link or the member's index entry first, then asks the adapter for `head` and `stream`.
4. Not built: the Hostinger disk + Drive archive backend (draft PR #320, rules 991-1015) would be a second `StorageAdapter`; a second payment processor would be a second `PaymentProvider` chosen in `paymentProvider()`.

### 85.1 Releases

No stored shape changes: one release, no expand/contract step.

---
## 86. Tooling, lint and measured performance (2026-10-17, branch `claude/wave2-perf-a11y-lint-cced78`, rules 2090-2101)

Wave 2e of the supastarter gap study. Nothing a customer sees changes; the checks that guard the code get stricter and the build gets smaller.

1. jsx-a11y (rules 2090-2093). `eslint.config.mjs` applies the plugin's recommended rules to `src/**/*.tsx` through `a11yRules()` in `eslint-rules/tooling-rules.mjs`. The plugin is a direct devDependency; `eslint-config-next` already registered it with six rules as warnings. A rule with no violation is an `error` and fails `npm run lint`. A rule with violations (29 in all: modal backdrops, autofocus in dialogs and sign-in, two videos without captions) is a `warn` and sits in `eslint-rules/ratchet.json`.
2. Type-aware rules (rules 2094-2097). `src/**/*.{ts,tsx}` is linted with `projectService` against `tsconfig.json`: `no-floating-promises` and `no-misused-promises` (not for JSX attributes) are errors, `await-thenable` is a warning with ten hits. A promise nobody waits for in an event handler now says `void` on the call, so the next reader can tell it was meant.
3. The ratchet (rules 2092, 2096). `tests/lint-ratchet.test.ts` lints `src` through the ESLint API and fails on any lint error or any ratcheted rule above its count. After a clean-up, `node scripts/lint-ratchet.mjs --write` lowers `ratchet.json`; a rule that reaches 0 is deleted there and loses its entry in `A11Y_RATCHETED`/`TYPE_RATCHETED`, which makes it an error. The count never goes up in a change that is not also removing a violation.
4. Measured performance (rules 2098-2101). `scripts/route-sizes.mjs` reads a `next build --webpack` output (Next 16 prints no First Load JS column): shared runtime, each route's own page and layout chunks, and the total, all gzip. Two changes moved the numbers: the founder skills console no longer imports the whole dictionary (the page passes `pickSkillsDict(appDict[lang])`, rule 2098), and `MeetingPanel` loads on the first click (rule 2099). The public site has no raster image to optimise (rule 2101); `next/image` stays out of the inbox and library, which show media from hosts that are not ours.

### 86.1 Releases

No stored shape changes: one release, no expand/contract step. The lockfile gains one root `devDependencies` line (the package was already locked at 6.10.2).

## 87. Help centre stable URLs (2026-10-18, branch `claude/wave3-help-urls-4919c5`, rules 2140-2149)

Wave 3b of the supastarter gap study. supastarter's docs give every page its own URL; the pattern is ported onto the help centre's own data (`src/lib/help.ts`), and no supastarter code is copied.

1. One page per topic (rules 2140, 2143). `/{lang}/help/{topic}` (`src/app/[lang]/help/[topic]/page.tsx`) is built at build time for every id in `HELP` and both languages; any other id calls `notFound()`, which `[lang]/not-found.tsx` draws. Its title is the topic's name, its description the topic's own questions (at most 160 characters), its canonical and hreflang come from `pageMeta`. `/{lang}/help` stays the index with search and topic pills; each topic heading there links its page.
2. Stable anchors (rules 2141, 2142). Every answer has a hand-written `slug`, never made from the question text, so a reworded question keeps its links. `tests/help-urls.test.ts` pins every slug: renaming, moving or dropping one fails it. On the topic page each answer is a `<section id={slug}>`; on the index each `<details id={slug}>` opens and scrolls into view when the hash names it. Both show a "Link to this answer" link.
3. Search and machines (rules 2144, 2145). The sitemap lists each topic page in both languages (anchors are not pages), and `/llms.txt` follows it with a section named by the resources card's own "Help center" title, each link the topic's title. The topic page carries FAQPage JSON-LD from `helpFaqJsonLd` (`src/lib/home-ld.ts`): each question and answer exactly as shown, serialised with `ldJson`, which escapes `<`.
4. In-app help (rules 2146, 2147). `helpHref(lang, topic, slug?)` is typed from `HELP`: an unknown topic, or a slug from another topic, fails `tsc`. `HelpLink` (`src/components/ui/HelpLink.tsx`, "How does this work?", new tab) uses it on Settings, Billing (`credits#task-costs`), Security (`privacy#two-step-and-devices`), Brand file (`start#dialect-and-tone`) and Connections (`connect#platforms`), and `EmptyState` draws it for the empty inbox, files, to-dos, reports and activity (`emptyHelp` in `src/lib/empty-copy.ts`).
5. One new answer (rule 2148): "How do I turn on two-step sign-in and see my devices?" in Privacy and account, written from the Security screen's own copy. It is `chat: false`, so the chat prompt keeps its size (rule 1328 now pins four help-page-only answers). `src/lib/help.ts` still imports only the language type and the import-free pricing and task tables (rule 2149), so the client help centre can read it.

### 87.1 Releases

No stored shape changes: one release, no expand/contract step. The help data gains a field in code only.

## 88. Account id separate from the sign-in address, release R1 (2026-10-18, branch `claude/wave3-account-id-r1`, rules 1980-1999)

The expand step of `docs/specs/2026-10-17-account-id.md`, with the nine changes `docs/specs/2026-10-18-multi-org.md` forces on it. Section 87 is the help centre (#386). Nothing a customer sees changes: every account's id is still its first address (`OPAQUE_IDS = false` in `src/lib/account-id.ts`), so every key on production keeps its bytes. What changes is which value the code builds keys from, so that R2 can give new accounts an opaque id and move an owner's address in place.

1. The account id (rules 1980, 1985). `User.id` is stored on every account record `saveUser` writes, and `acct(u) = u.id ?? u.email` is the only value account keys are built from: `user:`, `brands:`, `members:`, `invites:`, `lock:team:`, `lock:user:`, notifications, referrals, usage, credits, chat counters, the default scope. `sc(u) = u.scope ?? acct(u)`. `getAccount(id)` reads `user:{id}` and trusts the key (a stored id that differs is replaced in memory and logged); `getUser` is its alias. The per-address rate buckets (`allow(...)`) stay on the address on purpose. A test can make an account with an opaque id (`createUser(input, { opaque: true })`, `newOpaqueUser`); production cannot.
2. The login index (rules 1981-1983, multi-org change 1). `login:{address}` = `{kind:"person", pid}` names the person who signs in with that address: an owner's pid is its account id, a member's is its frozen `Member.id` (its first address in R1, multi-org change 2). `claimLogin` writes it under `lock:login:{address}` after `getLogin` found nothing (signup, Google sign-in, invite acceptance, an email change); `releaseLogin(address, pid)` deletes it only while it still names that pid. `getLogin(address)` reads the index first and accepts an account only while its `email` is still the address (`ownsLogin`), or a member whose `memberPid` matches (`memberHolds`). With no index (every account from before R1) it falls back to `user:{address}`, accepted only when the record's `email` is the address and its id is unset or the address. A malformed index is logged (`parseStored`, zod `LoginIndex`) and read as none. The account-id spec's first shape `{kind:"owner", id}` is read too.
3. Sessions (rule 1984, multi-org change 3). `createSession` adds `pid` and `acct` (the active workspace: the owner's own account, or a member's team). `resolveSession` reads `login:`, `user:{acct or address}`, `member:` and `activebrand:` in one round; a session whose `pid` the index no longer names, or whose `acct` is not the login's workspace, resolves to nothing. A session from before R1 (`{email, at}`) resolves as before; one more round runs only when it has no `acct` and the index names an account stored elsewhere (only test accounts in R1).
4. Scopes (rule 1986). `accountOfScope(scope)` and `brandOfScope(scope)` are the only parsers of `{account}#{brand}`; the 17 former `split("#")[0]` sites call them.
5. Paymob (rule 1991). An intent stores `acct` (the account), `billingEmail` (the address Paymob sees) and keeps `email` = the account id for an older build. `intentAccount(i) = i.acct ?? i.email` and `intentBillingEmail(i) = i.billingEmail ?? i.email` read both shapes; the card-token callback compares against `billingEmail`. A txn record adds `acct`.
6. Moves (R1 is still a copy). An owner's address change (`moveOwnerLogin`, allowed only with no data, `ownerHasData`) saves the copy with `id = to`, claims `login:{to}` and wipes the old account by its id. A member's move saves `id = to`, claims the new index and releases the old.
7. Deletion (rule 1999). `wipeAccount(id)` takes the account id, finds the address on the record, and deletes the owner's and every member's index only where it names them; the deletion list row is `{id, email, at}`.
8. The founder console's user page resolves the address in its URL with `getLogin` and acts on `acct(u)`; so do the routes that name an account by an address in the URL or body (`accountByLogin` in `src/lib/auth.ts`: top-ups, payments, plan, fair use, founding, brand cap, agent-scope release). A member's address there is 404.
9. Security review fixes (2026-10-18, founder-approved). M1: while `OPAQUE_IDS` is off a member's id is its address, whatever is stored (`getMember` replaces a stored id that differs, in memory, and logs only the shape); the script counts such a member as a conflict and writes no index for it; signup refuses an address that still has a member record, under the login lock, as Google sign-in does. M2: the MCP token slot, OAuth codes and grants, the chat trial and monthly counters, partner self-referral and the referral set, the last checkout, payment history, the Paymob renewal, mark-all-read, the reset route's roster, every `memberOf` check (account, sessions, profile, password, login) and the seat enforcement key by `acct(u)`; per-address rate buckets stay on the address. L1: `releaseLogin` re-reads and deletes the index under `lock:login:{address}`; callers already holding it (the member move, the owner move's wipe) pass `locked`/`loginLocked`. Lock order is team, then member, then login, so nothing waits on itself.

### 88.1 Releases

R1 = **expand** (rule 679). New shapes: `User.id`, `Member.id`, `login:*`, session `pid`/`acct`, intent `acct`/`billingEmail`, txn `acct`. An older build ignores all of them, and `GET /api/profile` never sends `id` (rule 787), so rolling back is safe. New signups, invites and moves write both; older records get theirs from `scripts/account-ids.ts`, run by hand once R1 is live and only on the founder's yes (it writes production data):

```bash
npx --no-install jiti scripts/account-ids.ts
```

is the dry run (read-only, prints counts); `--apply` writes. It is idempotent, never overwrites, and exits 1 when it finds a conflict or an orphan. Since 2026-10-24 the same function also runs from the founder console with no terminal (section 125). Nothing reads the new shapes as required before R2, so a run that stops halfway is harmless. R2 (contract: `OPAQUE_IDS` on, the owner move in place, the legacy fallback removed) needs R1 live for 7 days, a run with 0 conflicts and 0 orphans, the rule 1985 source scans clean (`tests/account-id-r1.test.ts`), and a Paymob check on staging.

### 88.2 Release R2, built behind the constant (2026-10-21, branch `claude/wave3a-account-id-r2`, rules 2740-2749)

Everything R2 needs is in code and runs only while `OPAQUE_IDS` (`src/lib/account-id.ts`) is `true`. The constant stays `false` on this branch, so nothing a customer sees changes; the flip is a later one-line pull request. **Since the enable release (section 93.4, rules 3810-3822) the constant is `true`.** The tests force it on (`tests/account-id-r2.test.ts`; `tests/account-id-r2-both-flags.test.ts` also turns on `MULTI_ORG`, multi-workspace decision 11: both flags turn on together with no further code).

1. New accounts are opaque (rule 2740). `createUser` gives an `a_` id while the constant is on. L3: a legacy owner that moved keeps its key, so `user:{old}` and `person:{old}` hold the NEW address; while they do, `addressBlocked(old)` answers `moved` and nothing may write a record at that key again: a legacy invite acceptance refuses `unavailable` (unless `MULTI_ORG` makes a `p_` person), a member's address move refuses `exists`, and `createUser` makes an opaque id for that address. `addressBlocked` is read whatever the constant says, so the guard holds even if the constant is ever turned off again after accounts moved. A signup there gets a new opaque account.
2. The move in place (rule 2741, `movePersonLogin` in `src/lib/email-move.ts`). Called by the address change (`confirmEmailChange`, gated on the proof only, three a day) and the typo fix (`moveUnverifiedAccount`, gated on `gatedSignup` and not verified, no proof, no hold). Locks: both `lock:login` (alphabetical), `lock:person:{pid}` (60 s), then each owned account's `lock:user`. Re-checked under them: `login:{old}` names this person, the target is nobody else's login, member record or hold, the old address is not a partner's, the caller's gate, the day's limit. Writes in this order: `login:{to}`; every owned account (`id` kept, `email`, a fresh `sessionsAfter`, `emailMoves`, the proof); the person row with the same stamp; the per-login carry (two-step, `activebrand:{login}`, the Google link, the legacy feed and booking pointers and their records' `login`, each linked calendar's `login`); the target's leftovers dropped; the hold and revert token (a proved move); the old address's devices, codes, pending change, two-step and active brand dropped; `login:{old}` released. Nothing is copied or wiped: the person id, every account id and scope, `users:all`, referral and partner keys and passkeys (keyed by the person) stay. A legacy member record (`member:{login}`) keeps R1's copy (`moveMemberLogin`). A stop part way leaves a state the retry finishes: `login:{to}` already naming this person counts as a retry, and a left-over `login:{old}` signs nothing in because the person's address is already the new one (rule 1981).
3. A partner's address is refused (rule 2742): `confirm` answers 409 `partner`; the programme is keyed by the address and support moves it.
4. The hold (rule 2743, decision D1, `src/lib/email-hold.ts`). A proved move writes `emailhold:{old}` = `{pid, to, at, until}` (30 days). While the person exists nobody else may take the address: signup 409, Google sign-up `google_taken`, invite acceptance `unavailable`, another login's change `taken`. The person may move back (the hold goes).
5. The revert (rule 2744). The notice to the old address carries `/{lang}/app/reset#r=<token>` (fragment, 64 hex, 7 days, stored as `emailrevert:{sha256}`). `ResetForm` changes nothing on load (mail scanners run scripts): it reads the token, takes it out of the address bar and shows a confirm screen, and only its button posts it to `POST /api/auth/email-revert` (public, 10 per IP per 15 min). The token is read first (`peekRevert`), and used up with GETDEL only under the locks once every check passed (`claimRevert`); a failure after that puts it back (`restoreRevert`), so a busy lock or a refused check leaves the link usable, and a second click finds it gone. Ruling (review 2026-10-21): under D1 the holder of the old mailbox is the rightful owner, so the revert fails toward them. The person moves back in place (not counted, no new hold) and then, under the same locks: every session ends, the password becomes 32 unknown random bytes (`forgetPassword` in `src/lib/person.ts`), EVERY passkey goes in one batch (`removeAllPasskeys`), two-step enrolled after the move or within 7 days before it goes, the Google link goes, and every MCP token, calendar feed, booking link and marketing calendar link of the owned accounts and their members ends (the calls the reset uses). `emailreverted:{pid}` makes the token of any move made after the reverted one dead (the hijacker's own move cannot be reverted back to them). The answer is a one-hour `pwreset:` token for the old address, which the reset form uses at once.
6. Two-step for the change (rule 2745, D2). While the constant is on, `start` on a login with two-step on needs its code (`mfaCode`, a recovery code too): without it 401 `mfa_required`, a wrong one 400 `bad_code`. The settings card asks for it. Known gap: a change started before the flip and confirmed within its 15 minutes after it skips the code (the confirm does not ask again). Another: a move stopped between the account writes and the carry leaves two-step at `mfa:{old}` until the retry carries it.
7. `ownerHasData` (rule 2746) still refuses an owner's move while the constant is off (R1's copy would lose the work) and is skipped while it is on. It and R1's copy are removed in the contract release after the flip.
8. Left for R2 (rules 2747, 2748). The script's `claimIfAbsent` re-reads the record under the login lock and writes no index for one that no longer holds the address (L2); a moved or opaque account is checked, not counted as a conflict, and its index is never written by the script. The founder console's user rows carry `signsIn` (L4: a badge when the stored address does not sign in to that account). `listPartners` resolves a partner's account with `accountByLogin`. Referral rows carry `id` (and `email` for older builds). Wishlist votes are keyed by `acct(u)` (the same bytes for every account made before the flip); `wipeAccount` forgets by id and by the current address.

Release step: **expand** (rule 679) with the constant off. Rollback floor unchanged: nothing new is written while it is off (referral rows gain an `id` field older builds ignore; the wishlist key is the same bytes for every existing account; console rows gain a field). The flip pull request later carries "rollback floor: this R2-expand release" (not R1: an R1 build has no L3 guard and writes a moved owner's `user:{old}` again; this release is above R1 and M1, so it covers both). Preconditions for the flip, not for merging this branch: R1 live 7 days (from 2026-10-19, so on or after 2026-10-26), `scripts/account-ids.ts` reports 0 conflicts, and the Paymob or Geidea saved-card check of the spec's section 7 passes on staging.

## 89. Money decisions of 2026-10-19: card after a refund, a hand-set plan's end, a short batch (branch `claude/money-decisions-19oct`, rules 2270-2280)

Files: `src/lib/billing.ts` (`endPlanOnRefund`, `saveCard`), `src/lib/hand-plan.ts` (`handPlanEnded`), `src/lib/hand-plan-end.ts` (`endHandPlanIfDue`), `src/lib/auth.ts` (`trialOver`), `src/lib/entitlements.ts`, `src/app/api/cron/daily/route.ts`, `src/lib/generate-batch.ts` (`batchKeeps`), `src/lib/credits.ts` (`refundOncePart`). Founder decisions of 2026-10-19; partial refunds stay as they are (rule 1301) and the console's refund button is later. Card payments stay off. No route, permission or environment variable changed.

1. Card after a refund (rules 2270-2272). A plan or renewal payment refunded by the founder deletes the saved card token inside `endPlanOnRefund`'s ledger lock, once per transaction: after reading `paymob:refund_ended:{txnId}` and before writing it, so a retry of a run that stopped before its marker deletes it again, and a repeat of a finished refund keeps the card of a plan bought since (ruling of 2026-10-19 on the merge with multi-workspace M1, section 91). `chargeSavedCard` re-reads the card under that lock (rule 1956), so a renewal that has not yet passed that check stops; the charge itself runs after the lock is released, so a refund that lands in that window still lets that one renewal charge (the existing rule 1956 design). `saveCard` ignores a token callback whose intent's transaction was refunded (`paymob:refunded:{txnId}`), so a late or replayed callback cannot bring the card back; it reads the refund mark and writes the card under the same account lock (rule 2280), so a refund cannot land between the check and the write. The owner's next checkout therefore starts with `autoRenew` false (`applyPaid` sets it from a saved card) until a new card token arrives for that new order. A pack's refund keeps the card: the plan goes on. The change is in billing, not in the provider seam (section 85), so Geidea gets it unchanged.
2. A hand-set plan's end (rules 2273-2275). `handPlanEnded(u, today)` is true for a paid plan with no online subscription whose `User.handPlan.paidThrough` is before today in Riyadh; never for the trial, a subscription or a record without the day. Read: `trialOver` and the entitlements (`trial_over`, `resetsAt` null) say so from that day, so every spending gate refuses at once. Written: the daily cron, on the account's default-brand entry before the credit renewal, calls `endHandPlanIfDue`: under the ledger lock it sets `plan` to "trial" and `trialEnds` a second in the past (unless already past), saves, and writes one `hand_plan_ended` row (actor `system`) to `admin:log`; a second run changes nothing. No data is deleted: brands, tasks, files, members, invites, the balance, packs, a deal, the overrides and `handPlan` itself stay, and seats are not refitted. The founder extends the trial (`extendTrial`) or sets a plan again (`POST /api/admin/plan`), which restores the plan, its credits and its seats as for any trial. Rule 679: the expand step only; no new field, only values every build already reads. A refund of such an account's payment still ends its plan (`endPlanOnRefund` skips only a real ended trial).
3. A short batch (rules 2276-2279): section 8 item 13 step 5b. One refund after the commit, so the catch's whole refund can never add to it.

## 90. Enforced nonce-based script policy (2026-10-19, branch `claude/csp-nonce-enforce-f45324`, rules 2320-2329)

Security scan of 2026-10-19, low-severity leftover: the script policy was report-only and allowed `'unsafe-inline'`, so an injected script would have run. Coverage in `docs/system/tests/2320-csp-nonce.md`.

1. Nonce (rules 2320-2321). `src/proxy.ts` makes a fresh nonce (16 random bytes, base64, `cspNonce` in `src/lib/csp.ts`) for every request to a page path and sets it on the request (`x-nonce` and `content-security-policy`, overwriting anything the browser sent) and on the response (`Content-Security-Policy` enforced, `Content-Security-Policy-Report-Only` twin). Enforced: `script-src 'self' 'nonce-…' 'strict-dynamic' https://challenges.cloudflare.com; frame-ancestors 'none'; base-uri 'self'; form-action 'self'; object-src 'none'`. `'strict-dynamic'` lets the nonced scripts load Next's chunks and the Turnstile script; `form-action 'self'` stays (the OAuth consent page posts with `fetch` and leaves with `location.assign`, rule 2305).
2. Rendering (rule 2322). Next reads the nonce from the request's policy and puts it on its own scripts only when it renders the page, so every page now renders per request: `src/app/[lang]/layout.tsx` reads `x-nonce` (for the theme script) and `src/app/global-not-found.tsx` calls `connection()`; the catch-all `src/app/[lang]/[...rest]/page.tsx` is `force-dynamic`, and the proxy's stray-path rewrite passes the request headers on so the not-found page gets the nonce too. The marketing pages lost their build-time HTML; see the cost note in the pull request.
3. Other answers (rule 2323). `/api/*`, `/m/*` (which sends its own `sandbox` policy) and `/p/*` keep the four directives from `next.config.ts` and get no nonce.
4. Styles and the rest (rules 2324-2325). `style-src` is not enforced: React `style={...}` attributes are used across the app and a nonce cannot cover an attribute. The rest of the full policy stays report-only. Development alone adds `'unsafe-eval'` (React's error stacks).
5. Markup (rule 2326). Every `<script>` in `src/app` and `src/components` is the nonced theme script or a JSON-LD data block (never run, so never blocked); no inline event handlers, no `javascript:` links.

## 91. "Not now" in the approvals inbox (2026-10-19, branch `claude/inbox-not-now`, rules 2340-2349)

Pattern 1 of the Fizzy and Orca study (`docs/reports/2026-10-19-PRD-fizzy-orca_AR.md`, Fizzy's entropy): no item sits in the inbox for more than 14 days with nobody deciding it. The founder chose the 14-day default on 2026-10-19. Coverage in `docs/system/tests/2340-inbox-not-now.md`.

1. Shape (rule 2340). A set-aside item keeps `status: "pending"` and gains `notNow: {at, by: "owner" | "auto"}`; `touchedAt` records the last time a person acted on it without deciding (edit, date, reopen, bring back). Both are optional, and both are copied to the light row (`liteOf`). Older code ignores them and shows the item as pending.
2. Sets (rule 2341). `notnow:{scope}` holds the pending ids that are set aside, written by `saveTask` next to `pending:{scope}` and rebuilt with it by `rebuildPending` (the daily reconcile), so an id older code left behind is gone the next morning. `pendingIds` still returns every pending id (the inbox shows the "Not now" chip from it); `waitingIds` is `pending` without `notnow`.
3. Counts (rule 2342). `isWaiting(t)` = pending and not set aside. The badge (`pendingCount`), Reem's walk and agenda, the KPIs, the daily brief, the lifecycle emails, the chat page's "waiting for you" card, the 48-hour and low-score nudges, the chat's `list_inbox` and MCP `list_inbox status: "pending"` count only waiting items. MCP `list_inbox` takes `not_now` for the set-aside ones, and the chat's `list_inbox` names how many are set aside (rule 2347).
4. The move (rule 2343). `runDaily` (the daily cron, brief on or off) calls `moveIdle` (`src/lib/not-now.ts`) before the reconcile: a waiting item whose clock is at least `NOT_NOW_DAYS` (14) old gets `notNow.by = "auto"`, each under its own task lock and re-checked on the task as stored, so an approval or edit that landed after the rows were read wins. The clock starts at the latest of `createdAt`, `touchedAt` and `Schedule.notNowFrom`, the workspace's first daily run with this code, stamped by `runDaily` only (PUT /api/schedule copies only the switches). That stamp is why release day moves nothing: an old backlog gets its warning 11 days after it. Nothing is deleted, published or charged.
5. Notices (rule 2344). At 75% of the time (day 10.5 to 14) `followUps` pushes one `pending` notice per set of items ("N items move to Not now in D days", fingerprint `notnow-soon:{ids}`, 5 days). The move pushes one notice per brand per day (`notnow:{day}`) linking to `/{lang}/app/?view=notnow`. Items older than the 180 days `followUps` reads still move but get no warning.
6. The route (rule 2345). `PATCH /api/tasks/{id} {notNow: true | false}` (role `drafts`, like edit and reject): sets aside or brings back a pending item, else 409 `not_pending` and nothing saved. It is not a decision: no `decidedAt`, no decision record, nothing learned. Approve, reject or reopen clears `notNow`; bring back, an edit, a date or a reopen sets `touchedAt`.
7. The screen (rule 2346). The inbox has a "Not now" chip between Waiting and Approved, a "Not now" button on waiting cards, and "Bring back" with the date and who moved it on set-aside cards. `?view=notnow` opens on the chip.

### 91.1 Releases

Expand only (rule 679): two optional task fields, one optional schedule field and one new set. Rolling back leaves `notNow` on some pending tasks, which the old code shows as plain pending items; the stale `notnow:` set is ignored by the old code and rebuilt by the new one. No data conversion. The owner-chosen length (Fizzy's per-board setting) and pattern 2 (one notice per item with a count, chosen digest) are later steps. Rules 2348-2349 are held for this work and unused. Pattern 2 is section 92.

## 92. One bell entry per subject, and the digest email (2026-10-19, branch `claude/inbox-not-now`, rules 2350-2359)

Pattern 2 of the Fizzy and Orca study (`docs/reports/2026-10-19-PRD-fizzy-orca_AR.md`, Fizzy's notifications and bundles): the owner gets at most one bell entry per subject, however often it comes up, and chooses whether and how often an email gathers them. The founder asked for it on 2026-10-19; the digest is off until the owner turns it on. Coverage in `docs/system/tests/2350-notif-collapse-digest.md`.

1. Subject (rule 2350). A bell entry may carry `about`, built by `aboutKey` (`src/lib/notifications.ts`) from our own values, never a Redis key: `due:{scope}`, `pending:{scope}` (the 48-hour nudge), `notnow:{scope}` (the warning and the move of section 91), `quality:{scope}`, `reconnect:{scope}`, and `todo:{scope}:{itemId}` for Reem's reminder and her overdue alerts of one to-do. Credits, trial, the weekly plan and the other notices carry none and arrive as before.
2. Collapse (rule 2351). `pushNotifs` writes the bell through `collapse` under the bell lock: a fresh entry with an `about` replaces every older entry about the same subject. Its `count` is the older one's (1 when it had none) plus one while that one was unread; a read one leaves no count, so the counter starts again. The fingerprints still decide whether a notice is sent at all; the subject decides where it lands. Old code ignores `about` and `count` and shows the entry as before.
3. Counter on screen (rule 2352). The bell and the notifications page show "×N" after the title when `count` is 2 or more (99+ past 99), with "N notices about this" for screen readers (`Times`, `src/components/app/NotificationCenter.tsx`).
4. Choice (rule 2353). Settings, Notifications has a third card, "Notifications digest by email": off, every 4 hours, every day, every week. The choice is the account's (the bell is per account), in `notifydigest:{account}` with the owner's address and language at the time of the save. On adds the account to `notifydigests` and starts a window now (`digestlast:{account}`); a change of length starts again; off deletes all three.
5. Send (rule 2354). Reem's 15-minute tick (`POST /api/cron/secretary`) runs `sweepDigests` after the reminders and calendars, 8 accounts at a time within the tick's deadline. For each: no record leaves the set; no window starts one; a window not yet over (less 10 minutes of slack) waits; else `digestlock:{account}` (10 minutes) is claimed and the unread entries that arrived in the window go in one `notice` email (20 written out, the rest counted, each with its "×N", its body and its link; the button opens the notifications page). Nothing new closes the window with no email. A refused send keeps the window open, so a later tick sends the same entries once the claim runs out.
6. The route (rule 2355). `PUT /api/notifications/digest {every}` (owner, `profile.account`, 60 saves an hour; permissions row 251) writes the session's own account only.
7. No second email (rule 2356). With a digest on, the daily job (`/api/cron/daily`) no longer emails the follow-ups: Noor's brief still goes as chosen, without the follow-up block, and the follow-up-only email is not sent. The entries reach the owner in the digest.
8. Wipe (rule 2357). Deleting the account deletes the three digest records and takes it out of `notifydigests`.

### 92.1 Releases

Expand only (rule 679): two optional fields on a bell entry and four new keys. Rolling back leaves `about` and `count` on some entries, which old code ignores, and leaves the digest records unread, so no digest goes and the daily job emails the follow-ups again. No data conversion. Rules 2358-2359 are held for this work and unused.


## 93. Multi-workspace membership, release M1 expand (2026-10-19, branch `claude/wave3-m1-expand`, rules 2110-2139)

The expand step of `docs/specs/2026-10-18-multi-org.md` (sections 4 to 15): a login becomes a PERSON who can hold memberships in several accounts, and every per-login key that named one account is keyed by (person, account). `MULTI_ORG = false` (a code constant in `src/lib/multi-org.ts`, not an environment variable), so production behaves as before: one person holds one account (its home), invites to an address that already signs in answer `unavailable`, there is no switcher and no create-workspace route, and every legacy record is still written with the bytes R1 wrote, for any build rolled back to R1. Files: `src/lib/person.ts`, `src/lib/membership.ts`, `src/lib/multi-org.ts`, `src/lib/keys.ts` (`personKey`, `accountKey.membership`), `src/lib/stored.ts`, `src/lib/auth.ts`, `src/lib/brands.ts`, `src/lib/team.ts`, `src/lib/teamlog.ts`, `src/lib/feed-token.ts`, `src/lib/booking-token.ts`, `src/lib/account.ts`, `src/lib/admin-workspace.ts`, the team, account, profile, auth, secretary feed and booking routes, and the founder's plan and user routes.

1. Persons (rules 2115, 2132, 2136). A person is `person:{pid}` (`PersonRow` in `src/lib/stored.ts`): address, name, language, password hash, `emailVerifiedAt`, the revocation stamps `sessionsAfter` and `devicesAfter`, `createdAt`, and the person-only `suspended` and `trialUsedAt`. Ids: a legacy owner's person id is its account id (the owner's address while `OPAQUE_IDS` is off), a legacy member's is its address, and a new person's is `p_` and 24 base32 characters (`newPersonId`), made only by `createUser` under `opts.person: "p"`, which no route passes (rule 2132, so production makes no `p_` person in M1). `getPerson`/`personFromRaw` read the row and fall back to the legacy record it came from, and a row the legacy record is provably newer than loses (architecture 4(f) item 7). Signup, Google signup and accept write the row whole (`createPersonRecord`); script S1 writes it for every older login (93.1). `isPersonSuspended` reads a person's `suspended`; nothing writes it in M1 (the console's person view comes with M3).
2. Accounts and `ownerOf` (rules 2115, 2118, 2134). `ownerOf(u)` (`src/lib/membership.ts`) is the only answer to "who owns this account": `u.ownerPid`, else the legacy owner, whose person id is the account id. The owner is never a membership row, and `roleIn` gives `owner` from `ownerOf` only, so a forged `mbr:` row claiming `owner` grants nothing. `User.ownerPid` is optional and absent on every production account in M1 (written only with a `p_` person under `opts.person: "p"`; from M2 on every new account, and after an ownership transfer in M3). Seats, plans, credits, charges, billing and the card stay on the account record and are counted per account (rule 2118): a person in two accounts uses a seat in each. `createUser` writes the owner's `orgs:{pid}` entry beside the account.
3. Memberships. `writeMembership(aid, pid, row)` writes `mbr:{aid}:{pid}`, the roster entry `members:{aid}` and `orgs:{pid}` in one round. For the person's HOME membership (the legacy `member:{email}` whose person id is `pid` and whose `memberOf` is `aid`) it also writes the record's `role`, `acceptedAt` and `disabled`, and both writes run in one round under that record's `lock:member`: a `lock:member` that cannot be had throws before either is written, so the row and the legacy mirror never disagree (a pause the row has and the mirror lacks would come back unpaused after a rollback to R1). When there is no legacy record (a `p_` person, or a record already gone) the row is written alone. The write is in place and only when a field differs, so the record keeps R1's field order, and `invitedBy` and `memberOf` are never rewritten. Accepting an invite (`insertMember`) takes `lock:person:{pid}` and then, for a home member, `lock:member:{email}`, both before its first write, and under them writes the `person:` row fresh (`createPersonRecord`, born at `acceptedAt`), the legacy record exactly as R1 wrote it, the membership (the row, the roster and `orgs:` directly, not through `writeMembership`, whose second `lock:member` would come after the person, the legacy record and the login were written) and `login:{email}`: a person or member lock that cannot be had leaves none of them, the invite stays pending, and the retry joins. `listMembers` reads the roster, then the membership (`getMembership`) and the person (`getPerson`) of every entry in one `pmap` round. A removal, a role change and `leaveTeam` find the target on that roster, so the person id never comes from the request. `seatsUsed`, `createInvite`, `acceptInvite` and `enforceSeats` count one account's memberships only (rule 2118). A person in two accounts uses a seat in each, and a seat refit in one leaves the other untouched. `deleteMembership(aid, pid)` (removal, leave, `forceRemoveMember`, `wipeMemberships`) first takes `lock:login:{email}` and then `lock:person:{pid}`, before its first write. In one round it deletes the row, the roster and `orgs:` entries, `activebrand:{pid}:{aid}` and the invites the person sent there. For the home membership `lock:member` is taken before the first delete of that round, and the round also deletes `member:{email}` and `activebrand:{email}` while the record is still this membership's: a stale member lock deletes nothing. It then revokes that membership's feed and booking links (item 11). A person with no owned account and no other membership is then deleted as R1 deleted a removed member's login (rule 2124 M1 half): the `person:` row, `orgs:` and `lastorg:`, and, while the index still names this person, `login:{email}`, two-step sign-in, the waiting address change, the Google link both ways and every device session. A person who still holds another membership keeps it, keeps signing in there, and keeps its login. The owner cannot leave (`forbidden`).
4. Invites. The record adds `aid` (the same value as `owner`) and `invitedByPid` (the inviter's person id) beside R1's fields. Readers use `aid ?? owner` and `invitedByPid ?? invitedBy`, so an invite written before M1 still works. An invite holds while its inviter is the owner (`ownerOf`, or, for an old invite, the owner's address or id), or while the inviter's membership of this account is an enabled admin inviting a plain member (rule 2125). An admin of one account holds nothing in another: its actor there fails `actorStillHolds`, and its session cannot name that account. With `MULTI_ORG` off, `createInvite` and `acceptInvite` answer `unavailable` for an address that already signs in anywhere, as in R1.
5. Keys (rules 2117, 2137). `personKey` in `src/lib/keys.ts` is the only producer of `person:{pid}`, `lock:person:{pid}`, `orgs:{pid}`, `lastorg:{pid}`, `activebrand:{pid}:{aid}`, `sec:feedof:{pid}:{aid}`, `sec:bookof:{pid}:{aid}:{provider}` and their leaf locks, and `accountKey.membership` / `accountKey.teamLog` of `mbr:{aid}:{pid}` and `teamlog:{aid}`; every id in them comes from a session's checked actor or a record this app wrote, never from a request. The legacy per-login keys (`activebrand:{login}`, `sec:feedof:{login}`, `sec:bookof:{login}:{provider}`) are still read and written for the home account only. `tests/multi-org-scans.test.ts` pins that no other file builds these prefixes, that the legacy templates keep their placeholder counts, and that the raw-key ratchet stays at 524 (rule 2137). M1 writes and reads every family except `lastorg:` (read by the pick, written by nothing until M2's switch).
6. Sessions and the pick (rules 2110, 2111, 2112, 2119, 2131, 2135; section 5 "Session ids" and "Resolution on persons and memberships", architecture 4(f) item 8). Sign-in writes the session's `pid` and `acct` (`pickAccount`: with `MULTI_ORG` off always the home account). Each request re-reads the person, the account and the membership named by the session in one round and resolves only into that account, through `roleIn` and `membershipHolds`; nothing in a request (a body, a query, a header, a path segment) names the account or the person (rule 2112), so a forged `orgs:` entry, a forged `mbr:` row or a paused or removed membership grants nothing at the next request. The active brand is per (person, account). A session written before R1 (no `pid`/`acct`) resolves through `resolveLegacy` exactly as before, without the person-suspension check (93.1).
7. Credential writes (rules 2132, 2139; section 5 "Credential writes"). `updatePerson` is the one writer of a person's password, name, language, verification and revocation stamps; it writes the legacy record first, under `lock:person` then `lock:member` or `lock:user`, then the row, and the revocation stamps are always the later of both records, so a sign-out a rolled-back build wrote is never undone.
8. Team log (rule 2129, write half). `teamlog:{aid}` is a list capped at 500 (`CAPS.teamLog`), newest first, written by `logTeam` in `src/lib/teamlog.ts` under the same `lock:team:{aid}` as the change it records. Each event writes exactly one row: `invited`, `invite_cancelled` (via `web`, or via `system` when the invite route undoes its own invite after a failed mail), `joined`, `role_changed` (only when the role changes), `removed` (via `console` from the founder console, which names the account's owner as the actor: staff are never named), `left`, and `paused_for_seats` / `resumed` (via `system`). A row holds `at`, `kind`, `actor {pid, email}`, `target {pid?, email}`, `role` and `via`, and nothing else: the writer builds it from named fields, so a token, an invite id or a password cannot get in. A refused action writes no row. Nothing reads the log in a route in M1; the owner's view comes with M3.
9. Lock order (rule 2139), always outer to inner: `inviteuse:{hash}` → `lock:team:{aid}` → `lock:login:{email}` → `lock:person:{pid}` → `lock:member:{email}` → `lock:user:{aid}`. Two locks of the same kind (an address move holds both logins and both persons) are taken smaller address first. The founder's plan route (`POST /api/admin/plan`) takes `lock:team` then `lock:user`, while the owner address move takes `lock:user` then `lock:team` (its documented exception, 60 s TTL): if both run on the same account, each waits about 3 s and times out (the plan route with `user_lock_timeout`, the move with `team_lock_timeout`), and nothing is written by either. The feed and booking locks (`lock:feed:{pid}:{aid}` and the legacy `lock:feed:{login}`, `lock:book:{pid}:{aid}:{provider}` and the legacy `lock:book:{login}:{provider}`) are LEAF locks: they may be taken while any lock of the order is held (`deleteMembership` takes them under `lock:team`, `lock:login` and `lock:person`), and nothing is taken while they are held, except that a home link takes the new one first and the legacy one inside it. No lock is reentrant: a function called under a held lock takes a flag (`teamLocked`, `loginLocked`, `personLocked`). A person lock that is still held after the wait throws `person_lock_timeout`; a team, login or member lock throws `team_lock_timeout` (`isTeamLockTimeout`). Accept, member removal, leave, account deletion, the address change and the founder's member removal and delete answer a person lock timeout as 409 `busy`; member removal, leave, account deletion and the founder's member removal and delete answer a team, login or member lock timeout the same way. A removal takes its login, person and member locks before its first write, so a `busy` answer leaves the membership whole and a retry finishes the removal. Account deletion removes the members before the owner's records (section 26 item 14), so a `busy` there leaves the owner's account whole too.
10. Owner address moves and known limits until the contract release. Both owner moves, the unverified-address move (`moveUnverifiedAccount`) and the change of a proved address (`moveOwnerLogin` from src/lib/email-change.ts), take both login locks, then (since customer passkeys, section 100.4, rule 2414) both person locks, the old owner person's and the new address's, the smaller pid first, then the old account's `lock:user`, then right away the account's `lock:team` (`withMoveTeamLock`), and hold it until `wipeAccount` returns (`teamLocked`, so `wipeMemberships` does not take it again). `lock:team` after `lock:user` is the out-of-order step this path always had through `wipeAccount`, now taken at the start instead of at the end. Under it no invite and no accept of the account can run, so the one check, before the first write, that `members:{id}` and `invites:{id}` are empty is final (both callers already refuse an account with a team; a refusal is `not_allowed` and writes nothing). The wipe therefore never meets a member and never takes a member's `lock:login` or `lock:person` under `lock:user`; there is no residual window. An accept attempted during the move waits for `lock:team`, and either times out (`team_lock_timeout`) or gets the lock after the move and finds no owner: it never joins. A `lock:team` that cannot be had at the start throws `team_lock_timeout` before any write: both routes answer 409 `busy`, nothing exists at the new address and the old account is whole (on the proved path the code was used, so a new one is asked; it is not refused as `taken`). Lock lifetime: the shared lock (src/lib/locks.ts) has no renewal, so the move's `lock:team` alone gets a 60 s expiry instead of 10 s (`MOVE_TEAM_LOCK_TTL_SECONDS`, the `ttlSeconds` argument of `withNamedLock`) against the move's 30-50 round trips; it is released as soon as the move ends. The move's two login locks and `lock:user` keep their 10 s expiry, the bound the move always had. The person row of a member wins over its legacy record, so a name or language that a rolled-back build saved only on `member:{email}` is not read until the next `sessionsAfter` change. Owners have the same limit (item 7).
11. Feed and booking (rules 2119, 2131, 2132, 2139; `src/lib/feed-token.ts`, `src/lib/booking-token.ts`, `src/lib/secretary-feed.ts`, the two management routes). The calendar feed and the booking links are per `(pid, aid)`. The management routes pass `linkOwner(u)`: `{pid: u.actor.pid, aid: acct(u), login, home}`, where `login` is the address R1 named the legacy pointer with (the member's own, the account's for an owner) and `home` is the actor's. `createFeed(who)` takes `lock:feed:{pid}:{aid}`, and for the home account `lock:feed:{login}` inside it (either held: `busy`), reads the old pointers in one round, writes the record (`pid`, `aid`, and `home: true` for the home account), then `sec:feedof:{pid}:{aid}` and for the home account `sec:feedof:{login}` in one round, then deletes the records the old pointers named (a home link made before M1 included). `revokeFeed(who)` takes the same locks (20 tries 50 ms apart each, then revokes anyway) and deletes both pointers and the records they name in one round. `resolveFeed` reads the record, then in one round the pointers that can name it: a record with `pid` is live while `sec:feedof:{pid}:{aid}` names it and, for a home record, `sec:feedof:{login}` too; a record made before M1 is live while the legacy pointer names it. `getFeedStatus(who)` reads both pointers, then their records, and shows the live one that belongs to `who` (a record made before M1 counts for the home account only). `createBook`, `revokeBook`, `resolveBook` and `getBookStatus` do the same per provider with `sec:bookof:{pid}:{aid}:{provider}` and `lock:book:{pid}:{aid}:{provider}`. `loginHolds` (the feed's `feedHolds`) for a record with `pid` checks `accountOfScope(scope) === aid` first, then reads the person, the account, the membership and the account's brands in one round (permissions "Calendar feed" item 9); a record without `pid` keeps R1's check. `deleteMembership` revokes `{pid, aid}`'s links, and for the home membership the legacy pointers too; a link minted in a race after the removal is refused at read time. Every function still takes a plain login, the pre-M1 form, for the callers that revoke by address (password reset, console, account deletion, `wipeMemberships`); revoking by address also deletes the `(pid, aid)` pointer of the home record the legacy pointer names, while that pointer still names the record (a newer link stays). `getFeedStatus`/`getBookStatus` read the legacy pointer a home record names even for an actor that is not home in it, so status and resolve agree. A record without `pid` is also refused when the person is suspended (rule 2131).
