Known limit until M2: those address-based revokes reach the home link only; a link in another account (only test persons have one in M1) ends at read time, with the person's `sessionsAfter` or suspension, or the account's suspension or deletion. The reverse also waits for M2: the address-based revokes over a roster (the reset route, the founder's user route, `wipeAccount`'s `revokeFeeds([owner, ...roster])`) name a member by address, so for a legacy person whose home is another account (only test persons in M1, since `MULTI_ORG` is off) they end that person's HOME link in the other account. Fail closed, but a cross-account side effect: M2 moves those callers to the `{pid, aid}` form for the account being reset.
12. Deleting an account (rule 2126 M1 half; `src/lib/account.ts` `wipeAccount`, `src/lib/team.ts` `wipeMemberships`). `wipeMemberships(aid, { finish, teamLocked })` replaces `wipeTeam` with the same guarantees: under `lock:team:{aid}` (unless the caller holds it, `teamLocked`) it reads `members:{aid}`, `invites:{aid}` and the account in one round, then in one `pmap` calls `deleteMembership(aid, pid, { home, teamLocked: true })` for every roster entry, `home` read from the member's legacy record (`memberOf === aid`) in that same round, then deletes every invite, both indexes and the team log `teamlog:{aid}` (its rows hold member addresses, and a legacy account id is an address that a later signup reuses, so a kept log would pass to the new owner), runs `finish` (the owner's records, below) and only then removes this account from the OWNER's `orgs:{owner pid}`. A member, login or person lock that cannot be had throws before the owner is touched: DELETE /api/account and the console's delete answer 409 `busy` and the retry finishes. A member who belongs to another account, or owns one, keeps it, keeps signing in there, and keeps every key there (`mbr:`, `orgs:`, `activebrand:{pid}:{aid}`, its links in that account); a member with nothing left goes as R1 deleted a removed member's login (item 3). The owner's per-login security pack (two-step sign-in, a waiting address change, the Google link both ways, every device session), `person:`, `orgs:` and `lastorg:` of the owner person, its passkeys (`passkeys:{pid}`, the `pkcred:` entries that still name the pid, `pkreg:{pid}`; section 100.4) and the owner's login index go only when the owner holds nothing else (`holdsElsewhere`), decided in `finish` under the owner's `lock:login` after `user:{owner}` is deleted; member addresses are no longer listed by `wipeAccount` at all. With `MULTI_ORG` off a legacy owner never holds anything else, so this is R1's deletion (rules 1955, 1999). The calendar feed and booking links are revoked first, per (person, account): the owner's and each roster member's in this account only (`rosterLinks`: the legacy per-login link only for a member whose home this account is). The owner address move still calls `wipeAccount(id, { userLocked: true, loginLocked: true, teamLocked: true })` under login, person (both pids, since passkeys Task 6), user and team, with `keepPasskeys` when its passkey move answered `skipped`; `finish` takes no person lock, so the lock order (item 9) holds. Also in M1: `createInvite` refuses (`forbidden`) when the account is gone by the time it holds `lock:team` (no invite, index entry or team log row), and a team lock timeout answers `busy` instead of a 500: 409 in the accept route; 503 in the Paymob callback (`applyTransaction`, the same retry contract as its transaction lock: nothing is marked done and no payment record, invoice, paid status or `paymob_paid` line is written; the retry fits the seats on a plan transaction already applied, then records the payment once and logs `paymob_paid` from the payment record's own `already`; `chargeSavedCard` reads that 503 as `pending`); 409 in the console refund (`refundPaymobPayment`; the retry fits the seats and still answers `planEnded: true`: `paymob:refund_ended:{txnId}`, written under `lock:user` right after the save, means this transaction already ended a plan, so `endPlanOnRefund` never ends one again for it, also when the customer bought a new plan since; its value is the payment id, or `none` when the refund found no plan to end (an ended trial with no subscription, for example the second of two refunds in one period), written under the same `lock:user` with the same 400-day expiry, so a later repeat of that refund never ends a plan and answers `planEnded: false`); 409 in the console plan route, which takes the account's `lock:team` BEFORE `lock:user` whenever the seat limit can move (a plan, a seat number or a cleared deal; team before user is rule 2139's order) and fits the team inside it (`enforceSeats(aid, { teamLocked: true })`, `withAccountTeamLock`), so a timeout there writes nothing (no plan, credits or audit row) and the retry applies the change once; once the change is saved, a lock the refit itself cannot have (a home member's `lock:member`, `team_lock_timeout` from `enforceSeats` only) answers 200 `{ ok: true, team: "busy" }` with the change kept and its audit row carrying `team: "busy"`, and a seats-only POST refits later (the route has no request idempotency: repeating the whole POST would add the credits again). A credits-only change takes no team lock. The owner's own deletion has no `MULTI_ORG` guard, unlike `forgetPersonIfAlone`: with the flag off the owner holds nothing else, so it is R1's deletion; with it on, the owner asked for the deletion, and a person kept with nothing would keep a login index naming a deleted account.
13. Console (rules 1982, 2079, 2132; `src/lib/admin-workspace.ts`, the founder's user and plan routes). The workspace view finds the account a chosen address belongs to as sign-in does (`getLogin`: the account it owns, else the account its login stands for), lists the roster through `listMembers`, shows a `p_` inviter's address (`inviterAddress`), and shows how many OTHER workspaces each member holds as a count only (`alsoIn`): their names, ids and owners are another tenant's. The member removal (`forceRemoveMember`) removes the one membership the view shows (item 3), and the plan route takes `lock:team` before `lock:user` (item 12).
14. Test fixtures (rule 2132 fixture part; `tests/helpers/session.ts`, `tests/multi-org-helper.test.ts`). The helper's `newUser`, `newOpaqueUser` and `newMember` make `p_` persons by default (`TEST_PERSONS` unset: credentials on `person:{pid}` only, a member with a membership row and no legacy `member:` record), the shape release M2 signs up; `TEST_PERSONS=legacy` makes today's legacy persons, the shape production holds through M1. In both modes each person also gets a shadow membership (`addShadowMembership`): a plain `member` seat in a fresh account `shadow{n}-{hex}@test.example`, written with `db.set` only so it is not in `users:all`, accepted one second after the home membership so sign-in still picks the home account, holding a brand and a pending task named `SHADOW-{n}`; a site that reads the wrong account returns that text. `signIn` writes the ids production's `createSession` writes, for an impersonation too. A fixture opts out with `{ legacy: true }` (a test that pins R1's records by address, commented "Pins the legacy shape (multi-org M1)") or `{ shadow: false }` (a person who holds nothing else, so a removal or a deletion takes the login with it). Two product sites the `p_` run found are fixed: the MCP bearer refuses a token whose address is a `p_` member's login (the login index names another person; before, only a legacy `member:` record refused it), the console's workspace view shows a `p_` inviter's address (`inviterAddress` in `src/lib/team.ts`), not the person id, and the console's member removal (`forceRemoveMember`, POST /api/admin/users/{email} `removeMember`) finds a `p_` member with no legacy record through its login (`getLogin`: the membership the console's GET shows) and removes that membership only. A brand-scope test seeds and reads the active brand with `seedActiveBrand` / `readActiveBrand` (`setActiveBrandIn` / `activeBrandIn` for the login's person and account), so it runs in both modes. The triage and its counts are in `docs/system/tests/2110-multi-org.md`, "Fixtures". Cross-cutting proofs: `tests/multi-org-isolation.test.ts` (rule 2110, session half) builds a seeded random graph of 5 accounts, their persons and members, extra and paused memberships, forged `orgs:` entries and forged `mbr:` rows, then sends 200 requests over 19 routes with foreign ids and scopes in bodies and queries and forged workspace headers, and checks with a spy on every store command that each request touches only its own active account and is refused (401) exactly when the session's account is not held (seed in `ISOLATION_SEED`, default 2110, printed on a failure; about 2 s). `tests/multi-org-roundtrip.test.ts` (rules 2122 and 2121): one person who owns A and is admin of B and C writes and reads a brand, a file and a task in each, a credit charged in A through `charge` leaves B's and C's money bytes equal, and connections and `composioUserId` stay per scope with their pre-M1 values. `tests/multi-org-scans.test.ts`: rules 2112 (scan half), 2137 and 2132. Per-rule status: `docs/system/tests/2110-multi-org.md`.

### 93.1 Releases

M1 = **expand** (rule 679). Rollback: safe to any earlier build. What an older build (R1 or before) does with each new shape:
- `person:{pid}`, `orgs:{pid}`, `mbr:{aid}:{pid}`, `teamlog:{aid}`, `lock:person:{pid}`: ignored. Every credential write lands on the legacy record first (`user:` or `member:`), and every membership write on `member:{email}` and `members:{aid}`, so an older build reads the current password, name, stamps, role and pause.
- `activebrand:{pid}:{aid}`: ignored. In the home account the legacy `activebrand:{login}` is written alike, so the choice survives a rollback; a choice the older build makes wins after the roll forward.
- Feed and booking records with `pid`, `aid` and `home`, and the `(pid, aid)` pointers: a home link also writes the legacy pointer, which an older build resolves; a link in another account (only test persons hold one in M1) is not resolved by an older build (fail closed).
- Invite `aid` and `invitedByPid`: ignored; `owner` and `invitedBy` are still written.
- Session `pid`/`acct`: written and read since R1, unchanged in meaning.
- `lastorg:{pid}`: not written in M1.
- `User.ownerPid` and `p_` persons: not written for any production login in M1 (`opts.person` is passed by tests only).

Legacy sessions and person suspension (decided 2026-10-19): a session written before R1 (no `pid`/`acct`) goes through `resolveLegacy`, which checks the account's and the member's suspension but not the person's (`isPersonSuspended`). M1 accepts this because nothing writes a person's `suspended` until M3. M3, or M4 if M4 is the release that refuses sessions without `pid`, adds the person check to the legacy path before any writer of person suspension ships. Cost if that is missed: a session issued before R1 to a member whose person is later suspended keeps working until its 30-day expiry. Done in M3 (2026-10-21, rule 2131 pre-R1 half, `tests/multi-org-m3-legacy.test.ts`): `resolveLegacy` reads the session person's `person:` row (its `pid`, else its address) in the same first round and refuses a suspended owner or member, failing closed on a value it does not know, with or without `MULTI_ORG`.

Next steps, in order:
1. Script S1 (`scripts/multi-org-index.ts`, rule 2133, built 2026-10-21 on branch `claude/s1-multi-org-index`), run by hand once M1 is live and only on the founder's yes (it writes production data), with the app's own `KV_REST_API_URL` / `KV_REST_API_TOKEN` set privately:

   ```bash
   npx --no-install jiti scripts/multi-org-index.ts
   ```

   is the dry run (read-only, prints counts); `--apply` writes. For every account in `users:all`, 50 at a time, it fills only what is absent: the owner's `person:` (copied from the legacy owner record by `createPersonRecord`, the revocation stamps as they are) and `orgs:`; for every roster entry the member's `person:` and `mbr:{aid}:{pid}` (from the legacy `member:` record through `writeMembership`, so a member paused for seats stays paused and the legacy record is not rewritten) and `orgs:`; and each address's `login:` index. Every write runs under M1's locks (`lock:team` → `lock:login` → `lock:person` → `lock:member`) after reading the records again under them. It never overwrites and counts instead: a `person:` row whose hash or address differs from the legacy record, a `login:` index naming someone else, an owner record it cannot read as a person (or an `ownerPid` with no person), a member record whose `memberOf` account is missing, a roster entry without a record, and a lock that stayed held (run again). Output is counts only; exit 0 clean, 1 finished with a conflict, orphan or busy lock, 2 could not run. A second run writes nothing. Nothing reads these records as required before M2, so a run that stops halfway is harmless.
2. M2 (enable: `MULTI_ORG = true`, merged with account-id R2) needs M1 live for 7 days with no fix release touching it, and an S1 run with 0 conflicts. Its rollback floor is M1 (a build before M1 cannot sign in a `p_` person and ignores `mbr:`), and its release pull request says "rollback floor: M1". The workspace is chosen in the URL (decision 7, changed by the founder on 2026-10-19): workspace pages move under `/{lang}/app/w/{handle}/…`, the handle is a selector among the person's own memberships re-checked on every request, the session's `acct` becomes the workspace last chosen (written by sign-in, the switch route, create workspace and accept, never by visiting a link), and `x-thakeen-ws` stays as the second guard (a state-changing call without it answers 409 `workspace_missing`).
3. Known items M2 must close:
   - `getLogin` for a `p_` person whose only memberships are paused finds no login, so the console answers 404 and a signup at that address would create a second person. M2 returns a disabled member login instead.
   - `resolveLegacy` and `ownsLogin` ignore `ownerPid`; they must follow `ownerOf` once accounts carry it.
   - The member address move refuses a `p_` person only implicitly (it finds no legacy `member:` record); M2 makes the refusal, or the move of a `p_` person, explicit.
   - The address-based link revokes (item 11) move to the `{pid, aid}` form.
4. M3 (lifecycle) and M4 (contract, at least 30 days after M2), then script S2, as the spec's Releases table says. The owner address move deletes the old id's `teamlog:` with the old account (item 12); M3 copies it to the new id, or R2's stable account ids make it moot.

### 93.2 Release M2 enable (2026-10-21, branch `claude/wave3-m2-workspace-url-8776d4`, plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-20-multi-org-m2.md`)

Everything below is behind `MULTI_ORG` in `src/lib/multi-org.ts`, a code constant that stays `false` in this release: with it off every path gives M1's answer (`tests/multi-org-flag-off.test.ts`), and the M2 test files turn it on per file. Release step: **expand** (rule 679). Turning the constant on is its own pull request, merged with account-id R2 (decision 11), after the founder's yes. **Since the enable release (section 93.4, rules 3810-3822) the constant is `true`.**

1. Workspace in the address (decision 7, rules 2113, 2114, 2135). `wsHandle(aid)` = `w_` + 16 base32 characters of `HMAC(CONNECT_SECRET, "ws:handle:v1:" + aid)`: stable, not reversible, never stored. Pages live at `/{lang}/app/w/{handle}/…`; the proxy rewrites that to the page folder, sets `x-thakeen-ws` from the path (stripping a browser-sent one), `x-pathname` and `x-search`. `resolveSession` takes the handle as a selector among the person's own accounts (own `acct` in two rounds; any other in three, through `orgs:{pid}` and the legacy home, then `resolveIds`); a miss is `notFound()` for every case and writes nothing. Person pages (`/login`, `/signup`, `/join`, `/workspaces`, `/settings/security`, `/admin`, …, `PERSON_PAGES`) stay outside `w/`.
2. The second guard (rule 2113). A state-changing `/api/` call of a signed-in browser with no valid handle answers 409 `workspace_missing`, in the proxy and again in `handler()`; `WORKSPACE_FREE` lists the person-level and token routes, and `WORKSPACE_FREE_METHODS` the person-level methods of a workspace route (`PATCH /api/account`, the password change; rule 3826). The client sends the header from the page's own link through the one `window.fetch` wrapper (`installBrandFetch({ workspace: true })` in `src/lib/brand-fetch.ts`, item 8), so two tabs on two handles write into their own accounts. `tests/multi-org-guard-coverage.test.ts` keeps every workspace route inside the guard.
3. Old addresses (rule 2113). `/{lang}/app/…` without `w/` answers 307 from the layout to the same page of the session's workspace, query kept, never a loop (`oldAppTarget`). Every app link is built through `appHref` (`useAppLink` / `useBrandHref` in client components, `appHrefFor`/`appHrefIn` in `src/lib/ws-href.ts` and `pageHref` on the server); `tests/multi-org-links-scan.test.ts` keeps raw `/{lang}/app` links out. Stored links (notifications) are moved into the workspace when read (`inWorkspace`).
4. Workspaces (rules 2112, 2123). `GET /api/workspaces` lists the person's held accounts by handle, name and role. `POST /api/workspaces` creates one: verified person, 3 per person per day (`wscreate`), owned cap `ownedCap ?? 3`, the trial once per person (`trialUsedAt`), an `a_` account owned through `ownerPid`, then the session and `lastorg:` move to it. `POST /api/workspaces/switch {handle}` moves the session's `acct` (`SET XX`, TTL kept) and `lastorg:`, never the brand choice. The page `/{lang}/app/workspaces` lists, opens and creates; the sidebar switcher (above the brand, only with two workspaces or more) switches and then loads the same page of the new workspace with a full navigation, a record's query dropped (`switchTarget`).
5. Invites to existing persons (rule 2116). An address that already signs in is invited like a new one; only this account's owner, its members and the inviter get `already_member`. The preview says `signIn` when the address has a login. The invitee accepts from their own signed-in session (`{token, join: true}`): the verified address must equal the invite's; no password is set and none is asked. The password way answers 409 `signin_required` for such an address.
6. New persons (rule 2117). An invite accepted with a password makes a `p_` person and a membership, no account. A sign-up (the form and Google) makes a `p_` person with the credentials and `trialUsedAt` and an opaque `a_` account that names it in `ownerPid`.
7. A person with no workspace (rules 2124, 2126). Leaving, removal or deletion of the last workspace keeps the person (`forgetPersonIfAlone` is off with the flag on); `getLogin` answers a member login with no account, so the person signs in, the layout sends them to the workspaces page, and the address stays taken. Decided default (2026-10-21, delegated): a person whose memberships are all paused signs in the same way instead of getting the 403 `disabled` of a single-workspace member, so they can still open or create another workspace; this closes the M1 item "getLogin finds no login for a person with only paused memberships" (section 93.1, item 3). A `p_` owner who holds nothing else still goes with its account (reconciliation 7).
8. The workspace and the brand in one link (merge with Part C, 2026-10-21, rules 889, 2112, 2113). A brand page of workspace `h` is `/{lang}/app/w/{h}/b/{brand}/…`. The proxy strips `w/{h}` in ONE rewrite (to `/{lang}/app/b/{brand}/…`, the real brand page folders) and sets `x-thakeen-ws` from it; `x-thakeen-brand` comes from the browser's path as in section 106 (`brandFromPath` reads both forms). `resolveSession` picks the account by the handle first (`resolveInHandle`, a miss is `notFound()`), then the brand among THAT account's brands (`selectedBrand`), so handle B with a brand only A has is a 404. One builder: `appHref(lang, path, handle, brand)` in `src/lib/ws-path.ts` delegates to brand-path's `appHref`; a person page drops both, an account page keeps the handle only. One fetch wrapper: `installBrandFetch` sends the brand header always and the handle only when the provider passes `workspace: MULTI_ORG`. A navigation carries `brand=` and `ws=` (`withBrandQuery`, `apiHref`); the proxy copies `ws` into the header only with `MULTI_ORG` on, only on GET/HEAD to `/api/`, only without a header and only a valid handle. A workspace switch from a brand page opens the same section root in the new workspace without the old brand (`switchTarget`), and the `[...legacy]` page then picks the new workspace's brand. With `MULTI_ORG` off no handle exists, so every link is section 106's (`tests/multi-org-brand-url-off.test.ts`); the switch-on half is `tests/multi-org-brand-url.test.ts`. A builder's result is never used as a prefix (`${base}/x`): a brand page's prefix would put an account path under `/b/{id}`.

Founder actions for M2: merge the release pull request (this expand, `MULTI_ORG` still false) into `production` after its checks; later, the separate pull request that sets `MULTI_ORG = true`, merged with account-id R2, only after M1 has been live 7 days with no fix release touching it and S1 reported 0 conflicts (R2's data script runs only on the founder's yes, not before 2026-10-26). Rollback floor of the flip: M1.

Not done in M2 (follow-ups): the console has no field for `ownedCap`; "forgot password" sends no link to a person with no workspace (the reset flow needs an account); the lifecycle mails of an account created without a trial (`trialEnds` = its creation) were not reviewed; the other M1 items of section 93.1 item 3 (`resolveLegacy` and `ownsLogin` following `ownerPid`, the explicit refusal of a `p_` person's member address move, the `{pid, aid}` link revokes) are not part of this branch.

### 93.3 Release M3 lifecycle (2026-10-21, branch `claude/wave3a-m3-lifecycle`, plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-21-multi-org-m3.md`)

Everything below is behind `MULTI_ORG` (still `false`): with it off each new route answers 404 before it reads a session, the Members page shows neither new section and an invite writes no inbox entry (`tests/multi-org-flag-off.test.ts`). Release step: expand; rollback is M2. Rules: `docs/system/tests/2750-multi-org-m3.md`.

1. Invitations inbox (rule 2753). `createInvite` also adds the invite id to `invitesfor:{email}`; `dropInvites` (accept, cancel, discard) takes it out. `GET /api/workspaces/invites` reads the set only for the signed-in person's own verified address and answers workspace name, inviter, role and expiry; accepting stays on the mailed link. The workspaces page lists them.
2. Team log (rule 2129, read half). `GET /api/team/log` (owner and admin, `team.log.view`) returns the session's own account's `teamlog:` rows with addresses only; the Members page shows them on demand.
3. Ownership offer (rules 2128, 2750). On the Members page the owner names an enabled admin by address and confirms. `POST /api/team/transfer`: owner actor only, 5 a day per person, mail must be set up, then `freshProof` (the person's password and, with two-step on, a fresh authenticator code; never a recovery code). Only an `a_` account owned by a `p_` person, never a partner account, to a `p_` admin whose `person:` is verified. One offer per account in `xfer:{aid}` (the token's SHA-256, 7 days); the token goes only in the mailed link's fragment `#xfer={handle}.{token}`; a failed send withdraws the offer. `DELETE` withdraws it.
4. Accept (rules 2751, 2752). The workspaces page reads the fragment and shows an accept card. `POST /api/workspaces/transfer {handle, token}` (10 an hour per person): the handle must be one of the person's held accounts and the hash match (`sameSecret`). Under the team → login → person → user locks (rule 2139) the offer, the account and the roster are read again; a changed owner, a demoted or disabled admin or a partner record voids the offer, a full owned-workspace cap keeps it (409). Effects, single use: offer deleted; MCP tokens and grants revoked before and after; `ownerPid`, contact address, name and verification from the new owner, phone cleared; saved card dropped and renewals off (paid-through kept); the old owner becomes an admin; the new owner's membership row and roster entry go; one `ownership_transferred` row in the team log; after the locks the old owner's Reem calendar links are revoked. The accepting device switches to the workspace.
5. Delete one's login (rules 2127, 2754). The workspaces page has a "Delete my login" card (password, and a two-step or recovery code when on) that asks in the app's own dialog. `DELETE /api/workspaces/person` (5 an hour per person): only a `p_` person from its own `person:` row (a legacy-shaped one waits for M4/S2), a fresh proof, refused while the person owns any account (delete it or hand it over first). Then `deletePerson` (`src/lib/person-delete.ts`): every membership the person has a row in, paused ones included (`heldAnywhere`), is deleted under its own team lock one at a time (`deleteMembership`, one "left" row each); under `lock:login` → `lock:person` the person is read again (anything held now answers `busy`) and `forgetPerson` deletes `person:`, `orgs:`, `lastorg:`, passkeys, two-step state, the waiting address change, the Google link, `invitesfor:`, every device session and the login index in one round. This device is signed out and goes to the sign-in page.
6. The console people view (rules 2130, 2755). `GET /api/admin/people/{email}` (founder or staff with `workspace.view`; 404 on a build with `MULTI_ORG` off, the rollback floor; on since the enable release, rule 3810): the address picks the person through `getLogin`, then `peopleView` (`src/lib/admin-people.ts`) reads every account the person owns or has a membership row in, paused ones included, in one `pmap` round, and answers the person (address, name, verified, suspended, last sign-in) and each account by handle, display name, role, plan, account suspension and paused. No id, hash, secret or token. One audit row before the answer; nothing else is written. An impersonation session has no person, so it cannot switch accounts (`POST /api/workspaces/switch` answers 401 and writes nothing).
7. Legacy sessions and a person with nothing left (rules 2131, 2124). A session written before R1 is refused while its person is suspended (`resolveLegacy`, section 91.1), as every other resolver already did. With `MULTI_ORG` on, a person removed from (or leaving) its last membership keeps its person row and login, signs in to no account (the workspaces page) and creates a workspace of its own, which it owns; with the switch off the same removal deletes the person as in R1 (plan reconciliation 7).

8. Card after a handover (rule 2757, review B fix round 1). The accept stamps `ownerSince` on the account (ISO, under the same locks; optional, older builds ignore it). Any card save (`saveCard`, the shared billing path) whose intent was created before `ownerSince`, or has no valid `createdAt` on an account that has one, is refused under the user lock and logged with the intent reference only; a payment for such an intent extends the paid month but neither saves its card nor turns renewals on. The accept is also void when the offering or the receiving person is suspended (rule 2758).
9. Smaller fixes from the same round. The offer answers 503 `not_configured` in production without `CONNECT_SECRET`, and any throw while the link and mail are built withdraws it (502). The console people view answers 503 `not_configured` there too, and audits a lookup that finds nobody (`people view (not found)`). Deleting one's login while a join lands answers 409 `left_partway` with the number of workspaces left; the same request again finishes. The rail switcher asks for the invitations inbox when it is opened, not on every workspace change.
10. Links script (rule 2756), the precondition of M4. `scripts/multi-org-links.ts`, run by hand from the live M3 build after S1 and before the M4 release, dry first: every link made before M1 (a feed or booking record with no `pid`/`aid`, named by the legacy per-login pointer of a home login) gets its M1-era pointer (SET NX) and then its owner's `pid`, `aid` and `home: true` (SET XX, so a record revoked meanwhile stays gone), under the app's own link locks, only when M3's `loginHolds` answers the same for the stamped record; the legacy pointer stays. The home brand choice goes to `activebrand:{pid}:{aid}`: carried when absent, and written over an M1-era choice that differs (M3 shows the legacy one). The brand overwrite reads the legacy choice again just before writing and skips (counted busy) if it changed. `--retire`, a separate mode, revokes what `--apply` cannot carry (a link whose answer would change, a malformed record) through the app's own `revokeFeed`/`revokeBook` with `locked: true`, called under the script's own locks so no link created meanwhile is revoked by mistake; so M4's S2 finds no record without ids. It also deletes a stale M1-era home pointer and the record it names (the R1-rollback re-create case: the pointer names a missing record, or a home record of this login's own `pid`/`aid`), after which the next `--apply` carries the R1 link; any other pointer or login conflict is never written, only counted, for the founder by hand. The dry run splits the retires into links live in M3 today (they stop) and links already dead. Counts only; exit 1 when anything is left, and a dry run exits 1 while anything is still to stamp, align or retire; a second run writes nothing. M4's stack merge deletes the script (it uses helpers M4 removes).

Founder actions for M3: merging the release pull request (expand, `MULTI_ORG` still false). Before M4: the links script, dry run then `--apply` on the founder's yes (it writes production data), a second dry run, `--retire` only on a separate yes after reading that dry run's counts (how many links live in M3 today would stop), then `--apply` again (it carries the R1 links freed by `--retire`) and a final dry run; any pointer or login conflict left is looked at by hand. M4 precondition: the final dry run shows 0 stamped / 0 aligned / 0 to retire, no conflict and no busy, and exits 0.

### 93.4 Release M2 + R2 enable: the switches on (2026-10-24, branch `claude/multi-org-switch-on-620420`, rules 3810-3822)

One release turns on both constants (decision 11): `MULTI_ORG` in `src/lib/multi-org.ts` (multi-workspace M2) and `OPAQUE_IDS` in `src/lib/account-id.ts` (account-id R2). Release step: **enable**. No stored shape changes and `docs/system/variables.md` does not change; every path behind the two constants (sections 88.2, 93.2, 93.3) now runs on production.

1. What changes for a customer (rule 3810). A sign-up is a `p_` person owning an `a_` account (`createUser`); an invite to an address that already has a login is accepted signed in; the workspace switcher, `/app/workspaces`, `POST /api/workspaces`, the switch route and the M3 routes answer; app pages live under `/{lang}/app/w/{handle}`; a state-changing workspace API without `x-thakeen-ws` answers 409 `workspace_missing`; an owner's address move is in place with the old address held 30 days and a revert link. Accounts made before this release keep their address ids and resolve as before (the legacy fallback, sections 88 and 93).
2. The route wrapper's order (rule 3812). `handler()` reads the session first and answers a signed-out caller with the route's own 401; only then does a state-changing request with no workspace handle get 409 `workspace_missing`. The proxy already answered 409 only to a request with a session cookie, so both now agree.
3. Partner approval (rule 3813). `approvePartner` writes the partner code on the owner's account by `acct(login.user)`; it locked `user:{address}`, which an `a_` account never has (`user_not_found`).
4. Rollback floor (rule 3814). M1 for multi-workspace and the R2-expand release for account ids: a build before either cannot sign in a `p_` person or reads a moved owner's old key. Turning the constants off again gives M1's answers: `tests/multi-org-flag-off.test.ts`, `tests/multi-org-brand-url-off.test.ts`, `tests/multi-org-links-scan.test.ts` and `tests/account-id-r1.test.ts` mock them off for the whole file, `tests/passkeys-moves.test.ts` turns them off for the whole file too, and five files turn them off for the tests that pin the floor's answer.
5. Tests (rules 3811, 3815). The test client sends the session account's workspace handle on every JSON request after `signIn` (`useWorkspace(null)` sends none), as the page does. A fixture may ask for an account made before this release (`newUser(..., { addressId: true })`, `createUser`'s test-only `opaque: false`); no route passes `createUser` options.
6. Founder alerts (rule 3816). The fair-use pause, the daily margin alert and the golden regression alert find each `ADMIN_EMAILS` founder through the login (`accountByLogin`) and file the bell item at the founder's account id; they read `user:{address}`, which an `a_` founder never has, so no founder was told. `staff-alert.ts` emails the address and stays.
7. Calendar links made before M1 (rule 3817). `legacyHolds` in `src/lib/secretary-feed.ts` checks the link's scope against the brands of `acct(owner)`, not the owner's address: an owner whose address moved in place keeps the account id.
8. Passkeys (rule 3818). A `p_` person's passkey set stays at its pid through an owner's in-place address move and a member's removal; the copy to a new pid is the floor's path only.
9. The founder's log (rule 3819). `hand_refill` and `suspension.expired` rows name the account's sign-in address, read from the saved record, not the account id they are called with.
10. Partners (rule 3820). The bank route saves under the partner's sign-in address; the commission's same-team check finds the partner's person and account through its login, as `claimPartnerCode` does.
11. Analytics (rule 3821). A login is counted under the account id in the active sets, and the 7-day retention reads them with the account id.
12. Scope fallback (rule 3822). A user without `scope` falls back to its account id, never to the sign-in address.
13. The Security page's writes (rule 3826). Settings › Security is a person page with no handle in its path, so its writes carried no `x-thakeen-ws` and answered 409 `workspace_missing` with the switch on: adding, renaming or removing a passkey (`/api/account/passkeys`, the person's `passkeys:{pid}`) and changing the password (`PATCH /api/account`, the person's hash). Both are person-level and need no workspace: the passkey route is in `WORKSPACE_FREE`, and `PATCH` on `/api/account` in `WORKSPACE_FREE_METHODS`. `DELETE /api/account`, the account wipe, stays guarded.
14. The end-to-end suite with the switches on (rule 3827). A spec's direct API call names the workspace the session has open (`e2e/fixtures/workspace-header.ts`, the entry marked `current` in `GET /api/workspaces`), as the page's fetch wrapper does; URL checks compare the path without its `/w/{handle}` (`appPath`). The seeded owner is an account made before R2 enable (`user:{address}`, id = its address) so the held-routine seed can name it; accounts a spec signs up are opaque. A removed member, or one who left, or the member of a deleted account, still signs in, with no workspace (rules 2124, 2126).
15. A sign-in lands with a full load (rule 3828). The log-in page, a password reset and an invite accept go into the app with `window.location.assign`, as the verify page and the workspace switcher already did. `/{lang}/app/w/{handle}/…` is served by a rewrite, and a server redirect inside such a page during a client navigation lost the `/w/{handle}` segment; with the `refresh()` that followed the push, the router refetched the page without end. A new owner who logged in before making a brand file (the inbox redirects to the start page) saw a blank page while the browser sent hundreds of requests a second.

Preconditions before it goes live (flows 88.2 and 93.1): M1 live at least 7 days, the S1 conversion applied with 0 conflicts, R1 live at least 7 days, `scripts/account-ids.ts` reporting 0 conflicts, and the saved-card check on staging. The release pull request lists their state.

## 94. Agent tool authority (2026-10-20, branch `claude/lucid-faraday-kq261a`, rules 2430-2436)

The first piece adopted from the CopilotKit/OpenDots study (`docs/OpenDots_Adoption.md`): OpenDots gives each agent its own tool switches ("Enabled", "Ask first") and re-checks them at execution. Thakeen had hard-coded tool lists per agent and no owner control. Coverage in `docs/system/tests/2430-agent-tool-authority.md`.

1. Setting (rules 2430, 2435). Governance gains "What each agent may do in chat": for each of the eight agents and five capabilities (create content, images, write workspace files, publish, import the catalog) one of Allowed, Ask first (create content and images only) or Not allowed. Stored as `Governance.toolPolicy` with only `ask` and `off` entries; `mergePolicy` drops unknown agents, capabilities and values on every read of a request or a record. The page sends every agent and capability on Save, `on` included, so switching back to Allowed takes effect. History, restore and the activity row work as for any setting.
2. Turn start (rule 2431). chat() reads governance with its other reads (one `Promise.all`), offers `toolsFor(agent, gov.toolPolicy)`, and adds one line to the workspace block of the prompt naming what is off or ask-first for this agent (`policyNote`). Prompt text only explains.
3. Each call (rules 2432-2433). `runTool` reads governance again at the call: off refuses with `offRefusal` and nothing ran; ask passes `{ ask: true }` to `spendGate`, which stores the card (`reason: "ask"`) whatever the owner wrote, at most two a reply, or refuses over MCP. The card says the owner set ask-first in Governance.
4. Run (rule 2434). `decideSpend` re-reads the setting for the card's agent before the claim's charge: off answers 409 `revoked`, the card shows that nothing ran, and it stays open.
4a. Unattended spending (rule 3014). Majed's auto-run (section 35) starts an item only while the item's agent has `produce` on: `off` or `ask` blocks it in `autoBlock` (`authority`), so the cron, the Governance queue and Majed's reply all leave it to the owner's Run. A login's Run of a handoff card, the Generate page and MCP `generate_content` are a person's own request and stay outside the policy (rule 3015, permissions.md).
5. Shapes (rule 2436, rule 679 expand). `Governance.toolPolicy` and `SpendProposal.reason` are new and optional. A record without them reads as all on and as the spend gate's own reason, which is what every turn did before. Older code ignores both; an older build's governance save would drop `toolPolicy` (back to all on, the earlier behaviour). No data conversion. The contract step has nothing to remove.

## 95. Edits from an older view (2026-10-20, branch `claude/lucid-faraday-kq261a`, rules 2440-2449)

Item 1 of the OpenDots adoption order (`docs/OpenDots_Adoption.md` section 4): OpenDots refuses a page write whose `expectedRevision` is not current and keeps the person's draft. Thakeen checked the version only when a change ended approved; a member's text edit and a Files save were last-write-wins. Coverage in `docs/system/tests/2440-stale-writes.md`.

1. Inbox edits (rule 2440). `PATCH /api/tasks/{id}` already receives the card's `version` with every change (src/components/app/Inbox.tsx). A text edit that carries it is saved only while it is still the item's version, read under the task lock; otherwise 409 `changed` with the stored task, and the inbox shows the current text with the typed edit kept in the editor (its existing `changed` path). A new image counts as a change, as it does for approval.
2. Files (rule 2441). The Files page sends `baseAt`: the opened file's `updatedAt` on Save, null for "New file". `writeFileChecked` takes `filelock:{scope}:{name}`, reads, and writes only when the file is still that one: otherwise 409 `stale` (changed or deleted) or `exists` (a new name already taken, which used to overwrite the file with a heading), with the file as it is now. The page keeps the text in the editor and offers "Save mine over it" (sent with the newer file's time) or "Discard mine and show the latest".
3. Shapes (rule 679, expand). Both fields are optional request fields; no stored shape changes. A request without them is saved as before: uploads from the Library page, agents' `write_file`, imports, and pages loaded before the release. The contract step, making `baseAt` required on the Files page's route, is optional and not planned: other writers rightly send none.
4. Every writer (rules 2520-2522, 2026-10-20, branch `claude/agent-file-lock`). Since then every `writeFile` takes the same `filelock:{scope}:{name}`, so no write lands between a checked save's read and its write (the gap this item named before: an agent's or an import's write in that one round trip, which the person's save then overwrote without a word). `writeFileChecked` holds the lock and writes with `locked: true`. Any other writer (agents, imports, catalog syncs, uploads, saves without a base) waits up to 2 s (40 × 50 ms) and then writes anyway with a `file lock not had` warning, so a lock left by a killed request (10 s at most) delays it and never fails it (rule 2521). An agent's write redirected to the `-imported` sibling of a person's file takes the sibling's lock too, inside the first; the sibling's name is longer, so locks are always taken shorter name first and two writers never wait on each other in a circle (rule 2522). Each write costs two more store commands (lock and unlock). Rules 2442-2449 are held.

## 96. What Thakeen has switched on, for customers (2026-10-20, branch `claude/lucid-faraday-kq261a`, rules 2450-2459)

Item 2 of the OpenDots adoption order (`docs/OpenDots_Adoption.md` section 4): OpenDots computes what is missing on the server and shows it before a feature is tried. Thakeen showed it to the founder only (section 69, `integration-status.ts`). Coverage in `docs/system/tests/2450-feature-status.md`.

1. Source (rule 2450). `customerFeatures()` (src/lib/feature-status.ts) maps six customer features to the founder page's integrations: the team (Anthropic, or Straitly with `LLM_PROVIDER`), images (fal.ai), higher-quality images and video (kie.ai or Higgsfield with `MEDIA_MODELS_ON`), email notices (Resend), card payments (Paymob with `PAYMOB_LIVE`), LinkedIn/TikTok/ads connections (Composio). A feature is on only when `integrationsView` says `on` and none of its code switches is off. Presence only, as on the founder page.
2. Page (rule 2451). The customer's Connections page (`/app/settings/connections`) opens with "What Thakeen has switched on": each feature, one line on what it does, and a text badge (On / Not on yet). Server-rendered; no request, no route.
3. Names (rule 2452). No variable name, partial state or gate wording reaches the customer; the founder page keeps them.
4. Not changed. Each surface's own message when a feature is off (the image placeholder label in the inbox, the chat's "not available right now", the billing page's payment links) stays as it is. Rules 2453-2459 are held.

## 97. Agent limits in the founder's workspace view (2026-10-20, branch `claude/lucid-faraday-kq261a`, rules 2460-2469)

Item 3 of the OpenDots adoption order. The account sheet's Workspace tab (section 84, read only, one audit row per open) now lists, under each brand, that brand's agent tool authority (section 94) as its restrictions only: "Noor · publish · not allowed". An empty list reads "Every agent may do everything". Each brand's governance is read from `scopeFor(owner.email, brand.id)`, both from stored records, in the same `pmap` round as the members' last sign-ins. Nothing is written and nothing else is shown. Coverage in `docs/system/tests/2460-workspace-limits.md`; rules 2461-2469 are held.

## 98. Paid scheduled work: charge once, refund always, hold for review (2026-10-20, branch `claude/lucid-faraday-kq261a`, rules 2470-2489)

Item 4 of the OpenDots adoption order, built after the founder's yes on 2026-10-20 to the spec's three recommendations (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-20-paid-cron-held-for-review-design.md` section 7): charge once and refund automatically; a held run waits for the owner's Run; charges lost before the release are refunded by hand, no scan. Coverage in `docs/system/tests/2470-paid-cron-held.md`.

1. Claims (rules 2470-2471). Routines charge under `routine:{scope}:{id}:{period}` and every handoff item run (the owner's and the auto-run's) under `handoff:{scope}:{id}:{n}`, through `chargeOnce`, as the Sunday plan already did. A retry with the same claim reuses the hold; a failure gives it back once. The routine's last-run marker and its receipt, and the handoff item's `done` with its tasks, are now written inside the batch's refund guard (`commit`).
2. The receipt (rules 2476-2478). Before the charge, under the run's lock, `beginRun(claim)`:
   - `delivered`: nothing runs (the lost marker is put back);
   - `started` under 10 minutes old: `retry:in_flight`;
   - `started` older: the cron marks it `held`, tells the owner once (an in-app notice to Scheduled tasks, a `run_held` row) and answers `held`, which the batch counts as done for the day;
   - otherwise `started`, and the run goes on.

   A refusal before the charge clears the receipt; a refunded failure marks it `refunded`. The handoff item's own status (`proposed`, `running`, `done`, `failed`) is its receipt: the auto-run never starts a `running` item.
3. The owner's Run (rule 2479). The weekly card's "Run now" and, for a held routine, the Run button under it on Scheduled tasks (`POST /api/schedule {run: "routine", id}`) resume the held run on its claim: a charge the killed run took is reused. Only a held routine can be run this way (`not_held` otherwise).
4. The sweep (rules 2473-2475, 2480). The daily cron's `sweepHolds` (src/lib/hold-sweep.ts) replaces the weekly-only sweep. It resolves the holds on the account record the charges were taken from, `acct(u)` (rule 1980), never the owner's address, so an account with an opaque id is refunded too (the 2026-10-20 integration check found both sweeps reading the address):
   - Weekly holds of ended weeks are resolved as before; a hold of this week whose run the cron held is refunded the next day, as the held notice promises.
   - Routine and handoff holds older than 10 minutes are kept when the run delivered (receipt `delivered`, item `done`) and refunded otherwise. A held run nobody ran is refunded the next day, so the notice's "comes back within a day" holds.
   - A delivered run's owed share (a short batch's refund that failed, recorded by `generateBatch` through `oweRun`) is given back in the same ledger save that drops its hold.

   Each refund writes a `run_refunded` row in its brand, and the owner gets one notice for the day's refunds. The cron report counts `weekly_*` and `run_*` refunds and settles.
5. The founder (rules 2484-2485). `runCronBatch` stores its last hop's counts in `cronreport:{job}` (no address, scope or result text), and the Usage page shows them under each heartbeat: done, ran, held, failed, left.
6. Earlier losses (rule 2486, founder decision 3). `scripts/lost-scheduled-charges.ts` (run by hand: `npx --no-install jiti scripts/lost-scheduled-charges.ts` with the store's variables set privately) prints, read only, the routine charges of this and the previous period that were neither refunded nor delivered (`routinetry:` less a run recorded in the period) and handoff items left `running` past their lock (marked "check": the record cannot tell whether the killed run reached its charge). It skips anything with a receipt or a claim hold, which the sweep refunds. Older losses left no trace. The founder refunds from the console.
7. Not built. Rule 2483 (a `run_missed` row for a period whose run only ever answered `retry:*`) is not built: it needs a record of every routine's periods, and the case (the model paused or a lock held for a whole week or month) is rare. Rules 2487-2489 are held.
8. Shapes (rule 679, expand). New keys `runrec:` and `cronreport:`; new claim prefixes in `User.paidOnce` (older code reads the map only by exact claim); new activity kinds `run_held` and `run_refunded`; `RoutineView.held`. No conversion: routine and handoff charges made before the release have no hold, and the founder refunds any of them by hand (founder decision 3).

## 99. The ops agent: checks, morning report and approve-to-act on Telegram (2026-10-20, branch `claude/thakeen-ops-agent`, rules 2380-2390)

The founder asked for an operations agent that watches the site, reports, and proposes, and never acts on its own. It runs on Claude Managed Agents (agents/thakeen-ops/: `agent.md`, `environment.yaml`, `vault.yaml`, two deployments), outside this app; the app gives it three routes (src/lib/ops.ts). Coverage in `docs/system/tests/2380-ops-agent.md`.

1. Schedule (rule 2390). `deployment-checks.yaml` fires every three hours (`0 */3 * * *`, Asia/Riyadh) and stays silent when all is well; `deployment-morning.yaml` fires at 07:00 Riyadh and always sends a report. Each fired session carries a budget of 100 US cents of list cost. Opus 5.5 at low effort; the built-in toolset with web search and web fetch off, `auto` permissions, no MCP server and no custom tool, so a run with nobody attached never waits for an approval. The sandbox reaches `thakeen.com`, `www.thakeen.com` and `api.github.com` only.
2. Reading (rules 2380-2382). The agent reads `GET /api/health`, the public pages, `GET /api/ops/summary` (bearer `OPS_AGENT_TOKEN`: the build, the store, every cron heartbeat, today's and yesterday's event counters, the last margin run's counts, the number of accounts; no email, name or workspace), and, with its own read-only GitHub token, open pull requests, the last CI runs on `main` and `production` and `docs/Owner_Tasks_AR.md`. Both tokens live in the agent's vault as `environment_variable` credentials limited to their hosts and injected in headers only; the sandbox never sees their values.
3. Telling the founder (rules 2383-2385). The agent posts `{ text, alert_key?, proposal? }` to `POST /api/ops/telegram` (same bearer). Telegram puts the bot token in the URL path, where a vault cannot substitute a secret, so the app sends the message: to `TELEGRAM_CHAT_ID` only, as plain text with link previews off. An alert key is sent at most once per 12 hours (`ops:alert:{key}`, `setNx`), so a problem that lasts is reported twice a day at most. A proposal (only `github_issue`, title up to 120 characters, body up to 2500) is shown in full in the message and stored for 3 days under a random id (`ops:proposal:{id}`); the app adds the «موافق» and «رفض» buttons.
4. The press (rules 2386-2389). Telegram calls `POST /api/ops/telegram/callback` with `X-Telegram-Bot-Api-Secret-Token`. A press counts only when the presser and the chat are both `TELEGRAM_CHAT_ID`. The proposal is taken with GETDEL whatever the button: «رفض» removes it, «موافق» opens one issue on `iAbulkhair/Nashr` (a constant) with `OPS_GITHUB_TOKEN`, titled `[ops] {title}`, with a footer saying when the agent proposed it and when the founder approved. The message is edited to say what happened and loses its buttons; a second press finds nothing.
5. What the model cannot do. It cannot pick the chat, the buttons, the repository or the action: those are in code. It cannot open the issue: only the founder's press does, and only once. Its text may quote untrusted pages and is sent as plain text. Setup, the supervised first run and turning the schedules on are the founder's steps in `agents/thakeen-ops/README.md`.

## 100. Customer passkeys (2026-10-20, branch `claude/wave3c-passkeys`, rules 2400-2429, release "expand")

Spec `docs/specs/2026-10-20-passkeys.md` (flows A-E). Owners and members add passkeys in Settings > Security and sign in with them on the log-in page. Permissions in docs/system/permissions.md rows 254-257 and "Account security" item 2a; tests in `docs/system/tests/2400-passkeys.md`. Code: the WebAuthn seam `src/lib/webauthn.ts` (the only importer of `@simplewebauthn/server`, rule 2427), the store `src/lib/passkeys.ts`, the off switch `src/lib/passkey-switch.ts`, the limits `src/lib/passkey-limits.ts` (import-free, shared with the browser), the manage route `src/app/api/account/passkeys/route.ts`, the card `src/components/app/settings/PasskeySettings.tsx`, the sign-in routes `src/app/api/auth/passkey/options/route.ts` and `src/app/api/auth/passkey/verify/route.ts`, the log-in form `LoginForm` in `src/components/app/AuthForm.tsx`, and the console switch `src/app/api/admin/passkeys/route.ts` with its row `PasskeysSwitch` in `src/components/admin/IntegrationsPanel.tsx`. Every key is per person: `passkeys:{pid}` (the set: user handle `uid`, birth `born`, at most 10 credentials), `pkcred:{sha256(credentialId)}` (reverse index, SET NX), `pkreg:{pid}` (open registration, 300 s). The pid comes only from our own records, never from the request (rule 2400): on the manage routes it is the one `actingLogin` resolves from the session; on passkey sign-in it is the one the reverse index names, forward-checked (`getPerson`, `getLogin(person.email)` naming the same pid, the credential in that pid's set; rule 2405); in an address move it is the old and the new pid the move resolved. The birth stamp is the person's `createdAt`; a set stamped with another birth is ignored (rule 2402).

### 100.1 A. Add a passkey

1. `POST /api/account/passkeys {action: "register-options", password? | code?}`: `currentUser()` (401) → `forbid(u, "passkeys.manage")` (member-level, refused to a founder impersonating, 403) → `actingLogin(u)` and the person (null: 403 `forbidden`) → the switch on (404 `unavailable`) → fewer than 10 passkeys (409 `limit`) → the proof (`freshProof`, before any lock, pre-flight ruling 9): two-step on → `checkMfaCode(req, login, bornAt, code, {recovery: true})` (400 `bad_code`); off → `ownPasswordMatches` (401 `wrong_password`); neither a password nor two-step → 403 `reauth_unavailable`. Every try counts in `rate:pkpw:{login}` (10 per hour, 429 `slow_down` with Retry-After) before the compare; a right one is given back. Then `ensureUid` (creates or replaces the set under the person lock; a set that does not fit the shape: 400 `invalid`), the options through the seam (user id = `uid`, user name = the login address, display name = the person's name, the set's credentials excluded), and `pkreg:{pid}` = `{challenge, sess: sha256(session key), at}` for 300 s. Answer `{ok, options}`.
2. The card asks for the name together with the proof, before step 1 (prefilled with the device family from the user agent; Cancel there starts nothing), then runs `startRegistration({optionsJSON})` and sends step 3 as soon as it resolves, with no user step between the ceremony and verify (`runAdd`), so the device never keeps a passkey the server did not hear of. A cancelled ceremony sends no verify.
3. `POST {action: "register-verify", response, name}`: the same gates without the proof → `getdel pkreg:{pid}` (missing, older than 300 s, or opened by another session: 410 `expired`; the challenge is gone either way, rule 2407) → `verifyRegistration` through the seam (400 `invalid`) → `addPasskey` under `lock:person:{pid}`: the set re-read, the cap re-checked (409 `limit`), `SET NX pkcred:` (409 `credential_taken`, nothing stored), the credential appended. After the lock, one notice email in the person's language (`sendEmail`, template `notice`: the passkey's name, the time in Asia/Riyadh labelled as Riyadh time (the zone the card shows dates in), a button to Security settings, no credential data); a failed or throwing send is logged as "passkey notice failed" without values and never undoes the add (rule 2415). Answer `{ok, item}`.

### 100.2 B. List, rename, remove

- `GET /api/account/passkeys`: the same gates without the proof and the switch; `{ok, items: [{ref, name, createdAt, lastUsedAt?, synced, transports}], max: 10, enabled, proof}`. `ref` is the first 16 hex characters of the credential id's SHA-256; no credential id, public key, AAGUID or counter (rule 2413). `proof` (`password`, `code` or `none`) tells the card which proof to ask for.
- `POST {action: "rename", ref, name}`: the session only (rule 2411); under the person lock, the entry whose `ref` is in the caller's own set (404 `not_found`); a name that is empty after cleaning is 400 `invalid`.
- `POST {action: "remove", ref, password? | code?}`: a `ref` not in the caller's own set is 404 `not_found` first, so a stale ref never spends a recovery code or a proof try (refs are looked up only in the caller's set, so this reveals nothing about anyone else); then the proof of A1 in this request, then under the person lock the entry goes, and its reverse entry only while it still names this pid (rule 2416). The last one may go; password sign-in is unaffected.
- A held person lock answers 409 `busy` on every write (rule 2414). An unknown action or a body that does not fit is 400 `bad_request`. With the switch off (`passkeys:off` = "1"), both register steps answer 404 `unavailable` and the card shows "turned off for now" in place of the add button; the list, rename and remove keep working (rule 2426).
- The card is rendered once `browserSupportsWebAuthn()` has been read on the client (nothing on the server). Without WebAuthn the card still lists, renames and removes, so a lost device's passkey can be removed from any browser; only the add button gives way to its own line, "this browser cannot add a passkey", distinct from the switch-off line (rule 2429). Each row's Rename and Remove buttons are named with the passkey's name. A founder viewing the account sees the read-only note instead.

### 100.3 C. Sign in with a passkey

Public: nobody is signed in yet. Two global keys: `pkauth:{sha256(nonce)}` (the sign-in challenge, 300 s) and the rate buckets `pk:ip:{ip}`, `pkv:ip:{ip}`, `pkv:cred:{sha256(credentialId)}`.

1. `POST /api/auth/passkey/options` (rules 2417, 2408): the switch on (404 `unavailable`) → `pk:ip:{ip}` 60 per 15 min (429 `slow_down` with `Retry-After`) → the options through the seam (`allowCredentials: []`, user verification required: the answer names no person) → a nonce of 32 random bytes; its SHA-256 keys `pkauth:` with `{challenge, at}` for 300 s, and the nonce itself travels only in the cookie `thakeen_pk` (httpOnly, Secure outside loopback http, SameSite=Lax, path `/api/auth/passkey`, 300 s, from `sessionCookie`). No person is read and no lock is taken.
2. The log-in page passes `passkeys` (the switch, read failing closed). With it on, the address field says `autocomplete="username webauthn"`, and on load the form checks `browserSupportsWebAuthnAutofill()`, fetches options and waits on `startAuthentication({optionsJSON, useBrowserAutofill: true})` (conditional UI) in the background; the password form is never blocked and a refused options call or a dismissed prompt says nothing. The waiting ceremony is replaced with fresh options every 270 s (`AUTOFILL_REFRESH_MS`, inside the 300 s challenge), unless a passkey was already picked. After it settles, `nextAutofill` decides: a sign-in arms nothing; any other end arms it again after 2 s, then 4 s, and stops at the third quick failure in a row, so a browser that refuses conditional requests never loops. A ceremony that ended with no verify answer after waiting 30 s or more starts the count again; a refused verify answer always counts. The options carry `timeout: 300000` (the challenge's life, set in `authenticationOptions`), so the browser's 60 s default never ends a waiting conditional request early. A `passkey_failed` answer is silent while the autofill re-arms and shown when it stops. The effect's cleanup aborts the options request and the pending ceremony (`WebAuthnAbortService`), and the run checks it is still current after every await. The button "Sign in with a passkey" is drawn when `showPasskeyButton(passkeys, browserSupportsWebAuthn())` (read with `useSyncExternalStore`, nothing on the server) and runs the same ceremony without autofill; while it runs the autofill is ended, and its `finally` arms the autofill again with the count reset. Client code imports `@simplewebauthn/browser` only, never the store.
3. `POST /api/auth/passkey/verify {response}` (rules 2403, 2405, 2408, 2409, 2418-2423), in this order: read `thakeen_pk` and clear it (`delete({name, path: "/api/auth/passkey"})`), so every outcome clears it, 404 and 429 included → the switch on (404 `unavailable`) → `pkv:ip:{ip}` 20 per 15 min (429) → `getdel pkauth:{sha256(nonce)}` (missing, malformed or older than 300 s: refused) → the response's credential id (`responseId`), its hash, `pkv:cred:{hash}` 10 per 15 min (429), both buckets before any person is read → `resolvePasskeyLogin(hash)`: the reverse entry → `getPerson` → `getLogin(person.email)` naming the same pid → the set born at the person's `createdAt` holding the credential → the user handle is present and equals the set's `uid` (`sameSecret`) → `verifyAuthentication` through the seam (challenge, origin and RP ID from `appOrigin()`, UV required; the handle it read must be the same `uid`) → `recordUse` under `lock:person:{pid}` (409 `busy` on timeout): a counter that did not move writes one `admin:log` row (`actor: system`, section `users`, action `passkey.counter_refused`, an 8-hex hash of the credential, never the id or the key) and is refused; otherwise the counter and `lastUsedAt` are written with `SET XX` → `finishAnswer(await finishLogin(login, {via: "passkey", codeDone: true}))`: every refusal of the password path (suspended account or person, a member whose owner is gone, unverified or newer, a seat-paused member), the session with `pid` and `acct`, the device row and `track("login")`; no two-step challenge is ever opened (rule 2420), and an owner who has not proved the address gets `{ok: true, verify: true}` like the password path. The counter and `lastUsedAt` are written before `finishLogin` runs (rule 2422), so a sign-in that `finishLogin` then refuses (a suspended account or person, a seat-paused member) still leaves the new counter stored; that is harmless, since the authenticator's next assertion carries a higher counter.
4. Every failure before `finishLogin` answers the same 401 `{ok: false, error: "passkey_failed"}` (rule 2423) and logs a fixed reason only (`no_challenge`, `bad_response`, `unknown_credential`, `user_handle`, `signature`, `counter`, `gone`), never a value. The form shows one generic line for it.

### 100.4 D. Address moves, password reset, deletion (rules 2402, 2414, 2424, 2425)

1. Password reset (`/api/auth/reset`) changes `passHash` and `sessionsAfter` only: `passkeys:{pid}`, its `pkcred:` entries and an open `pkreg:` stay byte for byte, and the passkey still signs in.
2. Member address move (`moveMemberLogin`, `src/lib/team.ts`, through the settings address change `confirmEmailChange`): under `lock:team` → both `lock:login` → both `lock:person` (the smaller first) → `lock:member`, after the record, row, roster and login index are written at the new address and `carry` ran, `movePasskeys(oldPid, toPid, { personLocked: true })` moves the set (same `uid`, `born` = the destination person's `createdAt`, every reverse entry compare-and-set to the new pid), then the old pid's remaining passkey keys (`passkeyKeys(oldPid)`: an open registration, any entry still naming it) go in the same round as the old record.
3. Owner address move (`moveOwnerLogin`, `src/lib/verify-email.ts`; the proved change through `/api/account/email` and the unproved typo fix through `/api/auth/verify/email`): both `lock:login` (the smaller address first) → both `lock:person` (the old owner person and the new address's, the smaller pid first; both moves' person locks expire after 60 s, `withMovePersonLock`, like the owner move's `lock:team`) → `lock:user` → `lock:team` (rule 2139; a person lock that cannot be had answers 409 `busy` in both routes, nothing written). After the new person and login index are written and `carry` ran, `movePasskeys(pid, toPid, { personLocked: true })`, then `wipeAccount` deletes the old person's remaining passkey keys with the rest of its pack. So an `addPasskey` or a sign-in counter write of either person waits for the move and never leaves a passkey under the old pid.
4. A move whose passkey step answers `skipped` (a person could not be read; unreachable in practice, both persons were just read or written) logs `passkey_move_skipped`, finishes the rest of the move and keeps the old pid's passkey keys for good (the owner move passes `keepPasskeys` to `wipeAccount`; nothing retries a move): inert, since no login names the old pid (rule 2405) and a new person there has another birth (rule 2402). A resumed move whose destination person was written again between the runs re-stamps `to` with the current birth.
5. Deletion (rule 2425): `wipeAccount` reads `passkeyKeys(owner pid)` in the same round as the Google link and deletes them with the owner's pack only when the owner holds nothing else; `forgetPersonIfAlone` (member removal, leave, forced removal, a member left with nothing by an account deletion) deletes `passkeyKeys(pid)` unconditionally, whoever holds the address now (the keys are the pid's, and only entries still naming the pid are listed). A person still held by another membership keeps its set.
6. A new person at a reused legacy address (same pid, another `createdAt`) never inherits a set: a set planted back with the old birth is ignored by sign-in (401 `passkey_failed`) and by the list (rule 2402).

### 100.5 E. The console off switch (rule 2426)

The switch is for a WebAuthn library or browser defect: passwords keep working, so turning passkeys off locks nobody out. It sits on the `skills:paused` pattern and is fail-open by design: passkeys are on unless `passkeys:off` holds `"1"` (`passkeysOn()`, a read error is a 500, never "on").

1. The console Integrations page (`/{lang}/admin/integrations`) shows a "Passkeys" row (`PasskeysSwitch`): `GET /api/admin/passkeys` → `adminActor(req)` (401) → `{ok, on, may}`, no audit row. `may` is `staffMay(consoleRole(req), "passkeys.switch")`, the check POST runs: for a support or read-only staff member the toggle is disabled and an "only a console owner can change this switch" line (Arabic and English) is shown, and the server still refuses a POST. A failed read shows its error line with a "Try again" button that reads again. The page header says the service list is read-only and the Passkeys row is a runtime switch a console owner changes there.
2. The toggle opens the console's reason form (`useAdminForm`), then `POST /api/admin/passkeys {on, reason}` through `adminFetch`: `adminActor(req)` (401) → `staffMay(consoleRole(req), "passkeys.switch")` (owner only; a support staff session gets 403 `forbidden`) → `secondFactorRefusal(req, who)` (403 `second_factor` until the step-up or a right code; `adminFetch` asks the code and sends the request again) → `reasonRefused(reason)` (400) → `on` a boolean (400 `bad_request`) → `setPasskeysOn(on)` (off: SET NX `passkeys:off` = `"1"`; on: GETDEL; either way the write answers whether passkeys were on just before, so `before` is the state this write replaced, not one read a command earlier) → `adminAudit({actor, via: viaOf(req), section: "system", action: "passkeys.off" | "passkeys.on", target: "passkeys", what, before: {on}, after: {on}, reason})` → `{ok, on}`. An audit write that throws puts the switch back (`setPasskeysOn(was)`) and answers 500 `audit_failed`, so no flip stays without its row; if the undo fails too, a `log.error` line ("audit row failed and the undo failed") is the record of a switch changed with no row.
3. While it is off: `POST /api/auth/passkey/options` and `/verify` answer 404 `unavailable` (the verify route clears the `thakeen_pk` cookie first), the log-in page renders no passkey button and no autofill, and the `register-options` and `register-verify` steps of `/api/account/passkeys` answer 404 `unavailable` (the card says adding is turned off). `GET /api/account/passkeys` lists with `enabled: false`; rename and remove keep working (removing is always safe).
4. No stored passkey is read, changed or deleted by either direction: every `passkeys:{pid}`, `pkcred:` and `pkreg:` key stays byte for byte, so turning the switch on again lets the same passkeys sign in (their counters carry on).

## 101. Line notes on a draft, sent in one revision (2026-10-20, branch `claude/draft-line-notes`, rules 2490-2499)

Pattern 3 of `docs/reports/2026-10-19-PRD-fizzy-orca_AR.md`, after Orca's notes on diff lines: an owner who wants three small changes to a post used to rewrite it by hand or reject it and pay for a new batch. Now they leave a note on each line and send them together; one model call writes one revision at one draft's price. Section number 101 because open branches already take 99 and 100. Coverage in `docs/system/tests/2490-draft-line-notes.md`.

1. Where (rules 2490, 2498). The inbox card of a pending item of a draft type (social, whatsapp, ads, gbp) shows "Line notes" next to Edit, for a role that may edit (`drafts`) while the agents run (`src/components/app/LineNotes.tsx`). The draft is shown line by line as plain text; + under a non-blank line opens a one-line note (300 characters, 10 notes at most). The send button carries the price, which the page reads from `revisePrice` (`src/lib/revise.ts`), the function the route charges with.
2. Request (rule 2491). `POST /api/tasks/{id}/revise` with `{version, notes:[{line, quote, note}]}`: `line` an index into the shown text (`editedBody ?? body`) split on newlines, `quote` that line's text, `note` the typed text cleaned by `cleanNote` (`src/lib/task-notes.ts`, now shared with the edit and reject reasons of `PATCH /api/tasks/{id}`). A note that does not anchor is 400 `bad_notes`; no `version` 400 `version_required`; a version that is no longer the item's is 409 `changed` with the item as it is now, and the notes stay on screen.
3. Order of checks (rules 2492, 2497, 2499). Session (401), role `drafts` (403), `rate:revise:{email}` 30 an hour (429), the agent-scope preflight, then `reviseTask`: the item under `sc(u)` only (404), its type (400 `not_revisable`) and state (409 `not_pending`), the version, the notes, the pause (503), the brand (400), the model the batch would use for the type (`generateRouteFor`, 503 when the catalog offers none), the trial (402), fair use (402), the notes' input gate (`checkGenerationNotes`, `where: "revise"`; enforce 422 or 429), then the charge: `creditsFor(draftCredits(type, 1), model, "generate", type)` against the owning agent's cap (402 `agent_cap` or `no_credits`, with the refused row in the activity log). Every refusal before the charge costs nothing.
4. The call (rule 2493). `reviseDraft` in `src/lib/noor.ts`: the agent's own system prompt (brand file, learned preferences, memory, feedback, the brand's active agent instructions, the scope paragraph), then the task with the draft and the notes as data blocks (`asData`), the draft marked as data that is never followed and the notes as edit requests for this draft only. One JSON item back; the channel is the draft's; the title changes only if a note asks; em dashes stripped when the brand asks; a refusal or an empty reply throws. The revision then passes the output gate (`scopeCheck("output", …, where: "revise")`): enforce refuses and refunds (422), shadow logs.
5. Save (rules 2494-2495). `updateTask` re-reads the item under its lock: still pending and still the version the notes were written on, it sets `editedBody` (at most 20000 characters), the title and `touchedAt`; otherwise nothing is written, the charge is refunded with its receipt and the answer is 409 `not_pending` or `changed`. Any failure after the charge refunds it (502, or 503 when the model paused mid-call). The item stays pending: nothing is approved or published by a revision, and the owner decides on the new text as on any draft.
6. After the answer (rule 2496). In `after()`: an `edit` decision with `editPct` and the notes, an activity row `revised` (`count` = notes, `credits` = price), and, with learning on, one learned note "Owner edited a {type} item ({channel}). Reason: line notes: …" in `prefs:{scope}` and `preferences.md` (under `prefsfile:{scope}`, now built by `wsKey.prefsFileLock`).
7. Shapes (rule 679, expand). No stored shape changes: the revision writes fields a Task already has. New: the activity kind `revised` (older code drops a kind it does not know), the rate bucket `rate:revise:{email}`, and the `prefsfile:{scope}` lock moved into the key registry with the same string. One release, no contract step.
8. Not covered. A render test of the line-notes panel (that a refusal keeps the typed notes) is not written; the route, the pipeline and the copy are. MCP and chat do not offer line notes: chat already revises a draft by talking to the agent.

## 103. Each agent's state, and a phone notification while you are away (2026-10-20, branch `claude/agent-status-push-0d7320`, rules 2550-2573)

Pattern 8 of the Fizzy and Orca study (`docs/reports/2026-10-19-PRD-fizzy-orca_AR.md`, after Orca). Coverage in `docs/system/tests/2550-agent-status-push.md`.

1. The state (rules 2550-2555). `src/lib/agent-status.ts` reads two things that already exist: the brand's newest 200 activity rows (`recentActivity(sc(u), 200)`, one LRANGE) and the drafts waiting in the inbox (`isWaiting`). Nothing is stored. Each row says working (a render started), stuck (the agent's cap or the account's credits ran out, a render failed, a paid run was held, a routine skipped for a reason that blocks it, a handoff run failed), done (drafts written, published, a render ready, a routine ran, a file written, and the like) or nothing (approvals, settings, refunds). Per agent, in this order: stuck when its newest row that says anything is a failure, or a render still working after 30 minutes, within 3 days; waiting for you when any of its drafts waits; working; done when it has finished anything; otherwise quiet, shown without a badge. Reem keeps the owner's own to-dos and is not listed.
2. The board (rules 2556-2557). The app's home shows one card per agent above the inbox: avatar, name, the state in the page's language (يعمل / ينتظرك / انتهى / متعثر; Working / Waiting for you / Done / Stuck), how many of its drafts wait, and a folded "how it got here" with its last 5 rows (kind and time only, never a row's text). The page reads the rows in its existing single `Promise.all`.
3. Switching notifications on (rules 2560-2563, 2570). Push is off until the founder sets the VAPID key pair (`variables.md` section 5c); then the board shows "Notify me on this device" to owners and members in their own session (never on a founder's view of the account), hidden where the browser has no Web Push (an iPhone outside the installed app). Pressing it asks the browser's permission, registers `public/sw.js`, subscribes with the public key and posts the endpoint to `POST /api/push`, which keeps it under the caller's workspace (`pushsubs:{sc(u)}`, at most 10 devices). Only endpoints of the browsers' own push services are accepted (Google FCM, Mozilla, Apple, Windows), so the server never posts to an address a request chose. Switching off deletes it on the server, then in the browser.
4. Away (rule 2564). While an app tab is visible it posts `POST /api/push/seen` every 2 minutes and when it comes back into view (at most once a minute), which marks the login present for 5 minutes. Away means no visible tab of that login within the last 5 minutes. Decision: 5 minutes is long enough that switching tabs or locking the phone briefly does not trigger a push, and short enough that the owner who walked away hears within minutes; the beat (2 minutes) is well inside it so one missed beat does not count as away.
5. Sending (rules 2565-2568, 2572). After `recordActivity` writes rows, and only while push is on, `pushForActivity` runs after the response (`after()`). Only a row that makes an agent wait for the owner (drafts written, a routine that wrote some, a proposed handoff, a draft from a connected app) or stuck sends; done and working never do. One push per workspace, agent and state per 30 minutes (SET NX). Only devices of logins that are away get it. The push has no body: it is a POST with a VAPID token (ES256, signed with Node's `crypto`, 12-hour expiry, audience = the push service's origin) through `safeFetch` with every hop https, TTL 6 hours. A 404 or 410 deletes the device; any other failure is logged and changes nothing. The text the device shows is read by the worker from `GET /api/push/latest` with its own session: «ذكين» or "Thakeen" and "agent name: state" in the person's language, or a generic line. No draft, brand, count or address is in it.
6. Clean-up (rules 2563, 2571). A device whose session is gone gets 401 from `/api/push/latest` and unsubscribes itself; the next push to it is answered 410 and its record goes. A rotated subscription (`pushsubscriptionchange`) subscribes again and posts the new endpoint. Deleting the account deletes every brand's devices and the owner's markers. A removed, departed or paused member's device may stay in the workspace's list, but the send keeps only logins that still hold the account (`loginHoldsWorkspace`: `getLogin` and `heldAccounts`, person not suspended, rule 3020), so it gets nothing.
7. Defaults the founder delegated (2026-10-20): away after 5 minutes, heartbeat every 2 minutes; only waiting and stuck push; one push per agent and state per 30 minutes; 10 devices per workspace; stuck shown for 3 days; a render older than 30 minutes is stuck; owner and members may subscribe, tokens and founder views may not; a push kept 6 hours by the push service; no third-party gateway and no new library.

## 104. Skill stubs, on-demand references and provenance (2026-10-20, branch `claude/skills-stub-provenance-79a1f1`, rules 2523-2549)

Pattern 7 of the Fizzy/Orca study, Orca's skill layout for the agent skills portal (rules 930-959). Coverage and the delegated decisions: `docs/system/tests/2523-skills-stub-provenance.md`.

1. Where a file came from (rules 2523-2524, 2528). Every writer passes `{ src: fileSrc(kind) }` to `writeFile` and the file keeps it as `WFile.src` (`owner`, `crawl` with the site, `gdocs`, `gsheets`, `drive`, `store`, `agent`, `builtin`). `provenanceOf` (`src/lib/ref-provenance.ts`) reads it, or works it out for a file without one (the Google record in `skillrefs:{scope}`, a `drive-` name, then `by`), and gives the last refresh (the Google import's time for a Google file, else `updatedAt`), the age in days and `stale` past the source's days (store 7, Google and drive 30, crawl 60, agent 90, owner 180, built-in never).
2. The chat's stub (rule 2525). `chat()` calls `loadLayers(..., "stub")`: the owner instructions are as before, and the attached files are listed by name, source, date and `stale` (at most 2,000 characters, whole entries), with no text. The agent is told to read one with `read_file` when the owner's request needs it, and to say when a file it uses is marked stale.
3.
