The on-demand read (rule 2526). `read_file` in the chat reads the file from the turn's own workspace and answers it as data inside `<file>` with its source and date (`fileResult`, 12,000 characters). It is free: not a role-gated action, no charge, no write. It runs only inside the owner's message, after the input gate, and what the agent writes from it passes the output gate.
4. One-shot callers (rule 2527). The batch, media, reviewer, judge and the instruction test route have no tool loop, so they keep the file text inline under the caps of rule 943, now with each file's source and date.
5. The owner's page (rule 2529). The instructions page reads the provenance of every attached file (`provenanceFor`, one `mget`) and each chip shows its source and age, with a «قد يكون قديماً» / "May be out of date" badge; a note above the list counts the stale ones.
6. Shapes (rule 679, expand). `WFile.src` is optional and new; older code ignores it, and the new code reads a file without it by the fallback in item 1, which stays (no contract step, no conversion script). No new key, no new route, no new environment variable.

## 105. Background jobs carry the workspace they act for (2026-10-20, branch `claude/jobs-carry-account-dfe36f`, rules 2500-2509)

Pattern 6 of `docs/reports/2026-10-19-PRD-fizzy-orca_AR.md`: Fizzy serializes `Current.account` into every job and restores it when the job runs. Coverage and the audit of every job: `docs/system/tests/2500-jobs-carry-account.md`.

1. Carry (rule 2500). The daily, weekly, routines, evening and stores crons list their roster (`dailyRoster`) once per hop, then start items for up to four minutes. Each item now carries a `JobRef` `{account, scope}`, taken with `jobRef(u)` (`acct(u)`, `sc(u)`) from the roster's own record when the item is listed. Nothing comes from the request: the bearer opens the route, the chain hop passes only `chain`, and the roster is listed again on every hop.
2. Re-check (rules 2501-2505). The item's work starts only through `runAsAccount(ref, run)` (`src/lib/job-account.ts`, rule 2506). It refuses a scope that is not the carried account's before any read, then reads the account and its brand list in one round and skips when the account is gone or keyed elsewhere, suspended, its owner unverified, or the scope's brand archived or missing. The work gets the fresh `User` (balance, plan, billing as they are now), never the roster's copy.
3. Skip safely. A skip is `skip:{reason}`, the item's final outcome for the period (its done marker is set): no write, no charge, no email. Charges inside a run still go through `withUserLock`, which re-reads the account and throws `user_not_found` for a deleted one, so an account deleted after the re-check is not charged either; paid-once holds left by a run that died are refunded by the daily sweep (section 98).
4. The digest (rules 2507-2509). Reem's tick reads every account in `notifydigests`. Once a window has ended, and before the send is claimed, the account is read again: gone (or keyed elsewhere) means it leaves the set and its choice goes, as the account wipe does; suspended or unverified means `held`, no email, the window stays open. The email goes to the account's current address, never the `to` saved with the choice, so an owner who moved their address is not mailed at the old one. The stored shape does not change (`to` is still written, only no longer read), so there is no expand/contract step.
5. Already built, unchanged: the reminder sweep (`sweepAccount`: account read again, gone leaves the index, suspended/unverified/inactive brand held) and the calendar sync (`syncLocked`: account, suspension, linked login and brand status read again under the brand's lock), which both carry the scope in their due set; and media jobs, whose record carries `scope`, the paying account and `accountSince`, so a refund goes only to the account that paid (spec A32).

## 106. Brand in the URL (2026-10-20, branch `claude/brand-parts-bc-29277a`, rules 885-899; plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-20-brand-url.md`)

Later tasks of the branch extend this section. What exists after Task 3 (rules 887-888):

1. A brand page lives at `/{lang}/app/b/{brandId}/…`. The proxy (`src/proxy.ts`) gives every page request the header `x-thakeen-brand` from that path only (`brandFromPath`): whatever the browser sent is deleted first, and a malformed segment sets none. Browser API calls carry the header through one wrapper on `window.fetch` (`installBrandFetch` in `src/lib/brand-fetch.ts`, installed by the shell's `WorkspaceProvider` on its first render, always on): a same-origin `/api/` request from a page whose path names a brand (`brandFromPath`, which also reads `/{lang}/app/w/{handle}/b/{id}/…` of draft #418) gets the header unless the caller set one; since the merge with M2 (section 93.2 item 8) it is the only wrapper: with `MULTI_ORG` on it also sends `x-thakeen-ws`. A navigation cannot send a header, so `apiHref(path)` (and `useApiHref()` for a link drawn in the markup, `withBrandQuery` on the server) adds `brand={id}`: the connector start, the pptx links, the activity export, the series export and the calendar link card. A shell component on an account page that writes a brand route (the rail's new conversation, the account settings save) sends the session's brand itself through `sessionBrandHeaders(useBrandId())`, only when the page path has no brand. On an `/api/` request the proxy leaves a header as it came; a GET or HEAD without one may name the brand in a `brand` query value, which the proxy copies into the header (links and downloads cannot send headers, R-C13); a mutation never takes it from the query.
2. `currentUser()` takes the header only as a selector among the session account's own active brands (rule 886); anything else is a 404.
3. The brand guard (`src/lib/brand-guard.ts`, rule 888): a POST, PUT, PATCH or DELETE with a session cookie to a `BRAND_API` route and no header is noticed. `BRAND_GUARD_MODE` is `"log"`: the request goes on and `brand_missing` (path only) is logged. The contract release switches it to `"enforce"`, which answers 409 `{"ok":false,"error":"brand_missing"}`.

Task 4 (rules 885, 891) moves the pages:

4. Every page that reads or writes the brand's data (`sc(u)`) lives under `src/app/[lang]/app/b/[brand]/`: the inbox, `chat`, `conversations`, `tasks`, `calendar`, `files`, `generate`, `reports`, `activity`, `todo`, `inspo`, `instructions`, `start`, and the settings sections `brand`, `connections`, `governance`, `notifications` (R-C6, R-C10). `b/[brand]/layout.tsx` signs in (login otherwise) and answers 404 when the segment is not `u.brandId`; a brand outside the account was already a 404 from `currentUser()` (rule 886). Account pages keep their path and no longer call `sc(`. Redirects inside the moved pages go through `appHref` with the session's brand.
5. `/{lang}/app` (`src/app/[lang]/app/page.tsx`) is a 307 to `/{lang}/app/b/{u.brandId}` (R-C5); signed out it goes to login.
6. Old paths (`src/app/[lang]/app/[...legacy]/page.tsx`): a signed-out visitor goes to login; a brand path (`/app/chat`, `/app/settings/brand`, `/app/tasks/x`, …) is a 307 to the same path under `/b/{u.brandId}` with the query rebuilt from `searchParams` through `URLSearchParams` (repeated keys kept in order); the old names `onboarding`, `integrations` and `governance` go to their brand settings section the same way; anything else is a 404. The brand id is the session's, never the request's. The proxy's `legacySettingsPath` keeps only `profile`, `team`, `brands` and `billing` and leaves the three brand names to this page, so each old link costs one hop. Route order: Next sorts `/[lang]/app/[...legacy]` before `/[lang]/[...rest]` (a static `app` segment beats a dynamic one), pinned in `tests/brand-url-pages.test.ts`. No `loading.tsx` sits above a redirecting page (`/app`, `b/[brand]`, `[...legacy]`), so each answers a real 307.

Task 7 (rules 892, 893, 895, 896) adds the switcher and fixes what the page's brand may write:

7. The page's brand is a selector and never a write (rule 892): resolving the header and rendering `b/[brand]/layout` write no `activebrand` key. The one write is the switch (`POST /api/brands` `{action:"switch", id}`), which remembers the person's last brand in the account for `/{lang}/app` and for a link without a brand.
8. The rail's brand button is `BrandSwitcher` (`src/components/app/BrandSwitcher.tsx`, rule 893). Opened, it lists the account's active brands (archived ones are managed on the brands page), marks the current one, and offers «أضف براند» / "Add a brand" (owner or admin under the plan's cap) and «إدارة البراندات» / "Manage brands". Picking a brand posts the switch and, when the server accepts it, goes to the same section of that brand (`switchBrandPath`: `/b/x/tasks` to `/b/y/tasks`; a record page falls back to its section, since the record belongs to the old brand, and the query is dropped on purpose, since `?conv=` and the like name the old brand's records); a switch to an archived brand is refused (404, nothing written); a refusal shows a toast and stays. The brands page does the same with its «انتقل» / "Switch" button. The rail's new conversation opens at the brand path of the brand it was created in, not at the legacy path, which would go to the stored choice (another tab may have changed it).
9. Two tabs on two brands (rule 896): each tab's requests carry its own page's header, so a save in one tab writes that tab's brand file whatever the stored choice says.

Task 8 (2026-10-21, after #418; rules 2600-2603, `docs/system/tests/2600-brand-url-links.md`) puts the brand into the links that still took the `[...legacy]` hop:

10. Links the server makes about one brand name it (rules 894, 2601-2602): the bell's daily follow-ups (`followUps`: calendar, inbox, connections), Reem's reminders, the series purge notice, the evening wrap (bell and mail), the lost-calendar notice, a media job's notice, the daily brief and follow-up mails and the no-brand nudge (cron `daily`), the agent cap warning (to `settings/governance` of the cap's brand), and the calendar feed's link. `appHrefInBrand(scope, lang, path)` (`src/lib/ws-href.ts`) takes the brand from a scope read from our own records (`brandOfScope`, "default" for the account's own scope). `notifyItem` puts the brand of `brandScope`, else of the scope the entry is filed under, into a relative href; with neither (an account-wide notice, the hold sweep across several brands) the old path stays and the `[...legacy]` page picks the session's brand. Account-level mails (lifecycle, billing returns) open the app root or an account page, which carry no brand. A stale entry for a brand archived since answers 404 (rule 891), never another brand's page.
11. Links on a page outside `/b/{id}` name the shell's brand (rules 890, 2603): `useAppLink(lang)` falls back to `useBrandId()` (the session's stored choice on an account page) when the page has no `/b/{id}` and the caller passed no fallback; `pageHref(lang, u.brandId)` does the same on the billing page; the shell's trial banner passes `user.brandId`. Adding a brand opens the new brand's start page by its id; a new account's signup (form and Google) opens `/b/default/start`.

### 106.1 Releases

Expand (this release): the proxy sets the header from the path, the guard only logs, and a request without a header falls back to the session's brand as before. The old page paths answer 307 (temporary, so a rollback finds its pages again and no browser keeps the move); the contract step that drops `[...legacy]` waits until nothing links the old paths (Task 6 moves the sidebar and the client links). Contract (a later release, after one release of logs shows no `brand_missing` from current pages): `BRAND_GUARD_MODE` becomes `"enforce"`. Rolling back to the code before this release ignores the header safely: the proxy of that code leaves a header alone and `currentUser()` ignores it. Pinned by `tests/brand-url-proxy.test.ts` and `tests/brand-url-switch.test.ts`; coverage rows, the rulings (R-B1, R-C1 to R-C13, R-M1) and the release step in `docs/system/tests/0885-brand-url.md`. The `[...legacy]` page goes in a still later release, once mails and bell entries stop naming old paths (Task 8 moves the links the server builds, after #418 lands).

## 107. Per-client AI usage: who ran each call, for which brand, and what it cost (2026-10-22, branch `claude/thakeen-nashr-bugs-tests-tracking-9eac9c`, rules 2900-2909)

Rule file `docs/system/tests/2900-ai-usage-tracking.md`. Files: `src/lib/usage.ts` (`usageWho`, `record`, `breakdownWrites`, `getUsageBreakdown`, `rankAccounts`), `src/lib/usage-report.ts`, `src/app/api/usage/route.ts`, `src/app/api/admin/usage/clients/route.ts`, `src/app/[lang]/app/settings/usage/page.tsx`, `src/components/admin/{ClientUsage,UsageBreakdown}.tsx`, `src/components/app/settings/OwnerUsagePanel.tsx`.

1. Who and which brand (rule 2901). Each customer-facing model call site passes `usageWho(u, scope)`: the person comes from `byOf(u)` (owner, `member:{login}`, `support` for an impersonation, `app` for a bearer token, `system` for a cron or script), the brand from `brandOfScope(scope)`. Nothing in it is read from a request. Chat, generation (and its judge and critique), revise, the instructions test, onboarding, media prompts, Reem's meeting notes and the free tools pass it; the classifier, the founder's skill test and the golden set do not (they are not a customer's).
2. The writes (rules 2902-2905). `record` adds, in the same `Promise.all` as the existing counters, calls (only when the call counts) and micro-dollars to four hashes per account per month (`usage:{acct}:{m}:by:{person|brand|model|day}:*`), and the cost to the month's ranking `usage:_all:{m}:rank`. A missing or malformed person or brand counts as `unknown`, so each split adds up to the account's own total. `meterRender` adds a render's cost to the ranking too. Months before this release read as empty (no backfill).
3. Deletion (rule 2904). `usageMonthKeys` lists the eight hashes, so `wipeAccount` deletes them with the other usage keys, and it removes the account from 36 months of the ranking.
4. The founder (rules 2905, 2906, 2908). Console → Usage → "Clients by AI cost": `GET /api/admin/usage/clients?month=` ranks accounts by model plus media cost with plan and margin. The user sheet's usage tab shows the account's four splits with cost.
5. The owner (rule 2907). Settings → AI usage (`/{lang}/app/settings/usage`, owner and admins, `usage.view`) and `GET /api/usage?month=`: requests and share per person, brand, agent, model and day. No dollar figure: what a call costs Thakeen stays in the console; credits stay on the billing page.
6. Release step: expand only. New hashes and one new sorted set, written alongside the old counters; old code never reads them, and nothing old changes shape. Rollback leaves them in place, to be removed with the account.

## 108. Customers as workspaces (2026-10-23, branch `claude/m2-console-workspaces-e22a0c`, rules 3720-3749)

The founder console's Users section opens on one row per workspace, not one row per email. Expand only: two read-only routes and one cache key, no stored shape changes.

1. The list (rules 3720-3724). `/{lang}/admin/users` renders `Customers`, which calls `GET /api/admin/workspaces` with the query in the address (`q`, `plan`, `filter`, `sort`, `dir`, `page`, `per`; `parseWsQuery` validates every value). The route needs a console actor (401) and `staffMay(consoleRole(req), "workspace.view")` (403). `workspaceList` reads `users:all`, every account once with `pmap`, the cached aggregate from `admin:wsagg` while it is under 5 minutes old and covers every account, else a rebuild for all accounts (12 at a time, each reading its brands' light task index, the newest 300 activity rows per brand, the roster, the pending invites and the last sign-ins) saved for an hour. The row's plan, state, seat limit, brand cap and allowance are read live; the counts are the cached ones. `refresh=1` counts again. The filters, the sort and the page run on rows.
2. Search by a member (rule 3730). A search text with an `@` is also looked up as a login (`getLogin`); the person's workspaces come from `heldAnywhere`, with the role they hold in each (`getMembership`, paused ones included). Those workspaces pass the text filter and carry `asRole`. The route writes the audit row `workspace.search` (section `users`, target the person's address) before it answers. With `MULTI_ORG` off a member holds one workspace; with it on, as many as the person holds.
3. The workspace page (rules 3725-3729). A row links to `/{lang}/admin/users/{owner address}`; the page calls `GET /api/admin/workspaces/{email}`: the account is found by `accountForChosen` (the address it owns, else the account its login stands for), the audit row `workspace.view` (`page: true`, and `openedFrom` for a member's address) is written, and only then does the answer go out. Tabs: Overview (30-day bars, last activity), Team (each member's role, status, joined, last sign-in and, from the account's activity rows by the login a row names, tasks requested and completed, credits and last action in 30 days), Brands, Billing (a read-only summary, a link to the account controls) and Log (the newest 100 rows across the brands). `complete` is false when a brand's log is full inside the window.
4. The account table. The old flat table moved unchanged to `/{lang}/admin/users/accounts`; the list and the Billing tab link to it. Plan, seats, deal, brand cap, chat allowance, suspension and every other action stay there.
5. Roles. Every role name is drawn by `roleLabel`, which shows a role it does not know (the customer-roles phase adds `editor` and `viewer`) as the stored word.
6. Release step: expand only. One new key `admin:wsagg` (a JSON value with an hour's expiry), written by the list; old code never reads it. Rollback leaves it to expire. No environment variable. Past about 500 accounts, replace the on-demand rebuild with a daily cron that writes the same key.


## 109. Card payments through Geidea KSA (2026-10-24, branch `claude/sup-geidea`, rules 3900-3918; spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-18-geidea-payment-provider-design.md`)

Geidea replaces Paymob (section 47) behind the same provider seam (section 85). Off until `GEIDEA_PUBLIC_KEY` and `GEIDEA_API_PASSWORD` are set; Paymob stays readable and off.

1. Checkout. The owner presses a plan or a pack. `startCheckout` stores `pay:intent:{ref}` (a UUID v4 reference, our price, a new agreement id for a plan), adds the ref to `pay:open`, then asks Geidea for a session (signed, no phone, wallets hidden) and sends the owner to Geidea's hosted page built from the session id alone.
2. Callback. Geidea posts the order to `POST /api/billing/geidea/webhook` (permissions row 151b). The signature inside the body is checked first; the account and amount come from our intent. A paid order applies the plan or pack once and, for a plan, saves the card (token and agreement) in `pay:card:{account}`. A declined attempt leaves the checkout failed but open to a later paid attempt of the same order.
3. Return page. `/billing?pay=return` asks `GET /api/billing/status`; for a checkout a minute to two days old that is still open or failed, it asks Geidea itself (at most once per 20 s).
4. Daily job. The first hop of `/api/cron/daily` runs `reconcileOpenCheckouts`: refs in `pay:open` aged 20 minutes to two days are asked of Geidea and applied like a callback; older or settled ones leave the set. Then the renewal step charges a due month by saved card only with `GEIDEA_MIT=1` (a Merchant session, then `pay/token`); without it, and for a Paymob subscription, the month goes past due, then grace, then lock, and the owner pays from the billing page.
5. Refunds. The founder's console refund (permissions row 130) refunds the whole order at Geidea, ends the plan and deletes the card. A refund made in Geidea's dashboard is only logged (`geidea_external_refund`).
6. Release step: expand. New `pay:*` keys only; Paymob's keys, payment ids and log lines unchanged. Rollback: unset the two variables; open `pay:*` records expire or are ignored.
7. Founder decisions, 2026-10-24 (spec section 10), all as built:
   - Renewals by our own MIT (F1).
   - Cards only at launch (F3).
   - Name and email sent, no phone (F4).
   - No partial refund for now (F5).
   - A dashboard refund is only logged (F6).
   - A second paid order is refunded by hand.
   - An old Paymob subscription goes past due, then grace, then lock.
   - Reconciliation runs in the daily job and on the return page.
8. Privacy. The privacy policy (version 2026-10-24) and the help's processors answer name Geidea for card payments, with Paymob only if switched on instead (rule 3918).

## 110. Yearly billing next to monthly, off until the founder sets a yearly price (2026-10-23, branch `claude/sup-yearly-pricing`, rules 3530-3542)

Rule file `docs/system/tests/3530-yearly-pricing.md`. Files: `src/lib/pricing.ts` (`BillingInterval`, `INTERVAL_MONTHS`, `readInterval`, `PLAN_YEARLY_PRICE_SAR`, `PLAN_YEARLY_MONTHS_FREE`, `yearlyPriceSar`, `yearlyOffered`, `anyYearlyOffered`, `intervalPriceSar`, `yearlyPerMonthSar`, `priceOf`, `cheapestCreditUsd`), `src/lib/billing-state.ts` (`Billing.interval`, `billingInterval`, `billingMonths`), `src/lib/billing.ts` (`priceFor`, `startCheckout`, `yearRunning`, `applyPaid`, `chargeSavedCard`, `billingView`), `src/lib/payments/provider.ts` (`CheckoutInput.interval`), `src/lib/paymob.ts`, `src/app/api/billing/checkout/route.ts`, `src/lib/i18n.ts` (`yearlyPlanCards`), `src/components/{Pricing,PlanCard,IntervalSwitch}.tsx`, `src/components/app/PaymobBilling.tsx`, `src/app/[lang]/app/settings/billing/page.tsx`. Tests: `tests/yearly-pricing.test.ts` (prices null), `tests/yearly-pricing-on.test.ts` (a test-only price).

1. The founder's input (rule 3530). `src/lib/pricing.ts` stays the only file with a price. Under the comment `>>> FOUNDER: FILL IN THE YEARLY PRICES HERE. <<<` each self-serve plan has `PLAN_YEARLY_PRICE_SAR` (whole SAR excluding VAT) and `PLAN_YEARLY_MONTHS_FREE` (the alternative: the monthly price × (12 − months)). Both are null for every plan today: no amount was invented. A plan with both set, a price that is not a whole number from 1 to 12 × the monthly price, or months free outside 0-6 is not offered, so a typo never sells. There is no default discount. While every entry is null the pricing page, the home plans, the billing page and checkout render and answer exactly as before (the markup was compared byte for byte), and `tests/yearly-pricing.test.ts` fails on purpose once a price is set, so the change is made knowingly.
2. Pricing and VAT (rule 3531). `priceFor(kind, {plan, interval}, offer, lock)`: no interval or "month" is the monthly price as before; "year" is `yearlyPriceSar`, null when not offered; anything else is null. VAT is `vatOf` on the ex-VAT halalas for both periods. The 15% first-month offer and the last-day offer are a month's discount: a yearly charge never takes them, and a yearly checkout leaves the offer unused. A price lock (rule 1706) holds one month's ex-VAT price: a yearly renewal is charged the lower of twelve of them and the yearly list price. A pack takes no interval.
3. Checkout (rule 3532). `POST /api/billing/checkout` accepts `interval: "month" | "year"` for a plan (absent is "month"). The server refuses, before any read or provider call: an unknown interval or one on a pack (400 `bad_interval`), "year" for a plan with no yearly price (409 `yearly_not_offered`). The page hiding the switch is never the only check. The intent stores `interval: "year"` only for a yearly checkout; the callback re-prices the intent with `priceFor` (rule 2051), so a yearly intent paid after its price was withdrawn is a mismatch, recorded and not applied, and the founder refunds it.
4. The stored shape (rule 3533, expand). `Billing.interval` and `PayIntent.interval` are optional and written only as "year"; a monthly payment drops the field. A record or intent written before this release has none and reads as monthly (`readInterval`), so every existing subscription keeps renewing monthly. Contract: none planned; the absent field stays the monthly form.
5. Paid-through, renewal and trial end (rule 3534). A plan payment moves `paidThrough` by `INTERVAL_MONTHS[interval]` (1 or 12) on the anchor day. A first payment (the trial's end, or after a lock) starts the period today, so a yearly purchase is paid through today + 12 months. The credit allowance still renews every month on the anchor day while the paid period runs (`src/lib/renewal.ts`), so a yearly plan gets the same monthly credits, never twelve at once. The daily renewal charges a yearly subscription the yearly price and moves twelve months; if the founder has withdrawn that plan's yearly price it renews one month at the monthly price and the record becomes monthly (never a price no longer in pricing.ts). The 7-day refund proposal is unchanged: it values the credits used at the monthly rate and subtracts them from what was paid.
6. Changing interval or plan: no proration (rule 3535). Same plan, other interval: the payment adds its period after the one already paid (monthly to yearly on day 20 of a month: the month runs out, then the year), and the interval paid last governs the renewals from then on, so nothing is lost and nothing is refunded. Yearly to monthly: the owner turns renewal off and pays a month when the year ends (the server also accepts a month of the same plan during the year; it is added after the year and the subscription renews monthly from then; the billing page offers only the yearly renewal for a yearly plan). Another plan while a paid yearly period of a different plan runs: refused at checkout and again when a payment arrives (`yearly_active`, 409; a payment from a checkout opened earlier is recorded as paid but not applied, and the founder refunds it), because starting the new plan today would drop the rest of the paid year. The owner changes plan when the year ends, or the founder changes it by hand from the console. Monthly to another plan is unchanged (starts today). Rule 3536: the billing page shows a second button "Pay yearly: {total} SAR / year" next to the monthly one only for a plan with a yearly price, and the subscription card says "Yearly" for a yearly subscription.
7. The provider seam and Geidea (rules 3539-3540). `CheckoutInput.interval` carries the period to the adapter, for the label only: the amount is always `priceFor`'s and is charged once; renewals are our own saved-card charges, never a provider-side subscription. Paymob has no period field: the request body is unchanged and the item text says "1 year". Geidea (on main since #456, section 109) sends no item text at all: its session carries only `amountCents` (in riyals), our reference and, for a plan, our own "Unscheduled" card-on-file agreement, which has no period or amount of its own, so a yearly checkout and a yearly renewal are the same calls with the yearly amount. Price, VAT and every refusal stay in `src/lib/billing.ts`. `tests/geidea.test.ts` "yearly through Geidea" pins a yearly checkout and a yearly renewal through the Geidea fake.
8. Money and reporting (rules 3537-3538). `priceOf` (MRR, the users CSV) counts a yearly subscription at its yearly price ÷ 12 while that price is set. The margin floor (`cheapestCreditUsd`, rule 241) also counts each yearly credit (yearly net price ÷ 12 ÷ monthly credits), so a yearly discount lowers the cost ceiling instead of slipping under the 60% floor; setting a price may fail `tests/pricing-floor.test.ts`, which is the point. Not changed: the console's hand-recorded payments still expect the monthly price (the founder types the amount), and Agency stays sold by contract.
9. The page (rules 3541-3542). With at least one yearly price set, `/pricing` and the industry pages (`Pricing`) draw a "Billing period" switch (Arabic "مدة الفوترة": شهري / سنوي) above the cards: two native buttons in a labelled group with `aria-pressed`, Tab and Enter or Space work, logical spacing so it follows RTL. Monthly is shown first. Both card lists are rendered on the server from the dictionary; the switch only shows one and hides the other. A yearly card shows the yearly total ("2,990 SAR / year" style) and under it the same price per month to the halala ("That is 249.17 SAR a month, paid once a year"), both from pricing.ts. A plan without a yearly price keeps its monthly card in the yearly list. The home page's short plan list and the structured data (`src/lib/home-ld.ts`) stay monthly.

### 108.1 Releases

Expand only: no new route, Redis key or environment variable; one optional field on two stored shapes and one optional field in the checkout body (permissions row 149). Old code ignores `interval` and would renew a yearly subscription monthly, and an old build seeing a yearly intent's callback finds a price mismatch and applies nothing. So the founder sets a yearly price only after this release is live on production, and a rollback after yearly sales needs the founder to check those accounts by hand.

10. Extra seats on a yearly plan (rules 3372, 3539). A yearly renewal charges each paid extra seat for the twelve months it pays for: seats × `EXTRA_SEAT_MONTHLY_SAR` × 12, VAT on the sum, with no free months on seats (the two free months are the plan's). `settleExtraSeats` runs before it exactly as for a monthly renewal. A seat bought during a paid year is charged at once for the months left of that year (rule 4043, section 121 item 5): the next yearly renewal then charges it for twelve months with the others. A yearly subscription already due (`paidThrough` today or before) adds the seat like a monthly one, charged by the coming renewal.

## 120. Customer roles: Viewer, Editor, brand scope, approval and per-member cap (2026-10-23, branch `claude/m3-customer-roles-b97625`, rules 3750-3789)

Phase م3 of the customer team. Permissions in `permissions.md` 0.1a and rows 7e and 7r; tests in `docs/system/tests/3750-customer-roles.md`. Rules 3765-3769 and 3775-3789 are reserved and unused.

1. Roles. Owner, admin, Editor and Viewer. The Editor keeps its stored name `"member"` in this release; requests may say `"editor"`. A Viewer reads reports, the calendar and drafts, comments, and approves only with the approve toggle; every write, agent run and credit spend is refused in code (routes, chat tools, MCP).
2. Seats. `seatCount`: owner + admins + Editors not paused + unexpired invites, with the first 3 Viewers free (the 4th takes a paid seat). The Members page shows seats used and free Viewers left.
3. Brand scope. A membership may hold a list of the account's own brand ids. The resolvers keep a scoped person inside it (a link outside is a 404; a stale active brand falls back to an allowed one), and `/api/brands` lists and switches to allowed brands only. The same scope holds where the resolvers are not in the path (rules 3772-3773): a calendar link is checked against it on every fetch (`personHolds` and `legacyHolds` in `src/lib/secretary-feed.ts`; a link made before M1 takes the scope from the membership and reads the record's role as `resolveLegacy` does, so a Viewer's link holds too), the bell shows a narrowed member their brands' entries and the account's (`visibleNotifs`; an entry keeps its `brand` from `pushNotifs`, older ones fall back to their `about` key or `/b/{id}` link; `credits` and `trial` describe the account), and the month's usage (`monthUsage`) and the approval stats (`teamApprovals`) count the allowed brands only (`brandAllowed`).
4. Approval. Owners and admins approve; an Editor or Viewer approves only with the toggle on. With the owner's policy on (PATCH /api/team), `publishOnce` refuses (409 `approval_required`) unless the copy being sent was approved by someone who may still approve that brand. Tasks record `approvedBy` and, for a fresh agent draft, `assignee` (one approver of the brand in turn, with a bell entry).
5. Per-member monthly credit cap. Optional, set by the owner, checked in `debit` under the account lock before the spend, counted per person in `User.personSpend` and lowered by refunds, a reused Sunday-plan hold's refund too (rule 3774: its receipt keeps the hold's `person`). Chat messages use the chat allowance and are not capped.
6. Members page (`/{lang}/app/settings/members`, Arabic and English, RTL): invite with role, brand scope and the toggle; change them later; the owner sets caps and the policy. It works with `MULTI_ORG` off and on (the membership row is read in both resolvers).

### 120.1 Release steps (rule 679)

- **This release: expand.** New code reads `"member"`, `"editor"` and `"viewer"` and writes `"member"` for an Editor. New optional fields (`brands`, `approves`, `creditCap` on a membership, `brands` and `approves` on an invite; `approvedBy`, `assignee`, `comments` on a task; `approvalRequired`, `personSpend` on the account; team log kind `access_changed`) are ignored by older code. A rollback to a build before this one signs out Viewers (their role does not parse there) and drops the new fields' effect, never widening anyone.
- **Contract, step 1 (a later release).** Write `"editor"` for an Editor (`setRole`, invites, `insertMember`, the team log). Once it is live and the rollback window has passed, run `scripts/editor-role.ts` by hand on the founder's yes: a dry run first (`npx --no-install jiti scripts/editor-role.ts`, counts only), then `--apply`. It rewrites `"member"` to `"editor"` in every `mbr:{aid}:{pid}` row and in every legacy `member:{pid}` record of the same account, under `lock:team` → `lock:member`, re-reading each value under the locks, every other field kept; idempotent (a second run rewrites nothing); exit 1 means a lock was busy (run again). Invites (7 days) and team log rows (history) are not rewritten.
- **Contract, step 2 (the release after).** Drop the `"member"` reading and the alias.

### 120.2 Merge order with paid seats

`refs/heads/claude/sup-paid-seats` (rules 3360-3379) bills extra seats. It must bill `seatCount` from `src/lib/team.ts` (this branch), never its own count of memberships: merge this branch first, then rebase paid seats on it so `settleExtraSeats` counts Viewers through `seatCount` and the first three are never billed. If paid seats lands first, the merge of this branch must switch its count to `seatCount` in the same pull request.

## 123. Profile picture, signed uploads, workspace slugs, welcome and the overview (2026-10-23, branch `claude/sup-profile-workspace`, rules 3500-3507, release "expand")

Ideas from Supastarter (signed upload URL, `user.image`, organization slug, welcome mail, dashboard), built the Thakeen way. No environment variable, no dependency and no removed page.

1. **Upload seam (rules 3500-3501).** `StorageAdapter.signedUpload?(spec)` is optional and provider-neutral: `spec` is `{name, contentTypes, maxBytes, expiresInS}`, the name made by the server (`newUploadName`, 128 random bits in `MEDIA_NAME` shape), the limits from the upload kind's policy (`UploadPolicy` in `src/lib/upload.ts`). `planUpload(owner, policy, {contentType, size})` refuses a wrong declared type or size, then answers `server` when the backend cannot sign (the disk, today's only one), is not configured, or its signing throws (logged with kind and backend only); otherwise it stores `upload:{owner}:{name}` for the policy's TTL and returns the grant. `owner` is the route's, from the session. `takeUpload` reads and deletes the ticket under that owner only (`getdel`), reads the object back, deletes it and checks the bytes by magic number. A follow-up branch adds a bucket adapter and a workspace logo kind on the same seam.
2. **Profile picture (rules 3502-3503).** Settings → Account shows the picture card (Arabic and English). The browser plans (`POST /api/account/avatar/upload`), then either sends the bytes to the signed URL and posts `{name}`, or (today) posts the image to `POST /api/account/avatar`. The server checks type and size again on the bytes, re-encodes with sharp (upright, 256 px square, white behind transparency, fresh JPEG: no EXIF survives) and, under `lock:avatar:{pid}`, checks room, writes the file, sets `avatar:{pid}`, counts the bytes and deletes the old file last. `DELETE` removes it. The person is `actingLogin` (a founder impersonating cannot). The sidebar account menu and the team list show it (`avatarUrls`, one `mget` for the whole list) with the initial as fallback, also when the image fails to load. It is served only by `GET /api/avatar/{name}?s=` to a signed-in user; the file is in no workspace's `mfile:` index. `forgetPerson` and `wipeAccount` (when the owner holds nothing else) delete it.
3. **Workspace slug (rules 3504-3505).** `createWorkspace` claims a slug from the name before the account is saved (`claimSlug`: Arabic transliterated by sound, accents dropped, lowercase, single hyphens, 3-32 characters; candidates base, two suffixed, one random `ws-`, all claimed with SET NX in one `Promise.all`, the first won kept and the rest released) and releases it if the save fails. Reserved words (every route folder under `src/app`, `src/app/[lang]`, `src/app/[lang]/app`, locales, short segments, our names, staff and abuse words) go straight to the random name; a test fails when a new route folder is not reserved. The slug is shown in the workspaces list; links and selectors stay the `w_` handle (rule 2114). Accounts made before this have none (expand: nothing to convert).
4. **Welcome (rule 3506).** The password sign-up and Google's new-account branch call `welcomeOnSignup`: one `info` notice in the bell in the sign-up language, linking to the start page, claimed with the bell's fingerprint `notifseen:{account}:welcome` (SET NX, 365 days), so a retry adds nothing. A second workspace and a team join never call it. A failure is logged and never fails the sign-up. `wipeAccount` deletes the claim.
5. **Overview (rule 3507).** `/{lang}/app/b/{id}/overview`, linked in the sidebar above Reports, which it links to and does not replace: tiles (created and credits with the change against the previous 30 days, approved, published, waiting), daily bars, created → approved → published, and by type, as server-rendered SVG with the values written beside each bar and a table. The numbers are `kpis` over the brand's own tasks (one `listLite` and the waiting set); with nothing stored and nothing waiting, the empty state and no chart.

### 123.1 Releases

Expand only: `avatar:`, `lock:avatar:`, `upload:`, `wsslug:` and `User.workspaceSlug` are new and optional; an older build ignores them. Nothing to contract and no data script.

## 124. Workspace logo, square crop and the S3-compatible store (2026-10-23, branch `claude/sup-workspace-logo`, rules 3610-3621, release "expand")

The follow-up named in 123 item 1: a workspace logo kind and a bucket adapter on the same upload seam, plus a crop step before both image uploads. No dependency; seven new optional variables (`variables.md`), all unset by default, so the disk stays the store.

1. **Workspace logo (rules 3610-3612).** Settings → Account shows a "Workspace logo" card (Arabic and English) beside the picture. The owner or an unscoped admin (`workspace.logo`) sets and removes it; an admin scoped to some brands is refused, since the logo is the whole workspace's (rule 3621, `SCOPED_BLOCKED` with M3's `team.access`); Editors and Viewers see it read-only; a founder impersonating and an MCP token are refused. The account is always `aidOf(u)` from the session: `wslogo:{aid}` (per workspace, not per brand), its lock `lock:wslogo:{aid}`, its upload owner `w:{aid}`, a ticket kind `logo` that never redeems as an avatar (and the reverse). The server re-encodes with sharp: upright, the whole mark fitted inside a 256 px white square, fresh JPEG with no metadata. It is served only by `GET /api/logo/{name}?s=` to a signed-in user, under a signature key of its own (`wslogo:v1`). The shell shows it beside the workspace's name (sidebar brand switcher, workspace switcher trigger and list, Settings → Workspaces) with the initial as fallback, also when the image fails to load; the switcher's list reads every held workspace's logo in one `mget` (`logoUrls`). `wipeAccount` deletes the pointer and the file (`forgetLogo`). Every route is rate limited.
2. **Crop (rules 3618-3620).** Choosing a picture or a logo opens `CropDialog` (`src/components/ui/CropDialog.tsx`), built on `Dialog` (focus trap, Escape closes): a square canvas frame, drag or arrow keys to move (Shift: one pixel), + and − or a slider to zoom (1-4×). "cover" for a picture, "contain" for a logo (the whole mark fits, white around it). The arithmetic is pure (`src/lib/crop.ts`). Save draws the frame onto a 512 px white square and hands a JPEG `Blob` to `sendImage` (`src/lib/upload-client.ts`), the one upload client both cards use; the server checks and re-encodes it again. Logical layout only; the image is never mirrored in RTL.
3. **S3-compatible store (rules 3613-3617).** `MEDIA_STORAGE=s3` with `S3_ENDPOINT`, `S3_REGION`, `S3_BUCKET`, `S3_ACCESS_KEY_ID` and `S3_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY` switches `storage()` to `s3Storage` (`src/lib/media/s3.ts`); any missing or malformed value leaves it "not configured" (the store off), never a half-working bucket. Requests are signed with AWS Signature Version 4 from `node:crypto` (`src/lib/media/sigv4.ts`, pure, tested against AWS's published examples), go path-style to the configured origin only and follow no redirect; names are the store's own; `put` never overwrites (`If-None-Match: *`); objects stay private and are streamed through `/m/`, `/api/avatar/` and `/api/logo/`. `signedUpload` grants the browser one direct upload: a POST policy by default (the bucket enforces the exact key, the exact type, 1..maxBytes and the expiry), a presigned PUT pinning the exact declared length with `S3_UPLOAD=put`, none with `S3_UPLOAD=off`. The bucket's origin joins the report-only `connect-src` only while the switch and direct upload are on; the enforced policy has no `connect-src`. Nothing logs a key, a signature, an owner, a name or a URL.

### 124.1 Releases

Expand only: `wslogo:`, `lock:wslogo:`, the `w:{aid}` upload owner, the `logo` ticket kind and `WorkspaceRow.logo` are new and optional; an older build ignores them. The S3 backend is off until `MEDIA_STORAGE=s3` is set; switching it on starts with an empty bucket (files already on the disk are not moved: a copy script would be a later, separate step). Nothing to contract and no data script.


## 121. Paid extra seats (2026-10-23, branch `claude/sup-paid-seats` on `claude/m3-customer-roles-b97625`, rules 3360-3379)

Rule file `docs/system/tests/3360-paid-seats.md`, spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-23-paid-seats.md`. Files: `src/lib/pricing.ts` (`EXTRA_SEAT_MONTHLY_SAR`, `extraSeatsSold`, `includedSeats`, `paidExtraSeats`, `seatLimit`, `billableExtraSeats`, `vatHalalasOf`, `extraSeatsLine`, `monthlyTotal`), `src/lib/team.ts` (`createInvite` with `addSeat`, `settleExtraSeats`, `teamView`), `src/lib/billing.ts` (`priceFor`, `renewPaymob`, `chargeSavedCard`, `endPlanOnRefund`), `src/lib/roles.ts` (`billing.seats`), `src/app/api/team/invite/route.ts`, `src/components/app/TeamPanel.tsx`, `src/components/app/settings/SeatsBillingCard.tsx`, `src/lib/admin-workspace.ts`.

1. The price (rules 3360-3362). 25 SAR per extra seat per month before VAT, written once as `EXTRA_SEAT_MONTHLY_SAR` in `pricing.ts`; every screen and the renewal read it from there. VAT is added the same way as for a plan: 15% on the sum of plan and seats, rounded to the halala. A seat is priced by the month: a yearly renewal charges twelve months of each seat (section 110 item 10).
2. Who may buy (rules 3363, 3366-3368). The owner's own session only (`billing.seats`: never an admin, never the founder impersonating, never an AI client's token; there is no billing role). Only a plan that sells seats: starter, pro and business. A trial cannot buy: its 3 seats are the trial's, and paying for a seat would mean paying before the plan. Agency cannot buy: its seats are the founder's `seats` override. A locked bill cannot buy. Starter includes 1 seat (the owner), so a starter owner's first invite is already a paid seat.
3. The count (rules 3363-3364). One source of truth: `extraSeats` on the account record (expand: absent reads as 0, junk reads as 0, capped at `MAX_SEAT_OVERRIDE`). The limit is `includedSeats(u) + paidExtraSeats(u)`, and the founder's `seats` override still sets the included part. Seats used are computed in exactly one place, M3's `seatCount(members, invites)` in `src/lib/team.ts` (section 120, rule 3753; `seatsUsed` wraps it): owner + admins + Editors not paused + unexpired invites, with the first `FREE_VIEWERS` (3) Viewers free. The invite limit check (the count with the new invite added, so a free Viewer invite never asks to pay), `settleExtraSeats` (so the seats billed at 25 SAR), `teamView`, `entitlements` and the console `workspaceView` all call it, so a free Viewer is never billed and the 4th Viewer is one paid seat. `enforceSeats` is M3's and already skips the free Viewers. Pending invites count because an invite already holds a seat in the existing accounting (`createInvite` and `acceptInvite` both count it), and accepting never buys a seat: if invites did not count, a pending invite would take a seat nobody paid for when accepted. Billable = max(0, used − included).
4. Buying (rules 3366, 3369, 3373). An invite over the limit answers 409 `{error:"seats", used, limit, included, extra, unitSar, canBuy}`. The Members page shows the confirm dialog in Arabic or English ("adds 25 SAR/month + VAT", with the timing and the leave rule; on a yearly plan the total charged now, item 5) and only then sends `addSeat: true`. The server refuses without that boolean and checks everything again under `lock:team:{aid}`, then under `lock:user` re-reads the account before it raises `extraSeats` by one: exactly one seat may be missing, so a team already over its limit (a downgrade) is never fitted by buying several seats behind one click. Concurrent invites cannot oversell: the second waits for the team lock and counts the first.
5. When it is charged (rule 3370, rules 4040-4051 in docs/system/tests/4040-yearly-seat-charge.md). On a monthly plan the seat is usable at once and charged from the next renewal, with no proration for the rest of the current month. On an active yearly subscription paid past today the seat is charged now, before it exists (rule 4043):
   - The price is the months left of the paid year (`seatMonthsLeft`, rule 4041: whole months on the subscription's anchor day, a started month counts in full; on the anchor day itself the month ending that day does not) × `EXTRA_SEAT_MONTHLY_SAR`, VAT on the sum, priced in code by `priceFor("seat")` (rule 4042), so the callback's amount check recomputes it. The 409 and GET /api/team carry `charge` {months, baseHalalas, totalHalalas}; the confirm dialog states the total charged now, and the server charges only when the confirm sends that same total back as `seatHalalas` (rule 4050), otherwise it answers the 409 again with today's amount.
   - The charge is a saved-card charge of the subscription's provider (`chargeSeat`), sent with no team or account lock held: one per account at a time (`pay:seatlock:{acct}`, 409 `seat_busy`), none while an earlier one is unanswered (`pay:seatwait:{acct}`, 409 `seat_pending`, rules 4044-4045). The account lock re-reads the subscription and the card right before the charge. A decline is 402 `seat_declined`, a provider failure before the charge 502 `seat_failed`; neither adds a seat or an invite.
   - A seat intent (`kind: "seat"`) that is paid, through our own call, a late callback or Geidea's reconciliation, raises `extraSeats` by one under the account lock (`applySeat`) and is recorded as a payment (a partner's commission follows it as for a renewal, rule 4048); it never changes the plan, `paidThrough`, the anchor or the credits. Money always means a seat: a charge answered late still grants it, and the owner then invites with no new payment.
   - Then the invite is made under `lock:team` with everything checked again. Paid but the subscription moved meanwhile (another period, provider, plan or owner): refunded at once, 409 `seat_changed` (rule 4046). Paid but the invite cannot be made (the address was taken meanwhile, the actor lost the right): the seat goes back and the charge is refunded, the answer carries `refunded: true` (rule 4047); a seat someone already fills stays paid. A refund the provider refuses is logged (`seat_refund_failed`) for the founder.
   - No saved-card charge for it (Geidea without `GEIDEA_MIT=1`, a subscription of another provider, renewal off or no card): no seat is sold online on a yearly plan (rule 4049). `canBuy` is false and `blocked` is `no_card` (the page asks to turn renewal on with a card on the Subscription page) or `no_saved_charge` (the page says to write to us).
   - The founder's refund of a seat payment voids it and never ends the plan or removes the card (rule 4051); the seat stays until the renewal trims it.
6. Leaving (rule 3371). When a member leaves or is removed, or an invite is cancelled or expires, nothing changes mid-period and nothing is refunded. At the renewal `settleExtraSeats` lowers `extraSeats` to min(stored, billable now) before the period is priced; it never raises it. A seat bought for an invite whose email then failed (the invite is withdrawn) is trimmed the same way, so it is never charged. A yearly seat already paid for the rest of the year (item 5) stays usable until the renewal, with no refund, and is trimmed then if unused.
7. The renewal (rule 3372). `renewPaymob` (Paymob and Geidea alike) settles the seats first (answering `{provider}_seats_busy` on a lock timeout, before the day's attempt is spent), then `chargeSavedCard` charges `priceFor("renewal", {plan, extraSeats})` = (plan, or a lower locked price, + seats × 25) + VAT, stores `extraSeats` on the intent, and the callback's amount check recomputes the same. A first checkout or a plan switch never includes seats; the seats ride on the next renewal. The payment row (`recordPayment`) and the partner commission use the charged amount, so they include the seats. A refund that ends the plan clears `extraSeats`; a founder plan change to a plan that sells no seats clears it too.
8. Geidea (rule 3376). Since Geidea landed on main (#456, flows section 109) the same `renewPaymob` renews it: `settleExtraSeats`, then `priceFor("renewal", {plan, extraSeats})`, with `extraSeats` stored on the Geidea intent so the shared callback amount check includes the seats.
9. Hand plans (rule 3377). Seats on a plan the founder set by hand (no Paymob billing) are counted and shown but no code charges them; the founder invoices them. `settleExtraSeats` runs only in the Paymob renewal.
10. Screens (rules 3365, 3375). Billing page: included seats, paid extra seats, seats in use, the price per seat, and plan + seats + VAT = monthly total (list price; a price lock can lower the plan part). Members page: used of available seats, the paid seats line, and the confirm step. Console Workspace tab: included and extra seats, read only; staff cannot add a paid seat (`billing.seats` is in `IMPERSONATION_BLOCKED` and the console has no write for it).
11. Release step: expand. `extraSeats` is a new optional field: old code ignores it (its `seatLimit` reads only `plan` and `seats`, so on a rollback a team with paid seats reads as over its limit, as after a downgrade, and new invites are refused until a seat frees), and the new code reads its absence as 0. `PayIntent.extraSeats` is optional the same way. No conversion script.

## 125. One-time data fills from the console (2026-10-24, branch `claude/console-account-ids`, rules 4100-4106)

The founder could not run `scripts/account-ids.ts` from a terminal. The same work now runs from the console, on the app's own store connection: no terminal and no store credentials anywhere. Permissions in `permissions.md` row 265; tests in `docs/system/tests/4100-console-conversions.md`.

1. The work moved (rule 4100). `convertAccountIds` lives in `src/lib/conversions/account-ids.ts` and S1's `run` in `src/lib/conversions/multi-org-index.ts`, unchanged. `scripts/account-ids.ts` and `scripts/multi-org-index.ts` are thin wrappers that import and re-export them, so the terminal commands in sections 88.1 and the S1 step still work as written.
2. The card. The console Integrations page (`/{lang}/admin/integrations`) shows "One-time data fills" (`ConversionsCard`), Arabic and English, one row per fill: "Account numbers" (R1) with "Check (changes nothing)" (the dry run) and "Apply", and "Workspace lists" (S1) with the check only (it was applied on 2026-10-23). The words are written for the founder, not an engineer (since 2026-10-24): the intro explains the fixed numbers (a national id next to a phone number), the R1/S1 code is shown small beside each title, the count labels say which lines must be 0, the help line says that 0 in every "to fill" line means done and that conflicts mean stop and contact engineering, and the apply dialog says the reason is an audit-log note and shows an example in the reason box (`reasonPlaceholder` in `AdminForm`, never sent). The card says a second run writes nothing.
3. Dry run. "Dry run" posts `POST /api/admin/conversions/{name} {apply: false}`: console actor (401) → `data.convert`, owner only (403) → a known name (404) → 20 runs an hour per actor (429) → the console write check → the run under `lock:conversion` (409 `busy`). It reads every account (50 at a time, `pmap`) and writes nothing but its clean mark: a run with 0 conflicts and 0 orphans sets `conversion:clean:{name}` for 15 minutes, a run that is not clean deletes it. The card shows the counts, clean or not clean, and how long it took.
4. Apply. "Apply" opens only after a clean dry run in the same view and asks for a reason; the server asks the second factor, checks the reason, and takes the clean mark with GETDEL, so an apply with no clean dry run in the last 15 minutes is 409 `dry_run_first` and two applies need two dry runs. The fill never overwrites and is idempotent: a second dry run shows 0 ids and 0 indexes to set.
5. Audit. Each run writes one `admin:log` row in the new section `data`: `account_ids_dry_run`, `account_ids_apply` or `multi_org_index_dry_run`, the counts in `what` (`account_ids_apply accounts 12 missing 0 ...`) and `after`, the reason on an apply. The answer and the row hold counts only: never an address, an id or a key.
6. Time. The route sets `maxDuration = 300` and returns `ms`. If a run is cut by the host's request timeout, nothing is half-written that a second run does not finish, and the lock ends on its own after 300 seconds.

### 125.1 Release step (rule 679)

**Expand.** New: the audit section `data` (older readers pass an unknown section through), the keys `lock:conversion` and `conversion:clean:{name}`, and the staff action `data.convert`. No stored shape changes. Rules 4107-4119 are reserved and unused.

## 126. Thakeen support edits a customer workspace from the console (2026-10-23, branch `claude/sup-admin-workspace-edit`, rules 3550-3569)

Rule file `docs/system/tests/3550-admin-workspace-edit.md`. After supastarter's admin organization edit. Files: `src/lib/admin-workspace-edit.ts` (`parseEdit`, `cleanWorkspaceName`, `consoleActor`, `applyEdit`, `tellOwnerOfEdit`), `src/app/api/admin/users/[email]/workspace/edit/route.ts` (permissions row 266), `src/components/admin/WorkspaceEdit.tsx` (`useWorkspaceEdit`), the Workspace tab `src/components/admin/WorkspacePanel.tsx`, `src/lib/team-invite-mail.ts` (the invitation mail, moved out of the customer route so both send the same words).

1. Who (rule 3550). Console → Users → an account → Workspace. The key, the founder's own session and staff with the console role `owner` (`STAFF_MAY["workspace.edit"]`) see Rename, Invite, and on each member row Remove and on each invite row Cancel; support staff see the same tab read only. Every change asks for the console code like the other sensitive console writes, and a reason of 3 to 200 characters.
2. Which account (rules 3551-3552). The path's address picks the account exactly as the view does (`accountForChosen`); the body names the owner address the panel showed, and a mismatch (the address now stands for another account) is refused with 409. The change runs as the account OWNER (`consoleActor`), through the customer's own functions, so the team rules, seat limits and protections are the owner's, never wider.
3. The four changes (rules 3553-3555). Rename sets `workspaceName` (1 to 80 characters, control and bidi characters removed). Invite calls `createInvite` with the owner as inviter: the seat limit holds, the invite is mailed only to the invitee (so staff never hold a join link), the console refuses it while mail is not configured, and a failed send withdraws it. Remove calls `removeMember`; the owner can never be removed, and no role change or ownership transfer exists here (transfer stays the owner's flow). Cancel finds the invite by address in the account's own invite index and calls `cancelInvite`.
4. Limits (rule 3556). 30 changes an hour per console actor, 30 a day per account, and the customer route's own 5 invitations a day per address, shared.
5. What the customer sees (rules 3557, 3561). The team log row carries `via: "console"`, which the team page shows as done by Thakeen support, with the owner as the actor; the staff member is never named. The owner gets one in-app notice (bell) saying Thakeen support changed the team, without the address or role, since the bell is shared by every login of the account.
6. The trail (rule 3559). One `admin:log` row per change: `section: users`, `action: workspace.rename|invite|remove|cancel_invite`, the owner as target, the reason, before and after. If that row cannot be written, a rename or an invite is put back and the answer is 500; a removal or cancellation cannot be put back, so a `log.error` line records it.
7. The view (rule 3558). `GET /api/admin/users/{email}/workspace` also answers `workspaceName` and `may`; `may` only decides whether the buttons show. Each change asks for a confirmation; Remove and Cancel ask to type the address. After a change the tab reads the view again.

### 126.1 Releases

Expand only. One new team log kind, `workspace_renamed`, which `readTeamLog` of the previous release skips as unparsable (it drops rows it cannot read), and `workspaceName`, a field `createWorkspace` already writes. New rate counters `rate:wsedit:*` expire on their own. No environment variable and no contract step.

## 127. Workspace name, invite decline and the folding rail (2026-10-23, branch `claude/sup-workspace-ui`, rules 3115-3129)

Rule file `docs/system/tests/3115-workspace-ui.md`, test `tests/workspace-ui.test.ts`. Ideas taken from Supastarter's organization settings, invitation page and sidebar; built on Thakeen's own account, team and rail code.

1. Rename (rules 3115-3118). Settings, Account shows the owner a "Workspace name" card (`AccountSettings`, owner only on the page and checked again on the server). `PUT /api/account/workspace {name}`: `currentUser` (401), `forbid(u, "profile.account")` (403 for admins and members), `cleanWorkspaceName` (control characters and bidi embedding, override and isolate marks removed, white space folded, trimmed; empty is 400 `required`, over 80 characters 400 `too_long`, never cut), the bucket `wsname:{acct}` 10 an hour (429 `slow_down`), then `withUserLock(acct(u))` writes `workspaceName` on the session's own account record. The field existed since M2 (`createWorkspace`), so the stored shape does not change; the handle (`w/{handle}`, from the account id) does not change. `workspaceLabel` (`src/lib/workspace-name.ts`) is now the one display order everywhere (switcher, workspaces page, invitations inbox, join preview, founder people view): the name the owner set, else the default brand's name, else the owner's name (rule 3116; before, the brand's name came first and would have hidden a rename).
2. Decline (rules 3119-3122). The join page (`JoinForm`) offers "Decline invitation" under every invite preview (new login, sign in, join as, other address), asks once, then sends `POST /api/team/decline {token}`. Public like accept, rate limited per IP (`team:decline:ip:{ip}`, 10 per 15 min), on the workspace guard's free list. `declineInvite` (`src/lib/team.ts`) checks the token's shape, finds the invite from its SHA-256 only, takes `inviteuse:{hash}` (so an accept and a decline of one invite never both run) and `lock:team:{aid}`, re-reads the invite (stored, unexpired, in its account's index, account exists; else 400 `invalid`), then `dropInvites` (record, index entry, the invitee's inbox entry) and one team-log row `invite_declined` with the invited address as actor and target and no person id. The route rings the owner's bell (`notifyItem`, info, link to the team page); a failure there never fails the request. The page forgets the token in this tab and shows a calm confirmation with a link home. Release step: expand. `invite_declined` is appended to `TEAM_LOG_KINDS` and the team-log actor's `pid` is now optional; an older build skips a row whose kind it does not know (`parseStored` drops it), so a rollback hides those rows and nothing else. Contract: none needed.
3. Folding rail (rules 3123-3127). On a desktop the rail has a fold button at its top (`aria-expanded`, label "Collapse sidebar" or "Expand sidebar" in the page's language). Folded it is `w-16`: the fold button, the bell, every destination as an icon link with its name as `aria-label` and `title` (the approvals count in the label), and the account menu from the avatar. The choice is the cookie `thakeen_rail` (`collapsed` or `open`, a year, lax, Secure on https), written by the button and read by the app layout with `cookies()`, so the first paint has the chosen width. The arrow is the directional `next` icon with `mirror` (it flips in Arabic) and turned toward the rail's edge while open. The phone header and its sheet are unchanged and never fold.
4. Rules 3128-3129 are reserved for this change and unused.

## 128. The owner's PostHog through Composio (read only, 2026-10-25, branch `claude/posthog-composio-98602d`, rules 4230-4237)

Files: `src/lib/product-stats.ts`, `src/lib/connectors/composio-catalog.ts` (`posthog`), `src/lib/connectors/via-composio.ts` (`projectsOf`, `selectProjectVia`), `src/lib/connectors/registry.ts`, `src/lib/connectors/working.ts` (`READS_PRODUCT`), `api/connect/[provider]` and its callback, `api/cron/daily`, `src/lib/stats.ts`, `src/components/app/IntegrationsPanel.tsx`.

1. Connect: `posthog` is a Composio-only provider (toolkit `posthog`, auth type API key, auth config `COMPOSIO_AUTH_POSTHOG`, version `20261018_00`). The customer types their own PostHog personal API key, and picks the US or EU cloud, on Composio's hosted page: Thakeen never sees or stores the key. `connectViaComposio` lists the projects with `POSTHOG_LIST_ALL_PROJECTS_ACROSS_ORGANIZATIONS`, keeps numeric ids only, chooses the first (`meta.phProject`, `phName`) and keeps the list in `meta.phProjects` when there are two or more. The callback reads once at once (`syncProduct`); a failure only logs.
2. Read (`syncProduct`): through `POSTHOG_CREATE_QUERY_IN_PROJECT_BY_ID` with `{project_id, query: {kind: "HogQLQuery", query}}`, four constant queries over `$pageview` events for the 28 days ending yesterday (UTC) and the 28 before: pageviews, unique visitors (`count(DISTINCT person_id)`), sessions (`count(DISTINCT $session_id)`), the top 10 page paths and the top 10 referring domains. No event payload, no person-level row and no property other than the path and the referring domain is ever selected. Only the totals are fatal.
3. The numbers that reach reports: pageviews, unique visitors and sessions (each with the previous 28 days where PostHog returned a figure above 0), the top 10 pages (paths scrubbed of query, fragment, ids, addresses and UUIDs) and the top 10 referring domains. Stored as `productstats:{scope}`, deleted with the account.
4. Daily and refresh: after `syncSiteIfDue`, `dailyFor` calls `syncProductIfDue` (a Composio record whose last read is over 20 hours old; a refused token marks the record for reconnect). `select_project` (admin+) saves a stored project and reads it at once; `sync_product` (member) reads at once; both only while `productsync:{scope}` grants (6 an hour per workspace).
5. Dana: `statsFor` adds `productDataBlock` after the site block: the figures inside `<product_data>` serialised with `asData`, paths and domains labelled as data from the public, with rules (null is not read; compare only with `prev`; never add these figures to the Google Analytics ones; no visitor-level detail exists).
6. Nothing writes to PostHog and no person-level or event-level data is stored: the catalog lists no write tool, and `READS_PRODUCT` keeps the figures out of the store and ad banners.
7. Founder steps (not done by the code): create the PostHog (API key) auth config in the Composio dashboard and set its id as `COMPOSIO_AUTH_POSTHOG` (staging first), add `posthog` to `COMPOSIO_PROVIDERS`, run `scripts/check-composio-pins.ts` for the toolkit, and connect a test PostHog project on staging to measure the two response shapes (rule 4238). The customer's personal API key needs read scopes: at least `query:read` and `project:read` (and likely `organization:read` for the project list).


## 129. Thakeen v2 phase 0: the action gateway in log mode and the run log (2026-10-25, branch `claude/thakeen-v2-intelligence-152d6b`, rules 4500-4518)

The plan: the Claude Doc "Thakeen v2 — PRD, Spec and Roadmap" (components C1 and C2). Files: `src/lib/v2-flags.ts`, `src/lib/gateway.ts`, `src/lib/runs.ts`, wired in `src/lib/chat-run-tool.ts` (`runTool`), `src/app/api/mcp/route.ts` (`tools/call`), `src/lib/chat.ts` (`chat`), `src/lib/generate-batch.ts` (`generateBatch`), `src/lib/metered.ts`, `src/lib/publish-once.ts` and the account wipe (`src/lib/account.ts`).

Action gateway ("Action gateway", C1, `GATEWAY_MODE`):

1. Every chat tool call (`runTool`) and every MCP `tools/call` passes `gatedTool` / `gatedMcp`. Off (the shipped value), they are plain calls: nothing is read or written.
2. In log mode the gateway reads its inputs the way the old path reads them, from the session user and stored settings, never from the model's words: whether the tool is offered to the talking agent (`toolsFor`), the role's action (`TOOL_ACTION` / `MCP_TOOL_ACTION` with `can`), and the owner's tool authority for the agent (`authorityOf` on `getGov`). Who started it: `mcp` over MCP, `agent` in a chat turn with an agent, `person` otherwise (`routine` is reserved for phase 2).
3. `decide` is a pure policy table, first match wins: not offered → deny; role → deny; MCP and `publish_item` or `handoff_note` → deny; authority off → deny; `publish_item` → ask (a person's approval of the item, checked on the stored item by the old path); authority ask → ask; else allow.
4. The `check` row (tool, initiator, agent, verdict, rule, the open run's id) is pushed to `audit:{scope}` before the call runs; the `done` row (outcome `ran`, `refused` or `error`, and `mismatch`) after. A mismatch is a call the gateway would deny that the old path ran; it is also logged as a warning. The outcome of a chat tool is read from its answer's first words (`refusedAnswer`), of an MCP call from the JSON-RPC answer (`isError` or `error`).
5. Log mode never blocks and never changes an answer; a failure inside the gateway is logged and the call goes on. Enforcement is phase 4 and needs the mismatch evidence first.

Run log ("Run log", C2, `RUN_LOG`):

6. `withRun` opens one run per chat turn, MCP tool call and batch; a run opened inside another joins it (Node `AsyncLocalStorage`), so one request is one record. Off (the shipped value) it is a plain call.
7. Steps: each metered model call (model, purpose, agent, input tokens including cache reads and writes, output tokens, ok), each tool call (tool, outcome, milliseconds, and the gateway's verdict and rule when it judged), each publish attempt (platform, ok, the answer's code). Never a prompt, reply, tool input or item text. At most 200 steps; the rest are counted in `dropped`.
8. When the run settles, its record is written to `run:{scope}:{id}` (30 days) and its id pushed to `runs:{scope}` (the newest 200, 30 days from the last run). A write failure is logged and never changes the answer. `replay` reads a run back in order with its totals; no route shows runs yet (the work view is phase 3).
9. The account wipe deletes every run record, the index and the audit list of each brand.

### 129.1 Release step (rule 679)

Expand: three new key families, no stored shape changes, both switches off. Turning either on is a later release that changes one constant in `src/lib/v2-flags.ts`; turning it off again leaves rows that expire on their own.

## 130. Thakeen v2 phase 1: scoped memory and recall (2026-10-25, branch `claude/thakeen-v2-p1-memory`, rules 4550-4562)

The plan: the Claude Doc "Thakeen v2 — PRD, Spec and Roadmap" (component C5). Files: `src/lib/memory.ts`, `src/lib/memory-tool.ts`, `src/lib/recall.ts`, `src/lib/memory-index.ts`, the routes `src/app/api/memory/route.ts` and `src/app/api/memory/proposals/[id]/route.ts`, the card `src/components/app/MemoryProposalCard.tsx`, wired in `src/lib/chat.ts`, `src/lib/chat-tools.ts`, `src/lib/chat-reply.ts`, `src/lib/chat-history.ts` (`historyFor`) and the account wipe (`src/lib/account.ts`). Switch: `MEMORY_V2` in `src/lib/v2-flags.ts`, off.

Propose (the `remember` tool):

1. Off, the tool is not offered (`toolsFor`) and `runRemember` answers "Unknown tool."; no route answers but 404; recall reads nothing.
2. On, an agent proposes one fact (at most 300 characters) with a scope: `brand` (every agent), `agent` (its own notes) or `conversation` (this thread). The agent and thread labels come from the turn, never from the model's input. `inferred` marks a fact the agent concluded itself; the card says so.
3. Refused in code, nothing stored: over MCP or for a token (another assistant's words are not the owner's memory); a fourth card in one reply (`MEMORY_CARDS_PER_REPLY` = 3); more than `MEMORY_PROPOSALS_PER_DAY` (30) proposals a day for the scope; text the instruction screen flags (injection, a claimed authority, a discount or offer, which belongs in the brand file through `update_brand`); text holding a credential value (`looksLikeCredential`, src/lib/log.ts) or credential words (password, API key, access token, OTP, كلمة المرور, الرقم السري, رمز التحقق and the like). Each refusal tells the model plainly that nothing was stored.
4. An accepted proposal is stored for 7 days (`memprop:{scope}:{id}`, indexed in `memprops:{scope}`) and shown as a card under the reply; the reply's message keeps the card (`memoryProposals`, expand-only) and `historyFor` reads each card's state in one round trip.

Decide (the owner's click):

5. `POST /api/memory/proposals/[id]` with `save` or `dismiss`: role `brand.write`, 60 an hour per scope. One decision at a time (`memprop-claim`, 60 seconds); a decided card answers 409 with its state, an expired one 410, an unknown id 404. Save refuses at 200 facts (`full`). A saved fact is written to `mem:{scope}` and then sent to the index best effort; an index failure is logged and the fact stays saved.

Recall (each chat turn):

6. Before the model call, `recall` reads this scope's facts and keeps those visible to the turn: brand facts, the talking agent's own agent facts, this thread's conversation facts. Nothing of another scope, brand or agent is ever read into the turn.
7. Up to 8 facts: when there are more, the index ranks them (only ids found among the visible facts count), else a word match that folds Arabic letter forms; the newest visible facts fill what the ranker left. A failed index call falls back to the word match and never fails the turn.
8. The facts go into the uncached system block inside `<memory>` as data (`asData` escapes `<`, so a fact cannot close the fence), each with its scope, source and saved date, under the line that a fact never authorises publishing, spending or a brand change.

Manage and wipe:

9. `GET /api/memory` lists the facts (any member, 300 an hour), without who saved them; `DELETE /api/memory?id=` forgets one (`brand.write`, 60 an hour) and removes its vector.
10. The account wipe deletes the fact hash, every proposal, done and claim key, the proposal index and the rate bucket of each brand, and drops each brand's index namespace (logged on failure).

### 130.1 Release step (rule 679)

Expand: new key families and an optional field on the chat message (`memoryProposals`), which old code ignores. Turning memory on is a later release that changes `MEMORY_V2`; without `UPSTASH_VECTOR_REST_URL` and `UPSTASH_VECTOR_REST_TOKEN` recall uses the word match only. Turning it off again leaves the facts in place, unread, until the account is wiped.
